WINNOW DEVELOPER DOCUMENTATION


*** FORMAT CONVENTIONS ***

Soft wrap. Body paragraphs are ONE long line each; the editor wraps them. Never hard-wrap prose — a re-wrapped paragraph is a whole-paragraph diff, and full-width rule bars wrap into garbage in a narrow pane.

Blank lines do all the structural work. One blank line between paragraphs, between bullets that run to more than one visual line, and after a heading. Two blank lines before a heading.

Headings carry the full breadcrumb, so any heading read out of context still says where it lives. The leading ⏺ marks the depth.

  Level 1     `*** ALL CAPS ***`, no breadcrumb (it is the root)
  Level 2     `⏺ Top Level > Subsection Title`
  Level 3     `⏺⏺ Top Level > Subsection > Sub-subsection Title`

Breadcrumbs are Title Case, leaving short function words (and, the, a, of, to, vs, with) in lower case.

Indentation is minimal: prose and top-level bullets sit at column 0. Only these indent — nested bullets (2 spaces), fixed-width blocks (2 spaces), and the definition-style lists above.

Bullets use `-` at the top level and `•` when nested. Fixed-width material (call sequences, tables, code) is indented 2 spaces and left exactly as written, so it never re-wraps.

The Table of Contents lists each heading's own title only — no breadcrumb, no ⏺ prefix — with depth shown by indent.

File references stay as `path/to/file:line`.


*** DIRECTIVES ***

When a change alters documented behavior, update this document in the same pass as the code. A doc that lags the code is worse than no doc, because it is trusted.

Keep the Table of Contents in sync. Whenever a heading is added, removed, or renamed — including a section whose content has outgrown its title — update the matching ToC entry in the same edit.

Follow the format conventions above for anything new. In particular, do not hard-wrap prose: leave each paragraph as one line and let the editor wrap it.

*** INITIALIZATION ***

Persistent settings are saved between sessions using QSettings. Persistent settings form two categories at runtime: action settings and preference settings. Action settings are boolean and maintained by the action item's setChecked value. Preferences can be anything and are maintained as public variables, mostly in MW (MainWindow) and managed in the PrefDlg class.

Not all settings can be read immediately, as the classes that use them may not yet have been created. Examples are IconView parameters and the recent folders menu list, since IconView and the menus have not been created yet. Therefore some settings are assigned up front and the rest are assigned as needed.


⏺ Initialization > Startup Sequence

- Load QSettings
- Set default values if no settings available (first time run)
- Set preference settings including:
  • General (previous folder etc.)
  • Slideshow
  • Cache
  • Full screen docks visible
  • List of external apps
  • Bookmarks
  • Recent folders
  • Ingest history folders
  • Workspaces
- Create actions and set checked based on persistent values from QSettings
- Create bookmarks with persistent values from QSettings
- Update external apps with persistent values from QSettings
- Load shortcuts (based on being able to edit shortcuts)
- Execute updateState function to implement all persistent state settings
- Select a folder
  • Load datamodel with QDir info on each image file
  • Add the rest of the metadata to datamodel (incl. thumbs as icons)
  • Update the image cache


⏺ Initialization > Dock Window State (Versioning)

The QMainWindow dock layout is persisted with saveState()/restoreState() under QSettings "WindowState" (MW::settings writes it; MW::showEvent restores it). Both calls pass

  MW::winnowStateVersion.

History: v1 added developDock, v2 added historyDock, v3 added presetsDock, v4 added catalogDock.

RULE: BUMP winnowStateVersion whenever the SET of docks changes (added or removed), AND add the new dock to the table in MW::placeDocksAddedSince. A WindowState saved by an older build does not contain the new dock; left to Qt that dock is dumped loose into whatever area it happens to be in, which leaves its group orphaned as empty "zombie" tab bars -- the dock layout then never converges and the tabbed docks flicker continuously.

MIGRATION, NOT REJECTION. The version tag identifies a state written by a different dock set; MW::restoreWindowState then restores that state AT ITS OWN VERSION (walking winnowStateVersion down until restoreState() accepts it) and calls MW::placeDocksAddedSince to tabify the docks added since into the group they belong to, taking their visibility from their dock action rather than from the stale state. The user keeps their layout across an upgrade. Only a state that is unreadable at every version falls through, and then MW::showEvent calls defaultWorkspace() rather than leaving the raw createDocks() layout on screen.

This replaced a straight reject (restoreState returns false, changes nothing, the createDocks/initialize() layout stands, re-saved at the current version on exit). That "self-healed after one launch" but silently reset the user's ENTIRE layout every time a dock was added -- most visibly thumbDock, which fell back from the bottom to the left area under the folder group. Adding catalogDock (v4) is what surfaced it.

WORKSPACES get the same migration. Their state blobs are written UNVERSIONED (plain saveState(), so an old workspace still restores), and a separate "stateVersion" key in each workspace's settings records the dock set it was saved with -- absent means 0. MW::invokeWorkspace calls placeDocksAddedSince with it after restoring; without that, invoking a pre-catalog workspace left the Catalog panel squatting loose in the left area.

Keep the built-in fallback layout sane regardless: do NOT tabify a dock in one area with a dock in another area (e.g. the left-area metadataDock with the right-area embelDock) -- a cross-area tabify drags both into one area and crams every dock into a single tab group, where the raised tab oscillates against the squeezed central widget. embel + develop + history + presets tab together on the RIGHT (see initialize() tabifyDockWidget calls).

developDock (added with the raw editor) was the first dock to trip the zombie-tab-bar flicker: the pre-existing saved state predated it AND, once saved during a flicker, the corrupt state perpetuated itself across launches until the version bump discarded it.


⏺ Initialization > Responsive Dock Tab Titles (Text <-> Graphic)

When several docks are tabbed together in a narrow area, their text titles ("Folders", "Bookmarks", ...) may not all fit. MW::updateDockTabGraphics (gated by G::useDockTitleGraphic) measures the width the text titles need against the width of the visible dock: if they fit the tabs show text, otherwise EVERY tab in the group switches to a small white glyph (a QLabel centred over each tab, since QMainWindow does not paint a dock's windowIcon on tabs and macOS paints the tab natively).

THE FAILURE MODE: if any dock in the group has NO registered glyph, the bar can never resolve to graphics -- there is no width at which all tabs are satisfied -- so the layout thrashes and the tabs flicker continuously. (This is separate from the versioning flicker above; same symptom, different cause.) developDock hit this because it was added to the group without a glyph.

RULE: EVERY dock that can be tabbed into the group MUST register a glyph. When adding a new dock, do all of:

1. Create images/icon16/<name>_white.png  -- a 16x16 white-on-transparent glyph matching the weight of the existing ones (foldertree/bookmarks/filters/ metadata/embellish/develop).
2. Register it in winnow.qrc.

3. Add <dockTabText> -> ":/images/icon16/<name>_white.png" to the graphicFor hash AND <dockTabText> -> <dock> to the dockFor hash, both in MW::updateDockTabGraphics.
4. Append <dockTabText> to dockTextNames in initialize() (dock-bar detection).
5. Add a tooltip case in MW::dockTabToolTip (dock name + shortcut).
6. Bump winnowStateVersion AND add a row for the dock to MW::placeDocksAddedSince (see "Dock Window State" above -- the set of docks changed, and a state saved before it existed has to be told where the dock goes).

A missing glyph resource is handled gracefully at runtime (the tab keeps its text title), but that just reproduces the flicker when the group is too narrow, so treat steps 1-3 as mandatory, not optional.


⏺ Initialization > Dropped Dock Lands Last (Rightmost Tab)   [WORK IN PROGRESS - DISABLED]

STATUS: disabled. The dockLocationChanged connection in initialize() is commented out because it broke dock-tab selection (clicking a tab no longer raised its dock). The code (MW::moveDroppedDockLast) is left intact for a later fix. The rest of this note describes the intended behavior.

QMainWindow inserts a dock dropped into an existing tab group at the drop position (often the front). MW::moveDroppedDockLast (connected to every group dock's dockLocationChanged, next to scheduleDockTabUpdate) overrides this: after the drop settles it reads the group in visual tab order and, if the dropped dock is not already last, re-tabifies it after the current last dock (tabifyDockWidget(last, moved)) so it becomes the rightmost tab. The "already last" early-out makes the re-tabify's own dockLocationChanged a no-op, so there is no recursion. Tab identity uses the text title, falling back to the learned dockTabTitleByKey map in graphic mode. (Reordering by dragging a TAB within a group is left to Qt -- only drops that fire dockLocationChanged are forced last.) A new dock added to the connect loop in initialize() inherits this.


⏺ Initialization > Workspaces (and the Winnow Default Workspace)

A workspace is a snapshot of the app appearance: window screen/geometry/maximised, the QMainWindow dock state, dock and status bar visibility, the central view (loupe, grid, table, compare), thumbView/gridView icon parameters, and a few process settings (colour manage, sort column/direction).  See `Main/workspaces.cpp`.

Up to 10 user workspaces live in MW::workspaces (Ctrl+0 .. Ctrl+9, Window > Workspace).  They are persisted as the QSettings array "Workspaces". MW::readWorkspaceSettings / MW::writeWorkspaceSettings read and write ONE workspace at the current QSettings position, so the array loops and the default workspace below share the same field list -- add a new WorkspaceData field in those two functions plus MW::snapshotWorkspace and MW::invokeWorkspace.

THE DEFAULT WORKSPACE (Ctrl+Shift+W, Window > Default Workspace) is separate from the 10 user workspaces.  It is also what is applied on a first run, when there are no QSettings to restore, and is the way back when part of the app is "stranded" on a monitor that is no longer attached.

- MW::builtInDefaultWorkspace is the hard-coded layout Winnow ships with.

- The user can replace it: Window > Manage Workspaces, select "Winnow default workspace" (the first dropdown item, above a separator and the saved workspaces -- G::isRory only, the item and its separator are omitted otherwise) and press "Update to current layout".  MW::updateDefaultWorkspace snapshots the current layout into MW::defaultWs, sets isCustomDefaultWorkspace and saves it to the QSettings group "DefaultWorkspace".

- MW::defaultWorkspace dispatches: the saved layout if isCustomDefaultWorkspace (set by MW::loadDefaultWorkspace when the group exists), else the built-in one.

- The default workspace cannot be deleted or renamed in the dialog (the Delete button is disabled and the combo's line edit is read-only while it is selected), and it never appears in the Ctrl+0..9 workspace menu.

In WorkspaceDlg the dropdown index is NOT the workspace number: index 0 is the default workspace, index 1 is a separator, and saved workspace n is at index n + firstWorkspaceIndex (2, or 0 when the default item is not shown).  Use WorkspaceDlg::workspaceIndex(), which returns -1 for the default and the separator.

The stylesheet also matters here: WidgetCSS::comboBox styles the popup ("QComboBox QAbstractItemView::item"), so QStyleSheetStyle paints Qt's separator item as a blank row and the line vanishes.  ComboSeparatorDelegate (workspacedlg.cpp) draws it.  Any other styled combo needing a separator has the same problem.


*** FOLDER LOADING PIPELINE ***

Use G::isFlowLogger or G::isLogger to trace program progress.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Overview

Loading a folder runs in two phases that do NOT overlap with each other. Phase 1 populates the DataModel with one row per eligible file and runs on the GUI thread. Phase 2 reads metadata and icons (MetaRead, on worker threads) and caches full-size images (ImageCache, on worker threads); these two activities within Phase 2 DO overlap.

Phase 1 - Build the DataModel (GUI thread)

- A folder or bookmark click calls MW::folderSelectionChange. The mouse modifier decides whether the folder replaces the current selection (resetDataModel = true) or is added to it.

- When resetDataModel is true the prior load is torn down first: MW::stop halts the worker threads, MW::reset clears state, and DataModel::clearDataModel empties the model. clearDataModel calls QCoreApplication::removePostedEvents(this, QEvent::MetaCall) BEFORE clear(). QStandardItemModel coalesces item edits into a posted _q_emitItemChanged meta-call; if that (or a stale worker setData/setIcon meta-call) fired after clear() freed the items it would dereference freed pointers and crash. DataModel::newInstance then bumps dm->instance so any surviving stale worker signals are ignored.

- Subfolders are counted (Utilities::subFolderTree) and the folder plus, in the recursive case, every subfolder are queued by DataModel::enqueueFolderSelection / enqueueOp. macOS .photoslibrary bundles are filtered out (they hold huge numbers of derivative files).

- DataModel::scheduleProcessing starts DataModel::processNextBatch, which drains the folder queue in time-sliced batches. It yields to the event loop every ~30 ms (kTimeBudgetMs), with a secondary cap of 64 folders per tick, rescheduling itself with a zero-delay timer. This time-slicing is what keeps the GUI responsive while a large recursive tree loads.

- DataModel::addFolder handles one directory: dir.entryInfoList() lists the eligible files, they are sorted (DataModel::lessThan, or lessThanCombineRawJpg when raw+jpg combining is on), and each file is appended as a row (setRowCount + DataModel::addFileDataForRow, which fills the OS file columns and performs raw+jpg pairing).

Cost note: the sort/filter proxy (dm->sf) inserts each new row into sorted position at O(N), so the whole load is O(N^2). Time-slicing bounds each GUI freeze to roughly one folder's insert time but does not change that total; a single very large folder loaded late can still block briefly.

- When the queue empties DataModel emits folderChange, handled by MW::folderChanged. It re-enables the panels, sets the current index, and either finishes immediately (DataModel::isAllMetadataAttempted, e.g. a pure removal with no new rows) or starts Phase 2.

Phase 2 - Read metadata/icons and cache images (worker threads)

- MW::folderChanged starts MetaRead with a queued QMetaObject::invokeMethod(metaRead, "initialize") followed by MetaRead::setStartRow(startRow). MetaRead lives on metaReadThread.

- MetaRead::dispatchReaders / dispatch assign rows to Reader workers, walking outward from startRow (the current image) so the user's position is served first. Each Reader reads metadata (Reader::readMetadata -> DataModel::addMetadataForItem) and a G::maxIconSize (256 px) icon (Reader::readIcon -> DataModel::setIcon1, stored in Qt::DecorationRole), delivered to the GUI thread over queued connections. Backpressure (dm->queuedReaderEvents, cap 4 x readerCount) defers dispatch when the GUI thread falls behind draining those events.

- Once the start row itself has been read, MetaRead emits select, which routes Selection::setCurrentIndex -> MW::updateChange -> MW::fileSelectionChange. That loads the current image and starts ImageCache, so ImageCache runs concurrently with the rest of MetaRead.

- ImageCache caches full-size images ahead of and behind the current position: ImageCache::setCurrentPosition -> updateTargets -> launchDecoders -> decodeNextImage -> ImageDecoder (decode / rotate / colorManage) -> ImageCache::fillCache -> cacheImage -> nextToCache.

- MetaRead::done signals MW::folderChangeCompleted, which builds the filters, re-enables the sort/filter menus, applies a single sort pass, and resizes the table columns.

Instances and aborting

- Every new primary folder increments dm->instance (DataModel::newInstance). Worker signals carry the instance they were issued under; DataModel slots (setIcon1, addMetadataForItem, setValSf, ...) drop any call whose instance no longer matches, so lagging signals from an abandoned load are harmless.

- G::stop and dm->abort are the cooperative stop flags. MW::stop sets them, signals the workers to abort, and waits (event-driven, 3 s cap) for them to acknowledge before resetting.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > New Folder Selected (Call Sequence)

  FSTree::mousePressEvent / FSTree::select / BookMarks item click
  MW::folderSelectionChange             // prep based on mouse modifier
  MW::stop                              // halt prior load (if reset)
  MW::reset                             // reset parameters
  DataModel::clearDataModel             // purge meta-calls, then clear()
  DataModel::newInstance                // bump instance (new folder)
  DataModel::enqueueFolderSelection
  DataModel::enqueueOp
  DataModel::scheduleProcessing
  DataModel::processNextBatch           // time-sliced; ~30 ms yields
  DataModel::addFolder                  // one directory per call
  DataModel::addFileDataForRow          // one row per eligible file
  DataModel::folderChange               // signal -> MW::folderChanged
  MW::folderChanged                     // set current; start Phase 2
  MetaRead::initialize                  // queued to metaReadThread
  MetaRead::setStartRow
  MetaRead::dispatchReaders
  MetaRead::dispatch
  Reader::read                          // prep + dispatch
  Reader::readMetadata                  // -> addMetadataForItem
  Reader::readIcon                      // -> setIcon1 (256 px)
  MetaRead::select                      // signal, at startRow
  Selection::setCurrentIndex
  DataModel::setCurrentSF               // current indexes + fPath
  MW::updateChange
  MW::fileSelectionChange               // loads image; start cache
  ImageView::loadImage
  ImageCache::setCurrentPosition
  ImageCache::updateTargets
  ImageCache::launchDecoders
  ImageCache::decodeNextImage
  ImageDecoder::decode
  ImageDecoder::rotate
  ImageDecoder::colorManage
  ImageCache::fillCache
  ImageCache::cacheImage
  ImageCache::nextToCache
  MetaRead::done                        // signal, all rows read
  MW::folderChangeCompleted             // filters, sort, resize cols


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Go to Folder (Path Entry)

Go > Go to Folder... (Ctrl+Shift+G, MW::gotoFolder in `Main/mainwindow.cpp`) is the type-a-path route into the pipeline above, as opposed to File > Open Folder... which has to be navigated.  It is a small modal dialog -- a path field with a folder-only QCompleter, a Browse... button that hands off to QFileDialog, and an "Include subfolders" checkbox -- and it ends in fsTree->select(path, recurse ? "Recurse" : "None"), the same call the Recent Folders and Bookmarks entry points make.  So the selection lands in the Folders dock and everything downstream is unchanged.

The field accepts what a path usually arrives as: leading/trailing whitespace, surrounding quotes, a file:// URL, and a leading ~.  MW::gotoFolder's expand() lambda normalises those before validating.  Validation runs on every keystroke and gates the Go button rather than reporting after the fact -- a file rather than a folder, or a non-existent path, each disable Go and say which inline beneath the field.  An empty field disables Go silently: the field's own label already says what belongs there, so a reason would only repeat it.  The Folders dock is shown if hidden, since that is where the result appears.

This action is deliberately the ONE Go menu item that is never gated by enableSelectionDependentMenus: it is how the user gets a folder in the first place.  Its default shortcut is set where the action is built, not in loadShortcuts' default block, because that block only runs for a profile with no saved Shortcuts group and an existing user would otherwise never receive it.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Concurrent Flow - New Folder Selected

- Selecting a new folder in the Folders or Bookmarks panel calls MW::folderSelectionChange.

- If the folder replaces the current selection, the prior load is torn down: all worker threads are stopped, the DataModel is cleared and all parameters are reset (MW::stop, MW::reset, DataModel::clearDataModel).

- The DataModel is loaded from the folder queue by DataModel::processNextBatch, which calls DataModel::addFolder and iterates DataModel::addFileDataForRow to populate basic file info per row. processNextBatch is time-sliced (yields every ~30 ms) so the GUI stays responsive. MW::folderChanged then sets the current index.

- MetaRead::setStartRow / dispatchReaders / dispatch send Reader workers outward from the start row, loading metadata (-> DataModel::addMetadataForItem) and 256 px icons (-> DataModel::setIcon1).

- When the start row has been read, MetaRead emits select, which (via MW::updateChange -> MW::fileSelectionChange) starts ImageCache. From there ImageCache walks setCurrentPosition -> updateTargets -> launchDecoders -> fillCache, caching concurrently with MetaRead.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Concurrent Flow - New Image Selected

A new image is selected, which triggers fileSelectionChange:

- If starting the program, select the first image in the folder.
- Record the current datamodel row and its file path.
- Update the thumbView and gridView delegates.
- Synchronize the thumb, grid and table views. If in loupe mode, load the current image.
- Update window title, statusbar, info panel and classification badges.
- Update the metadata and image caching.

- If the metadata has not been cached yet for the selected image (usually the first in a new folder), load the thumbnail.
- Update the cursor position on the image caching progress bar.

A new image is selected which triggers a scroll event:

- IconView selection change may trigger a scroll signal, based on the current view (thumb, grid or table). MW::thumbHasScrolled / gridHasScrolled / tableHasScrolled are signalled.
- Update the icon range (firstVisible / lastVisible).


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Flow Flags

  G::isInitializing
  G::stop
  G::dmEmpty
  G::allMetadataLoaded
  G::iconChunkLoaded
  G::isLinearLoadDone
  dm->loadingModel
  dm->basicFileInfoLoaded                 // not used
  G::isLinearLoading                      // not used
  G::ignoreScrollSignal
  isFilterChange


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Current Model Row

  dm->currentDmRow                        // current row in the DataModel
  dm->currentSfRow                        // current row in the proxy model
  dm->currentDmIdx                        // QModelIndex into the DataModel
  dm->currentSfIdx                        // QModelIndex into the proxy
  dm->currentFilePath


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Icons

  bool G::iconChunkLoaded
  bool G::loadOnlyVisibleIcons            // not used
  int  G::maxIconSize = 256;
  int  G::minIconSize = 40;
  int  G::maxIconChunk = 25000;
  bool G::useJitIconCache                 // false = brute force; true = JIT layers
  double G::jitIconCacheMemFraction = 0.5 // share of free-remainder for thumbnails
  std::atomic<qint64> G::imageCacheHeadroomMB  // image cache's remaining claim (MB)
  int  G::iconPressureTestLevel = -1      // Layer 3 test override (-1/0/1/2/3)

  int  dm->iconChunkSize                  // icons to cache (brute force = rows; JIT = budget/floor)
  int  dm->firstVisibleIcon;
  int  dm->lastVisibleIcon;
  int  dm->visibleIcons                   // visible icon count, shown views
  int  dm->startIconRange;
  int  dm->endIconRange;
  int  dm->scrollToIcon = 0;

  int  dm->iconCount();
  void dm->clearIconsOutsideChunkRange();
  bool dm->isAllIconChunkLoaded(int first, int last);
  bool dm->iconLoaded(int sfRow, int instance);
  bool dm->isIconRangeLoaded();
  void dm->setIconRange(int sfRow);
  void dm->setChunkSize(int chunkSize);
  void dm->resolveIconChunkSize();        // Layer 1: brute force vs JIT window
  void dm->refineIconChunkSize();         // Layer 2: re-decide with measured footprint
  void dm->applyIconCachePressure();      // Layer 3: shrink-only pressure valve
  int  dm->iconBudgetCount();             // icons that fit the memory budget
  int  dm->iconChunkFloor();              // 3x visible floor (overrides budget)
  double dm->avgIconMB();
  void dm->setIcon();

  bool MW::updateIconRange(QString src);
  void MW::reloadIconChunk();             // re-dispatch after a JIT chunk resize

MetaRead:

  firstIconRow = dm->startIconRange;
  lastIconRow  = dm->endIconRange;
  bool needIcon;
  MetaRead::needToRead:
      bool isIcon = sf->index(sfRow, G::IconLoadedColumn).data().toBool();
  bool MetaRead::allMetaIconLoaded();
  emit cleanupIcons();   // -> DataModel::clearIconsOutsideChunkRange


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Icon Chunk Size

Icons or thumbnails, 256 px on the long side, are stored in the DataModel in column 0 under Qt::DecorationRole (the QPixmap); the icon's QRect is kept on the same cell under G::IconRectRole. The number of icons to cache is dm->iconChunkSize, set per folder in DataModel::resolveIconChunkSize: in brute force (G::useJitIconCache == false) it equals the row count (all icons); in JIT it is the memory budget (iconBudgetCount), clamped to the row count and to the 3x-visible floor (iconChunkFloor). See the "Icon Caching" section below for the full brute-force vs JIT model (Layers 1-3). As the user moves through the images, either by selection or scrolling, the icon range is recalculated based on the midVisibleIcon, and iconChunkSize is held at the floor by MW::updateIconRange.

When the user progresses through the images, the current image is centred in the visible views (thumbView, gridView and tableView) using the

  midVisibleCell.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Thumbnail (Icon) Decoding

Thumb::loadThumb (Image/thumb.cpp), called by Reader::readIcon, decodes the icon for column 0. It prefers a small embedded preview over decoding the full image. The routing is:

  video                          -> loadFromVideo
  isEmbeddedThumb (offset+len)   -> loadFromJpgData
  cr3 (if loadFromJpgData fails) -> loadFromImageIO   (CR3 HDR PQ, see below)
  heic                           -> loadFromHeic
  tif (G::useMyTiff)             -> loadFromTiff
  else                           -> loadFromEntireFile (Qt, scaled)

Embedded thumbnail format by file type (verified against external format documentation - ExifTool, the DNG spec, and lclevy/canon_cr3):

- JPEG and the raw formats NEF, CR2, ARW, SR2, RAF, ORF, RW2: the embedded thumb is JPEG (TIFF tags 513/514 or makernote payloads).

- CR3: standard CR3 stores JPEG in the THMB (thumbnail) and PRVW (preview) boxes. BUT a CR3 shot in HDR PQ mode encodes BOTH THMB and PRVW as HEVC (hvc1) - a headerless HEIF bitstream, not JPEG. See "CR3 HDR PQ" below.

- DNG: the spec allows preview/thumbnail IFDs to be Uncompressed (RGB), JPEG or JPEG XL. Winnow's DNG parser scans for JPEG streams and selects the smallest JPEG, so m.offsetThumb/lengthThumb always point at a JPEG. The DNG parser deliberately does NOT scan the Photoshop IRB (tag 34377) for a thumbnail the way Tiff::parse does: DNG stores its previews in subIFDs (tag 330), which the parser already handles. An IRB thumb only exists if the DNG was round-tripped through Photoshop, so it would at most be a fallback when no subIFD JPEG preview is present (rare). ifdPhotoshopOffset is still read in dng.cpp so IRB::readThumb() can be added there as a !m.lengthThumb-gated fallback if ever needed.

- TIFF: IRB (Photoshop) thumbs are JPEG (m.thumbFormat = Jpg); chained-IFD and subIFD thumbs are TIFF format (m.thumbFormat = Tif). The TIFF-format thumbs leave m.lengthThumb == 0, so isEmbeddedThumb is false and they are handled by loadFromTiff rather than loadFromJpgData.

- HEIC / HIF (incl. Canon HEIF stills): handled natively by loadFromHeic (libheif on Windows, ImageIO on macOS).

- The remaining supported types (bmp, png, gif, webp, jp2, tga, svg, etc.) have no embedded thumbnail; they are decoded whole by loadFromEntireFile.

loadFromJpgData reads m.offsetThumb/m.lengthThumb and decodes with the fast "JPEG" hint, then falls back to Qt content-sniffing (loadFromData with no format hint) if that fails. The fallback makes the path tolerant of any Qt-decodable self-contained embedded thumbnail; undecodable buffers return false and fall through to the next loader (loadFromTiff / loadFromEntireFile).

CR3 HDR PQ: the THMB payload is a bare HEVC bitstream (no HEIF file header), so neither the "JPEG" decode nor the content-sniff fallback can read it and loadFromJpgData fails. loadThumb handles this with a failure-driven fallback: when ext == "cr3" and the embedded path did not succeed (and is not retrying a locked file), it calls loadFromImageIO before the loadFromEntireFile catch-all.

- macOS: loadFromImageIO -> macImageIOThumbnail (ImageIO natively supports CR3 and returns the embedded preview, or a downscaled decode of the raw). This is the supported, tested platform.

- Windows: loadFromImageIO returns Fail (no safe path - libheif rejects CR3 and Heic::decodeThumbnail does not guard read failures), so loadThumb falls through to loadFromEntireFile as before. A full Windows fix would need WIC plus the Canon raw codec.

This is failure-driven by design: standard CR3 files decode in loadFromJpgData and never reach loadFromImageIO, so their behaviour is unchanged. It also does not depend on parser-side detection of the hvc1 codec, which keeps the CR3 parser untouched.


⏺ Folder Loading Pipeline > Tracking Icon Parameters

The number of visible icons (visibleCellCount) can change whenever:

- the size of the view changes
- the size of the icons changes

When this happens:

- IconView::updateVisible is called. A number of IconView operations use the parameters defined in IconView::updateVisible.

- MW::updateIconRange updates the DataModel iconChunkSize based on which view has the most visible icons and the current iconChunkSize.

The first, mid and last visible icons change whenever there is a scroll event. The scroll event can be triggered by the user scrolling or selecting another image. See also SCROLLING below.

- MW::thumbHasScrolled, MW::gridHasScrolled or MW::tableHasScrolled is triggered.
- MW::updateIconRange calls IconView::updateVisible.
- Other visible views are scrolled to sync.

*** DATAMODEL CHANGES ***

NOTE: The Folder Change and Image Change call sequences below describe the legacy MetadataCache architecture (`Cache/mdcache.cpp`), which is no longer compiled. The current architecture uses MetaRead + Reader (see FOLDER LOADING PIPELINE and CACHING). The

sequences are retained here as historical reference and need a full rewrite.

⏺ DataModel Changes > Filter Change

  MW::filterChange

Selection recovery and the central view:

When no rows are filtered out, MW::filterChange restores the prior selection via Selection::recover(), which sets the current index with QItemSelectionModel::NoUpdate and therefore does NOT re-emit fileSelectionChange. The central view is thus not re-rendered for the current item. A still image keeps showing because imageView retains its decoded pixmap, but a video's first frame (rendered by videoView on the VideoTab) must be re-asserted explicitly. MW::refresh does this at its tail: for a video current item in Loupe mode it re-selects the VideoTab and reloads the first frame (guarded so a clip already shown paused is not reloaded).


⏺ DataModel Changes > Worker Threads and the Model (Snapshots, and What Still Reads It)

BUILDFILTERS IS A QThread AND IT USED TO READ THE MODEL DIRECTLY — around seventy calls of the shape dm->index(row, G::PickColumn).data() and dm->sf->index(row, ...).data(), spread over three pass groups. QStandardItemModel and QSortFilterProxyModel are not thread-safe, and the GUI thread writes into that same model continuously during a folder load (addMetadataForItem, setIcon1), so those reads were a real data race that happened to be quiet. Datamodel/filtersnapshot.h is the fix: makeSnapshot() copies every column the counting needs into plain data ON THE GUI THREAD, and the worker then reads nothing but that snapshot. Every entry point that starts the thread — build, update, updateAllCounts, updateCategory — calls publishSnapshot() first.

A SNAPSHOT IS IMMUTABLE AND PER-RUN, handed round as a shared_ptr<const>, and that is not over-engineering — it was a plain member first, and ThreadSanitizer found the hole within one folder load. MW::buildFiltersWhenModelReady calls build(), which STARTS this thread, and then recount() on the very next line; recount took a snapshot too, so it cleared the QVector out from under appendUniqueItems() reading it on the worker. run() now takes its own reference at entry and holds it for the whole run, so a snapshot taken on the GUI thread meanwhile is simply a different object, and recount() uses its own without touching pendingSnap at all. The general shape: moving a race off the model does not remove it if the thing you moved it to is still one shared mutable object.

THE STRINGS ARE CHEAP, which is what makes a whole-model copy affordable. QString is implicitly shared and QVariant::toString() hands back a reference to the same buffer, so a row of fourteen values is fourteen pointer copies rather than fourteen allocations.

inProxy IS CAPTURED, NOT RECOMPUTED. The filtered counts need "which rows survive the current filter", which is a proxy question, and asking the proxy per row from the worker is the race being removed. takeSnapshot walks the proxy ONCE and marks the datamodel rows it maps to. That also fixes something the old code never guaranteed: the filtered and unfiltered counts now describe the SAME INSTANT, where before they were two separate walks of two models that the GUI thread could change in between.

THE THREE PASS GROUPS COLLAPSED INTO ONE TABLE. appendUniqueItems, updateUnfilteredCounts and updateFilteredCounts were the same fifteen categories written out three times, differing only in which Filters sink they called and which rows they counted — 511 lines that had to be kept in step by hand. A category is now one row of BuildFilters::sinks() (a snapshot slot, a tree item, a name) plus one column in takeSnapshot's table, so a category added to the panel cannot reach two of the three and miss the third. Keywords stay outside the table because a row carries a LIST of them; they are counted by countKeywords and appended by each caller after the loop.

TWO BEHAVIOURS WERE DELIBERATELY PRESERVED rather than tidied. updateUnfilteredCounts does NOT count the Search category: it built that map and threw it away (its sink was commented out), because search's unfiltered count goes through updateUnfilteredSearchCount and updateSearchCategoryCount instead. And focal length is right-justified to four characters at snapshot time, since all three passes did it and it is what makes "50" and "400" sort as numbers.

ONE BEHAVIOUR CHANGED, for the better. updateCategoryItems (the edited-category path) read the model WITHOUT trimming while every other pass trimmed, so a value with stray whitespace could appear as a second, near-identical filter item. It reads the trimmed snapshot now like everything else.

METAREAD NO LONGER WRITES TO THE PROXY. MetaRead::needToRead marked a dispatched VIDEO row in flight with dm->sf->setData(..., G::MetadataReadingColumn, true) — a mutation of a QSortFilterProxyModel from metaReadThread, which is worse than any of the reads around it. Non-video rows had already moved to the worker-local rowsReading set; video could not follow, because a video's decode outlives its Reader (processReturningReader must not clear the marker or dispatch re-picks the file and spawns a second QMediaPlayer on it, which corrupts AVFoundation), and only the GUI thread learns when the frame lands.

A QUEUED WRITE WOULD NOT DO. The guard has to read true IMMEDIATELY or the same video is dispatched twice before the queued setValSf lands — which is the exact failure the flag exists to prevent. So the guard moved to a second worker-local set, videoRowsReading, and the GUI thread now REPORTS the outcome back: DataModel::videoReadingCleared(sfRow, instance) is emitted by both resolution paths, setIconFromVideoFrame (success) and clearVideoReadingFlag (failure), and MetaRead::onVideoReadingCleared drops the row. The signal carries the PROXY row because that is how MetaRead keys the set and only the GUI thread may map a datamodel row to one; a stale instance is ignored, since the sets are cleared wholesale on setStartRow and initialize and a late signal from the previous folder must not clear a row the current one just dispatched. The success emit is unconditional — if the icon was already set the row is done just the same, and leaving the marker would stop the dispatcher ever revisiting it. G::MetadataReadingColumn is still maintained through the queued setValSf so the table and the diagnostics report still show it; it is simply no longer what the guard reads.

THE WORKER RECORDS, THE GUI THREAD APPLIES. Counting off the model was only half the problem: run() also called filters->addCategoryItems(), updateFilteredCountPerItem(), updateCategoryItems(), update(), setEachCatTextColor() and setEnabled() DIRECTLY — mutating a QTreeWidget and its items from a worker thread, under nothing but a plain QMutex that the GUI thread does not take when it paints the tree or when the user clicks an item. Now the counting passes append a FilterOp (a kind, a map, the target category item) to a batch, and BuildFilters::applyOps performs them on the GUI thread. run() touches nothing on Filters at all — not even a debug print, deliberately, because a debug line is not a reason to make the invariant untrue. applyOps guards on G::isGuiThread() so a future caller cannot quietly reintroduce it.

DISPATCH IS INLINE OR QUEUED, NEVER BLOCKING. updateCategory(runSync) calls run() directly on the GUI thread precisely so the tree is updated BEFORE the caller goes on to MW::filterChange; deferring it there would reintroduce the use-after-free that runSync exists to prevent, so dispatchOps applies inline when it is already on the GUI thread. Otherwise the batch is stashed and posted with a queued invokeMethod. It must NOT be a BlockingQueuedConnection: abortProcessing() calls wait() from the GUI thread, so a worker blocking on the GUI thread would deadlock against it.

NOTHING IS DROPPED — abortProcessing DRAINS. A posted batch could otherwise land on a tree the caller had since reset, and the obvious guard (stamp the batch, discard a stale one) is wrong here: a discarded batch also discards its Done op, so Filters::buildingFilters would stay true and BuildFilters::build() early-returns on it, wedging the panel. Instead abortProcessing() calls flushOps() after wait(), applying whatever the worker stashed before the next build can start. flushOps swaps the batch out under the mutex, so calling it twice is harmless. A partial batch from an aborted build applies partially, which is exactly what the old code did as it went.

THE CATEGORY POINTERS IN A BATCH ARE STABLE. A FilterOp carries a QTreeWidgetItem* recorded on the worker and used later on the GUI thread, which would dangle if the tree were rebuilt in between. It cannot: Filters::reset() calls removeChildrenDynamicFilters(), which is takeChildren() on the TOP-LEVEL category items (picks, ratings, keywords ...), and those items live for the life of the widget. Reading those member pointers is the only thing the worker still touches on Filters.

done() NOW RUNS LAST, ON THE GUI THREAD, as the final op of the batch. It used to run at the end of run(), before the GUI had seen any of the work — so finishedBuildFilters could reach MW while the panel still held the previous folder's items. setIdle() and stopped() deliberately stay in run(): they mean "the thread has finished", which is what abortProcessing's wait() and the shutdown check in MW read them as.

IMAGECACHE OWNS ITS OWN BOOKKEEPING NOW. isCaching, isCached, attempts, decoderId, decoder status and error message used to live in the DATAMODEL: ImageCache wrote them through queued setValSf signals to the GUI thread and then read them straight back off the cache thread. Every read raced the GUI thread's write, and worse, the value read was whatever had been APPLIED so far rather than what the cache thread had just decided. A snapshot cannot fix that -- these change continuously while the cache runs, so any point-in-time copy is stale by construction. They are state, and state belongs to whoever makes the decision. ImageCache::RowCache is that state, KEYED BY FILE PATH rather than by row: proxy rows shift under filtering and sorting, and icd->imCache is already keyed by path, so the two can never disagree about the same image. The model still carries the columns and is still updated through the same queued signals -- the icon delegate draws its cached badge from G::IsCachedColumn and the table shows the rest -- but that is now a one-way MIRROR for display, never a source of truth. Writes go to our state FIRST and the model after.

EVERYTHING ELSE IMAGECACHE READS COMES THROUGH SIX ACCESSORS -- pathAt, sfRowOf, rowCountSf, isVideoAt, metaLoadedAt, cacheMBAt -- and those are the only places the cache thread touches dm at all. Each takes its own reference to a published view (Datamodel/modelsync.h) and answers from that. ProxySnapshot answers the STRUCTURAL questions (what is at proxy row N, which proxy row holds this path, how many rows), and RowSyncArray the LIVE ones (is the metadata loaded, how big will this decode be, is it a video). The split is not cosmetic: ImageCache::waitForMetaRead POLLS metadata status in a condition-wait loop, so a copy would never see it change, while the paths and the row order must NOT change under a pass or the cache trims the wrong rows.

THE SNAPSHOT IS REBUILT FROM THE PROXY'S OWN SIGNALS -- rowsInserted, rowsRemoved, modelReset, layoutChanged -- not from a list of call sites. There is no correct list to maintain: SortFilter::invalidate() is reached from MW, the Filters tree and combineRawJpg alike. The argument that those four are sufficient is that the VIEWS rely on exactly the same signals; a change that emitted none of them would leave the grid stale too. It is ALSO rebuilt explicitly in DataModel::endLoad and at the end of the batched insert, because the batch calls setRowCount BEFORE it writes any cell -- so the snapshot built from that rowsInserted has the right number of rows and no paths in them. RowSyncArray is grown from the SOURCE row count, not the proxy's: it is indexed by datamodel row, and a filtered-out row still needs its slot.

A LATENT BUG SURFACED IN THE MOVE. ImageCache::okToDecode skips a row whose previous decode failed terminally, and it read G::DecoderReturnStatusColumn with .toString() to compare against status NAMES ("Invalid", "Failed", ...) -- but the column is written as the enum's INT, so .toString() produced "3" and the list never matched. The check had never once fired, and a terminally-failed image was retried maxAttemptsToCacheImage times on every pass. Reading the int from RowCache makes it work; the terminal set is unchanged from that list, and still excludes Undefined, Abort and InstanceClash, which are transient and must stay retry-eligible.

STATE IS DROPPED ON AN INSTANCE CHANGE (initialize, updateInstance) and per path on removal (removeCachedImage, removeFromCache, which is what MW's delete/replace path already calls). Attempts and decode status describe the images that were loaded; a path reappearing in the next folder must not inherit its retry count, and one that had failed terminally would otherwise never be decoded again. In initialize() the ORDER matters: resetStaleIsCaching() reads our state to find rows left marked caching and mirrors isCaching=false to the datamodel for each, so clearing first would make it a no-op and leave those columns set.

VERIFIED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY INSPECTION. TSan alone only proves the races are gone; it says nothing about whether the cache still caches, and every one of these accessors fails SILENTLY (an empty path, a zero size, a false metaLoaded) by simply caching nothing. A temporary probe printing imCache count, the datamodel's isCached count and icd MB at the end of --selftest gave byte-identical numbers before and after the change (50-row folder: 13 images, 752.2 MB, datamodel agreeing with the cache on all 13), which is what actually establishes that pathAt, cacheMBAt and metaLoadedAt all resolve.

METAREAD FOLLOWED, AND STAGE 0.3 IS DONE. The same six-accessor arrangement (rowCountSf, rowCountDm, dmRowOf, pathAt, iconLoadedAt, metaAttemptedAt, isVideoAt) replaced its per-row reads in needToRead, the icon re-arm scan in setStartRow and the nextA walk. RowSyncArray already carried IconLoaded, MetaLoaded and IsVideo, so nothing new was needed for the values; ProxySnapshot gained sourceRows (the DATAMODEL row count -- progress and the completion checks count model rows, and a filtered row still exists in the model) and sfRowOfDmRow, the reverse of dmRowOf, because a Reader reports a datamodel row and metaRead works in proxy rows. That reverse map is a hash rather than a scan: the lookup runs once per returning reader, which is once per image.

A QModelIndex MUST NOT CROSS A QUEUED CONNECTION, and MetaRead was doing it. processReturningReader built one with dm->sf->index(r->dmRow, 0) on metaReadThread and emitted it to Selection::setCurrentIndex -- so the index was constructed from a read of the proxy on the wrong thread AND delivered later, by which time the model may have changed and the index names a different row or nothing at all. The signal now carries the proxy ROW (MetaRead::selectRow) and the connect site in MW::createDataModel builds a fresh index on the GUI thread at the moment it is used. emitFileSelectionChangeWithDelay, which held a QModelIndex across a QTimer for the same purpose, had no callers at all and was deleted rather than converted.

THREE READS WERE LEFT ALONE BECAUSE THEY WERE ALREADY SAFE -- SortFilter::isSuspended (a std::atomic<bool>), DataModel::metaReadHadFailure (two atomic counters) and dm->instance. One was NOT safe and became atomic: dm->iconChunkSize is written by the GUI thread's chunk-sizing logic and read in setStartRow, so it now sits beside startIconRange and endIconRange as a std::atomic<int>. And MetaRead::initialize logged dm->primaryFolderPath(), which walks folderList on the wrong thread for a log line; it logs the row count instead.

VERIFIED THE SAME WAY, AND THE CHECK MATTERS MORE HERE. MetaRead IS the metadata and icon loader, so a silently-failing accessor does not corrupt anything -- it just quietly loads nothing, and --selftest would still exit 0 because rows exist. A temporary probe at the selftest exit reported metaAttempted, metaLoaded, iconLoaded and the cache figures: on a 50-row folder, 50/50/50 with 13 images cached at 752.2 MB, identical to the pre-change cache baseline. 50 of 50 is complete by definition, which is what establishes that iconLoadedAt, metaAttemptedAt and pathAt all resolve.

NO WORKER THREAD READS THE MODEL ANY MORE. The audit that matters is mechanical and worth re-running after any change here: for BuildFilters, ImageCache and MetaRead, every dm-> reference on a worker thread is now either one of the published-view accessors, an atomic, or inside a report function that MW calls directly on the GUI thread.


⏺ DataModel Changes > The Row Store (What a Row Costs, and the Field Table)

WHAT A ROW COSTS, MEASURED. The plan to make the catalog browsable rests on one number -- whether a quarter of a million rows can be held in memory at all -- so it was measured on a prototype BEFORE any of DataModel was rewritten, at 5,000 and 20,000 rows (the two agreed to within 0.5%):

    QStandardItemModel, 92 columns      19,900 bytes/row      4.6 GB at 250k
    packed struct + interned repeats       573 bytes/row      137 MB at 250k
                                                              (34x)

THE 19,900 IS WHY THE CATALOG HAS A LOAD BUTTON. Each populated cell is a QStandardItem plus its QStandardItemData role list plus a pointer slot; addFileDataForRow sets ~30 and addMetadataForItem ~80, and 92 columns of that is roughly 20 KB before a single thumbnail. It is worse than the 15-18 KB the plan estimated from the type sizes, which is the ordinary reason for measuring rather than deriving.

573 IS THE PESSIMISTIC END -- every row given a title and five keywords. A sparse row (neither) measured 413 bytes, so a real library lands between 98 MB and 137 MB. Two design questions were settled by the same harness rather than by argument: storing the full path costs ~190 bytes of the 573, and it is KEPT anyway, because the path is the identity key that icd, fPathRow and the proxy snapshot all use and composing it per access would trade 45 MB for an allocation on every hot path; and interning the repeated strings (folder, type, make, model, lens, year, day, creator, copyright, keywords) is what makes the rest affordable, since those are the same handful of values across a whole shoot.

Datamodel/rowfields.h IS THE FIELD TABLE, and it is the answer to "should the index and the datamodel be one entity": YES, one SCHEMA and one loader, not one storage engine. The same facts about an image are currently written out four times by hand -- G::dataModelColumns, the datamodel cells, the catalog's image table, and Catalog::categorySql -- and that they agree is a matter of vigilance that has already been paid for, since tst_catalog::categoryItemsMatchWhatTheDatamodelWrites exists only to pin two of them against the other two. The table is an X-macro so a field declared once can generate all four.

IT ALSO DRAWS THE RESIDENT/SCRATCH LINE, which is what makes the 137 MB possible. Resident fields are what every row needs to be listed, sorted, filtered and drawn. Scratch fields -- segment offsets, ICC buffers, decoder id/status/error -- are needed only while a decoder or cache is working on that particular image, and they are roughly a third of the current column set; they belong in a side table populated for the handful of rows in flight. ImageCache's bookkeeping already moved out of the model on exactly this reasoning; this is the same argument applied to the rest.

tst_rowfields MAKES THE TABLE BINDING RATHER THAN DOCUMENTARY. It asks the real schema (PRAGMA table_info) rather than a pasted copy, which would be a fifth hand-maintained list, and it checks that every field naming a SQL column has one, that no scratch field is ever persisted, and that the resident/scratch/derived partition covers every field. It was verified to FAIL: a field pointing at a non-existent column was added deliberately, the test caught it by name, and it was removed. A drift test that has never been seen to fail is not evidence of anything.

THE STORE IS BEING INTRODUCED AS A SHADOW, NOT AS A SWAP. A model cannot be half-swapped: change DataModel's base class and nothing works until everything works, so there is no point along the way where a test or a probe can say whether it is still correct. Datamodel/imagerow.h is therefore written BESIDE the QStandardItems -- the same DataModel::setData maintains both -- and DataModel::verifyRowStore() walks every row and every covered column and asks the two to agree. When that is clean over real folders the reads move across and the items go. Until then nothing reads the store; it is the thing under test.

IT COVERS 37 COLUMNS, listed explicitly in RowStore::covers rather than inferred as "everything not scratch". A column silently assumed to be covered is a column the verification quietly stopped checking, which is the failure mode this whole arrangement exists to avoid.

THE VERIFICATION COMPARES WHAT data() RETURNS, not what was passed to setData, and on the variant's TEXT rather than with QVariant::operator==. The model holds a quint32 where the store holds a qint64 and an int where it holds a quint8; those compare unequal as variants and identical as values, and it is the value the views and the filters read.

IT FOUND TWO REAL BUGS ON ITS FIRST RUN, both in the store, both from assuming a column holds the type its name suggests:

- RATING IS A QString, NOT A NUMBER. ImageMetadata::rating is empty when the image is unrated, and an empty rating is a DIFFERENT THING from a rating of 0 -- the Filters category list shows it as a blank row, not a "0" row. Stored as an int it read back as "0", which would have put every unrated image into a rating category it does not belong to.
- MPix IS A FORMATTED STRING. The model writes QString::number(mp, 'f', 2), so a float in the store returned 24.15999984741211 where the model says 24.16 -- one category item per image instead of one per sensor size.

Neither would have crashed anything. Both would have quietly changed what the Filters panel offered, which is exactly the kind of drift that is unreadable from the code and obvious from a diff. THE RULE the store follows, and that both bugs broke: return exactly what the model returned, not a tidier version of it. A column that looks numeric and is stored as text is stored as text here too.

CLEAN OVER FIVE FOLDERS after the fix -- 4, 12, 13, 50 and 51 rows, including videos, a raw+jpg pair folder and a recursive load with navigation and a mid-load sort reversal: 0 mismatches out of 2,442 values checked. The interning is doing its job: 50 rows x 37 columns is 1,850 values held as 499 distinct strings.

GROWTH IS LAZY, REMOVAL IS NOT. Rows are only ever APPENDED, so setData resizes the store when it notices the row count has moved. Removal takes rows from the MIDDLE (removeFolder, remove) and a QVector resize truncates from the END, so every store row past the deletion point would describe a different image; rowsRemoved therefore rebuilds the store from the items. That is scaffolding -- when the store is the only copy, removal splices the vector and there are no items to rebuild from.

DataModel::data() NOW SERVES THE COVERED COLUMNS FROM THE STORE. This is the seam the whole storage change turns on: every view, delegate, proxy and saved column-width setting addresses the model by G::dataModelColumns and reads it through data(), and none of them care what is behind it -- so the storage can be replaced there, one column set at a time, and a rewrite that "cannot be landed incrementally" lands incrementally after all. The items are still written and verifyRowStore still compares them, so G::useRowStore = false falls straight back.

THE PROOF IS AN A/B FINGERPRINT, not an assertion that it looks right: a SHA-1 over what data() returns for every covered column of every row, with the store ON and OFF. Identical over four folders -- fixtures, a raw+jpg pair folder, videos and a 50-row mixed folder. Note what that exercise ALSO found: the Filters item count varies run to run (74 vs 75) WITHIN the same configuration, so the filter build has a load-timing dependency of its own that has nothing to do with the store. A comparison that had included it would have blamed the store for it.

THE TYPE CHECK IS THE POINT, AND IT WAS ADDED AFTER IT WAS NEEDED. The first verification compared only the variant's TEXT, reported clean over five folders, and the app then aborted on the first run with reads enabled:

    ExposureTimeItemDelegate::displayText(value=+Inf)   tableview.cpp:624
    qRound(+Inf)  ->  ASSERT

ImageMetadata::exposureTimeNum is a DOUBLE and the delegate guards with "value == 0" before computing 1/value. The store had interned it as text; same digits, so the text comparison passed, but the guard does not fire for a QString and the reciprocal of nothing is infinity. Aperture, focal length and ISO were numeric too and had the same fault. verifyRowStore now compares the variant's KIND as well as its text -- grouping the numeric types together, because the model itself is loose there (a quint32 in one column, an int in the next) and what must not change is text-vs-number, which is what a delegate branches on.

WITH THE TYPE CHECK IN PLACE IT FOUND THREE MORE, and one of them is a defect in the MODEL rather than in the store: three columns hold a different type depending on which code path wrote them last.

    Search   "false" (QString) at row creation, then m.isSearch and
             SearchTerms::matches() (bool) once metadata arrives
    Width    QString::number(m.width) from addMetadataForItem, but an
    Height   int from Image/thumb.cpp via setValDm

A store with one type per column cannot reproduce that and must not pretend to, so those three stay on the items and RowStore::covers names them and says why. An unstable column type is worth fixing on its own account: anything that branches on the type behaves differently depending on load ORDER, which is not a property anything should depend on.

COVERAGE IS PER (COLUMN, ROLE), not per column, which the same check forced. The path lives on PathColumn under G::PathRole and EditRole there is never set -- so the item returns an INVALID QVariant and the store was returning an empty QString, which is a different answer to anything testing isValid().

THE VERIFICATION HAD A BLIND SPOT, AND IT WAS EXACTLY WHERE THE BUG WAS. With reads enabled the app came up with NO THUMBNAILS AND NO LOUPE IMAGE. The cause: coverage became role-aware (PathColumn is covered at G::PathRole, not EditRole) but the WRITE side kept an older "EditRole or DisplayRole" guard in front of the covers() call, so PathRole writes were dropped while data() served PathRole from the store. Every path read came back empty, and everything downstream needs the path to find the file.

TWO GUARDS THAT MUST AGREE IS ONE GUARD TOO MANY. RowStore::covers(column, role) is now the single authority, asked on both sides with nothing in front of it.

THE REASON NEITHER CHECK CAUGHT IT is worth more than the bug. verifyRowStore looped over columns at Qt::EditRole ONLY, and so did the A/B fingerprint -- so PathColumn, the one column covered at a different role, was the one column neither ever looked at, and it was the one that broke. Both now iterate every role the store claims. A check that does not cover the same ground as the code it is checking proves nothing about the part it skips, and "0 mismatches" over 1,750 values was true and useless. The re-broken build reports it plainly now:

    ROWSTORE: rows=4 values=144 mismatches=4
      row 0 col 0 (Icon): item '/Users/roryhill/Projects/Winno'[QString] vs store ''[QString]
    PATHS: empty=4 of 4  icons=0  loupeImage=NO

and that was confirmed by deliberately re-injecting the guard, watching it fail, and removing it again -- the same discipline tst_rowfields was held to.

WIDTH AND HEIGHT WERE SETTLED ON int, which is a real fix rather than an accommodation. Both columns had two writers disagreeing about the type -- QString::number(m.width) from addMetadataForItem, an int from Thumb::setImageDimensions via setValDm -- so the column's type depended on which path filled the row last. The table sorts on EditRole and QVariant compares ints numerically and strings LEXICALLY, so "1000" sorted before "999" for rows the metadata path filled and after it for rows the thumb path filled. Every programmatic consumer (Image/pixmap.cpp, Image/stack.cpp) already called toInt() and the displayed text is identical either way, so settling on int changes nothing except that the sort is now consistently numeric. Coverage is 35 columns plus the path role.

SEARCH IS THE ONE LEFT, and deliberately: it cannot be settled by choosing a type here, because the FILTERS TREE is inconsistent in the same way and in the same direction -- searchTrue holds the QString "true" while searchFalse holds the bool false, and SortFilter::filterAcceptsRow compares the two against this column. (Note also that QVariant(QString("false")).toBool() is TRUE, a non-empty string, so the row-creation value means the opposite of what it reads as to anything calling toBool().) Deciding the type means deciding what the search filter matches, which is a question about filtering rather than storage, and it belongs with the compiled predicate.

THUMBNAILS MOVED TO A PATH-KEYED STORE (Datamodel/iconstore.h). They used to be QIcon on Qt::DecorationRole of column 0. Three reasons, and only the first is about memory. A thumbnail belongs to an IMAGE, not to a row: rows shift under sorting and filtering and are rebuilt on every folder change, so an icon held by row must be found again whenever the proxy moves, while an icon held by path is simply still there -- and icd->imCache has always been keyed by path, so the two now agree. It is also what the catalog needs: scrolling a 250,000-image catalog cannot open a file per row, so the next layer down is a thumbnail cached in the index keyed by pathkey, and "memory store, then index, then decode" only reads as one lookup chain if the memory layer is keyed the same way. And an icon on the row is an icon the row store cannot replace.

THE POLICY DID NOT MOVE, deliberately. What gets loaded and what gets dropped is still decided exactly where it was -- the icon chunk range (resolveIconChunkSize, refineIconChunkSize, applyIconCachePressure, clearIconsOutsideChunkRange). Those say "drop this thumbnail" by calling setData with an empty QVariant on DecorationRole, which now removes from the store. Only the storage changed, so if thumbnails start behaving differently it is the store's fault and not a policy change hiding inside a storage change.

THE SEAM IS THE SAME ONE THE ROW STORE USES: data() and setData() on DecorationRole, so no view or delegate changed. FIVE SITES BYPASSED IT and had to be converted -- two idempotency guards in setIcon/setIcon1 (itemFromIndex(dmIdx)->icon().isNull()), setIconFromVideoFrame's item->setIcon, two clipboard/drag paths in IconView (dm->item(row)->icon()), and the thumbnail ROTATION in MW, which additionally leaked a QStandardItem it allocated and immediately overwrote. A bypass that reads the item directly now always sees a null icon, which is silent, so these are worth grepping for after any change here: ->icon() and item->setIcon.

THE REMOVAL PATH IS NOT GATED ON ok. setData's other store writes happen only if QStandardItemModel accepted the write, but the item no longer holds the thumbnail, so a removal must reach the store whatever the base class does with the cell.

VERIFIED over four folders including videos and a recursive load with navigation: icons reached through data() exactly match the store's count in every case (4/4, 13/13, 50/50, 51/51), with the row-store check still clean and paths and the loupe image intact.

STILL TO DO, and deliberately not started in the same pass: the scratch columns (below), then a single pass deleting the item writes for every covered column, then the compiled filter predicate, then the base-class swap to QAbstractTableModel.


⏺ DataModel Changes > The Scratch Store (The Columns Only a Decoder Needs)

THE OTHER HALF OF THE RESIDENT/SCRATCH LINE. Datamodel/rowfields.h draws the distinction; Datamodel/rowscratch.h is where the scratch half actually goes. A resident field is needed to list, sort, filter and draw a row, so every row carries it. A scratch field -- a segment offset, an ICC buffer, a decoder id, a cache flag -- is needed only while a decoder or the ImageCache is on that row, and there are 24 of them out of the 92 columns. Held as QStandardItems they are paid for on every row of the scope regardless, which is the single largest slice of the ~19,900 bytes a row measured at.

SO THEY MOVED TO A HASH KEYED BY ROW, not into ImageRow. A row nothing has touched has NO ENTRY AT ALL; an entry that exists is a few dozen packed bytes instead of two dozen QStandardItems. That shape is what the eventual "populated only for the rows in flight" policy needs, and it is why the side table is a QHash rather than another parallel vector.

THIS STEP DOES NOT EVICT, and the reason is worth stating rather than leaving as an omission. Evicting a segment offset means being able to get it BACK -- a re-read of that file's metadata -- and that path belongs with Stage 4's gap-filling loader, not with a storage change. What this step buys is the storage shape and the per-column arithmetic; eviction then becomes a policy added in one place on top of it, instead of a rewrite.

UNSET IS A VALUE, and this is the case that would have failed silently. QStandardItemModel::data returns an INVALID QVariant for a cell nobody wrote, and callers branch on that. So every field carries a bit in RowScratch::setMask and value() returns an invalid QVariant until the bit is set: "never written" and "written as 0" stay different facts, exactly as they are in the items. Without it an unwritten offset reads back as 0 -- which is a legal offset, so nothing would have crashed; a decoder would simply have read from the front of the file, on some formats only.

INTERNING, FOR THE TWO FIELDS THAT WOULD OTHERWISE DOMINATE. iccBuf is a real ICC profile, commonly 500 bytes to 4 KB, and a shoot shares ONE of them; iccSpace is a handful of distinct strings across a whole library. Both are held once and referenced by a qint32. Measured on the mixed 50-file _ThumbTest folder: 50 rows, 4 distinct ICC profiles and 3 distinct space names. On the 12-file raw+jpg folder: one profile. Uninterned that column alone would be ~500 MB across a 250,000-row catalog. decoderErrMsg is deliberately NOT interned -- it is written only on a failure and can embed a row number, so interning it would grow a table nothing trims in exchange for nothing.

ifdOffsets IS HELD AS quint32s. The model holds QList<QVariant> because that is what a QStandardItem cell wanted; they are plain file offsets, so they are stored as such and the variant list is rebuilt on the way out.

SAME SEAM, SAME SHADOW VERIFICATION. ScratchStore is written beside the items by the same DataModel::setData, and DataModel::verifyRowStore() now asks BOTH stores -- RowStore::covers and ScratchStore::covers, which never claim the same column, so each column is checked on exactly the terms it is served on. data() answers a covered scratch column UNCONDITIONALLY, with no contains() guard: "no entry" is the store's real answer for a row nothing has decoded, and it is the same answer the unset item gives. A contains() guard would fall through to the items and hide precisely the case the table exists for.

THE CHECK ITSELF HAD TO BE STRENGTHENED FIRST, and this is the third time that has been true here. QVariant::toString() is EMPTY for a QByteArray and for a QVariantList, so the existing text comparison would have compared "" against "" for the ICC buffer and the IFD offsets -- reporting clean on the two columns least likely to be right. Both are now given a real rendering (hex for the blob, joined elements for the list).

PROVEN TO FAIL BEFORE BEING TRUSTED, twice, as the standing rule requires. Ignoring setMask was re-injected: 120 mismatches, named, all on the fields addMetadataForItem deliberately does not write (DecoderErrMsg, IsCaching, IsCached) -- which is exactly the population where unset-vs-zero is the difference. Truncating the interned ICC buffer and offsetting the IFD list by one was injected separately, and both were caught by name -- the same two columns the OLD rendering reported clean on.

CLEAN OVER FIVE FOLDERS: 4, 12, 13, 50 and 51 rows, including videos, a raw+jpg pair folder and a recursive load with navigation and a mid-load sort reversal. 0 mismatches out of 7,800 values checked, 60 per row (36 resident plus 24 scratch), with an entry per row -- because addMetadataForItem currently writes the segment offsets for every row it reads, which is the write pattern eviction will later change.

tst_rowscratch PINS THE BEHAVIOUR that the shadow check will stop being able to pin once the items go: that unwritten fields read back invalid while an explicit zero does not, that reading a row nobody wrote does not conjure an entry for it, that the two stores never claim the same column, that each column comes back as the TYPE the delegates branch on, and that 500 rows sharing one ICC profile intern to one copy.

A BUG FOUND IN PASSING, NOT FIXED HERE: DataModel::imMetadata reads m.iccSpace from G::ICCSegmentOffsetColumn rather than from G::ICCSpaceColumn (datamodel.cpp), so the colour-space NAME comes back as an offset. It is a one-word fix but it changes behaviour rather than storage, so it does not belong inside a storage change.


⏺ DataModel Changes > Retiring the Items (Alignment, Tooltips, and the Deletion Pass)

THE ITEM WRITES ARE GONE FOR EVERY COVERED COLUMN. This is the pass the shadow phase existed to make safe, and it was spent ONCE, on both stores together, because DataModel::verifyRowStore() compares the store against the QStandardItems and deleting the writes retires the ability to compare at all. It is retired here, along with G::useRowStore -- a fallback to the items is not a fallback to anything once the items are empty.

WHAT REPLACES IT AS PROOF: an A/B FINGERPRINT. A SHA-1 over what data() returns for every column of every row at four roles (Edit, Display, ToolTip, TextAlignment), taken from a temporary probe at the --selftest exit over the same four folders, before and after. It is a stronger check than verifyRowStore was, because it reads through data() -- the answer the views actually get -- rather than comparing two private copies.

THE FINGERPRINT HAD TO BE MADE DETERMINISTIC FIRST, and this is the same trap the Filters item count set last time. Three runs of the UNCHANGED binary produced three different hashes. Six columns vary run to run within one build:

    NS Thumb, NS Image, DecoderId    legitimately nondeterministic -- nanosecond
                                     timings, and which decoder thread took the row
    XmpSegmentLength, IsXMP,         NOT legitimate. A pre-existing metadata-read
    ICCSpace                         defect: it reproduces on the unmodified binary
                                     and does not converge at a 25-second settle

Excluding the first three is correct; excluding the second three is an admission. They are noted, not fixed -- but note what including them would have done: reported a difference on every comparison and made the whole exercise unreadable. A comparison is only evidence if it has been shown to be stable when nothing changed.

TOOLTIPS WERE A COPY OF THE CELL. Fifteen per-row Qt::ToolTipRole writes wrote the cell's own text back into a second role of the same cell -- Name, SearchText, Make, Model, Lens, GPSCoord, the three keyword columns, ShootingInfo, Title, Creator, Copyright, Email. That is not a fact about the image, it is a restatement of one, and holding a role keeps a QStandardItem alive, so it cost an item on 15 columns of every row to store a string the row store already had. They are derived in data() now (the keyword columns joined with the same Utilities::stringListToString their tooltip used). Fingerprint identical on all four folders.

ALIGNMENT IS A PROPERTY OF THE COLUMN, and treating it as a property of the CELL was hiding a bug. There were 28 per-row writes, plus one on every cross-thread setValueDm/setValueSF call -- those carried an `align` argument defaulting to Qt::AlignLeft, so whatever column a worker happened to write got stamped left-aligned. Measured mid-load, seven columns read "1 x15, unset x35": IsCaching, Attempts, DecoderId, DecoderReturnStatus, Meta Status, WidthPreview, NS Image. A column that renders differently depending on how far a background load got is a defect, not a layout.

So a static table in datamodel.cpp is authoritative and the `align` parameter is GONE -- from both slots, from all eleven signal declarations, and from the seventeen call sites that passed one. Leaving it would have left the same gun loaded for the next caller. Every value in the table came from the writes it replaces, and the census after the change shows why the ragged ones were the bug: each of the 28 uniform columns keeps its exact value and type on every row, NS Image goes from "130 x15, unset x35" to "130 x50", and the seven AlignLeft-noise columns have no alignment at all rather than one on an arbitrary subset of rows. No column changed its value; only its raggedness.

RawRenderColumn is deliberately left out of the table even though ImageDecoder wrote it with AlignRight, because addMetadataForItem sets the same column with no alignment -- so it was right-aligned only on rows where a raw had been rendered. Consistently unset is the smaller change.

THEN THE VALUE ROLES. setData no longer calls QStandardItemModel::setData when RowStore::covers or ScratchStore::covers claims the (column, role); it writes the store and emits the dataChanged the base class would have emitted. Edit, Display and ToolTip fingerprints IDENTICAL on all four folders, and align identical to the post-alignment-change baseline.

ONE THING BROKE THAT NO FINGERPRINT COULD SEE. setData maintains running counts (metadataAttempted, metadataLoaded, iconLoaded, videoRow) so isMetaReadFinished() and the icon-chunk check need not rescan, and it counted transitions by reading the OLD value first -- with QStandardItemModel::data. All three tracked columns are store-covered, so once the items went that read returned an unset cell every time, every write looked like a change, and the counts drifted upward past rowCount(). Reading the old value through data() fixes it. Verified against a full scan on five runs (50/50, 12/12, 13/13, 4/4, and 51/51 under the stress load), and PROVEN TO FAIL by re-injecting the item read: 56/50 attempted, 55/50 loaded, caught by name.

ROW SPLICING REPLACED THE REBUILD. Both stores are indexed by row, and inserts and removals both take rows from the MIDDLE (insertFiles, remove, removeFolder). That used to be handled by rebuilding the store from the items on rowsRemoved; with the items gone there is nothing to rebuild from, so rowsInserted and rowsRemoved splice both stores directly. A plain resize() could never have been right -- it appends or truncates at the END, so every row past the splice point would have silently pointed at a different image. Note that only removal was handled before; a mid-model INSERT was already unhandled.

THREE STALE ICON READS WERE FOUND AND FIXED IN PASSING, and they were worse than stale. iconCount(), the loaded-icon range report and the row report all asked itemFromIndex(...)->icon(), which has always returned null since thumbnails moved to the path-keyed icon store. In Qt 6 itemFromIndex LAZILY CREATES the item it cannot find, so each of those calls was allocating a QStandardItem per row to read a value that was guaranteed null. The same trap invalidated the first attempt to MEASURE this work: a probe walking itemFromIndex reported 93 items per row, having created every one of them. Use QStandardItemModel::item(row, column), which does not create.

WHAT IT ACTUALLY BOUGHT, measured with the non-creating accessor over the 50-file mixed folder: 93 items per row before, 31.4 after -- two thirds of them gone, and a covered column (Year, File Name, Type) now has no item at all. The remaining 31 are the columns NEITHER store covers yet: Duration, FocusX/Y, AspectRatio, IconAspectRatio, Msec/Mp, Orientation, Rotation, Email, Url, MetaReading, RawRender, Sidecar, Ingested, EC, the seven _original columns, Permissions, R/W, OrientationOffset, RotationDegrees, ShootingInfo, Developed, DevPreviewKey, NS Thumb, NS Image, Search, plus column 0's three raw+jpg pairing roles. Extending coverage to them is the obvious next increment and is a separate piece of work. THE 34x IS NOT HERE YET and claiming it would be wrong.

AND THEN THREADSANITIZER FOUND WHAT THE FINGERPRINTS COULD NOT. Both stores now take a QReadWriteLock, which reverses what imagerow.h previously asserted -- that nothing here would ever need one -- so the reason matters.

Stage 0.3 moved BuildFilters, MetaRead and the image cache's own bookkeeping onto the published views in modelsync.h, but it did not convert every off-thread read of dm->sf. ImageDecoder still reads OffsetFull, LengthFull, samplesPerPixel and ICCBuf that way; Reader reads OffsetThumb and LengthThumb; ImageCache reads the cache flags. Those are the known QSortFilterProxyModel exposure that tests/tsan/run_tsan_proxy.sh exists to reproduce and whose fix is deferred.

While the QStandardItems held the values, those reads were survivable BY ACCIDENT: a worker read an item pointer out of a list that only changes when a ROW is inserted. Now the value lives in the stores, and both of them move memory continuously during a load -- the scratch table takes a new hash entry on every scratch write and REHASHES, and the row store's interner appends a QString on every string written and REALLOCATES. A reader mid-lookup then walks freed memory, which is a different class of bug from a stale value. TSan reported the scratch one on the first run after the item writes went (ScratchStore::setValue racing ImageDecoder::decode) and the interner one on the proxy run.

Both were confirmed to be NEW by stashing the work and re-running: the unmodified tree passes. So the locks are the price of those deferred callers, not a design preference, and they come out with them. Reads here are decoder and cache bookkeeping rather than the paint path. With the locks, run_tsan.sh passes and run_tsan_proxy.sh reported no race on three consecutive runs against the 50-file folder.

STILL ON THE ITEMS, and next: the 31 uncovered columns (below); the compiled filter predicate (SortFilter::filterAcceptsRow still walks the whole Filters tree per row); and only then the base-class swap to QAbstractTableModel.

⏺ DataModel Changes > The Second Batch (Covering the Rest, and the Set Mask)

31 OF 93 ITEMS PER ROW SURVIVED THE FIRST DELETION PASS, on the columns neither store claimed. This pass covers 31 more of them and takes the count to 3.1.

WHERE EACH ONE WENT. Four are facts about a DECODE rather than about the image -- NS Thumb, NS Image, Msec/Mp and Raw Render -- so they joined the SCRATCH store, where a row nothing has decoded has no entry at all. The other 27 are properties of the image and joined ImageRow: EC, Duration, FocusX/Y, AspectRatio, IconAspectRatio, Orientation, Rotation, Email, Url, MetaReading, the seven _original columns, Permissions, R/W, Sidecar, OrientationOffset, RotationDegrees, ShootingInfo, Err, Developed and DevPreviewKey.

TWO ARE DELIBERATELY LEFT, and SearchColumn has now been joined by IngestedColumn for exactly the same reason: the column holds a different TYPE depending on which path wrote it last. Search is "false" (QString) at row creation, a bool once metadata arrives, "true" (QString) from DataModel::find. Ingested is "false" (QString) at row creation, true (bool) from the ingest pass, "true" (QString) from MW::setIngested.
