Width and Height had the same fault and were settled on int; these two cannot be settled in the store, because the Filters tree is inconsistent in the same direction -- searchTrue holds the QString "true" while searchFalse holds the bool false -- so choosing a type decides what the FILTER matches. They belong with the compiled predicate. tst_imagerow pins that they are NOT covered, so it stays a decision rather than an oversight someone quietly fixes.

Permissions looked like a third case and is not: both writers agree on uint.

THE SET MASK, and why this batch is what forced it. Every previously-covered column is written for every row by addFileDataForRow or addMetadataForItem, so "never written" never arose in practice and the store could get away with returning an interned empty string. This batch brought the first genuinely CONDITIONAL resident columns: Duration is set only for video, IconAspectRatio only once an icon has been decoded, Err only on a row whose metadata failed. An unset QStandardItem returns an INVALID QVariant and callers branch on it, so returning the field's zero would turn "no duration" into "a duration of nothing", which reads as a value to anything testing isValid(). ImageRow now carries a two-word mask (63 columns is already too close to 64 to leave the next one to chance) and value() returns invalid until the bit is set. The scratch store learned this first; see rowscratch.h.

ONE MAPPING, NOT TWO. covers() used to be a second list of columns beside the one in value()/setValue(). It is now derived from fieldBit(), which is also the mask position -- so "is this column held here" and "has this row's copy been written" are answered from the same table. Two lists that must agree is one list too many, which is the lesson the write-side guard already taught when it and covers() disagreed about PathRole.

A DEAD WRITE FOUND AND REMOVED. addMetadataForItem wrote Orientation twice: QString::number(m.orientation) and then, further down the same function, m.orientation as an int. The string never reached anything. Removing it is what settles the column's type, the way Width and Height were settled -- and the A/B fingerprint being unchanged by the removal is the proof it was dead.

VERIFIED BY THE SAME A/B FINGERPRINT: byte-identical on all four folders, with only the six known-nondeterministic columns differing in the per-column listing. Items went from 31.4 to 3.1 per row on the 50-file folder.

WHAT THE 3.1 IS, and all of it is deliberate: column 0's custom roles (the icon rect and the three raw+jpg pairing roles -- custom roles, not values); Search and Ingested; and ErrColumn on the rows that had an issue, where Qt::UserRole holds a QList<QSharedPointer<Issue>> that is not a value column either.

tst_imagerow REPLACES WHAT verifyRowStore USED TO PROVE. The shadow comparison is gone with the items, so the two things it caught are pinned as unit cases instead: unset is not zero (including that an EXPLICITLY empty string is a value, which interns to the same -1 as never-written and would otherwise be indistinguishable), and text is not number (the ExposureTimeItemDelegate case that aborted the app, plus its reverse -- Rating and MPix look numeric and are held as text). It also pins the row splicing, which nothing had covered since it replaced the rebuild-from-items. Both new checks were PROVEN TO FAIL first: ignoring the mask fails three cases by name, and making removeRows truncate from the end instead of splicing fails the fourth.

⏺ DataModel Changes > Compiling the Filters (Off the Tree, Once per Change)

SortFilter::filterAcceptsRow USED TO WALK A WIDGET, ONCE PER ROW. It answered "is this row visible" with a QTreeWidgetItemIterator over the whole Filters tree, comparing QVariants as it went. Two things were wrong with that and only one of them was about speed.

IT WAS O(rows x items). The tree holds one item per DISTINCT VALUE the folder contains -- every keyword, every camera model, every lens -- so a few hundred items is ordinary: the 50-file _ThumbTest folder builds 201. The whole tree was walked for every row on every filter change, sort and insert. At 250,000 rows that is tens of millions of pointer-chasing compares to answer a question whose inputs changed once. Worse, it fetched the row's cell once per ITEM, so a category holding 200 keywords read the same cell 200 times.

IT READ A WIDGET ANOTHER PASS WAS WRITING. BuildFilters adds and removes items as metadata arrives, and filterAcceptsRow read (*filter)->checkState(0) while it did -- there was a `// crash` comment on exactly that line. Suspending filtering during a rebuild papers over it; it does not make the read safe.

BOTH GO AWAY TOGETHER. The tree is compiled once per change, on the GUI thread, into the plain data in Datamodel/filterpredicate.h, and the compiled form is published as a shared_ptr<const> -- the same shape ProxySnapshot uses in modelsync.h, so a row test holds its own reference alive while the GUI thread swaps in a rebuilt one.

RECOMPILATION IS DRIVEN BY THE TREE'S OWN SIGNALS, not by a list of call sites: itemChanged for a check state, and the model's structural signals for BuildFilters adding items. A path that mutates the tree cannot forget to say so, which is the reasoning rebuildProxySnapshot already follows.

THE SEMANTICS WERE COPIED, NOT REDESIGNED, and the structure now says so instead of a flag: `includes` being empty IS the category not narrowing anything, which is what isCategoryUnchecked meant, and excludes live in their own list so an exclusion cannot accidentally make its category count as filtering. That last rule is the one that is easy to get wrong -- a category holding only exclusions subtracts rather than narrows, and were it to count as filtering every row lacking any of its items would be rejected, which is the whole set.

ORDER OF REJECTION DOES NOT MATTER, which is what makes evaluating a category completely before moving to the next equivalent to the old interleaved walk. The old code checked category N's include verdict when it reached category N+1's header, so an exclude in N+1 could be reached before N's verdict; but every path either returns false or continues and the function is pure, so whichever rejection wins the answer is the same.

TWO COMPARISONS WERE KEPT DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT, because the old code made them differently. An INCLUDE on a QStringList column did .contains(filterValue) with filterValue still a QVariant, so QString == QVariant promoted the left side and compared as VARIANTS; an EXCLUDE did .contains(filterValue.toString()), a plain string compare. Every filter value on a keyword category is a QString so the two agree today. They are kept apart so that if one ever stops being a QString, the behaviour changes where it always would have.

VERIFIED BY RUNNING BOTH. The old implementation was kept temporarily as filterAcceptsRowLegacy and a probe randomised the REAL Filters tree's check states -- 400 trials per folder, 1 to 5 items each, a third of them exclusions -- comparing the two row by row: 1,600 trials over four folders, 0 mismatches. Random rather than hand-picked because the combinations that matter are the ones nobody thinks to write down. PROVEN TO FAIL twice, as always: making an exclude-only category count as filtering, and inverting the exclude test, each produced mismatches by name (the second, 1,900 of them).

MEASURED: a full pass over the 50-row folder with a 200-item tree and three items checked cost 228-467 microseconds walking the tree and 36-40 compiled -- 6x to 12x depending on what is checked. The number that matters is not the ratio but its SHAPE: the compiled cost is proportional to the CHECKED items and the compiled-away cost was proportional to the whole tree, so the gap widens with every distinct value a folder contains.

tst_filterpredicate REPLACES THE SHADOW, which went with the old implementation. The evaluation is a template taking a callable rather than a model, so the whole decision is testable without one -- including that a category is read once per row and not once per item.

STILL DEFERRED, and now the last thing on the items: SearchColumn and IngestedColumn. Both hold a different type depending on which path wrote them last, and both the model and the Filters tree are inconsistent in the same direction, so settling them decides what the filter matches.

⏺ DataModel Changes > Sort Change

  MW::sortThumbnails
  thumbView->sortThumbs
  ImageCache::rebuildImageCacheParameters
  MW::scrollToCurrentRow


⏺ DataModel Changes > Combine Raw+JPG

When a folder holds raw+jpg pairs (same base name), the "Combine Raw+JPG" toggle decides whether each pair is shown as a single combined row (raw is primary, the jpg folded into it) or as two separate rows. The state lives in the bool combineRawJpg, which is mirrored to G::combineRawJpg, dm->sf, fsTree and bookmarks (the folder and bookmark panels show image counts that depend on the setting).

Entry points (both route to MW::setCombineRawJpg):

- combineRawJpgAction — File menu item, shortcut Opt/Alt + J. Being checkable, the action flips its own checked state before triggered fires.

- rawJpgStatusBtn — status-bar button. Its lambda calls combineRawJpgAction->toggle() (which does not re-emit triggered) then calls setCombineRawJpg directly.

combineRawJpgAction->isChecked() is the source of truth; setCombineRawJpg reads it into combineRawJpg. Because the trigger has already flipped the action before setCombineRawJpg runs, any early return must restore the action with setChecked(combineRawJpg) to keep the action and flag in sync — otherwise they desync and a later click is spent re-aligning them instead of toggling.

Three load states:

- Folder loaded (dm->rowCount() && G::allMetadataAttempted): full apply — persist to QSettings, rewrite the Type column for jpg duplicates ("JPG" vs "JPG+<raw>"), dm->rebuildTypeFilter, filterChange (re-pairs/splits the proxy) and buildFilters->updateAllCounts (the proxy baseline changed without a Filters-tree change, so unfiltered counts must be recomputed too).

- No folder loaded (dm->rowCount() == 0): toggling is allowed; setCombineRawJpg records the setting and refreshes the folder/bookmark counts, then returns before the datamodel/proxy rebuild.

- Folder loading (rows exist but !G::allMetadataAttempted): toggling is blocked. rawJpgStatusBtn and combineRawJpgAction are disabled in MW::folderChanged at load start and re-enabled in MW::folderChangeCompleted; updateStatusBar also enforces the disabled state. setCombineRawJpg additionally guards this case (restores the action and shows a "folder is still loading" popup) so a queued shortcut cannot slip through.

Key files: Main/setandupdate.cpp (setCombineRawJpg), Main/status.cpp (updateStatusBar — button icon/tooltip/enabled state), Main/initialize.cpp (rawJpgStatusBtn wiring), Main/menusandactions.cpp (combineRawJpgAction), Main/mainwindow.cpp (folderChanged / folderChangeCompleted enable-disable).

THE PAIRING ITSELF is recorded by DataModel::rawJpgPairing on COLUMN 0 of both halves: the raw row gets G::DupHideRawRole, the jpg row gets G::DupIsJpgRole plus G::DupRawTypeRole ("NEF", "ORF"), and BOTH get G::DupOtherIdxRole pointing at the other. Read them through DataModel::isDupJpg / isDupHiddenRaw / dupOtherRow / dupRawType, never through data(G::Dup...Role) directly. The roles live on column 0 alone, so reading one off any other column — or off the proxy — returns an INVALID QVariant, which reads as "not a pair" rather than as an error. That failure is silent by construction, and it had already happened twice: MW::setTagValue tested the role on the tag's own column, so tag edits never propagated to the hidden raw file, and MW::setColorClass looked the paired row up through dm->sf when the row it holds is a DATAMODEL row — and a hidden raw is filtered OUT of the proxy, so the colour-class log named a different image. The accessors range-check as well, which is why they return -1 or false rather than an index.

G::DupOtherIdxRole HOLDS A PLAIN INT ROW, not a QModelIndex. A QModelIndex is a persistent reference into a model that is cleared and rebuilt on every folder change, it cannot be range-checked once stale, and it would not survive the row store becoming anything other than a QStandardItemModel. The bulk-load tracker beside it (prevRawRow) is an int for the same reason and is reset by clearDataModel.


⏺ DataModel Changes > Folder Tree Auto-Refresh

The Folders panel (FSTree) updates itself when subfolders are added or removed on disk outside Winnow (e.g. in Finder/Explorer) — no manual Refresh is needed.

Mechanism: a cross-platform QFileSystemWatcher (FSTree::folderWatcher) wired up in FSTree::setupFolderWatcher(). Qt maps this onto the native OS facility (FSEvents on macOS, ReadDirectoryChangesW on Windows, inotify on Linux), so one code path serves both platforms.

Scope: QFileSystemWatcher only reports paths explicitly registered, so rather than watch the whole filesystem Winnow watches only the folders currently visible in the viewport. updateWatchedFolders() diffs the visible set against the watched set and applies the delta; scheduleWatchResync() re-runs it (debounced 150 ms) on scroll, expand, collapse, selection, resize and refresh.

On a directoryChanged signal, onWatchedDirectoryChanged() debounces (300 ms) then calls FSFilter::refresh() — which uses invalidate() so the view re-queries the live FSModel::hasChildren() and the dropdown decoration appears/disappears — and re-syncs the watch set so a newly created subfolder is itself watched.

Note: drive/volume mount/unmount is a separate path (Windows WM_DEVICECHANGE via WinNativeEventFilter); this section covers folder content changes only.

Key files: File/fstree.cpp / File/fstree.h.


⏺ DataModel Changes > Insertions, Deletions, Modifications

  Images inserted:   (TBD)
  Images deleted:    (TBD)
  Images modified:   (TBD)


⏺ DataModel Changes > Instance

The instance is a specific datamodel. Every time the datamodel changes — either a folder is added or removed — the instance is incremented. When the instance changes:

- MetaRead must be called.
- Filtering must be updated.


*** CACHING ***

Metadata caching has been replaced by `MetaRead` (`Cache/metaread.cpp`) dispatching a pool of `Reader` workers (`Cache/reader.cpp`). The earlier `MetadataCache` / `metadataCacheThread` system (`Cache/mdcache.cpp`, `Cache/mdcacher.cpp`, `Cache/mdcachemgr.cpp`) is no longer compiled — those files are not listed in `winnow64.pro` and are retained on disk for reference only.


⏺ Caching > Overview

- When a new folder or a new file is selected, or when a scroll event occurs in IconView, MetaRead is started. MetaRead dispatches Reader workers to load metadata and icons into the DataModel.

- As metadata accumulates, MetaRead signals ImageCache::addCacheItemImageMetadata so the image cache can begin loading full-size images. When MetaRead crosses imageCacheTriggerCount it signals the ImageCache to start.

- If a new image is selected, MW::fileSelectionChange repositions MetaRead and the ImageCache around the new current row.

- If the user scrolls in the thumbView or gridView, scroll events are sent to MW::thumbHasScrolled / MW::gridHasScrolled. A delay ensures that rapid scrolling coalesces — the next scroll event supersedes the prior, and MetaRead is only re-dispatched when there is a pause or the scrolling is slow. ImageCache is not restarted here because a new image has not been selected.

See `Cache/metaread.cpp` and `Cache/reader.cpp` top-of-file comments for more detail.


⏺ Caching > Cache Status

- Two status lights on the right side of the status bar (metadataThreadRunningLabel and imageThreadRunningLabel) turn from green to red to indicate datamodel metadata and image caching respectively. A status light on the lower right of each thumbnail is made visible when the associated image is cached.

The two lights are shown in Preview mode only. Develop mode works on the single current image with the read-ahead trimmed to it, so folder caching progress is not meaningful there and both lights are hidden. MW::updateStatusBar owns the visibility (driven by G::operationMode) and MW::setOperationMode calls it on every mode change (see "Operation Mode").

- An image cache progress bar shows the status of every image in the folder: target range, isCached and cursor position.

- The progress display is the Progress class (Utilities/progress.h/.cpp), a self- contained container widget held by MW::progress and added to the status bar. It replaced the older ProgressBar (Utilities/progressbar.*, now removed). Unlike ProgressBar, Progress does not reference MW: it owns its own pixmaps, width, colors and palette (no MW::progressLabel/progressPixmap), and communicates with MW only through public methods and the heightChanged() / clicked() signals. Because of this it can later be reparented into a standalone window with no internal changes.

- Progress holds any number of rows. Each row has a text column and a bar column, its own bar height, color, font and a Fill style. Each row owns its bar pixmap, so an incremental paint (e.g. the moving cache cursor) touches only that row.

- Rows are added at runtime with addRow(name, barHeight, color, fill), which returns an int id; the owner holds the id and drives the row with the generic updateProgress(id, item, items, color) / clearProgress(id). A row's Fill style decides how updateProgress paints: Fill::FromStart fills 0->current item (sequential progress, e.g. FocusStack, RawDenoise); Fill::Cell paints only the current item's cell (items that complete out of order, e.g. MetaRead reading metadata across threads). An invalid color argument defaults to the row's own bar color. clearProgress refills the bar background and hides the row.

- The ImageCache row is the one exception: its moving cursor and directional range fills are not expressible as a Fill, so Progress creates it in its ctor and keeps its own updateImageCacheProgress()/updateCursor()/ clearImageCacheProgress(). Its id is exposed via imageCacheRow() for MW's gate.

- All other rows are added by their owner (MW) at startup in MW::createDataModel: MetaRead "Metadata" (4 px, Fill::Cell), FocusStack "Focus Stack" (2 px, Fill::FromStart) and RawDenoise "Raw Denoise" (2 px, Fill::FromStart). MW stores their ids in progressMetaReadRow / progressFocusStackRow / progressRawDenoiseRow. Labels default to empty; setRowText() sets them.

- All rows start hidden and are only shown while active, so at startup (before a folder is selected) the container is empty. updateProgress showRow(true) and clearProgress showRow(false). The ImageCache row is revealed by updateImageCacheProgress/updateCursor (it is not hidden by its clear, since the cache "All" repaint clears-then-draws every tick). Hiding a row collapses it entirely (text + bar), so the container height fits only the live rows. A row's effective visibility is active && enabled: update functions set "active", and MW::setCacheProgressEnabled(G::showCacheProgress) calls setRowEnabled() on the ImageCache + MetaRead rows so they honor the "Hide caching progress" preference even when the widget is shown for a focus stack (FocusStack is not gated). The rows block is vertically centered within the widget (paintEvent) with topPad/botPad reserved, so a single row lines up with the other status text and a filled Progress shows an equal top/bottom margin. MW floors the status-bar height at its pre-Progress height (MW::statusBarBaseHeight) so collapsing rows never clip the other status text.

- Progress manages its own container visibility: it shows the widget whenever a row is visible and hides it when none are (updateContainerVisibility, called from relayout). Hosts therefore never call progress->setVisible() for a per-row concern -- doing so would hide a concurrent FocusStack/RawDenoise row when cache progress is turned off. Two host hooks remain: setSuppressed(true) force-hides the whole container regardless of rows (used for a random slideshow, which does no caching), and reset() clears + hides every row when the folder is closed (MW::reset / MW::nullFiltration). The cache "Hide caching progress" preference is applied only via MW::setCacheProgressEnabled -> setRowEnabled on the ImageCache + MetaRead rows; the container then follows automatically.

- setContainerWidth() sizes the bars to the available status-bar width (driven by MW::updateProgressBarWidth via availableSpaceForProgressBar). setTextFontSize() is the separate, settable text-size variable. setBackgroundColor() keeps the container background matching the status bar.

- The container height grows to fit the visible rows. Progress::sizeHint() reports that height so the status bar grows automatically; heightChanged() is also connected in MW to set the status-bar minimum height explicitly. Because QStatusBar centers its widgets and reserves its own vertical margin, the heightChanged handler requests a few extra pixels (h + overhead) so Progress is never compressed below its preferred height — otherwise the reserved top/bottom padding gets squeezed out and the margins collapse when Progress fills the bar.

- clicked() opens Cache preferences (replacing the old StatusProgressLabel event- filter branch).

- The single global flag G::showCacheProgress gates both ImageCache and MetaRead caching progress: when false, progress is neither calculated nor displayed (it replaces the former MW::isShowCacheProgressBar, ImageCache::isShowCacheStatus and MetaRead::showProgressInStatusbar). ImageCache::updateStatus forces InfoOnly, MetaRead::dispatch skips its progress emit, and MW::progress is hidden. It is set by the "Hide caching progress" preference (Productivity), persisted under the settings key "showCacheProgress" (written in MW::writeSettings, read in MW::loadSettings), and defaults to true.
- isCached is stored in datamodel as dm->sf->index(row, G::IsCachedColumn).data().

Functions:

- MW::createStatusBar                      status labels, right of status bar
- MW::refreshAfterImageCacheSizeChange      pref calls update cache status
- MW::setThreadRunStatusInactive           caching status lights gray
- MW::updateImageCachingThreadRunStatus    image caching light green/red
- MW::updateMetadataCachingThreadRunStatus metadata caching light green/red
- MW::setCacheStatusVisibility             updateState toggles progressBar
- MW::updateImageCacheStatus               ImageCache updates cache status
- MW::setCachedStatus                      sets isCached, refreshes views


⏺ Caching > Icon Caching

- Each icon is a 256 px longside QIcon, so we want to balance how many we load when weighing performance vs memory footprint.

- Icons are stored in the DataModel at dm->(row, 0).data(Qt::DecorationRole). The loaded status can be determined two ways:

  1. dm->itemFromIndex(dmIdx)->icon().isNull()      // used up to 2023

  2. dmIdx.data(Qt::DecorationRole).isNull()        // switched to in 2023

- The number of icons to cache is governed by dm->iconChunkSize. How it is set is decided per folder in DataModel::resolveIconChunkSize (called from endLoad) and depends on the G::useJitIconCache flag.

Brute force (G::useJitIconCache == false, the default)

- dm->iconChunkSize is set to the DataModel row count, so every icon is cached regardless of folder size. Simplest behaviour; preferred for typical folders. (The G::memoryOverrunFlag bail in DataModel::setIcon1 remains the crash backstop.)

Just-in-time, JIT (G::useJitIconCache == true)

- A folder is cached fully only when its projected thumbnail footprint fits a memory budget; otherwise dm->iconChunkSize is clamped to a bounded sliding window and the existing machinery (setIconRange, MetaRead::needToRead, clearIconsOutsideChunkRange) loads and evicts icons as the user moves. JIT has three layers:

Layer 1 - predictive budget (resolveIconChunkSize / iconBudgetCount)

At folder load the per-icon footprint is estimated (worst case 256x256x4 bytes) and compared to a budget = free memory after a safety reserve and the image cache's own remaining claim (G::imageCacheHeadroomMB, published by ImageCache::memChk), times G::jitIconCacheMemFraction. Fits -> full; else -> bounded window. iconChunkSize is then clamped to the row count and to a hard floor (iconChunkFloor): max(256, 3 x visibleIcons). The floor OVERRIDES the budget — if the image cache has claimed most of memory and the budget would fall below 3x the visible icons, the floor wins and the thumbnails take the memory they need; the image cache self-throttles because its own ceiling is derived from G::availableMemoryMB, which drops as the thumbnails are held. The floor is re-applied by MW::updateIconRange whenever the visible count changes (e.g. switching to the denser Grid view). Note: sizing is purely budget + floor; there is no separate "visible x 3" target beyond this floor.

Layer 2 - measured refinement (refineIconChunkSize)

As real icons load, DataModel::setIcon1 accumulates their true size. After ~200 icons the budget is recomputed with the measured average and the window may grow, or be promoted back to full, when thumbnails turn out smaller than the worst-case estimate. Grow-only and one-shot per folder.

Layer 3 - defensive pressure valve (applyIconCachePressure)

A 1 s GUI-thread timer watches memory pressure (DataModel::memoryPressureLevel, derived from G::availableMemoryMB). On warn it halves the window; on critical it clamps to the visible page and evicts. It never grows. A 60 s cooldown plus a 2 GB available-memory high-water mark (hysteresis) gate when the latch releases, so the cache cannot thrash between shrink and re-grow. Releasing does not re-grow; Layer 1/2 re-decide on the next folder load. Brute force is not touched by the

  valve.

- Independent of the above, MW::updateIconRange still grows dm->iconChunkSize to at least the number of visible icons when more thumbnails are visible (in thumbView, gridView or tableView) than the current chunk, e.g. after an app or thumbnail resize.

- When dm->iconChunkSize changes or there is a scroll event, the metadata cache read process is rerun to update the icons.


⏺ Caching > Load Responsiveness (Keeping the GUI Usable During a Large/Recursive Load)

The cost of a folder load on the GUI thread is dominated not by decoding but by the flood of QStandardItemModel signals each row change emits, every one of which runs the proxy (SortFilter) plus all three views' (thumbView, gridView, tableView) handlers synchronously. On a ~9000-image recursive tree this was ~30 s of GUI-thread time, starving keystrokes/mouse for seconds. Three measures cut it to near zero:

1. Batch per-row writes (always on). DataModel::setIcon1 and setValDm wrap their setData calls in a QSignalBlocker and emit ONE dataChanged, mirroring addMetadataForItem. Four/two signals per icon -> one.

2. Notify only visible rows (G::useVisibleOnlyIconEmit, default true). setIcon1/setValDm emit their one dataChanged only when the row is within [firstVisibleIcon, lastVisibleIcon] (DataModel::iconRowVisible). Off-screen icons are stored without notification and paint correctly when scrolled to. ~9000 emits -> ~visible-count.

3. Batch the folder insert (G::useBatchedFolderInsert, DEFAULT FALSE). DataModel::addFolder can add a folder's rows with a single setRowCount (one rowsInserted, one view relayout) and fill the cells with signals blocked + one dataChanged for the range, instead of one rowsInserted + ~20 dataChanged per file (the enumeration freeze).

The Z-A reordering that previously made this unusable is now fixed: while a batched load runs, DataModel::scheduleProcessing turns the proxy's dynamic sort/filter OFF (sf->setDynamicSortFilter(false)), so the one wide dataChanged per folder no longer re-sorts the inserted block. Inserted rows map in source (lessThan / name) order; DataModel::restoreProxySortAfterLoad re-enables dynamic sort in one pass when the load finishes or aborts (every processNextBatch exit path), restoring the same final order the per-row path produces. Still DEFAULT FALSE pending A/B measurement on the recursive pictures tree (see G::isPerfProbe below); flip it to true to test.

Items 1-2 are independent of brute force vs JIT — they help any mode. Set the flags false to restore the prior per-signal behavior.


⏺ Caching > How to Test Icon Caching

Flags (Main/global.cpp) — flip at runtime via the debugger or a temporary code change:

  G::useJitIconCache          false = brute force (cache all); true = JIT layers active.

G::jitIconCacheMemFraction  share of the free-memory remainder budgeted for thumbnails (default 0.5).

- G::iconPressureTestLevel — forces the Layer 3 pressure level for deterministic testing (see values below).
- G::useVisibleOnlyIconEmit — true = setIcon1/setValDm notify views only for visible rows.

- G::useBatchedFolderInsert — true (default) = addFolder inserts a folder's rows in one batch (one rowsInserted + one dataChanged); cuts Phase-1 insert ~34%. Dynamic sort is disabled during the load and restored once at the end so order is preserved — see Load Responsiveness item 3.

- G::isPerfProbe — true = emit concise "[PERF]" lines (off in production): Phase1 load: rows, folders, enum(ms), sort(ms), msg(ms), insert(ms), other(ms) (= wall - measured buckets: event-loop yields / paint / end-of-load re-sort), wall(ms). Phase2 metaRead: rows, cycles, dispatch(ms) (metaReadThread busy), needToRead(ms) (proxy read hot loop, subset), ntrCalls, ntrAvg(us), thumbRetries (G::probeThumbRetryCount), wall(ms) (folderChanged -> done). To compare on the recursive pictures tree, clear the OS file cache between runs (`sudo purge`) and flip the flag under test.

  G::throttleFolderLoadMsg    true (default) = DataModel::addFolder throttles its
                              "Searching for images…" progress message to ~50 ms instead
                              of once per folder, removing ~1.3 s of synchronous repaints
                              on a 1333-folder load. Set false to restore the per-folder
                              emit (the msg(ms) A/B baseline).

  G::decodeRawEngine          which engine decodes RAW -> WorkingImage (demosaic
                              + global colour/baseline luma NR + start WB/black/
                              white). A/B knob:
                                winnowDecodeRawEngine (default) = in-house
                                  decoder; portable, the only engine on Windows
                                  and the canonical focus-stacking engine
                                  (deterministic, CFA-level control).
                                appleDecodeRawEngine = macOS Core Image
                                  (CIRAWFilter); GPU demosaic + NR, macOS-ONLY --
                                  callers fall back to winnow off-mac. Both
                                  converge on WorkingImage, so Develop / local
                                  luma NR / OutputTransform are engine-agnostic.
                              Sticky: persisted in QSettings ("decodeRawEngine",
                              saved/loaded next to "useRaw"), so the Develop
                              "Demosaic" combo survives app restarts. The combo is
                              rebuilt from G::decodeRawEngine (not a hardcoded
                              default) so folder/image selection keeps the choice.
                              Off-mac an appleDecodeRawEngine value is normalized
                              to winnow on load.

Load performance findings (~9000-image / 1333-folder recursive tree, cold cache)

Phase 1 (build DataModel, GUI thread): baseline wall 2408 ms -> 542 ms (4.4x) after three fixes. Instrumentation overturned the prior assumption that the proxy/view insert dominated:

- msg ~1299 ms (58%) — emit centralMsg per folder, each a synchronous repaint() in MW::setCentralMessage. Fixed by G::throttleFolderLoadMsg.

- enum ~580 ms, cold≈warm (not I/O-bound) — QDir recompiled ~50 wildcard name-filter regexes per folder. Fixed by entryList(QDir::Files, NoSort) + a QSet<QString> suffix check (DataModel::supportedExtSet); ~230 ms after.
- insert ~370 -> ~250 ms — batched per-folder insert (G::useBatchedFolderInsert).
- sort ~9 ms — negligible (per-folder std::sort of ~7 files).

Phase 2 (read metadata/icons, worker threads): wall ~9.6 s, and it is I/O/decode-bound in the Reader pool. The metaReadThread is ~1% busy (dispatch ~87 ms, needToRead ~10 ms), thumbRetries = 0 (the 100 ms loadThumb contention loop never fires), and the removed Reader::readIcon double-scale was redundant (loadThumb already scales to thumbMax). Consequence: the worker-thread QSortFilterProxyModel access (a latent thread-safety hazard) is NOT worth a snapshot rewrite for speed — it is ~0.1% of the load. The only real Phase-2 lever is reducing decode volume (G::useJitIconCache icon windowing) or reader-pool tuning, both bigger policy/architecture changes.

Phantom sort column: a persisted sortColumn could be out of range (== G::TotalColumns) and reach the proxy via QTableView::setSortingEnabled (which calls sort() directly, bypassing MW::sortChange/IconView::sortThumbs). Sorting by an invalid column reverses the model under DescendingOrder (Z-A). SortFilter::sort() now clamps any out-of-range column to -1 (no sort / source order) — the single chokepoint covering every path; the workspace load also sanitizes a stale persisted sortColumn.

Diagnostics — Help > diagnostics. The DataModel report shows the live state:

iconChunkSize, icon cache mode (JIT window / brute force), icon loaded (row ranges), icon avg footprint (measured vs worst-case), icon budget (icons), icon chunk floor (3x vis), imageCacheHeadroomMB, iconChunkRefined, memoryPressureLevel,

  iconCachePressureLatched.

Status indicator — the metadata thread "circle" (MW::updateMetadataThreadRunStatus) is colour-coded on success so the active icon-cache mode is visible at a glance:

  red     metadata thread running
  green   idle, brute force (G::useJitIconCache == false)
  blue    idle, JIT caching active (G::useJitIconCache == true)
  orange  idle, forced pressure (G::iconPressureTestLevel >= 2)  [precedence: orange > blue]
  yellow  idle, load failed

Brute force

- Set G::useJitIconCache = false and load any folder. Confirm "icon loaded" spans every row and "icon cache mode" = brute force (full).

Layer 1 - predictive budget

- Set G::useJitIconCache = true. A folder small enough to fit the budget caches fully; a large folder (or a lower G::jitIconCacheMemFraction) shows "icon cache mode" = JIT window with iconChunkSize < row count.

Layer 2 - measured refinement

- In JIT window mode, scroll/load past 200 icons. Watch the refineIconChunkSize log line; "icon avg footprint" switches to "(measured, n=...)" and iconChunkSize may grow or be promoted to full (iconChunkRefined = true).

Layer 3 - pressure valve (use the test override so you need not starve the machine)

  G::iconPressureTestLevel values:
    -1  use the real signal (availableMemoryMB)
     0  normal AND memory recovered      -> latch releases after the 60 s cooldown
     1  warn                             -> window halved, latched
     2  critical                         -> window clamped to the visible page, latched
     3  normal BUT memory not recovered  -> reaches the release branch, held by high-water

Suggested sequence (G::useJitIconCache = true, a windowed folder loaded):

1. = 2   critical: iconChunkSize drops to the visible page, icons evicted, latched.
2. = 3   pressure "cleared" but not recovered: after 60 s, still latched (roomy=false).
3. = 0   recovered: latch releases on the next 1 s tick (iconCachePressureLatched=false).

4. = -1  restore the real signal. Confirm each transition in the DataModel diagnostics and the applyIconCachePressure log lines.


⏺ Caching > Memory Governor (Process Footprint, Decode Concurrency)

Winnow bounds its resident process footprint so a large folder cannot run the machine out of memory. There are three independent consumers of memory and three cooperating governors. The important thing to understand first: the ImageCache (imCache) is usually NOT the biggest consumer. When Decode Raw is on, in-flight raw decoders are.

The three memory consumers

- ImageCache (Cache/cachedata.h imCache): the decoded 8-bit display QImages, W*H*4 bytes each. Byte-accounted by icd->sizeMB(). Bounded by the cache ceiling (below).

- WorkingImageCache (Develop/workingimagecache.h): a SEPARATE, byte-budgeted LRU of the scene-linear float WorkingImage (W*H*3 floats = 3x the QImage) kept so a Develop slider re-render skips demosaic. Default budget 768 MB; setMaxBytes() is not called, so it stays at the default. It is NOT counted by icd->sizeMB().

- In-flight decoders: each of decoderCount (= QThread::idealThreadCount) decoder threads, while decoding, transiently holds its working buffers. For an embedded-JPG or ordinary JPEG/HEIC decode that is ~one QImage. For a FULL-SENSOR RAW decode (UnpackCfa -> Demosaic -> RawColor) it is the float WorkingImage plus demosaic scratch -- about 8x the cached QImage. This is the key asymmetry: the CACHED result is identical for a raw and a same-resolution JPEG, but the raw DECODE working set is ~8x, and it is multiplied across all decoder threads.

Why Decode Raw off is cheap

With Decode Raw off (G::useRaw == false) a raw file is shown from its EMBEDDED JPG preview (ImageDecoder gates the sensor path on G::useRaw). That is one libjpeg pass, ~QImage-sized. So none of the raw-specific governing below engages, and that path runs at full decoder concurrency, unchanged. Raw-off performance/stability is the priority and is deliberately left untouched.

Governor 1 - raw decode concurrency cap (Cache/imagecache.cpp, the primary control) Because N raw decodes at ~8x-QImage each can exceed the memory cap even while imCache is nearly empty, the number of CONCURRENT full-sensor raw decodes is bounded:

rawDecodeLimit = clamp( (memoryAbortMB * 0.50) / (decodedQImageMB * 8), 1, decoderCount )

Gated at decodeNextImage (the single choke point reached from both launchDecoders and fillCache): if the row willUseSensorDecode() and activeRawDecodes >= rawDecodeLimit, the decoder is parked (cycling[id] = false) instead of starting another decode. A finishing sensor decode decrements the counter in fillCache and frees a slot; the remaining active decoders keep the pipeline full, so throughput holds at the limit. willUseSensorDecode() is false for JPEG/HEIC and for the Decode-Raw-off embedded path, so those are never gated. RawFormat::HasSensorDecoder(ext) is the cheap, no-alloc test (a hash lookup, no decoder constructed). It and RawFormat::Create() both derive from a single extension->decoder-factory table, rawFactories() in ImageFormats/Raw/rawformat.cpp -- so "which extensions have a sensor decoder" lives in exactly one place and the two cannot drift out of sync. Adding a new format's sensor decoder is a one-line change there. (Note ext membership is an over-approximation: e.g. a compressed ARW matches but its Decode() falls back to the embedded JPG. That is the safe direction for a memory guard -- the first decode must assume the heavy path -- and such fallbacks decode cheaply anyway, so throttling them costs nothing.)

decoderIsRaw[id] marks a decoder on the sensor path so the decrement fires exactly once per decode; both are reset per folder in initialize(). On a big-RAM machine the larger memoryAbortMB opens the limit back toward full concurrency.

Governor 2 - image cache ceiling clamp (ImageCache::memChk)

maxMBCeiling (the cache's growth target) is derived from availableMemoryMB but then clamped so cache budget + measured non-cache footprint + a per-decoder reserve stays under memoryAbortMB. Because "non-cache footprint" is measured live, it already includes in-flight decoders, so the cache automatically plans fewer images while raw decodes are consuming memory.

Governor 3 - memory watchdog (MW::memoryWatchdogTick, 50 ms GUI-thread probe) A backstop, graduated rather than a single hard abort. cap = G::memoryAbortMB (computeMemoryAbortMB scales it to host RAM, e.g. ~18.4 GB on 24 GB):

- footprint >= cap        -> engage the ImageCache throttle: setMemoryThrottled(true) parks decoders at decodeNextImage, and memoryPause() (queued to the cache thread) shrinks/trims imCache. No dialog.
- footprint <= 0.88*cap   -> release the throttle, memoryResume() relaunches decoders.

- footprint >= cap + max(2 GB, cap/10) -> genuine runaway the throttle could not contain: fall back to the old hard onMemoryOverrun abort dialog (prevents OS OOM). With the concurrency cap in place the footprint should not approach the cap during raw traversal, so the watchdog rarely fires; it exists for the pathological case.

  Diagnostics
    ImageCache::diagnostics() prints a "Memory Footprint / Raw Decode" block (process
    footprint, imCache MB/count, WorkingImageCache MB/count/budget, G::useRaw, decoderCount,
    active/peak raw decodes, the computed limit and per-decode estimate) and a "Memory
    Pressure Warnings" ring buffer of timestamped PAUSE / RESUME / CRITICAL events. A large
    footprint with a small imCache and a WorkingImageCache still at ~budget confirms the
    memory is in the concurrent raw decoders, which the concurrency cap bounds.

  Tunables
    rawDecodeLimit()'s 0.50 budget fraction and x8 per-decode factor (Cache/imagecache.cpp);
    the watchdog's 0.88 resume hysteresis and critical headroom (MW::memoryWatchdogTick);
    computeMemoryAbortMB() (Main/global.cpp) for the host-scaled cap itself.


*** SCROLLING ***

When the user scrolls a datamodel view (thumbView, gridView or tableView), the scrollbar change signal is received by thumbHasScrolled, gridHasScrolled or tableHasScrolled. The other views are scrolled to sync with the source view's midVisibleRow. The first, mid and last visible items are determined in MW::updateIconRange and the metaReadThread is called to update the metadata and icons in the range (chunk).

However, when the program syncs the views this generates more scrollbar signals that would loop. This is prevented by the G::ignoreScrollSignal flag. The same flag is also employed in fileSelectionChange, where visible views are scrolled to centre on the current selection.

Shift + navigation key (extending a selection) does not move the current index, so the centre-on-current scroll above does not fire. Instead, Selection::select (Shift branch) follows the extending selection: it scrolls the visible view(s) just enough to expose the cell one beyond the new selection edge, in the direction of extension (the "look- ahead" cell). This keeps both the last selected item and the next item visible so the user can see where the selection is heading. The scroll uses EnsureVisible (not centre), so it is a no-op when the look-ahead cell is already on screen and is clamped to the start/end of the datamodel. See IconView::ensureRowVisible and TableView::ensureRowVisible.


⏺ Scrolling > Painting Delays

When the user changes modes in MW (i.e. from Grid to Loupe), an IconView instance (either thumbView or gridView) can change state from hidden to visible. Since hidden widgets cannot be accessed, we need to wait until the IconView becomes visible and fully repainted before attempting to scroll to the current index.

Also, when an IconView resize event occurs — triggered by a change in the gridView cell size (thumbWidth) that requires justification, a change in the thumbDock splitter, or a resize of the application window — we also need to wait until scrollbar repainting is completed.

The last paint event is identified by calculating the maximum of the scrollbar range and comparing it to the paint event, which updates the range each time. With larger datasets (i.e. 1500+ thumbs) it can take a number of paint events and hundreds of ms to complete.

NOTE: An earlier IconView::waitUntilScrollReady helper has been removed. The wait- for-paint logic now lives inline in the scroll-handling paths in IconView and MW.


⏺ Scrolling > Scrollbar Change Flow

- The ScrollBar signal valueChanged triggers MW::thumbHasScrolled, MW::gridHasScrolled or MW::tableHasScrolled.

- MW::updateIconRange updates the first, mid and last visible icons from all views to find the greatest range of visible icons by calling IconView::updateVisible and TableView::updateVisible. dm->iconChunkSize is adjusted if it is less than the visible icon range.

- Concurrent metadata loading: MW::update(midVisibleIcon) is called and it loads any missing icons and cleans up any orphaned icons (not in the iconChunkRange).


⏺ Scrolling > Resizing Icons or the Icon Viewport

When the IconView viewport is resized or the icon size is changed, the result is a scroll event. Since we want to keep the view centred on its original state, the middle or centre icon is tracked as dm->scrollToIcon. G::resizingIcons is set for the resize and the subsequent scroll event does not reset dm->scrollToIcon. MW::thumbHasScrolled or MW::gridHasScrolled resets G::resizingIcons to false.

This allows the three datamodel views to be synced using dm->scrollToIcon.

Persisted icon size (between sessions and workspaces):

IconView::assignedIconWidth is the reference width that rejustify() uses to compute thumbs-per-row and then justify the cell width to exactly fill the row. The justified result lands in iconWidth, which can differ from assignedIconWidth. Critically, iconWidth is viewport-dependent: a rejustify() at a narrow width shrinks iconWidth toward ICON_MIN, while assignedIconWidth is unchanged.

Rule: persist (save) assignedIconWidth, not iconWidth. iconWidth can be corrupted by a transient rejustify() — at startup the hidden gridView receives resize events at a narrow width, each scheduling a rejustify() ~500ms later (IconView::resizeEvent) that justifies iconWidth down to e.g. 62. Saving iconWidth persisted that transient size, so the grid reverted to ~62 on the next launch; assignedIconWidth survives the transient. (thumbView has an extra guard: rejustify() skips "Thumbnails" while G::isInitializing, and MW::showEvent applies its size via setThumbSize().)

Rule: on restore, set assignedIconWidth = iconWidth BEFORE calling rejustify(). rejustify() justifies from assignedIconWidth, so if only iconWidth is restored the saved size is discarded and rejustify() uses a stale/uninitialised reference.

Where these rules are applied:

- MW::writeSettings / MW::createGridView  - between-sessions save/restore. writeSettings saves gridView->assignedIconWidth as thumbWidthGrid; createGridView loads it into iconWidth then copies to assignedIconWidth.

- MW::saveWorkspace / MW::invokeWorkspace  - per-workspace save/restore for both thumbView and gridView (save assignedIconWidth; on restore set assignedIconWidth = iconWidth before rejustify()).

- MW::builtInDefaultWorkspace  - sets assignedIconWidth = iconWidth before rejustify() so the hard-coded defaults (thumb 100, grid 160) are honoured.


*** FOCUS PREDICTOR ***

Focus Predictor uses a machine learning model to predict where in the image the user will want to review the image focus — for example, the eye of the subject. When enabled, and the loupe view is zoomed, as the user advances each image is panned to the predicted focus review location.


⏺ Focus Predictor > Architecture

  Data
      All the training images and a CSV file that records the focus point, type and
      path:
          /Users/roryhill/Documents/Documents - Quark/FocusPointTrainer

  Python
      Code used to create the model:
          /Users/roryhill/Projects/FocusPointTrainer

- config.py
- model.py
- train_focus_model.py              (creates the model)
- validate_focus_model.py

  Model
      focus_point_model at /Users/roryhill/Projects/FocusPointTrainer in two versions:
      .pth and .onnx.

  FocusPointTrainer
      A C++ class that creates the "labeling" data from the training images in
          /Users/roryhill/Documents/Documents - Quark/FocusPointTrainer/focuspoint.csv.

  FocusPredictor
      A C++ class that uses the model to predict the focus point for the current image.


⏺ Focus Predictor > Process

1. The training data is created using FocusPointTrainer to append the focus point, type and path for each training image to focuspoint.csv.

2. In Code, train_focus_model is run (run Python in terminal). This creates focus_point_model.pth and focus_point_model.onnx. The .pth file is the native torch format and is converted to .onnx — the format that OpenCV uses. This enables C++ to use the model with the OpenCV library.
3. Copy focus_point_model.onnx to /Users/roryhill/Projects/Winnow64/DetectionModels.

4. Use FocusPredictor::predict to return the normalized image coordinates for the predicted focus location.


⏺ Focus Predictor > Implementation

When focus prediction is enabled (Toggle Pan to Focus) and the loupe view is zoomed, the ImageView pans to the predicted focus point. See:

- end of ImageView::loadImage
- ImageView::predictPanToFocus              (get focus coord and pan)

When this occurs, the location of the ImageView viewport within the scene is signalled to IconView, which tells IconViewDelegate to show a rectangle in the thumbView thumbnail matching the viewport in the scene. See:

- ImageView::predictPanToFocus              emits the rectangle and image aspect
- IconView::loupeRect                       receives rect/aspect, updates delegate
- IconViewDelegate::setVpRect


*** HELP SYSTEM ***

All in-app help pages are HTML files loaded by HtmlWindow (Utilities/htmlwindow.cpp). Every page shares a single stylesheet, Docs/help.css, which is applied automatically by HtmlWindow via QTextDocument::setDefaultStyleSheet. To restyle every help page at once, edit help.css; no other files need to change.


⏺ Help System > Architecture

  Utilities/htmlwindow.cpp        HtmlWindow widget (a QScrollArea hosting a
                                  QTextBrowser). Loads the HTML and applies
                                  Docs/help.css as the document's default stylesheet,
                                  and sets a qrc base URL on the document so relative
                                  <img src="images/..."> resolves.

  Docs/help.css                   Shared dark-theme stylesheet for every help page.
                                  Defines body, h1-h3, a, code, pre, .highlight, .warn.
                                  Some values are fallbacks: at load time HtmlWindow
                                  overrides the body font-size with the app font size
                                  (G::strFontSize), scales the h1-h3 font-sizes by
                                  G::fontSize/12, and sets the h1-h3 colours from the
                                  theme-tied G::header1/2/3Color. The static values in
                                  help.css apply only when viewing the file outside the
                                  app (e.g. VS Code Live Preview).

  Docs/_template.html             Reference template demonstrating every supported
                                  element (headings, paragraphs, span colors, link,
                                  image, lists, code block, table). Copy this when
                                  creating a new page.

  Docs/<topic>.html               One file per help topic. The <head> only needs:
                                      <link rel="stylesheet" href="help.css">
                                  Qt ignores the <link> (it already loaded help.css
                                  as the default stylesheet); VS Code's Live Preview
                                  honors it, so the editor view matches Winnow.

  Docs/images/                    Image assets used by help pages. Reference with a
                                  relative path: <img src="images/Foo.png">.

  winnow.qrc                      Every .html and image referenced from help must be
                                  listed here so it is bundled into the binary.

  HtmlWindow lifecycle            Constructor sets Qt::Window (top-level with title bar)
                                  and Qt::WA_DeleteOnClose (self-cleanup on user close).
                                  Callers pass a parent (`window()` for embedded
                                  widgets, `this` for top-level dialogs) so the help
                                  window is destroyed with its owner. MW::closeEvent
                                  also sweeps findChildren<HtmlWindow*>() as a safety
                                  net.


⏺ Help System > Adding a New Help Page

1. Copy Docs/_template.html to Docs/<yourpage>.html.

2. Edit in VS Code (see "VS Code Workflow" below). Keep the existing <link rel="stylesheet" href="help.css"> in the <head>; only add a <style> block if you need per-page overrides.

OR use BBedit.

3. Place any images in Docs/images/ and reference them with a relative path, e.g. <img src="images/MyDiagram.png">.

4. Register the new files in winnow.qrc under the existing /Docs prefix: <file>Docs/<yourpage>.html</file> <file>Docs/images/<YourImage>.png</file>     (if any)
5. Open it from C++. Pattern (mirrors Filters::howThisWorks at filters.cpp:1767):

#include "Utilities/htmlwindow.h"

  void MyWidget::someHelp()
  {
      QRect r = QRect(mapToGlobal(geometry().topLeft()), geometry().size());
      new HtmlWindow("Winnow - My Topic",
                     ":/Docs/yourpage.html",
                     QSize(700, 600), r, window());
  }

The fifth argument (`window()` or `this`) sets the parent so the help window closes with its owner. Do not store the returned pointer or delete it manually; WA_DeleteOnClose handles cleanup.

6. Build. No other wiring is required.


⏺ Help System > Editing Help Formatting Globally

Edit Docs/help.css. Every help page picks up the change on the next launch (the .css is loaded fresh by HtmlWindow each time a help page opens).

Runtime overrides: HtmlWindow::applyStyle() appends rules on top of help.css so help text tracks the app font and theme. These win over the help.css values (later rule, equal specificity), so editing them in help.css alone has no effect in the app:

- body font-size  -> app font size (G::strFontSize)
- h1/h2/h3 font-size -> help.css design size scaled by G::fontSize/12

- h1/h2/h3 colour -> G::header1/2/3Color (theme-tied; set in WidgetCSS::css()) applyStyle() also re-runs on every font/theme change (HtmlWindow::refreshOpenWindows, called from MW), so open help windows update live. To change these, edit applyStyle() (font-size/colours) or the G::headerNColor values in WidgetCSS::css() (colours).

Constraint: QTextBrowser supports only Qt's HTML 4 / CSS 2.1 subset

  https://doc.qt.io/qt-6/richtext-html-subset.html

Avoid flexbox, grid, CSS custom properties, and HTML5 semantic tags — Qt silently ignores them.

For per-page overrides, add a <style> block to that page's <head>. It cascades on top of help.css.


⏺ Help System > VS Code Workflow

Recommended editor: VS Code (free) with the Microsoft "Live Preview" extension. Reasons:

- Plain-text HTML editing — will not silently inject CSS3 that Qt ignores (which is why WYSIWYG editors like Typora and Pinegrow are NOT recommended).
- Side-by-side browser preview that refreshes on save.

- The <link rel="stylesheet" href="help.css"> in each page makes the preview match Winnow's rendering closely.

Mac-native alternative: BBEdit (free tier) — same plain-text philosophy.

Expected differences between VS Code preview and Winnow:

- Font sizes will not match pixel-for-pixel (Chromium vs QTextBrowser defaults). help.css sets explicit sizes on body/h1/h2/h3 to narrow the gap, but Winnow overrides them at runtime (body = app font size, headings scaled), so the preview shows help.css's static fallback sizes, not Winnow's actual sizes.

- Heading colours differ: VS Code shows help.css's static h1/h2/h3 colours, while Winnow overrides them with the theme-tied G::header1/2/3Color.

- Anything outside the CSS 2.1 subset (above) will render in VS Code but not in Winnow. When in doubt, check in Winnow.


⏺ Help System > Entry Points

Each entry lists the UI trigger, then its caller and the HTML file opened:

Help menu / "?" shortcut

  MW::help  ->  winnowhelp.html

Folders panel "?" button

  FSTree::howThisWorks  ->  foldershelp.html

Filters panel "?" button

  Filters::howThisWorks  ->  filtershelp.html

Embellish panel "?" button

  EmbelProperties::coordHelp  ->  embelcoordsystem.html

Filmstrip / status bar help

  MW::helpThumbViewStatusBarSymbols  ->  helpfilmstrip.html

Find Duplicates "?" button

FindDuplicatesDlg::on_helpBtn_clicked  ->  findduplicateshelp.html

Find Duplicates delta info

FindDuplicatesDlg::on_deltaThresholdToolBtn_clicked  ->  pixeldeltahelp.html

Ingest dialog "?" button

  IngestDlg::on_helpBtn_clicked  ->  ingestautopath.html

Develop dock, Raw panel "?" button

RawPanel::tipsRequested -> DevelopProperties::howThisWorks  ->  develophelp.html

Develop dock, every band [:] menu — see "Develop panel help pages" below

  DevelopProperties::sectionHelp / editsHelp / submasksHelp

Not converted to HtmlWindow (intentionally):

- MW::helpShortcuts (helpform shortcutsform.ui) — interactive QTreeWidget, not HTML.


⏺ Help System > Develop Panel Help Pages (One Page per Band [:] Menu)

EVERY header band in the Develop dock ends its [:] menu with a "<band> help" item, after a separator, opening that band's own page in an HtmlWindow. One page per panel, not one long module page: each panel has its own grammar, and the help a user wants is the help for the band they are standing on. develophelp.html remains the module-wide page (Raw panel "?" and PV_Scope).

  Band / section          Menu item            Docs/ page
  ------------------      -----------------    ----------------------------
  Edits (scope list)      Edits help           developeditshelp.html
  Submasks                Submasks help        developsubmaskshelp.html
  Basic                   Basic help           developbasichelp.html
  Curves                  Curves help          developcurveshelp.html
  Color                   Color help           developcolorhelp.html
  Calibrate               Calibrate help       developcalibratehelp.html
  Color Grade             Color Grade help     developcolorgradehelp.html
  Detail                  Detail help          developdetailhelp.html
  Effects                 Effects help         developeffectshelp.html

Wiring:

- Tree SECTIONS (Basic .. Effects): DevelopProperties::showSectionMenu adds tr("%1 help").arg(groupLabel(group)) last and calls sectionHelp(group), which maps the PV_* code to its page. A new section therefore needs only a case in sectionHelp (PV_Scope falls back to howThisWorks) plus the page + qrc entry.

- The two BAND widgets own no Docs knowledge: ScopeHeaderBase::helpRequested and SubmaskList::helpRequested are emitted from their menus and connected in bindScopeHeader / the SubmaskList block of DevelopProperties to editsHelp / submasksHelp. ScopeHeaderLab::addHelpAction appends the item to every menu the panel pops (header band, editor band, minimal-layout bar, scope rows), so the item is reachable in all three Edits layouts; the legacy ScopeHeader carries it too. SubmaskList emits deferred, like every other emission there (the menu's button is destroyed by a rebuild).
- Pages must be listed in winnow.qrc (":/Docs/<name>.html") or the window opens empty.

*** ACTION ENABLE/DISABLE RULES ***


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Overview

Menu/toolbar actions that act on the loaded folder, the current selection, or the set of picked images are enabled/disabled centrally in MW::enableSelectionDependentMenus() (`Main/menusandactions.cpp`).  Pick-dependent actions are additionally kept in sync by MW::updatePickDependentActions() (`Main/mainwindow.cpp`), which is called from the pick-toggle paths.

Actions that are always valid (app/window/help/about, dock-visibility toggles, workspaces, preferences, open folder, color manage, combine raw+jpg, diagnostics, folder-tree/USB context actions handled by the rename* slots, and "Manage External Applications") are never gated here.


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Conditions and Triggers

Four state conditions drive enabling.  Each must have a trigger that re-runs the relevant update when the condition can change, otherwise actions get stuck disabled:

- dmHasRows  (dm->rowCount() > 0 — a folder is loaded) Triggers: fileSelectionChange (auto-select after load), selectionChanged (on clear), and view-mode switches — all call enableSelectionDependentMenus().

- hasSelection / selection count  (sel->count()) Trigger: the selection model's selectionChanged signal is connected directly to enableSelectionDependentMenus() in MW::createSelection() (`Main/initialize.cpp`).  This is essential: extending/shrinking a selection with shift/ctrl does NOT change the current index, so fileSelectionChange alone would never re-evaluate count-dependent actions (e.g. focusStack stuck disabled after growing the selection to two).

- isAnyPick  (dm->isAnyPick()) Triggers: updatePickDependentActions() is called from every pick-toggle path in `Main/pick.cpp` (togglePick, togglePickUnlessRejected, togglePickMouseOverItem, toggleReject, updatePickFromHistory, recoverPickLog) and after ingest in MW::setIngested() (`Main/setandupdate.cpp`).  enableSelectionDependentMenus() also calls it.

- isUpDown  (gridView or tableView visible) Trigger: view-mode switches call enableSelectionDependentMenus().


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > The Menu vs Shortcut Pitfall

Disabling a QMenu (menu->setEnabled(false)) only greys its entry in the menu bar.  It does NOT disable the window-level keyboard shortcuts of its child actions, because each child is added to MW via addAction() with its own shortcut (e.g. rate "1", label "6", filter "Shift+6").  Those shortcuts keep firing with no folder loaded.

Therefore menus whose children carry shortcuts are gated with the local setMenuEnabled() helper, which disables the menu AND every child action individually:

ratingsMenu, labelsMenu, sortMenu, filterMenu, utilitiesMenu, embelExportMenu. developExportPresetMenu is the exception that needs none of this: its items are built on aboutToShow and carry no shortcuts, so disabling the menu alone is enough. The Open With submenu is iterated the same way but stays enabled so manageAppAction remains reachable; only its per-app actions are gated.

The Go menu is a third pattern: the QMenu itself is never disabled, because "Go to Folder..." is a way IN to a folder and must be reachable with nothing loaded (the same reason Open With stays enabled for manageAppAction).  Every other Go item -- jump, the arrow/page/home/end navigation, the add-to-selection variants, the scroll keys, next/prev pick, random image -- is gated individually just below, so with no folder loaded the menu opens with one live item and the rest greyed with their reason.


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Return and Enter

Return and Enter are the SAME command key in Winnow: every operation bound to one must also fire on the other.  Two traps make this easy to get wrong, so never test the key codes by hand -- use the helpers in `Main/global.h`:

- G::isEnterKey(int) / G::isEnterKey(const QKeyEvent*) true for Qt::Key_Return and Qt::Key_Enter.

- G::bareModifiers(const QKeyEvent*) the event's modifiers with Qt::KeypadModifier removed.  Use it for every "bare key" gate, i.e. write

if (G::bareModifiers(e) == Qt::NoModifier) ...

NOT `e->modifiers() == Qt::NoModifier`.

Trap 1: the Enter key -- numeric keypad Enter, or Fn+Return on a MacBook -- reports Qt::Key_Enter, not Qt::Key_Return.  A handler testing only Key_Return ignores it.

Trap 2: that same press ALSO sets Qt::KeypadModifier.  A gate written as `modifiers() == Qt::NoModifier` therefore rejects keypad Enter even when the handler inside it does test Key_Enter.  This is the subtler of the two and it silently broke the Develop Transform commit and the spot-tool fill commit.

QAction shortcuts cannot use the helpers, so bind all three sequences:

  act->setShortcuts({QKeySequence(Qt::Key_Return),
                     QKeySequence(Qt::Key_Enter),
                     QKeySequence(Qt::KeypadModifier | Qt::Key_Enter)});

QShortcutMap does not reliably strip KeypadModifier on macOS, hence the third entry. ZoomDlg (`Dialogs/zoomdlg.cpp`) is the only Return/Enter QAction shortcut in the app.

Qt already treats the two keys identically in QLineEdit (returnPressed), QDialog (default-button activation) and QAbstractItemView (activated), so connections to those signals need nothing.  Only hand-rolled keyPressEvent / eventFilter / event() handlers do.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Return and Enter > Known Gap, Deliberately Not Fixed

Qt::KeypadModifier is handled NOWHERE else in Winnow.  MW::ownsShortcut and MW::disabledActionForShortcut both build a QKeySequence from e->keyCombination(), which keeps the modifier, so no keypad key can ever match a stored sequence.  The numeric keypad is therefore inert for every other shortcut.

This is left as-is on purpose: stripping the modifier there would newly make keypad 1-5 set ratings and keypad 6-0 set colour labels, which they do not do today.  It is recorded here so it stays a decision rather than a rediscovery.


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Disabled Shortcut Feedback

A disabled QAction does not fire and Qt does not consume its shortcut, so the key falls through as a normal KeyPress.  To tell the user why nothing happened, the qApp event filter (MW::eventFilter, `Main/mainwindow.cpp`) intercepts KeyPress events:

- MW::disabledActionForShortcut(seq) scans MW::actions() for an action whose shortcut matches the pressed key sequence.  It returns the match only if it is disabled AND no enabled action owns the same shortcut (an enabled action would already have handled the key, so there is nothing to explain).

- MW::actionDisabledReason(a) just reads the action's "disabledReason" dynamic property and formats it as "<action name> — <reason>."  The reason is recorded when the action is gated: enableSelectionDependentMenus() uses a gate() helper (and setMenuEnabled() for menu children) that sets both the enabled state and the property; updatePickDependentActions() does the same for ingest/nextPick/prevPick. Because the reason lives next to the gating, the two never drift.  A disabled action with no property set falls back to "not available right now".
- On a match the reason is shown via G::popup and the key is consumed (return true).

Guards: skipped during initialization, for auto-repeat, for modifier-only presses, when focus is in a text-entry widget (QLineEdit/QTextEdit/QPlainTextEdit), and when focus is in another top-level window (fw->window() != MW) so typing is never hijacked.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Disabled Shortcut Feedback > Modeless Dialog Shortcut Guard

MW's single-key shortcuts use the default Qt::WindowShortcut context, which Qt activates whenever the action's window is anywhere in the parent chain of the active window.  A modeless dialog parented to MW (e.g. the Preferences dialog) therefore does NOT shield these shortcuts on its own: a digit typed into a Preferences spin box would fire rate "1" instead of entering text.  The enabled-action case fires via Qt's shortcut map (QEvent::ShortcutOverride) before the KeyPress feedback handler runs, so the eventFilter also intercepts ShortcutOverride: when focus is in another window (fw->window() != MW) and the key matches an MW-owned shortcut (MW::ownsShortcut(seq)), it accepts the override and consumes the event, delivering the key to the focused widget as normal input.  This scopes suppression to MW's own shortcuts, leaving the dialog's own shortcuts (typically Ctrl-modified) free to fire.


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts

Nine keys mean something different in Develop mode than in Preview mode:

  R   Crop / Transform panel      developTransformAction   (Preview: unbound)
  N   New Mask                    developNewScopeAction    (Preview: unbound)
  M   Tweak Mask                  developAddToMaskAction   (Preview: unbound)
  O   Mask overlay tint on/off    toggleMaskOverlayAction  (Preview: Open folder)
  S   Spot removal tool           developSpotAction        (Preview: Slideshow)
  W   White balance sampler       developWbSamplerAction   (Preview: New workspace)
  X   Export developed images     developExportAction      (Preview: Reject)
  H   Develop History panel       historyDockVisibleAction (Preview: unbound)
  P   Develop Presets panel       presetsDockVisibleAction (Preview: Pick)

Plus three keys that apply only while a SUBMASK is being defined (it is still PENDING), handled in MW::eventFilter rather than by an action:

  Return      Commit the submask into the mask (same as the panel's button)
  Opt/Alt     (held) preview + commit that submask as a SUBTRACT
  Shift+Opt   (held) preview + commit that submask as an INTERSECT

Return also closes a submask RE-OPENED from the Submasks list ("Done") -- its edits are already part of the mask, so there is nothing to commit and the modifiers are inert there (its op is changed on its own row).

The modifiers are MOMENTARY: they are read live (press AND release are tracked, so the veil follows the key with the mouse stationary) and are never latched into the submask until it is committed.  Opt is skipped while a Brush/Object stroke is in flight, where it keeps its existing meaning of "erase from this stroke" -- the one rule is that Opt takes away from the STROKE while painting and from the MASK otherwise.  See "Scope & mask model (Global + masks)" in the Develop section.

H and P are the odd ones out in that their actions are NOT Develop-menu local: they are the View menu's "Develop History Panel" / "Develop Presets Panel" dock toggles, which stay enabled in both modes (the menu items work in Preview) while the KEYS are Develop-only, because those docks only exist alongside the Develop dock.

W is DOUBLY contextual: an ACTIVE Transform session claims it for Warp (arbiter rule 1a, which runs before this table), so W reaches the white balance dropper only when no Transform is up -- the tool-local > mode-local ranking below, applied to a panel tool rather than an ImageView one.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Why Not Just Give These Actions a Shortcut

Qt allows exactly ONE QAction per key sequence.  Bind "S" to both slideShowAction and developSpotAction and QShortcutMap reports an ambiguous overload and fires NEITHER, so a mode-local key cannot be expressed as a second QAction.  Nor can the keys be swapped on/off the actions at mode change: that fights the user's shortcut customisations in the "Shortcuts" settings group, and any desync leaves keys bound to the wrong mode.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Mechanism

The Develop actions are created with NO QKeySequence.  MW::loadDevelopShortcuts, called at the end of MW::loadShortcuts, fills developShortcuts -- a QHash<int, QAction*> of bare Qt::Key -> action.  MW::eventFilter routes both QEvent::ShortcutOverride and QEvent::KeyPress to MW::developShortcutIntercept while G::operationMode == Develop:

- ShortcutOverride is accepted, which is what cancels the global QAction (Qt sends the override to the focus widget before the shortcut system runs).

- The KeyPress that follows triggers the Develop action.  The work is done here and not on the override, because the override is only sent for keys a global action owns -- R/N/M/H own none -- and because consuming the KeyPress is what makes the selection rule below a real "ignore" rather than a fall-through.

In Preview mode the arbiter is never called and every key behaves as the global table says.  This is the same two-step pattern ReplacePanel::eventFilter uses.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Precedence: Tool-Local > Mode-Local > Global

A key an armed tool owns ([ / ] brush size, A auto-mask, Cmd+Z undo stroke, Enter and Esc) is deliberately NOT in developShortcuts.  ImageView::event claims those from their global actions itself while its tool is live, so tool-local keys outrank mode-local ones without the table needing to know tools exist.  The converse also matters: the spot tool must NOT claim S, or S could arm the tool but never disarm it.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Contextual Mode-Local Keys (Active Only While a Panel/Tool Is Up)

Some Develop keys are only valid while a specific tool is active, so they cannot live in the static developShortcuts table (that would steal them mode-wide). They are instead a guarded branch INSIDE developShortcutIntercept, ahead of rule 1, keyed off the tool's "active" flag. Today the only case is the Transform panel: while developCropEditing is true, C/L/W/A beat their window-level shortcuts (A Run Droplet, C Compare) and call TransformPanel::handleTransformShortcut(key); Esc calls MW::cancelDevelopTransform (discard the session + hide the panel); Enter/Return calls toggleDevelopTransform (commit + hide, i.e. R again), EXCEPT while a warp quad is traced (imageView->cropIsWarp()) where the branch falls through so ImageView commits the quad (rectify) via its own Enter path. See the Transform section. Doing it in the arbiter -- not in ImageView::event as tool-local keys are -- is deliberate: the crop overlay usually does NOT hold keyboard focus, so an ImageView ShortcutOverride claim never fires (this was the first, wrong, fix). The arbiter is a qApp filter, so it works regardless of focus. ADD FUTURE panel/tool-scoped letter keys here the same way (guard on the tool's active flag), NOT to developShortcuts and NOT to

  ImageView::event.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Space: Temporary Zoom/Pan over a Develop Tool

In Develop mode a mask / spot / crop tool owns the mouse (brush, lasso, eyedropper, spot pins, crop handles), so the loupe's click-to-zoom / drag-to-pan is unavailable while editing.  HOLDING Space borrows it back: the tool's mouse branches are skipped and the base loupe gesture runs (click toggles zoom, drag pans a zoomed image); releasing Space resumes the tool with no state lost.  It applies to every tool, including Crop.

The wheel / two-finger scroll zooms too: in Develop mode ImageView::wheelEvent zooms the image under the cursor (ImageView::wheelZoom, cursor-anchored) instead of the Preview-mode prev/next navigation.  While a Spot or Brush/Object tool is armed the wheel still resizes that tool, a plain wheel inside a Radial Gradient's core ellipse resizes THAT proportionally (see SPATIAL EDITING), and SHIFT + wheel over a Linear Gradient, Radial Gradient or Brush submask changes its FEATHER, so hold Space (spacePanOverride) to zoom with the wheel there -- the same flag that gates the mouse branches also bypasses both tool gestures.  macOS swaps the scroll axes while Shift is held, so the shared helper (wheelDelta) takes whichever axis reported rather than assuming y.

Space is bound to zoomToggleAction globally, and ImageView usually lacks keyboard focus in Develop, so this is driven from MW::eventFilter (a qApp filter that sees KeyPress AND KeyRelease), NOT developShortcutIntercept (which never sees KeyRelease and is not meant for held-state keys).  The ShortcutOverride pass is accepted to free the key from zoomToggleAction; KeyPress/KeyRelease call

ImageView::setSpacePanOverride, which flips the spacePanOverride flag that every ImageView mouse handler is gated on.

A value editor keeps Space as a literal character (same guard as the arbiter), auto- repeat is ignored, and ApplicationDeactivate clears the flag so a Space released in another app can't leave it stuck.  A change that arrives mid-stroke is deferred (see ImageView::applyDeferredSpacePan) and applied on the next mouse release so a partly- painted stroke isn't corrupted.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Guards (MW::developShortcutIntercept, in Order)

- Focus in a QDialog -> bail.  Mirrors the MODELESS DIALOG SHORTCUT GUARD, which cannot cover these keys because it matches via MW::ownsShortcut() and a Develop action has no sequence to match.  Without it a bare S typed into a Preferences field would arm the spot tool.  Floating QDockWidgets are separate top-level windows too but belong to the MW workspace, hence the QDialog test.

- Esc while a submask is open -> discard it if still PENDING, else just close it (escapeMaskTool) and consume. Placed BEFORE the value-editor bail so it fires even when focus is on a mask slider (Hue/Sat) or the Color Range wheel; skipped (falls through) while the WB dropper or Transform is up, whose Esc handlers run later.

- Focus in a value editor (QAbstractSlider / QAbstractSpinBox / QLineEdit / QComboBox) -> bail, so arrows still nudge a Develop slider and letters still type. EXCEPTION (2026-08-15): a LETTER that is in developShortcuts is still arbitrated over a slider or spin box (incl. a spin box's internal QLineEdit -- see isTextEntryWidget()).  Focus sits on a dock slider for most of a Develop session, and bailing there passed the letter to the GLOBAL action on that key: O opened the folder dialog instead of toggling the mask overlay (likewise S = Slideshow, X = Reject, G = Grid), the very collision the arbiter exists to stop.  A slider/spin box has no use for a letter; only real text entry (the search field, an editable QComboBox) keeps its letters unconditionally.  FIX (2026-08-29): isTextEntryWidget() had been treating EVERY QComboBox as text entry, editable or not, which bailed out of the arbiter entirely.  The Develop dock's rows are read-only combos (View transform is the first row, WB preset the next) and a combo keeps the focus after a pick, so after choosing a View the next mode-local key went to its GLOBAL action instead -- R stopped opening/committing Transform, and S/O/N/M/W/X/H/P were dead the same way.  A read-only combo now only loses its type-to-select-an-item search, which the mode-local keys outrank.
- Auto-repeat -> bail; every Develop key is a toggle or pops a dialog.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Selection Keys Are Ignored in Develop Mode

Arrows, PgUp/PgDn and Home/End (bare or Shift'd, plus the Ctrl+Shift+Alt pick-nav and Shift+Ctrl+Right random-image combos) are swallowed in Develop mode unless thumbView has the focus, so the keyboard cannot navigate off the image being developed.  Alt+key is EXCLUDED -- Alt+arrow / Alt+Home scroll and pan within the image, which is useful while developing -- but Ctrl+Shift+Alt+Left/Right IS pick navigation, hence the Ctrl test in isSelectionKey().  Focus is tested with MW::thumbViewHasFocus().

Note the bare Left/Right navigation intercept further down MW::eventFilter bypasses the shortcut system entirely (it is faster than a menu shortcut).  It needs no mode guard of its own only because the arbiter runs first and consumes the arrow; keep that

  ordering.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Only Loupe View Is Allowed in Develop Mode

Develop edits a single image, so the Grid (G), Table (T) and Compare (C) view modes are unavailable while in Develop -- only Loupe (E) remains.  This is NOT arbitrated in developShortcutIntercept: MW::setOperationMode forces Loupe on entry (loupeDisplay when G::mode != "Loupe") and MW::enableSelectionDependentMenus disables asGridAction, asTableAction and asCompareAction while G::operationMode == Develop.
