A disabled QAction greys its View-menu item AND stops its G/T/C shortcut firing (same idea as Save Develop Preset), so one gate covers both the menu and the key.  The disabled reason ("not available in Develop mode") surfaces via the Disabled Shortcut Feedback path.  Returning to Preview re-enables them (subject to the usual folder / two-selected gates).


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Discoverability

A key-less QAction renders no shortcut column, so each Develop action embeds its hint in its text after a tab ("New Mask\tN") -- the idiom pickAction and jumpAction already use for their multi-key hints.  The keys are surfaced in three places: the Utilities > Develop submenu (built in MW::createUtilMenu; MW::syncDevelopMenuEnabled greys the mode-local items outside Develop mode so they never advertise a key that does something else), the Develop action-row button tooltips, and Help > Shortcuts.

GOTCHA: syncDevelopMenuEnabled must run on the MODE CHANGE, not only on the menu's aboutToShow.  The arbiter dispatches the mode-local keys by calling QAction::trigger(), and trigger() is a no-op on a disabled action -- so opening the Develop menu once in Preview (which greys those items) left R/S/O/N/M/W/X dead for the whole next Develop session, until the menu happened to be opened again.  MW::setOperationMode therefore calls it too.

The one exception is Save Develop Preset (Cmd+Shift+N): a MODIFIED combo owned by no other action, so it is a normal QAction with a real QKeySequence (not in the arbiter's bare-key table). It is kept Develop-only by its enabled state -- setOperationMode enables it only in Develop mode and syncDevelopMenuEnabled greys the menu item in Preview -- since a disabled QAction's shortcut does not fire. See the "Develop presets (the Presets dock)" subsection in the DEVELOP section.


⏺⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Develop Mode Local Shortcuts > Export with Preset Is a Submenu, Not an Action

Develop > Export with preset is a QMenu rebuilt on aboutToShow from the export preset store (MW::buildDevelopExportPresetMenu), the way embelExportMenu is rebuilt from the embellish templates. It has no action and therefore no shortcut, so it is kept Develop-local by syncDevelopMenuEnabled disabling the MENU (which also disables it when no export preset has been saved yet). See "Export" in the DEVELOP section.


⏺ Action Enable/Disable Rules > Rules by Action

Requires dmHasRows (folder loaded):

- File: revealFile(+context), reportMetadata, copyFolderPathFromContext, copyImagePathFromContext, rename, saveAsFile, refreshCurrent (always available while a folder is loaded — needed to pick up files added externally).

- Edit: selectAll, invertSelection, shareFiles, copyFiles, copyImage, deleteImages, pick, pickMouseOver, reject, pickUnlessRejected, popPickHistory, tokenTemplateEditor, rotateRight/Left, ratingsMenu + rate0..5, labelsMenu + label0..5, utilitiesMenu (mediaReadSpeed, visCmpImages).

- Go: jump, random, and all horizontal/home/end/page navigation (+AddToSelection) and the scroll family except up/down.
- Filter: filterMenu + all filter* children.
- Sort: sortMenu + all sort* children.
- Embellish: embelExportMenu + children, embelNew/Read/SaveTemplate.

- View: asLoupe, asGrid, asTable, copyInfoTextToClipboard, slideShow, zoomTo/In/Out/Toggle, thumbsEnlarge/Shrink.

Requires Develop mode (syncDevelopMenuEnabled, not the gate() table):

- every action in the Develop-mode shortcut table above, plus the developExportPresetMenu submenu, which additionally requires at least one saved export preset.

Requires selection count >= 2:

- asCompare (compare view), meanStack, focusStack.

Requires isAnyPick (at least one picked image):

- ingest, nextPick, prevPick.

Requires a view that scrolls vertically (isUpDown — grid or table visible):

- keyUp/Down (+AddToSelection), keyScrollUp/Down.

Other:

- Open With per-app actions: require selection (hasSelection); manageAppAction always enabled.
- pasteFiles: enabled when the clipboard holds file URLs (Utilities::clipboardHasUrls()).


*** MEMORY CARD EJECT ***


⏺ Memory Card Eject > Overview

Ejecting a removable card (USB drive / memory card) safely unmounts it from the OS. The platform-specific work lives in UsbUtil (`Utilities/usbutil.cpp`):

- UsbUtil::eject(rootPath) — Windows: DISMOUNT_VOLUME then STORAGE_EJECT_MEDIA via DeviceIoControl; macOS: "diskutil eject <mount point>".  Returns true only on a real success (the Windows path reports failure if either ioctl fails).

- UsbUtil::isEjectable(rootPath) — true only when rootPath is a mounted volume that is valid (ready, writable) AND removable (DRIVE_REMOVABLE/DRIVE_CDROM on Windows, under /Volumes/ on macOS).  Both checks require rootPath to be the volume's mount root, not a subfolder.

MW::ejectUsb(path) (`Main/mainwindow.cpp`) wraps this: if the drive being ejected is currently loaded in the datamodel it first calls stop() and clears the FSTree selection (reading files from a drive after ejection would crash), then ejects and shows a confirmation/failure popup.


⏺ Memory Card Eject > Entry Points

Eject is reachable ONLY from the context menus of the Folders (FSTree) and Bookmarks panels — there is no main-menu eject action.  The context action (ejectActionFromContextMenu -> MW::ejectUsbFromContextMenu) ejects the drive of the moused-over folder (mouseOverFolderPath).  MW::renameEjectUsbMenu() sets the action's label ("Eject Memory Card <drive>") and enabled state from that folder as the context menu is built.


⏺ Memory Card Eject > Drive Root Normalization

The moused-over (or ingest source) path is usually a subfolder, e.g. a DCIM folder, but isEjectable/eject require the volume mount root.  Both MW::ejectUsb() and MW::renameEjectUsbMenu() therefore normalize with QStorageInfo(path).rootPath() before calling UsbUtil, so eject works (and the menu item enables) for any folder on the card, not only when the volume root itself is selected.


⏺ Memory Card Eject > Auto-Eject After Ingest

The ingest dialog has an "eject" checkbox bound to the persisted setting autoEjectUsb ("autoEjectUSB").  When set, MW::ingest() ejects the ingest source card after a successful FOREGROUND ingest — both the gotoIngestFolder early-return path and the normal path, after the copy has finished (IngestDlg runs the foreground copy synchronously during exec()).  The eject targets dm->primaryFolderPath(), normalized to the drive root by ejectUsb().

Auto-eject is foreground-only by design.  The ingest dialog disables AND clears the eject checkbox whenever background ingest is selected (IngestDlg::

on_backgroundIngestChk_stateChanged, `Dialogs/ingestdlg.cpp`), and the checkbox is initialized after the background checkbox so a persisted autoEjectUsb=true is reconciled to false at construction.  As a result autoEjectUsb is always false for a background ingest; MW::ingest() additionally guards the eject with !isBackgroundIngest. A background ingest never auto-ejects (ejecting mid-copy would fail / corrupt the transfer).


*** CHECK FOR UPDATES ***


⏺ Check for Updates > Overview

Winnow checks whether a newer release is available for download.  Distribution is plain downloads (notarized DMG on macOS, Inno Setup installer on Windows) from the

  DigitalOcean server at https://winnow.ca, so the check only NOTIFIES the user and, on

request, opens the platform download URL in the default browser — it does not auto-install.  (The old Windows-only Qt `maintenancetool` path is gone.)

Two entry points, both calling MW::checkForUpdate(bool silent) (`Main/mainwindow.cpp`):

- Help → "Check for updates" — silent == false (loud): also reports "up to date" and network errors via popups, and ignores the skip-version setting.

- Startup, from MW::showEvent — a deferred QTimer::singleShot fires checkForUpdate(true) 4 s after the window shows, gated by the checkIfUpdate preference and skipped when G::isTest (no network during headless / soak runs).  Silent: it only speaks when an update is available and has not been skipped.


⏺ Check for Updates > Mechanism

checkForUpdate() does an async GET (QNetworkAccessManager, no blocking wait) of a small per-platform descriptor:

  macOS:   https://winnow.ca/winnow_mac/version.json
  Windows: https://winnow.ca/winnow_win/version.json

Shape: { "version": "2.05",

  "url":     ".../current/Winnow2.05.dmg",
  "notes":   ".../winnow/versions.html" }

MW::onUpdateCheckReply() parses the JSON and compares with QVersionNumber (NOT string compare).  The running version is WINNOW_VERSION (`Main/mainwindow.h`, exposed as versionNumber), single-sourced from CMake project(Winnow VERSION ...).  If the remote version is greater it shows the UpdateAppDlg dialog (`Dialogs/updateAppDlg.*`):

- Download → MW::downloadAndOpenUpdate(url): the installer is fetched in-app (no browser, progress shown in G::popup) to the Downloads folder, then opened — on macOS `open` mounts the DMG and brings its Finder window to the front (on top of Winnow); on Windows the downloaded Inno Setup installer is launched.

- Skip this version → UpdateAppDlg::skipRequested() is true; the caller stores the offered version in updateSkipVersion ("updateSkipVersion") so the silent startup check no longer prompts for it (a loud manual check still does).
- Not now → no state change; offered again next launch.

Preference: checkIfUpdate ("checkIfUpdate", default true) — "Check for program updates at startup" in the preferences dialog (`Dialogs/prefdlg.*`, updateAppChk).


⏺ Check for Updates > Publishing version.json

The deploy scripts publish the descriptor (see packaging/README.md):

- macOS deploy.command promote step 7b writes/uploads winnow_mac/version.json from the promoted version, and curl-checks it.

- Windows deploy.ps1 writes/uploads winnow_win/version.json when $VersionJsonTarget is set.  Its url points at winnow_win/current/, so the Setup.exe must be in current/. Build dependency: the client needs Qt6::Network (added to CMakeLists.txt).


*** FIND DUPLICATES ***

FindDuplicatesDlg (`Dialogs/findduplicatesdlg.*`) finds duplicates of a set of candidate images among a set of target images. Launched from MW::findDuplicates, which runs it modally (exec) and, on accept, rebuilds the Compare filter so the flagged duplicates can be shown.

Two columns of data drive everything:

- The candidate "A" list — the images being checked. Held in the datamodel proxy dm->sf, addressed through the aSfRows mapping (see below).

- The target "B" list — bItems, built from the include/exclude folders dragged into the dialog. Each B entry caches name, type, createdDate, aspect, duration and a thumbnail (im) for pixel comparison.


⏺ Find Duplicates > Candidates (A List) and the Model Mapping

Candidates are the SELECTED images in dm->sf, not all filtered images. setupModel() builds aSfRows, a list mapping each candidate index (the loop variable 'a' / the row in the dialog's QStandardItemModel) to its dm->sf proxy row:

- aSfRows is populated from dm->isSelected(sfRow) over the filtered proxy.
- If nothing is selected it falls back to all filtered rows, so the dialog is never empty.

Because the candidate list is no longer 1:1 with dm->sf, every candidate access to the datamodel goes through aSfRow(a) (returns -1 when out of range, so an invalid QModelIndex yields safe empty data rather than throwing). The dialog model row and results[a]/matches[a] stay keyed by the candidate index a; dm->sf is reached only via aSfRow(a).


⏺ Find Duplicates > Targets (B List)

buildBList() expands the include folders (optionally recursing subfolders), subtracts the exclude folders, then appends a bItem per supported file found.

- If no candidate is a video (candidatesHaveVideo() is false, tested via G::VideoColumn), videos are excluded from the targets too — there is no point comparing against video duplicates when no candidate is a video. getMetadataBItems() then fills each bItem's metadata and thumbnail.


⏺ Find Duplicates > Comparison and Thumbnail Normalization

on_compareBtn_clicked() runs the search: clear → buildBList → getMetadataBItems → pixelCompare() (when "same pixels" is checked) or findMatches() (criteria match: name/type/date/aspect/duration). matched candidates are checked in the dialog table.

Pixel comparison only makes sense if the two thumbnails share dimensions, so both the candidate (A, from the decoration-role icon) and target (B) thumbnails are run through normalizeBThumb(): scaled to 256 px on the long side (aspect preserved, the same 256 box used to extract the A icon) and converted to QImage::Format_RGB32. For matching-aspect images this yields identical dimensions.

compareRGB() sums the per-channel absolute differences over the overlapping rectangle. It reads pixels directly from constScanLine() as QRgb (both inputs are already RGB32) — QImage::pixelColor() per pixel was ~50x slower and, run over every A x B combination, was the original cause of the long main-thread stall (beachball).


⏺ Find Duplicates > Video Frames (Async Sync)

Video target thumbnails come from FrameDecoder asynchronously: getMetadataBItems() queues each video (addToQueue) and increments pendingVideoFrames; the first frame arrives later at setImageFromVideoFrame(), which stores the normalized thumbnail and decrements the counter. getMetadataBItems() then WAITS for pendingVideoFrames to reach 0 before returning (pumping the event loop, with a 30 s timeout and abort check), so pixelCompare() never runs against null video thumbnails. pendingVideoFrames is reset in clear().


⏺ Find Duplicates > Responsiveness

The whole search runs synchronously on the GUI thread. The dialog's progress/abort hooks were originally gated on G::useProcessEvents, which is globally false, so the GUI froze for the entire run. The compute loops (getMetadataBItems, pixelCompare, findMatches) now pump the event loop unconditionally; pixelCompare throttles to one update/processEvents per percent to avoid flooding the loop. on_compareBtn_clicked guards re-entry (if (isRunning) return), disables compareBtn during the run, and resets isRunning / re-enables the button on every exit path.


⏺ Find Duplicates > Output

on_updateDupsAndQuitBtn_clicked() writes the result back to the datamodel: it first clears G::CompareColumn on ALL dm->sf rows (candidates are only a subset, so a candidate-only pass would leave stale flags), then sets it true for each checked candidate via aSfRow(a), and accepts the dialog. MW::findDuplicates then refreshes the Compare filter.


⏺ Find Duplicates > Dialog Layout

findduplicatesdlg.ui lays the upper area out as leftFrame then rightFrame inside horizontalLayout_13. leftFrame (on the left) holds the candidate images table (tv); rightFrame (on the right) holds the target include/exclude folder lists and the comparison controls. The frame names reflect their on-screen positions.


*** RAW DECODING (SENSOR DEMOSAIC) ***


⏺ Raw Decoding (Sensor Demosaic) > Architecture

Full-sensor RAW decoding (demosaic), based on LibRaw, is being ported in phases. It is separate from the existing embedded-JPG preview path: a RAW file still shows its embedded JPG until a per-format sensor decoder exists.

  Pipeline (ImageFormats/Raw/):
      ImageDecoder::load()
        -> RawFormat::Create(ext)          factory; "arw" -> SonyRaw, else nullptr (embedded JPG)
        -> RawFormat::Decode()             shared, camera-agnostic pipeline:

1. UnpackCfa()                 PER-FORMAT virtual (THE override point): vendor packed/compressed bitstream -> normalised CFA mosaic (RawImage). Lives in the vendor .cpp (canon.cpp, nikon.cpp ...) as a class SEPARATE from that file's metadata parser.
2. SubtractBlack()            shared per-2x2-position black subtraction + clip.

3. Demosaic::Run()            shared, camera-agnostic: Bilinear3x3 for Bayer, XTransWindow (5x5) for Fuji X-Trans; AHD/DCB later. Operates only on the CFA mosaic + pattern (Bayer) or raw.xtrans (6x6 map), so it is unit-testable.

4. RawColor::ToCameraNative() shared: CHARACTERISES the image (builds the camera->working matrix and the as-shot multipliers into WorkingImage::cam) but does NOT apply them. Pixels stay in the sensor's own primaries, tagged ColorSpace::CameraNative, unclamped. Real colour (dcraw cam_xyz_coeff); see COLOUR below.

5. Develop::Apply()           stage 0 is the INPUT PROFILE (camera-native -> working space), then the parametric edits in linear space.
6. OutputTransform::ToImage() baseline tone curve (RAW only) + primaries + gamma -> QImage.

WHY THE DECODE STOPS AT CAMERA-NATIVE. The buffer RawColor produces is what WorkingImageCache stores, so anything baked in there can only be undone by decoding the raw again. Applying the white balance and the colour matrix downstream of the cache -- in Develop -- makes a white-balance or camera-profile change a RE-RENDER rather than a full re-decode, which is the whole point of the split. The old code also clamped negatives to zero here, destroying every out-of-gamut sensor colour before the user had touched the image; that clamp is gone, and out-of-gamut values now survive to the output stage, the only stage that knows what gamut it is rendering into.

It costs nothing per pixel: Develop::buildPointCoeffs folds the input profile, the white balance, exposure, the Colour RGB sliders and the Calibrate matrix into a SINGLE 3x3 (PointCoeffs::preMat), so the fused point pass does one matrix where it used to do a matrix in the decoder plus three gains plus a second matrix. Develop::ToWorkingSpace is the standalone version, used only when the point pass cannot carry it: an identity edit (no point pass at all) and an active Denoise (op #1, which runs BEFORE the point pass and must see working-space pixels).

Demosaic is deliberately NOT the override point: the algorithm is shared across cameras;
only the unpack/decompress step is vendor-specific (mirrors LibRaw's real structure).

Threading: one RawFormat instance is constructed per decode (per decoder thread). Unlike the Metadata-owned parser singletons, RawFormat instances are never shared, so the per-decode scratch carries no cross-thread races.

SonyRaw::UnpackCfa (ImageFormats/Sony/sony.cpp) handles UNCOMPRESSED ARW (Compression 1, e.g. A9 II: 14-bit LE uint16, Bayer RGGB) AND lossy "ARW Compressed" (Compression 32767):

UNCOMPRESSED: reads StripOffsets/Counts, ImageWidth/Length, BitsPerSample, CFAPattern (33422), WhiteLevel (50717), via the plumbed RawSensorInfo or a self-walk. ARW2 COMPRESSED (decodeSonyArw2, tried first): each 16-byte group encodes 16 photosites of one Bayer colour across 32 columns -- an 11-bit max+min, the indices holding them, then 7-bit deltas shifted to fit (max-min). The 11-bit values expand through a Sony tone curve from tag 0x7010 (4 control points): breakpoints at point>>2, slope starts at 4 and doubles each segment; sample = curve[pix<<1]>>2. Columns are de-interleaved even-then-odd within each 32-col group. Ported from dcraw's sony_arw2_load_raw and VERIFIED byte-identical to libraw on an A9 ARW. Levels/WB come from the encrypted SR2Private block (applySr2Levels, below), as they do on the uncompressed path; the matrix is the per-model lookup. The NEWER "Lossless Compressed RAW" (Compression JPEG, e.g. A1/A7R5) is lossless JPEG -> can reuse LosslessJpeg later; not handled yet.

Other comp values -> Decode() returns false -> ImageDecoder falls back to the embedded JPG.

METADATA PLUMBING (RawSensorInfo): the per-file facts UnpackCfa needs (CFA strip offset/length, geometry, bitsPerSample, compression, CFA pattern, levels, sample byte order) live in struct RawSensorInfo (Metadata/rawsensorinfo.h -- dependency-free), carried on ImageMetadata::rawInfo. Sony::parse fills it once during the normal metadata read (from the SubIFD0 it already parses -- no second walk). Persistence to the cache decode path: DataModel keeps a lock-guarded QHash<fPath, RawSensorInfo> (fPathRawInfo*), written in addMetadataForItem and cleared on folder reload; ImageDecoder fetches it via dm->fPathRawInfoGet() in cache mode (indMeta carries it directly in independent mode) and hands it to RawFormat::Decode.

GATED ON G::useRaw: when raw decoding is OFF (previews only), both the parse-time fill and the addMetadataForItem store are skipped, so the MetaRead path pays nothing -- important because the parse-time extraction grows (makernote colour matrix etc.). If useRaw is toggled on after a folder is read, rawInfo will be absent from the store and UnpackCfa falls back to its self-walk, so correctness holds; the store repopulates on the next folder read. SonyRaw::UnpackCfa uses rawInfo when valid and otherwise falls back to its self-contained TIFF/EP walk (readSonyTiffSensorInfo). CRUCIAL: the self-walk fills the SAME fields as the parse path -- geometry AND colour (model->matrix via tag 272; the DNG-tag black/WB fallbacks). If it only filled geometry, a toggle-on after a useRaw-off load would render with an identity matrix / no WB (green tint). Both paths therefore produce identical colour -- and both then have their levels overwritten by applySr2Levels, so neither can drift on the values that matter. The CFAPattern->enum mapping is shared via cfaPatternFromPlaneColor() in rawimage.h.

COLOUR (RawColor::ToCameraNative, real): follows dcraw/libraw cam_xyz_coeff. Input is the model's XYZ(D65)->camera matrix (DNG ColorMatrix / adobe_coeff convention), looked up by model in ImageFormats/Raw/cameramatrix.cpp -- a FULL port of libraw's adobe_coeff for the mainstream still-camera makers (776 models; phone/action/cinema/bare-sensor and 4-colour CMYG/RGBE entries omitted). Each row also carries adobe_coeff t_black/t_maximum, read via cameraLevelsForModel() and used as a fallback black/white where a decoder would otherwise guess (e.g. Canon, whose saturation is below the bit-depth max -- 7D Mark II clips at 13584, not 16383). Lookup is a case-insensitive longest-prefix match, so one "Nikon D800" row covers D800/D800E and "Sony ILCE-9" covers ILCE-9/9M2, while the most specific key wins (a short "Nikon D3" never shadows "D300"). Regenerate from a newer libraw colordata.cpp via tools/gen_cameramatrix.py when bodies are added; a wrong matrix is a hue shift, not a crash, so verify visually. From it RawColor

builds camRgb = xyzToCam.rgbToXyz, row-normalises so neutral maps to neutral, derives the neutral white balance preMul = 1/rowSum, and inverts to get camera->linear-sRGB (rgbCam). It STORES the white balance (as-shot raw.camMul when the parser filled it, else preMul; normalised to green) and the matrix in WorkingImage::cam but does NOT apply them, and does not clamp -- Develop's stage 0 owns that now (see the Decode pipeline above), so the cached buffer stays sensor-native and unclamped. The matrix + camMul ride on RawSensorInfo (xyzToCam, camMul, hasColorMatrix), filled at parse time (model known) so they plumb to the cache path like the rest of rawInfo. An unknown model -> identity xyzToCam -> sRGB-assumed pass-through (approx, still renders).

SONY AS-SHOT WB + BLACK (SR2Private, ENCRYPTED): an ARW does NOT carry BlackLevel or the as-shot white balance in any plaintext IFD. WB_RGGBLevels (0x7313, R G1 G2 B), BlackLevel (0x7310) and WhiteLevel (0x787f) live in an SR2SubIFD that is obfuscated with Sony's LFSR stream cipher, reached from IFD0's DNGPrivateData (0xC634) -> a small plaintext IFD carrying 0x7200 (SR2SubIFD offset), 0x7201 (length) and 0x7221 (key). readSr2Levels (sony.cpp) decrypts that block (sonyDecrypt, dcraw's pad generator -- note the generator index starts at 127, where dcraw's init loop leaves it, not 0) and walks the plaintext IFD, rebasing value offsets by -0x7200offset because they are still absolute file offsets. applySr2Levels then overwrites raw.black/raw.camMul on the freshly unpacked mosaic; BOTH the compressed and uncompressed unpack paths call it, so it is the single source of Sony levels. WAS A REAL BUG: Winnow used to look these tag numbers up in the plaintext raw SubIFD, silently found nothing, and fell back to a hard-coded black of 512 with camMul left at 1,1,1,1 (so RawColor took the matrix-derived neutral, never the as-shot WB). Bodies whose real pedestal IS 512 -- most E-mount -- rendered correctly by luck; a DSC-RX100 (pedestal 800) came out visibly washed out, its blacks lifted ~1.8% of full scale. Validated tag-for-tag against ExifTool on nine bodies (RX100, A7R2/4/5, A9/A9II/A9III, A1II). raw.white is deliberately NOT taken from 0x787f: Sony declares 15360 but the ARW tone curve expands sensor values past it, and libraw -- the oracle this unpack is byte-validated against -- saturates at 16383; adopting 15360 would brighten every Sony render ~7% away from the reference. LIMITATION: clipped highlights can show a slight tint until highlight recovery lands.

ADOBE DNG (DngRaw, ImageFormats/Dng/dng.cpp): self-contained -- DngRaw::UnpackCfa walks the DNG's TIFF/EP IFDs (via the shared TiffWalk reader), finds the CFA image (Photometric == CFA 32803, full-res, most pixels), and decodes it. Handles UNCOMPRESSED (Compression 1) and LOSSLESS-JPEG (Compression 7), in STRIP or TILE layout (tiles placed left-to-right, top-to-bottom, edge-cropped). DNG carries its OWN colour, so DngRaw reads it from the file: xyzToCam = ColorMatrix2 (D65; else ColorMatrix1), WB camMul = 1 / AsShotNeutral, plus the DNG BlackLevel (50714) / WhiteLevel (50717) / CFAPattern (33422) -- no per-model table and NOTHING plumbed at parse time (the decode is on-demand, so there is no MetaRead cost; it always self-walks). Registered for "dng" in RawFormat::Create. VERIFIED: C++ CFA output is byte-identical to the validated reference on a single-strip lossless DNG (Leica M10) and a 726-tile lossless DNG (Nikon D850 / Lightroom), both of which render to coherent, correctly coloured images. LIMITATION: assumes ColorMatrix2's illuminant is D65 (no chromatic adaptation yet); packed sub-16-bit uncompressed storage not handled (16-bit storage only).

FUJIFILM RAF (FujiRaw, ImageFormats/Fuji/fuji.cpp): RAF is a Fuji container ("FUJIFILMCCD-RAW "): a big-endian header with offsets (92 = CFA metadata dir, 100/104 = CFA data offset/length) to an embedded JPEG, a CFA directory (tag 0x100 = raw H/W; tag 0x131 = the X-Trans 6x6 colour map, 36 bytes REVERSED -> [yy][xx], 0=R 1=G 2=B; tag 0xC000 = RAFData block with the WB), and the CFA data. The FujiIFD that prefixes the CFA block (cfaOff -> little-endian TIFF -> 0xf000 -> FujiIFD) carries BitsPerSample (0xf003 -> white), per-CFA BlackLevel (0xf00a) and the compressed-strip offset (0xf007). Two CFA encodings, routed on the 16-byte header at cfaOff + 0xf007 (signature 0x4953 = compressed):

- UNCOMPRESSED: the CFA block is rawW*rawH*2 bytes plus a small leading header (size = cfaLen - rawW*rawH*2, e.g. 2048), little-endian 16-bit samples.

- COMPRESSED (lossless/lossy, most current bodies): FujiCompressed::decode (ImageFormats/Fuji/fujicompressed.cpp) -- a faithful port of libraw's fuji_compressed.cpp with all decoder state in a per-call Ctx struct (no globals, so concurrent cache-thread decodes are safe). The 16-byte BE header gives lossless flag, raw_type (16=X-Trans, 0=Bayer), bits, geometry, block_size(0x300)/blocks_in_row/total_lines; each vertical block is a gradient-predicted, adaptive Golomb-Rice bitstream decoded into the rawW*rawH mosaic. Fuji Bayer bodies (GFX) are RGGB; X-Trans uses the 0x131 map. Active-area crop: the decoded mosaic is the FULL sensor (incl. optical-black margins, ~190 black columns on the right of a GFX); crop to RawImageCropTopLeft (0x0110 = top,left) + RawImageCroppedSize (0x0111 = H,W) to drop them, else they show as a black edge. Bayer keeps an even origin (RGGB phase); X-Trans rolls the 6x6 map by (top%6,left%6) so the demosaicer, which tiles from (0,0), still gets the right colours. WB: the 0x2FF0 tag when present, else the as-shot WB_GRBLevels [G,R,B] in the 0xC000 RAFData block. That block ALSO holds WB presets (daylight/tungsten/...) that look identical to a WB triple, so a plain first-match scan can grab the tungsten preset and badly over-saturate; the as-shot WB is instead written as THREE identical consecutive triples, so we require a 3x repeat (G = green reference, smallest). Robust across bodies without libraw's per-model wb_section offset table; within ~1% of libraw on GFX, ~3% on X-T2 (libraw additionally does two-record CCT averaging -- visually negligible for the preview/develop path). Matrix by model (RAF header camera id at 0x1C -> "Fujifilm X-T50"). X-Trans is demosaiced by Demosaic::XTransWindow (5x5 same-colour average over the tiled 6x6 map; native channel exact -- the X-Trans analogue of the bilinear Bayer path). VERIFIED byte-identical to libraw on the uncompressed X-T50 and the compressed X-Trans (X-T2) and Bayer (GFX 50S II) RAFs. LIMITATIONS: the lossy compressed path is ported but unexercised (no lossy test file); white level is the FujiIFD BitsPerSample max (e.g. 16383 at 14-bit) not libraw's tighter per-body saturation (e.g. GFX 15872), so highlights clip slightly late; WB is unaveraged (~3% on X-T2).

PANASONIC RW2 (PanasonicRaw, ImageFormats/Panasonic/panasonic.cpp): RW2 is a TIFF-like file (magic 0x55) whose IFD0 carries Panasonic raw tags -- SensorWidth/Height (0x02/0x03), RawFormat version (0x2D), RawDataOffset (0x118), CFA (0x09: 1=RGGB 2=GRBG 3=GBRG 4=BGGR), per-channel WB (0x24/0x25/0x26 = R/G/B, green==256) and black (0x1C..0x1E, stored 15 below true). Decodes the classic RawFormat-4 compression (dcraw panasonic_load_raw): a 14-column group code with a per-pair nibble predictor, bits pulled from Panasonic's reversed 16 KB "pana_bits" buffer (refilled rotated by loadFlags = 0x2008; byte index vbits>>3 ^ 0x3FF0). Ported from dcraw, validated BYTE-IDENTICAL to libraw on a DC-GX9 RW2; renders to a coherent, correctly-coloured image. Matrix from the per-model table (DC-GX9). LIMITATION: RawFormat 5/6/7 (newer cameras) use different loadFlags / a different codec -> returns false (fallback).

OLYMPUS ORF (OlympusRaw, ImageFormats/Olympus/olympus.cpp): the ORF TIFF LIES (tags the raw strip Compression 1 / 16-bit) but the strip is Olympus's proprietary 12-bit compression: a Huffman magnitude code (a direct 4096-entry 12-bit-prefix table) + an adaptive bit length per sample + a median predictor (w,n,nw) with a 2-column running carry; the bitstream starts 7 bytes into the strip. Ported from dcraw's olympus_load_raw, validated byte-identical to libraw on an E-M1 ORF. As-shot WB is read from the Olympus MakerNote (non-standard "OLYMPUS\0II\3\0" header -> TiffWalk::initEmbedded with base = makernote start, IFD at +12; ImageProcessing 0x2040 -> WB_RBLevels 0x0100, two SHORTs R,B with green == 256) -- essential, matrix-neutral renders badly green for Olympus. Matrix from the per-model table (E-M1). FIRST CUT: white 4095 (12-bit), E-M1-validated black 255 + BGGR pattern (ORF has no CFAPattern tag); per-model black/pattern/bit-depth is a refinement (the OM-1 may differ). NOTE: TiffWalk::init now accepts non-42 TIFF magics (ORF 0x4F52, RW2 0x55, CR2 0x4352) since the endian marker + a sane first-IFD offset is enough; standard TIFF (magic 42) still validates.

NIKON NEF (NikonRaw, ImageFormats/Nikon/nikon.cpp): self-contained. Walks the NEF IFDs (via TiffWalk) to the CFA SubIFD (Compression 34713 = Nikon NEF Compressed) -- some bodies (Z9) expose SEVERAL such SubIFDs (the full-res frame plus smaller/0x0 placeholders), so the largest valid-geometry candidate is chosen, not the last seen (else the 0x0 IFD fails the geometry check and the whole decode falls back to the embedded preview), then reads the linearization/Huffman metadata from the Nikon type-3 MakerNote (tag 0x96). The MakerNote is an EMBEDDED TIFF ("Nikon\0" + 2 version bytes + "\0\0" + its own header at base = mnOffset+10, offsets relative to base) -- TiffWalk::init(file, base) handles that. Nikon's compression is NOT lossless JPEG: each sample is a Huffman-coded difference (one of dcraw's 6 trees, chosen by version byte + bit depth) against DUAL predictors -- a vertical predictor for the first two columns (per row parity), then a running horizontal predictor per column parity -- mapped through a linearization curve (identity for the 0x46 lossless mode; interpolated with a tree "split" row for the 0x44 lossy mode). Ported from dcraw's nikon_load_raw. Colour matrix from the shared per-model table (added D810/D7200/D850/D2H/D100/D800E/Z9; an unknown model falls back to an identity matrix, which renders with a colour cast -- seen on the Z9 before its row was added); white = (1<<bps)-1; black from MakerNote

tag 0x3d (4-SHORT per-channel pedestal: D850 400, D810 600, Z9 1008 -- matches libraw), falling back to the decoded minimum only on older bodies that omit 0x3d (D2H/D100/D800E, ~0 pedestal). The minimum alone is fragile: one sub-pedestal hot/noisy pixel drags it below the true black, so the shared SubtractBlack under-subtracts and the frame renders washed-out (was seen on the D850, darkest pixel 78 vs true 400). BIT-DEPTH SCALING (essential): 0x3d is always written in the body's NATIVE 14-bit units, even when the frame was shot as a 12-bit NEF, whose samples are the 14-bit readout shifted down two stops -- so the tag is applied as blackTag >> (14 - bps). Taken at face value on a 12-bit file it subtracts 4x the true pedestal and clamps everything but the specular highlights to zero: the frame renders nearly black with a heavy green cast, since the green photosites sit ~40% above red/blue and are the only ones surviving the clamp. Seen on a 12-bit D7200 (tag 600 vs a measured pedestal of ~150: decoded histogram mode 160, frame minimum 128); 14-bit files are unaffected (shift 0). CFA phase read from the raw SubIFD's CFAPattern tag (0x828E) via cfaPatternFromPlaneColor() -- most bodies are RGGB but older sensors differ (D100 GRBG, D2H GBRG), and decoding those as RGGB rendered greens as magenta; falls back to RGGB when the tag is absent. ACTIVE-AREA CROP: most NEFs store exactly the active area, but a number of bodies pad it with non-image columns that fringe the left/right edges magenta when demosaiced -- the pad sits at or below black, so after black subtraction the red/blue WB gains amplify the remainder into magenta noise. A per-model crop (libraw margins -- D2H: left 6, width 2482; D800/D800E: 7424 -> width 7378, i.e. libraw's 46-column right trim) removes them. Margins are even so the CFA phase is unchanged; add a row per affected model. The pad is NOT always optical black: on the D800E the 46-column tail measured as 14 columns of a constant 600, then 2 saturated (16382) columns, then 30 columns of literal 1, and only the right edge is padded (left column and every row carry image data) -- which is why it rendered as a single magenta stripe down the right side. Because that pad can read BELOW the true pedestal, the crop now runs BEFORE the black-level step and the self-calibrated frame minimum is re-measured over the cropped active area; otherwise the D800E's 1s would pin the fallback black below black on every body lacking tag 0x3d. VERIFIED: the C++ decode is

BYTE-IDENTICAL to libraw on 14-bit (D810) and 12-bit (D7200) lossless NEF -- validated with a throwaway rawpy/libraw reference in scratch, since the bit-level scheme is too subtle to guess. As-shot WB: read from the UNENCRYPTED MakerNote tag 0x0C (WhiteBalanceRBLevels, 4 RATIONALs in order R, B, G1, G2 -- green-normalised multipliers) -> raw.camMul, matching libraw's cam_mul exactly on D810/D7200/D100. (Some bodies, e.g. D100, store only R/B and leave the green entries 0, meaning green=1; substitute 1.0 rather than rejecting the tag, else a zero green multiplier drops the WB and leaves a heavy green cast.) Old bodies that lack 0x0C (e.g. D2H) instead carry WB in the ColorBalance block 0x97; its early pre-encryption versions ("0100".."0103") store the same RGGB levels as 16-bit ints at a fixed, version-dependent offset (layout ported from dcraw's parse_makernote) -- reproduces libraw's cam_mul on D2H (611,256,397,256). Without it the matrix- derived neutral WB was used, giving a warm cast. (The version-0223+ cipher is still unbroken but those bodies all expose 0x0C, so it is unneeded.) LIMITATION: the 0x44 lossy-with-curve path is ported but not yet exercised by a test file.

CANON CR2 (CanonRaw, ImageFormats/Canon/canon.cpp): self-contained. Walks the CR2 IFD chain (via TiffWalk), finds the raw IFD (the one with Canon slice tag 0xC640), decodes its lossless-JPEG strip (Compression 6), then reassembles Canon's vertical SLICES: tag 0xC640 = [nFullSlices, sliceWidth, lastSliceWidth]; the component-interleaved JPEG raster maps back to sensor (row,col) by dcraw's formula. It then reads the Canon makernote (IFD0 -> ExifIFD 0x8769 -> MakerNote 0x927C) for SensorInfo (0xE0 -> active-area crop L/T/R/B) and ColorData (0x4001 -> WB_RGGBLevelsAsShot; offset 63 for modern ColorData, 25/24/142 for older lengths). Black is self-calibrated from the masked optical-black columns (x < left) before cropping; white = (1<<precision)-1; colour matrix from the shared per-model table (added 7D Mark II); pattern RGGB; crop origin forced even to keep the Bayer phase. VERIFIED: C++ un-sliced CFA is byte-identical to the validated reference on a 7D Mark II CR2 (which renders to a coherent, correctly-coloured image), and the makernote crop/WB/model read back correctly. LIMITATIONS: lossless-JPEG CR2 only (sRAW/mRAW and very old CR2 layouts not handled); Canon black is a masked-column average, not per-channel; ColorData WB offset table covers the common versions.

CANON CR3 / CRX (CanonCR3Raw, ImageFormats/Canon/crx.cpp): self-contained clean-room decode of Canon's CRX lossless codec (registered for "cr3"; CanonCR3 handles CR3 metadata, this is the sensor path). Walks the ISO-BMFF box tree for the full-resolution CRAW track, reads its CMP1 geometry (fW/fH, tile, nBits, planes, cfaLayout), the IAD1 active-area crop, and the CRX bitstream location (stbl co64 offset + stsz length). The CRX entropy stream is adaptive Golomb-Rice (escape 41 / 21 bits / kMax 15) + JPEG-LS-style MED prediction + T.87 run mode. KEY POINTS re-derived by byte-level instrumentation vs the libraw/rawpy oracle: (a) the tile's four Bayer planes share ONE CONTINUOUS bitstream -- do NOT re-seek per plane to the byte offsets (only R5 and R6II even planes land byte-aligned; odd planes start mid-byte); the reader runs continuously, only k and the run s_param reset at each plane's first line. (b) The non-top line adapts k from a LOOK-AHEAD-adjusted code bit_code=(bit_code+|2*(d-b)|)>>1 (the sample itself is decoded with adaptation OFF); the top line uses ordinary per-symbol adaptation. (c) run entry when coeff a==b==d; predictor med(a,b,c); s_param persists plane-wide. The four planes interleave ROW-MAJOR into the RGGB mosaic (plane 0/1/2/3 -> quad (0,0)(0,1)(1,0)(1,1)), then crop to IAD1. Black self-calibrated PER-CHANNEL from the masked columns; white=(1<<nBits)-1 refined by the per-model table; WB from the CMT3 makernote ColorData (0x4001, same offset table as CR2); matrix from the per-model table; pattern RGGB. VERIFIED: full-sensor mosaic is byte-identical (0 diffs) to the rawpy oracle on EOS R5 (46.65M px) and R6 II (25.49M px), all four planes. LIMITATIONS: level-0 (no wavelet) single-tile CRAW only -- current full-frame bodies; leveled/multi-tile or non-4-plane CRAW returns false (caller falls back to the embedded preview). CR3 HDR-PQ preview handling is unchanged (see the loadFromImageIO section above).

TiffWalk (ImageFormats/Raw/tiffwalk.*): a tiny shared TIFF/EP IFD reader (header, IFDs + SubIFDs, tag value accessors: scalar / u32 array / real array / bytes / ascii, handling the inline-vs-offset rule and endianness). DngRaw uses it; other vendor unpackers can adopt it to stop re-implementing the IFD-walk boilerplate (SonyRaw still has its own inline walk).

SHARED LOSSLESS-JPEG (ImageFormats/Raw/losslessjpeg.*): the compression behind Canon CR2, most compressed Adobe DNG, and lossless NEF/ARW. NOT DCT JPEG -- the lossless mode (SOF3): each sample is Huffman-coded as the difference from a spatial predictor of its neighbours. LosslessJpeg::Decode(bytes) -> Image{width,height,components,precision,samples} with samples interleaved by component (samples[(y*width+x)*comps + c]). It is camera-agnostic; each vendor decoder extracts the bytestream from its container and reassembles components/tiles/slices into the CFA (that mapping is format-specific, lives in the caller). SCOPE: single scan, Huffman, predictors 1-7, point transform; NO restart intervals yet (returns false on DRI>0). VERIFIED: the C++ output is byte-identical to a reference decoder that was validated end-to-end on a real lossless-JPEG DNG (Leica M10: 14-bit, 2 components, predictor 1) by demosaicing/colour-converting it to a coherent, correctly-coloured image.

Phasing: 1 scaffold (done) -> 2 first uncompressed format UnpackCfa: Sony ARW (done) -> 3 real colour: per-model matrix + matrix-derived WB + Sony as-shot WB/black (done); TODO more model matrices -> 4 better demosaic (AHD) -> 5 compression: shared lossless-JPEG decoder (done); wire into DNG/CR2 UnpackCfa next; compressed NEF/ARW later.

NOTE: an earlier (2025) unused scaffold (ImageFormats/Base/rawbase.* + ImageFormats/Tiff/tiffdecoder.*) used the rejected demosaic()-as-override axis and has been removed.

NOTE: RawColor now stops at CAMERA-NATIVE linear RGB -- it characterises the image (filling WorkingImage::cam) but applies no white balance, no colour matrix and no clamp. The camera->working conversion is Develop's stage 0 and the display conversion is owned by OutputTransform (see Develop section). This puts the WorkingImageCache boundary upstream of every colour decision, so changing a profile or the white balance re-renders instead of re-decoding.


*** OPERATION MODE (PREVIEW VS DEVELOP) ***

Winnow's top-level mode is G::OperationMode (global.h): Preview | Develop (more may follow).

Preview  fast image review -- embedded preview JPGs + a large forward read-ahead cache. Develop  view/edit a SINGLE image at BEST quality (scene-linear raw decode). A large forward cache is a LOW priority here, so Develop suspends read-ahead and, on a WorkingImage- cache miss for a raw, RE-DECODES to the scene-linear image rather than falling back to the tone-mapped display image (that fallback is only correct for non-raw files).

UI: a standout (Apple-blue) dropdown at the EXTREME LEFT of the status bar (createStatusBar, width pinned via stylesheet min/max-width to beat the global "QComboBox { min-width:6em }"), and the D shortcut (operationModeAction) -- both call MW::setOperationMode. (D previously enabled/disabled the Develop panel via developAction; that action is now shortcut-less in the Utilities menu.)

The KEYS are asymmetric, and deliberately so: leaving Develop you always have a view in mind, so the view key does both jobs.

  D    ENTERS Develop mode (it is not a toggle; pressing it again does nothing).  Develop
       always runs in Loupe, so entering forces Loupe.
  E    leaves Develop -> Preview mode, Loupe view   (asLoupeAction)
  G    leaves Develop -> Preview mode, Grid  view   (asGridAction)
  T    leaves Develop -> Preview mode, Table view   (asTableAction)

All three view actions call setOperationMode(Preview) BEFORE their display function, so the Preview decode / read-ahead is restored and the Develop panel greyed before the view is shown; in Preview the mode call is a no-op and they behave exactly as they always have. C (Compare) remains Preview-only -- it needs two selected images, which contradicts Develop's single image, so enableSelectionDependentMenus disables asCompareAction in Develop (a disabled QAction's shortcut does not fire) and re-enables it on return.

The mode also OWNS whether the Develop panel is usable: it is enabled only when the user's Develop toggle (developAction) is on AND the mode is Develop -- Preview always greys it out. MW::syncDevelopPanelEnabled() computes that AND and is called on both inputs (developAction toggled, setOperationMode). In Preview the panel is not kept in sync with the selected image either: fileSelectionChange skips developProperties->setCurrentImage (no edits are possible, so none are pending to flush), and setOperationMode re-points the panel at the current image when entering Develop / flushes edits when leaving it.

The mode OWNS the raw/preview decode (G::useRaw): setOperationMode calls toggleUseRaw(on) for Develop and toggleUseRaw(off) for Preview -- so Preview decodes the origPreview (the embedded JPG) and Develop decodes raw sensor data. In Preview mode a developed image may not be decoded at all: see "Original and Developed Previews > Read Paths", where a matching devPreview is served in place of the decode. Within Develop the "Edit: Raw / Embedded Preview" selector can still OVERRIDE to embedded preview (it drives the same toggleUseRaw); the override lasts until the next mode switch, which re-applies the mode default. The standalone status-bar useRawBtn is REMOVED (commented out; button still created/wired in case it returns).

The mode also hides the two cache running lights (metadataThreadRunningLabel, imageThreadRunningLabel) in Develop and shows them in Preview -- folder caching progress has no meaning when read-ahead is suspended. MW::updateStatusBar sets the visibility from G::operationMode; setOperationMode calls it (see "Cache Status").

Behaviour implemented: (1) setTargetRange targets ONLY the current image in Develop mode (no forward read-ahead -> the current image's scene-linear WorkingImage is not evicted); (2) renderDevelopPreview re-decodes the raw on a WorkingImage miss OR when the cached one is display-referred (a prior develop-miss fallback), via ImageDecoder::decodeIndependent (which caches the scene-linear WorkingImage). Non-raw files still build from the display image.

OPEN QUESTION (decide later): should THUMBNAIL generation ALWAYS use the embedded preview (useRaw=false) for raw files, even in Develop mode? Rationale: in Develop you edit ONE image; the thumb strip is for orientation and does not need the slow full-raw decode. Currently G::useRaw is global, so Develop=true also makes the strip decode raws (bounded by the Develop read-ahead suspension, but still). A dedicated "thumbnails always preview" path would decouple the loupe (raw) from the strip (preview).


*** DEVELOP / IMAGE EDIT (PARAMETRIC ADJUSTMENTS) ***


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Architecture

Lightroom-style non-destructive adjustments (denoise, white balance, exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows, whites, blacks, texture, dehaze ...). They apply to ALL image types (JPEG/TIFF/HEIC/RAW), so they are a shared stage AFTER decode and BEFORE the display tone/gamma/ICC transform -- NOT part of RawFormat.

Decisions (settled): adjustments run on a FLOAT LINEAR working buffer (exposure/tone/ dehaze need headroom), and apply to ALL image types (not raw-only).

Unifying representation -- WorkingImage (float linear scene-referred RGB + working colour space + white reference). Both decode paths converge on it:

  RAW:      ... -> Demosaic -> camera-RGB          (RawColor stops CAMERA-NATIVE)
  NON-RAW:  QImage decode -> un-gamma to linear -> working space

WorkingImage::space (ColorSpaceMath::ColorSpace) says which primaries the pixels are in RIGHT NOW, so no stage has to assume. The raw path arrives CameraNative and Develop's stage 0 converts; the non-raw path arrives already in the working space. It is orthogonal to sceneReferred, which says which REFERRING the pixels have (scene vs display) -- both are needed and neither implies the other.

THE INPUT PROFILE IS NOT AN EDIT, so no "nothing to adjust" fast path may skip it. A camera-native buffer that reaches OutputTransform is not caught: ColorSpaceMath::matrix returns the IDENTITY for CameraNative (it has no tabulated primaries), so the sensor values are written out unconverted and UNBALANCED -- which looks like a heavily GREEN image, since a Bayer sensor's green response dominates. Four paths skipped it and were fixed (2026-08): WorkingImageCache::render's identity fast path (now converts a copy -- the cache entry stays camera-native for the develop path), WorkingImageCache::renderStack's `if (!base.isIdentity()) Apply` (now calls Develop::Apply unconditionally, whose OWN identity early-out still runs stage 0), RawFormat::Decode's no-edit tail (converts a copy of *work), and ImageDecoder::load's cached-base shortcut -- the one that reuses an already-decoded WorkingImage instead of re-demosaicing. That last one went through OutputTransform::ToImage directly, so it skipped stage 0 AND the saved recipe (it sets developApplied, so applyDevelop would not put the recipe back); it now goes through WorkingImageCache::render, which does both plus the recipe's view transform. Symptoms that found them: double-clicking a Develop caption resets that adjustment, and on an otherwise unedited image that makes the params identity -- so the next render went green; and selecting a second edited image sometimes came back green and un-edited -- "sometimes" being exactly when a base happened to be cached for it. Rule for any new render path: convert first, then decide whether there is anything else to do.

The recipe's VIEW TRANSFORM travels the same way. OutputTransform::ToImage defaults to Filmic, so any call site that renders a recipe must pass OutputTransform::ViewFromInt(edit.viewTransform) or an AgX/None image renders Filmic whenever it comes through that path instead of the interactive one. WorkingImageCache::render/renderStack do this from the base (scope-0) params; RawFormat::Decode does it from its `edit` argument, falling back to Filmic when there is no recipe.

The working space itself is named once, as ColorSpaceMath::kWorking (Develop/colorspace.h) -- now LINEAR REC.2020, D65 -- and every op derives its luma weights from it via lumaWeights() instead of hardcoding Rec.709. That was ~15 sites writing out 0.2126/0.7152/0.0722, plus rangemask.h which used the Rec.601 triple instead -- an inconsistency, not a decision: a Luminance Range mask selected on a slightly different luma than every other op computed. Widening the working space is now one line plus re-blessed goldens.

Consumer:

WorkingImage -> Develop(EditParams) -> OutputTransform(tone+gamma+ICC) -> QImage

EditParams -- one versioned struct, identity defaults (absent = no-op = no behaviour change). The single source of truth for all adjustment values; every stage reads from it. Persisted per-image (datamodel column + sidecar; deferred). The slider UI binds to it.

Develop -- stateless/reentrant processor, constructed per decode (same discipline as Demosaic/RawFormat). FIXED, hard-coded operation order:

1. Denoise (luma/chroma)        earliest; for RAW ideally on the CFA mosaic
2. White balance                linear channel scale (RAW: camMul, from temp/tint)
3. Exposure                     linear gain 2^EV
4. Contrast                     S-curve about mid-grey

5. Highlights/Shadows/          luminance-masked parametric tone Whites/Blacks 5a. Tone curve                  the Curves panel's point curves, composed into the SAME perceptual LUT right after #4/#5 (Lightroom's order): the RGB composite first, then the per- channel Red/Green/Blue curve. Free at render -- the LUT is built anyway (Curves panel below) 2b. Calibration                 3x3 primary re-point (R/G/B hue + sat), in LINEAR light after #2 and BEFORE the tone curve; rows sum to 1 so neutrals are fixed (Calibrate panel below) 5b. Colour RGB                  per-channel linear gain (red/green/blue), into #2 5c. Colour HSL                  hue rotation + saturation + vibrance + luminance, after the tone curve 5d. Colour grading              tonal-range tinting: per shadows/mid/highlights a zero-luma hue+sat chroma push + a luminance nudge, weighted by smooth tonal windows (shaped by Blending/Balance), plus an unweighted GLOBAL range; after HSL in the same fused point-op pass (Color Grade panel below)

6. Texture                      mid-frequency local contrast 6.5 Clarity                     MID-radius local contrast, midtone-weighted and halo-guarded (sits between Texture and Dehaze)
7. Dehaze                       local contrast + black-point, haze estimate

8. Vignette                     radial corner exposure falloff (exposure/feather) 8.5 Sharpen                     capture sharpening: unsharp mask on luminance, edge-gated (amount/radius/detail/masking). The ONE op with an ABSOLUTE pixel radius, and Opt + a Masking drag shows the gate -- see below

9. Grain                        monochromatic film grain (amount/size/roughness) (the local-contrast ladder is now complete: 1px / 13px / 60px / 170px)

Two ops are special for RAW: white balance and denoise give better results applied inside the raw pipeline (WB at the camera-RGB stage; denoise on the CFA mosaic) rather than generically post-demosaic. EditParams is shared, so RawFormat::Decode() takes an optional const EditParams* and consumes those two fields at the right stages; Develop handles the rest. (Scaffold may start with everything in Develop and push those two down later -- a quality-only refactor, no interface change.)

  WIRING (done): the decode path now runs through these classes.
      NON-RAW: ImageDecoder::load() decodes to QImage as before; then ImageDecoder::
               applyDevelop() (after rotate(), before colorManage()) does
               InputTransform -> Develop -> OutputTransform. It is a NO-OP and is skipped
               entirely when EditParams is identity, so the unedited browsing path is
               unchanged and never round-trips through the float buffer. When it does run it
               goes through the WorkingImage cache (reuses / populates the pre-develop image).
      RAW:     RawFormat::Decode() develops in its native linear float (RawColor::ToCameraNative
               -> Develop -> OutputTransform), hands its pre-develop WorkingImage back via
               outWork (cached by ImageDecoder), and ImageDecoder sets developApplied so
               applyDevelop() does not run a second time. See PERF / WORKINGIMAGE CACHE below.

ABORT: RawFormat::Decode() takes the decoder's abort atomic and polls it between stages and once per row inside the demosaic loop, returning false (lastError "Aborted") if set. This matters because ImageCache::stop() (quit) uses a BlockingQueuedConnection to each decoder; without polling, an in-flight 24MP demosaic would run to completion while the main thread blocks waiting -> beachball. ImageCache::stop() now also calls decoders[id]->abortProcessing() (sets the atomic, non-blocking) BEFORE that blocking wait so the in-flight decode actually sees the flag and bails.

G::useRaw TOGGLE: ImageDecoder::load() only attempts the RAW path when G::useRaw is true; when false it skips RawFormat entirely and shows the embedded preview/JPG (prior behaviour). A status bar button (MW::useRawBtn, between reverseSortBtn and panToFocusToggleBtn) toggles it -- colored raw.png icon when on, grayscale raw_bw.png when off. MW::toggleUseRaw rebuilds the full-size image cache so the change is visible immediately. The value persists across sessions via QSettings ("useRaw", default false). NOTE: the raw decode is still also gated by RawFormat::Create() returning a decoder for the extension (arw/dng/cr2/nef/orf/rw2/raf today; others -> embedded JPG). Saved Develop edits are also gated on useRaw: MW::applyDevelopPreviewIfEdited() overlays a non-identity EditStack onto the loupe only when useRaw is on, since the edits are calibrated against the demosaiced RAW render. In preview mode (useRaw off) the embedded JPG is shown untouched; toggling useRaw on rebuilds the cache and the developed look returns. InputTransform (Develop/) is the non-raw "un-gamma to linear" step, the symmetric counterpart of OutputTransform.

THE VIEW TRANSFORM (was "baseline tone curve"): a scene-referred raw render is radiometrically linear and lands dark and flat next to a camera JPEG (measured ~+0.7 EV / much less contrast on the A9 II), so it needs tone mapping. WorkingImage carries a sceneReferred flag: RawColor sets it true (sensor data, with highlight headroom and NO clamp at all now), InputTransform sets it false (a JPEG already has the camera tone curve baked in).

The output stage is now THREE composable steps -- view transform, then output primaries, then transfer function -- of which only the first is a creative decision; the other two are colour management. OutputTransform::ViewTransform selects it:

  None     no tone mapping; scene-linear straight to the transfer function.
  Filmic   the long-standing default and still the default: exposure x kBaselineExposure (=1.6, ~+0.68 EV) then the ACES (Narkowicz) shoulder, in linear before the transfer. Lifts midtones and rolls highlights off smoothly (vs hard-clipping), landing close to the embedded preview (mean luma 0.434 vs 0.445; validated in numpy against the preview).
  AgX      LABELLED "Soft roll-off" in the UI (see below). Wide-latitude, for HIGHLIGHT HUE INTEGRITY. Inset the primaries toward the achromatic axis, run the curve in that compressed space over a 16.5-stop log2 window, outset back. Because the curve runs where the primaries are pulled together, a bright saturated colour desaturates smoothly toward white instead of losing its channels one at a time -- which is where per-channel filmic's hue march comes from (saturated red to orange, blue to purple) and why a brightened mask core currently clips to a flat patch, destroying the falloff calibrated in maskfalloff.h.

THE USER-FACING LABEL IS "Soft roll-off", NOT "AgX". The enum value, the sidecar key and every constant stay AgX -- it is the published name of the transform and what the maths implements -- but "AgX" is short for silver halide and is guessable by nobody, so the LABEL says what the option does. The rejected candidate was "Flat highlight protection": "flat" advertises AgX's best-known criticism (it looks flat next to a camera rendering, per the note in notes/Scratch.txt), "highlight protection" collides with highlight RECOVERY -- reconstructing blown detail from sensor headroom, which is the Highlights slider a few rows below and is NOT what this does -- and the phrase breaks parallel form with "Filmic" and "None". Docs/developbasichelp.html names AgX explicitly so the term stays searchable for anyone who has read about it elsewhere.

AgX PROVENANCE AND WHAT IS PINNED. The inset matrix and the log2 window are AgX's published constants; THE SIGMOID IS OURS -- two power segments meeting at the mid-grey pivot, written rather than ported, so no third-party licence rides along. It is therefore AgX's structure and behaviour, not a bit-exact reproduction of any particular AgX build, and it will not match a reference render pixel for pixel. Since there is no reference to diff against, tst_outputtransform pins the properties the transform is CHOSEN for: 18% scene grey renders as 18% display grey (the anchor -- if the pivot drifts, every exposure the user has dialled changes meaning), neutrals stay neutral, the curve is monotonic across the whole window, and a saturated colour driven up in exposure desaturates with its off-channels rising TOGETHER (a spread between them is a hue shift). The published inset's rows sum to 1 +/- 1.4e-4, so the code normalises them -- 0.014% is a tint on every neutral that no white balance can remove.

BOTH of those last two are asserted at 16 BITS, deliberately: at 8 bits a mis-derived pivot lands one level away and slips through a within-one tolerance, and the un-normalised rows are 0.04 of a level, i.e. invisible. Both were measured passing an 8-bit version of the same assertion before it was tightened. When an invariant's error is smaller than one part in 255, assert it through ToImage16, which runs the exact maths rather than the 8-bit table.

The Filmic transform WAS a hardcoded constant applied unconditionally to scene-referred data -- the app's entire look was one number nobody could choose. Making it a value is what lets a different transform (AgX and friends) be added as data rather than as a rewrite. A view transform TONE-MAPS, so it is forced to None for display-referred input; that rule lives in EffectiveView() and callers never special-case it, which is why the unedited-JPEG round trip stays identity.

SELECTING IT (the UI and persistence side). EditParams::viewTransform is an INT (an OutputTransform::ViewTransform cast), for the same reason wbPreset is: it rides the existing int machinery -- sanitizeParams clamp, kIntFields for multi-image, the preset round trip -- instead of being the first non-scalar field in EditParams. THE NUMBERING IS A PUBLISHED FORMAT (0 = Filmic, 1 = AgX, 2 = None): add freely, never renumber. Filmic is 0 deliberately so a default-constructed EditParams needs no special case -- the enum originally listed None first, which made an untouched raw ask for NO tone mapping at all; tst_inputprofile::defaultViewTransformIsFilmic pins it. An unknown value resolves to the default (OutputTransform::ViewFromInt) rather than failing, so a sidecar from a later build still opens.

It is the one EditParams field NOT consumed by Develop::Apply -- OutputTransform reads it, three stages later -- and it is a WHOLE-IMAGE property, so DevelopProperties::setViewTransform always writes SCOPE 0 whatever scope is active (like the Global raw params; a per-scope value would mean different looks in different parts of one picture). The render sites need no signature change: WorkingImageCache::render and ::renderStack already receive the params and read viewTransform from them. The control is a QComboBox (DevelopProperties::addViewTransformRow) in the value cell of a row at the HEAD OF BASIC'S TONE GROUP -- below white balance, above Exposure -- labelled "Tone mapping", modelled on the WB preset row (addItem(name, enum) + activated + findData under a QSignalBlocker); a combo gets no history for free, so setViewTransform calls noteScopeEdit itself.

LABELLED "Tone mapping", NOT "View". The code identifiers and the sidecar key stay `viewTransform` -- that key is a published format, and a label is free to change without it. "View" was the technically correct term (it is a view transform) but Winnow already spends that word on the central widget modes: loupe / grid / table / compare, MW::viewMenu, and the rule "Only Loupe View Is Allowed in Develop Mode". A second meaning in the Develop dock is a collision. "Tone mapping" is also plainly descriptive where "View" is opaque. It does UNDERSELL AgX slightly -- AgX's real point is the inset/outset gamut compression that stops saturated highlights marching in hue, not merely range compression -- which is what the tooltip and Docs/developbasichelp.html carry.

WHY AT THE HEAD OF THE TONE GROUP. The name and the position say the same thing: this control sets the overall mapping and the sliders beneath it adjust WITHIN that mapping. General to specific, and a REAL dependency rather than a visual grouping -- the same Exposure value does not mean the same thing under Filmic and AgX, because AgX leaves far more highlight room before anything clips. It is deliberately NOT beside Lightroom's Profile slot at the top of the panel: a camera profile characterises the CAMERA and feeds white balance, whereas this governs the tone sliders (see the placement plan below).

WHY BASIC AND NOT CALIBRATE (it was briefly in Calibrate, per notes/_ToDo.txt). Three reasons, and the Lightroom analogy that first argued for Calibrate actually argues against it: LR's Adobe Color / Camera Standard live in the PROFILE control at the top of the BASIC panel, while LR's CALIBRATION panel is the R/G/B primary rotation -- which is exactly what Winnow's Calibrate already is. Second, pipeline position: Calibrate runs mid-Develop in linear, the view transform runs last in OutputTransform, so grouping them implies an adjacency that does not exist. Third, workflow: it re-scopes every slider beneath it (AgX leaves far more highlight room before anything clips) and is a decision made early, whereas Calibrate is a rarely-opened fine-tuning section. When camera profiles land they belong NEXT TO it -- see the placement plan below.

MOVING IT COST the group bookkeeping the checklist warns about: EditParams::resetGroup's case (Calibrate -> Basic, so Reset Basic and the Basic preview eye cover it) and the preset leaf table (kCalibrateLeaves -> kBasicLeaves). Neither fails to compile if missed.

⏺⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Architecture > Where Camera Profiles Will Go (planned)

Not built. Recorded so the decision is not re-litigated when Phase 4 (DCP camera profiles) starts.

Profile goes at the TOP of Basic -- Lightroom's slot. It is NOT paired there with Tone mapping: an earlier draft of this plan put the two together, but Tone mapping moved to the head of the tone group, and each control now sits with what it actually governs. Profile characterises the camera and feeds white balance; Tone mapping governs the tone sliders:

  Profile        [ Adobe Standard  v ]   stage 0: camera characterisation
  ------------------------------------
  WB             [dropper] [ As Shot v ]
  Temp / Tint
  ------------------------------------
  Tone mapping   [ Filmic          v ]   output: the mapping the sliders work within
  Exposure / Contrast / Highlights / ...

WHY THERE. Unlike Tone mapping, this one genuinely IS Lightroom's Profile -- Adobe Standard / Camera Neutral / Camera Vivid -- so a user finds it exactly where they expect, with no mismatch to explain. Profile-first is also real DEPENDENCY order, not just visual preference: the camera profile feeds the white balance, since WhiteBalance::solve walks cam.xyzToCam, so a profile change moves what a given Kelvin means. And it leaves Calibrate as what it actually is -- the manual primary tweak ON TOP of whatever the profile produced, which is exactly the relationship Lightroom's Calibration panel has to its Profile.

TWO ROWS, NOT ONE. Lightroom folds camera-matching profiles and creative looks into a single Profile dropdown; that conflation mixes "how my sensor is characterised" with "an artistic grade" and is a recurring source of confusion there. Keep them separate. For the same reason, creative looks / .cube LUTs (if they ever land) do NOT belong in the Profile dropdown: a look is a third thing, later in the pipeline, and needs its own control.

GATED LIKE Tone mapping, BUT WITH TWO DISTINCT REASONS, and they are not interchangeable:
  - non-raw                    no camera characterisation at all (cam.valid is false) -> "raw only"
  - Apple Core Image engine    applerawdecode.mm bypasses RawColor entirely; Core Image has already applied its own profile and returns linear sRGB, so a camera profile is meaningless and must not silently do nothing -> "not available with the Apple decoder"

DECIDE BEFORE BUILDING, not after a bug report: changing the profile changes the camera matrix, so resolveAsShot lands somewhere different and the Temp/Tint reading shifts. Whether the stored numbers are held (and the rendering moves, as Lightroom does) or the appearance is held (and the numbers move) is a deliberate choice.


RAW ONLY, AND IT SAYS SO. OutputTransform forces the transform to None for display-referred input, so on a JPEG the control would be live and silently do nothing. Instead the row is disabled and the value cell reads "raw only" -- greyed control plus a brief inline reason, never a popup after the fact. currentIsRaw() gates it at build time, the same way the WB row gates its named illuminants.

WIDENING THE WORKING SPACE (Rec.709 -> Rec.2020). Rec.709 is narrower than every camera's own gamut, so a saturated sensor colour used to be clipped at DECODE -- before the user had touched the image, and irrecoverably. It is now carried through to the output stage, which is the only stage that knows what gamut it is rendering into. The flip is one line (kWorking) because everything else derives from it: the decoder composes sRGB->working into camToWorking, InputTransform converts on the way in, OutputTransform derives its primaries matrix from the source tag, and every op reads lumaWeights().

It was deliberately held at LinearSRGB while the camera->working conversion was moved out of the decoder, so the PLUMBING change and the PIXEL change could be attributed separately -- which paid off: both regressions found during that move were provably not the space. It was then held further until AgX landed, because preserving wide-gamut data only helps if something compresses it gracefully on the way out; otherwise it just clips per-channel at the output matrix instead of at the decode, which is the same hue-shift failure mode one stage later. Under Filmic an out-of-gamut colour is therefore no worse than before, just no better; AgX is what makes the wider space pay.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE HOT PATH: sRGB output is no longer an identity primaries matrix, so the FUSED single-table path below no longer serves the common case and every render takes the SPLIT path. That is why the split path had to become table-driven first rather than staying the exact-pow fallback it was when it was export-only.

WHAT BROKE WHEN THE TESTS DUPLICATED A DEFINITION: tst_calibrate and tst_colorgrade each carried their own hardcoded Rec.709 luma triple to assert "this op preserves luma" / "this tint has zero luma". Both agreed with the code only while both were hardcoded, and both failed the moment the working space widened -- the ops were still correct. Tests of a property defined BY a colour space must take the definition from ColorSpaceMath, not restate it.

PERFORMANCE -- TWO PATHS, NEITHER WITH A TRANSCENDENTAL. When the primaries matrix is the identity the whole per-channel chain is 1-D and ONE table replaces it (the fused path, ~4.8x the pow it replaced; see the TRANSFER LUT note in outputtransform.cpp). When it is not, the matrix sits between the view curve and the transfer so the two cannot fuse, and the chain becomes table -> matrix -> rolloff -> table. That split path used to run exact pow maths because it was export-only (P3 / Adobe RGB); it stops being rare the moment the working space is wider than the output space, when EVERY render takes it, which is why it is now table-driven too. ViewTransform::None is deliberately NOT tabulated on that path: its table would stop at 1.0 and would clamp away exactly the over-range values HighlightRolloff exists to redistribute (pinned by tst_outputtransform::viewTransformNoneIsPassThrough).

HIGHLIGHT ROLLOFF (OutputTransform::HighlightRolloff): the last thing before the transfer curve, in linear and in the OUTPUT primaries -- the space the clip happens in. Any channel over 1.0 would otherwise be hard-clipped by Transfer's Clamp01, which is what made a brightened radial mask look banded: the mask core clipped flat, hiding the smootherstep falloff, which then re-appeared with a derivative kink (a Mach-band ring) where the channel dropped back under 1.0, with a hue swing as the channels clipped one at a time. Instead the energy the peak channel loses to the clip is BLED into the other two, so an over-range colour desaturates smoothly toward white -- the Lightroom behaviour (a green frame pushed past white gives ~(154,255,151) here vs LR's ~(172,255,164), measured).

Pixels at or below white are bit-exact, so unedited images and their exports are untouched; that is why this is a bleed and not a compressive shoulder, since any soft knee below 1.0 would have to darken existing whites. The RAW baseline tone curve above still runs first (it rarely leaves anything over-range); the rolloff is applied to raw and non-raw alike, in both ToImage and ToImage16.

OUTPUT COLOUR SPACES: OutputTransform::ToImage / ToImage16 take an OutputTransform::Space -- sRGB (default), DisplayP3 or AdobeRGB. The pipeline works in scene-linear with sRGB (Rec.709) primaries at D65, so the conversion is a 3x3 to the target primaries applied in LINEAR (after the baseline tone curve, before the transfer), then the target's transfer curve: the sRGB piecewise curve for sRGB AND P3 (which is what the P3 display spec uses), gamma 563/256 = 2.19921875 for Adobe RGB. Both matrices are non-negative with rows summing to 1, so white stays white and in-gamut input cannot leave 0..1; the sRGB path skips the matrix entirely rather than multiplying by an identity, so the loupe hot path is unchanged.

Adobe RGB shares sRGB's red and blue primaries -- only green differs -- so its matrix is nearly identity. Whoever writes the QImage must TAG it to match, via OutputTransform::ColorSpaceOf(space); a mismatch is worse than no tag at all.

Validated against Qt's own colour engine as an oracle (QImage::convertedToColorSpace over 11 patches incl. primaries, greys and near-black): max channel error 0/255 for sRGB and Display P3, 1/255 for Adobe RGB (Qt's fixed-point rounding). Arbitrary destination ICC profiles via Lcms2 remain a later phase.

The space is threaded through WorkingImageCache::render / renderStack (Space, default sRGB) and MW::developComposite / developCompositeStack exactly as OutDepth is, and is chosen per export -- see COLOUR SPACE under Export.

PERF (keeps Winnow fast): WORKINGIMAGE CACHE (Develop/workingimagecache.h, DONE). The expensive part of a RAW is UnpackCfa -> Demosaic -> RawColor, which produces the pre-develop WorkingImage; Develop + OutputTransform that follow are cheap. So that WorkingImage is cached (keyed by absolute file path) and an interactive slider change re-renders via only Develop + OutputTransform -- never decode or demosaic again.

Storage: WorkingImageCache::instance().put(fPath, work). RAW caches the pre-develop WorkingImage that RawFormat::Decode() now hands back through its outWork out-param (a shared_ptr -- no pixel copy; the snapshot is always pre-develop, so a non-identity edit develops on a private copy and leaves the cached image pristine). Non-raw caches the post-InputTransform WorkingImage the first time applyDevelop() builds one. Re-render: WorkingImageCache::get(fPath) + WorkingImageCache::render(work, edit, out). render() is static/stateless: identity edit -> OutputTransform only (no copy); non-identity -> copy + Develop + OutputTransform. ImageDecoder::applyDevelop() now uses this fast path (cache hit skips InputTransform; miss builds + caches once). The editor (slider UI, deferred) calls get()+render() directly to re-render without a decode; a cache miss falls back to a full decode (which re-populates the entry). Memory: entries are shared_ptr<const WorkingImage> (large -- ~4x the 8-bit QImage), so the cache is byte-budgeted (default 768 MB, setMaxBytes()) with LRU eviction; the most-recently-used entry is never evicted (the active edit always hits). It is a small edit accelerator for recently-viewed images, NOT a second image cache. ImageCache::initialize() clear()s it on every folder change. Done (2026-06): downscaled proxy for live drag + full-res settle render run async off the GUI thread (see "Load Responsiveness" / renderDevelopFullResAsync). Still TODO: explicit pin of the active image so heavy ahead-caching can't evict it before the user edits.

Phasing: 1 WorkingImage + split RawColor at linear + InputTransform/OutputTransform + Develop skeleton (identity), wired no-op through decode (DONE) + WorkingImage cache hook (DONE) -> 2 implement ops (exposure/WB/contrast/tone first; texture/dehaze/denoise later) -> 3 push RAW white balance/denoise pre-demosaic for quality -> 4 persistence + slider UI. NOTE: the single-EditParams render above is the SCAFFOLD. The real editor generalises it to a SCOPE + MASK stack (next subsection); EditParams stays the per-scope unit, render() grows to take an EditStack, and the Develop op math is unchanged.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Panel Focus & Slider Arrow-Nudge

The Develop dock is a PropertyEditor (QTreeView) whose value cells are persistent editor widgets (SliderEditor -> Slider). Global image navigation (Left/Right = prev/next image) is handled in MW::eventFilter. The disambiguation between "navigate images" and "nudge this slider" is Qt keyboard focus:

- Slider has StrongFocus and takes focus on click (Slider::mousePressEvent). SliderEditor sets setFocusProxy(slider), so a click or Tab into the row lands focus on the slider.

- Slider::keyPressEvent nudges on Left/Right (Up/Down too; PageUp/PageDown = the larger step) and ACCEPTS the key. Accepting matters: MW's arrow handler is an APPLICATION event filter (qApp->installEventFilter), and application filters run once per object as an UNconsumed key propagates up the parent chain -- so an arrow the slider did not accept reaches MW::eventFilter with obj == "MWWindow" and moves the selection. Consuming it in the slider stops that propagation. (Relying on QAbstractSlider's own stepping was not enough in practice, hence the explicit override.)

- Belt-and-suspenders: MW::eventFilter's Left/Right nav is additionally guarded to no-op when QApplication::focusWidget() is a value editor (QAbstractSlider / QAbstractSpinBox / QLineEdit / QComboBox). When focus leaves the panel, arrows navigate images again.

Focus cue (which slider owns the arrows): SliderEditor::paintEvent fills the VALUE cell with a subtle translucent blue when slider->hasFocus(), and the delegate fills the CAPTION cell with the SAME blue (checking the value sibling is DT_Slider and SliderEditor::sliderHasFocus()), so the whole focused row reads as one blue band -- no separate accent bar. Slider::focusInEvent/focusOutEvent -> syncFocusRepaint() repaints the SliderEditor (value cell) AND its parent viewport (so the delegate re-tints the caption) as focus moves. Generic to every PropertyEditor slider (Develop, Embellish, Preferences), not only Develop.

Caption click (Develop): clicking a slider row's CAPTION (column 0, not the value cell) is intercepted in DevelopProperties::mousePressEvent -- it (a) focuses that slider via SliderEditor::focusSlider (so arrow keys nudge it without first clicking the track), (b) hides the mask overlay (maskTintHideRequested) so the effect shows -- EXCEPT for a mask-setting slider (child of a mask tool, e.g. Feather/Hue), which keeps the overlay since you are editing the mask -- and (c) FLASHES the caption white, fading to 0 over ~0.45s as

feedback. The flash is a generic PropertyEditor mechanism:

DevelopProperties::flashCaption animates the caption UR_FlashLevel role (0..1) with a QVariantAnimation, and PropertyDelegate blends the caption text toward white by that amount (0/absent = normal, no effect elsewhere). A caption DOUBLE-click resets the slider to default (base PropertyEditor) and then re-focuses the slider (DevelopProperties::mouseDoubleClickEvent) -- otherwise Qt's click-focus moves to the tree and the row would lose its blue cue.

Not yet done: a panel-LEVEL active-border cue across all docks (thumb/grid/table/folders/ Develop). DockWidget notes it has not solved drawing a border around a QDockWidget; the control-level cue above already shows where slider shortcuts land.

Develop diagnostics report (Help > Diagnostics > Develop diagnostics)
