MW::developDiagnostics() dumps everything the Develop pipeline depends on for the currently selected image, in one read-only snapshot: the operation-mode / raw-engine globals (G::operationMode, G::useRaw, G::decodeRawEngine); the current file (raw?, video?, ISO, camera model, and the devPreview cache payload for it); the cached scene-linear WorkingImage base (dims, sceneReferred, white, MB) -- the first thing to check, since a sceneReferred=false base, or dims that differ from the sensor active area, means Develop is editing the display-referred embedded JPG, not the sensor image; the rawInfo (active area, bits, compression, CFA, black/white, matrix); the effective EditParams for the Global scope and each enabled non-Global scope (+ mask counts); the crop/warp Geometry; and the interactive develop caches (PMRID "Denoise raw" base + keys, developWorkTriedPath / in-flight guards, proxy + full-res render state, mask reference caches). The DENOISE section also reports the PMRID (k,b) noise-model resolution FOR THE CURRENT IMAGE -- the tier that fired (DNG NoiseProfile / model table / blind / generic), the resolved k,b, and whether the file carried a DNG NoiseProfile -- the first thing to check if a file denoises wrong (e.g. source=generic on a Nikon means its modelMatch missed). The snapshot (developPmridResSource/K/B/HadNP) is captured in ensureRawDenoise on a PMRID decode and keyed to developPmridKey, so it is NEVER stale: it shows only while developPmridFull is this image's base, else "(no current PMRID base)" while the base is (re)building. PMRID::LastResolution() is the worker-side hand-off the snapshot is captured from. Wired in menusand- actions.cpp (diagnosticsDevelopAction) via MW::diagnosticsDevelop. The report ENDS with "Folders with cached devPreviews": the cache dir, the total preview count / bytes against the LRU cap, and one row per folder holding on-disk devPreviews (preview count, live count, MB), from DevPreviewCache::folderStats(). It describes the cache rather than the current image, so it is appended even when no image is selected; live < count means those source images were missing at the last sweep -- deleted, or sitting on an unmounted volume (the sweep deliberately cannot tell the two apart, so it demotes rather than deletes). RENDER VERIFICATION has TWO axes, so the two failure modes seen (edit silently dropped; demosaic/decode not triggered) are each caught:

[A] Develop DISPLAY vs the Preview (embedded) image. The Preview image is captured (downscaled) on entering Develop (MW::setOperationMode, before the re-decode); each develop display -- edited OR not -- is grid-sampled against it in MW::updateDevelopScopes (cheap, no scaling of the 50MP shown). maxAbs>0 confirms the decode change (demosaic) and/or edits took effect; maxAbs==0 on a raw in Edit=Raw means the demosaic did NOT apply (display still the embedded preview). This axis populates for an unedited image on mode entry. [B] recipe vs the un-developed base. On each full-res settle the worker renders the CLEAN base with identity params vs the actual base (incl. raw denoise) with the recipe at ~256px (geometry suppressed so sizes match). maxAbs==0 with a non-identity recipe = a dropped edit (the "denoise does nothing" class). Populates only on edited settle renders.

Both are sub-ms and stored on MW (developVerify* / developVerifyVsPreview*).

Entering Develop must re-decode the current image (MW::setOperationMode) ImageDecoder::load() picks the decode by (G::operationMode==Develop && G::useRaw): Preview shows the embedded preview, Develop demosaics the raw. Switching mode therefore changes the decode even when useRaw does not flip. setOperationMode drives the re-decode via toggleUseRaw WHEN useRaw changes; but if useRaw already matches the target (e.g. it was left true from a prior session / settings), toggleUseRaw is skipped and NOTHING re-decoded -- the loupe kept showing the Preview embedded image until the user toggled the Edit-source selector. FIX: the useRaw-unchanged branch now also sets imageView->currentImageHasChanged and emits imageCacheColorManageChange() (-> reloadImageCache -> refreshViewsOnCacheChange -> loupe), matching the toggleUseRaw refresh, so entering Develop always renders through the mode's decode path.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Scope & Mask Model (Global + Masks)

TERMINOLOGY (renamed 2026-07-30; was "layer" throughout). The dock band is captioned SCOPE and lists what each edit set applies to: row 0 is GLOBAL (the whole image) and every other row is a MASK ("Mask 1", or renamed "Sky"/"Subject"). In code the unit is an EditScope (EditStack::scopes); its mask is EditScope::components, an ordered list of MaskComponents. Do not confuse this "scope" with the Develop SCOPES panel (histogram + vectorscope, developScopes*) -- unrelated feature, same word.

GOAL. Apply any edit either globally or through one or more masks, with the SAME edit set shared across a scope's masks, performance critical. The user-facing concept is a MASK; it maps to exactly one internal object. This is the Lightroom MASKING model (a mask group carrying one edit set), NOT a Photoshop pixel-scope stack -- that distinction is what keeps it fast (see PERFORMANCE).

A SCOPE = one EditParams (the existing struct, unchanged) + a COLLECTION of mask components (radial + brush + linear ...) combined into a single per-pixel alpha + an opacity. The edit set applies wherever that combined alpha > 0, so "the same edits across more than one mask" falls out for free (the masks are unioned). A scope with NO mask components is GLOBAL (applies everywhere) -- so "globally OR on masks" needs no special case.

NOISE REDUCTION is split across the two stages (the decode pipeline and Develop):

- "DENOISE RAW" (Colour + luminance NR) is GLOBAL-ONLY, driven by EditParams denoiseLuma/denoiseChroma (two Global-scope 0..1 sliders, "Denoise Lum"/"Denoise Color"). UI: the two sliders are root-level SIBLINGS of the Global "Demosaic" row (not children), always shown with no collapse arrow; they appear only on the WINNOW engine (Apple omits them -- see DevelopProperties::addCoreItems). UR_ExtraIndent lines their captions up one tree level so they align with the Basic sliders. The Demosaic row is a plain leaf (no children, no UR_isDecoration), so it draws in the ordinary caption colour like "Edit:", not the teal header colour; the "Edit:" source row carries a small UR_ExtraRowHeight (8px) for breathing room. "Denoise Lum" defaults to 75 and "Denoise Color" to 100 -- a fresh raw carrying only those defaults still counts as identity (EditParams::kDefaultDenoiseLuma / kDefaultDenoiseChroma, mirrored in isIdentity). A saved recipe overrides them per image (paramsFromJson keeps the default only for keys absent from the JSON). The denoise rows share the Demosaic row's per-raw visibility (hidden when not editing raw or when the scope is collapsed -- applyCoreVisibility). RUN MODE: a row sits between the Demosaic row and "Denoise Lum" (DevelopProperties::addCoreItems; also hidden by applyCoreVisibility) with two checkboxes hosted in the two columns so they line up with the Demosaic row: "Denoise" in the CAPTION cell (aligned with the "Demosaic" caption) and "Auto run" in the VALUE cell (aligned with the Demosaic dropdown).

  • "Auto run" persists to QSettings Develop/autoRunDenoise (mirrored in the runtime flag MW::developAutoRunDenoise). It is now the FALLBACK for images whose recipe says nothing (denoiseRaw == -1), not the gate itself: the three ensureRawDenoise auto call sites (render settle in renderDevelopFullResAsync, image-select, and entering Develop) each ask that image's wantsDenoiseRaw(G::autoRunDenoise), so an image pinned on is denoised with Auto run off and an image pinned off is left alone with it on. When neither says otherwise, ON (the default) runs the heavy PMRID decode automatically and OFF uses the CLEAN base, as before. Turning it ON also runs the denoise immediately (MW::onAutoRunDenoiseToggled -> runRawDenoiseNow) -- unless the current image pins denoiseRaw OFF, which the preference must not override.

  • "Denoise" is the manual trigger + completion state. Checking it emits runRawDenoiseRequested -> MW::runRawDenoiseNow (forces one ensureRawDenoise regardless of the flag); unchecking emits clearRawDenoiseRequested -> MW::clearRawDenoiseNow (drops BOTH developDenoised and the full-strength developPmridFull base, re-renders clean). THE CHECKBOX ALSO WRITES THE RECIPE (DevelopProperties::setDenoiseRawFlag -> EditParams::denoiseRaw on the Global scope, with a History row): the amounts cannot carry the intent, since they default NON-ZERO, and before this a manual run existed only in the session caches -- so it reached neither the exporter nor the devPreview builder, and the loupe showed a denoised image while the stored preview stayed clean under a recipe hash claiming they matched. denoiseRaw is TRI-STATE: -1 unset (follow "Auto run"; the default, OMITTED from the JSON so an untouched raw keeps the recipe hash it has always had, and browsing a folder of raws in Develop still writes no sidecars), 0 off (not denoised even with Auto run ON), 1 on (denoised even with Auto run OFF). Every render site asks EditParams::wantsDenoiseRaw(G::autoRunDenoise) rather than testing the field -- the unset fallback belongs in one place. The checkbox writes 1/0 EXPLICITLY, never folding back to unset when it happens to agree with the preference: the user said something about THIS image, and a later flip of Auto run must not undo it. Its label is "Denoise" until the PMRID base is ready, then "Denoised". MW::rawDenoiseReadyForCurrent tests developPmridFull (the full-strength base, keyed path+iso and so AMOUNT-INDEPENDENT). It drives both the checkbox label and the amount-slider enabled state (DevelopProperties::updateDenoiseRunState, called on ensureRawDenoise completion and clear; seeded on buildTree). Because the base is amount-independent, once it exists a "Denoise Lum"/"Denoise Color" change is only a cheap re-blend (ensureRawDenoise reuses developPmridFull). The render gate is now the RECIPE alone -- wantsDenoiseRaw(G::autoRunDenoise). It used to be "Auto run OR rawDenoiseReadyForCurrent", the second half being how a manual-mode slider change stayed denoised instead of reverting to clean; the flag answers that (the checkbox pins denoiseRaw = 1), and the old OR would have rendered a deliberately UN-denoised image denoised whenever a base happened to be cached for it. The amount sliders are DISABLED (greyed caption + value) until the base exists (they only scale its blend), enabling when the denoise completes. applyItemsEnabled re-asserts that disable when the panel is (re-)enabled, so a panel-wide enable can't un-grey them. Signals (autoRunDenoiseToggled / runRawDenoiseRequested / clearRawDenoiseRequested) are wired in initialize.cpp. DEMOSAIC PROGRESS: with Auto run OFF the "Denoise raw" decode (which shows the "Raw Denoise" row) does not run on open, so the plain Winnow sensor demosaic that produces the image would otherwise show no feedback. It now shows a blue "Demosaic" row (progressDemosaicRow) for the CURRENT image. That demosaic runs in the ImageCache decoder threads (ImageDecoder::load, the Develop+useRaw raw-sensor branch -> RawFormat::Decode), so progress is routed as a SIGNAL, not a direct call: RawFormat::Decode drives its progress sink from Demosaic::Run's per-tile callback (a clean, non-denoise decode with a sink now drives the whole bar); ImageDecoder::load emits demosaicProgress(sfRow, fPath, done, total); ImageCache relays it as demosaicProgress(fPath, done, total); MW::onDemosaicProgress updates the row, gated to the current file + Develop + useRaw + Winnow + Auto run off, and clears it on setCached for the current row. (The rarely-used scene-linear fallback MW::ensureDevelopWork -> ImageDecoder::decodeIndependent instead passes its own decodeProgress callback, updating the same row directly.) IMPLEMENTED as PMRID (MegVii, Apache-2.0), a PRE-demosaic (CFA/Bayer-domain) learned denoiser: it runs on the raw mosaic BEFORE demosaic (ImageFormats/ Raw/pmrid.{h,cpp}, PMRID::Apply) through the unified inference layer (Utilities/inference/, ORT -- CoreML/ANE on macOS, DirectML on Windows). Needs a WINNOW_ENABLE_ORT build (default OFF -> no-op stub) plus pmrid.onnx (ReleaseExtras/, committed, warn-if-absent), else it no-ops. Global / not maskable (a Global op before Develop). Because it needs the CFA mosaic it is WINNOW-engine + BAYER only: inert on the macOS Apple decode engine (no CFA) and on X-Trans, where "Denoise raw" no-ops. Conditioned by ISO (ImageMetadata::ISONum); applied as an amount-scaled BLEND (see WHY TWO DEMOSAICS below), luma by denoiseLuma, chroma by max(luma,chroma). (History: an earlier post-demosaic RawRefinery "TreeNet" path via the ORT inference layer, plus an unwired NAFNet export, were removed 2026-07-12 when PMRID superseded them; recover from git if a post-demosaic engine is ever wanted -- e.g. to cover X-Trans/Apple.) LIVE PREVIEW: the cached pre-develop WorkingImage is kept CLEAN (RawFormat::Decode caches it BEFORE denoise; denoise runs only on the private develop copy, i.e. the non-interactive export path). The interactive develop render derives a raw-DENOISED base from the clean cache (MW::developRawDenoisedBase / ensureRawDenoise), cached by path+amounts+ISO and computed OFF the GUI thread (developRenderPool) -- so a denoise-amount change re-runs the model ONCE and later tone/exposure edits reuse the denoised base; both the proxy and the full-res render start from it. ISO for the conditioning comes from ImageMetadata::ISONum (populated in cache mode from G::ISOColumn; live preview reads it via MW::currentImageIso). NO SILENT NO-OP: PMRID can return an UNCHANGED mosaic -- no ORT in the build, pmrid.onnx missing, or a non-Bayer CFA. RawFormat::Decode reports that through outDenoiseApplied (relayed by ImageDecoder::decodeRawWorking), so MW::ensureRawDenoise drops the result instead of publishing a denoised base that is pixel-identical to the clean one. Without it the dock showed "Denoised" with the amount sliders enabled over an unchanged image -- the failure was invisible. MW::reportRawDenoiseUnavailable records WHY, via PMRID::IsAvailable(): the model cannot run (session-wide, rawDenoiseUnavailable) vs this sensor is unsupported (per path, rawDenoiseUnsupported). Both gate ensureRawDenoise, so a failed denoise is not retried as a full raw re-decode on every settle. The freshly- decoded CLEAN base is still published to WorkingImageCache -- it is good, and the render needs it. SAID ON THE PANEL, NOT IN A POPUP: the dock GREYS the denoise group (Denoise + Auto run + Lum/Color) and shows the reason where the controls were -- MW::rawDenoiseAvailable -> DevelopProperties::updateDenoiseAvailability -> RawPanel::setDenoiseAvailable, refreshed on every syncRawPanel. The build-level case needs no decode to detect: PMRID::IsSupportedBuild() is a CHEAP check (InferenceSession::BackendCompiledIn() + pmrid.onnx beside the binary -- no model load, unlike PMRID::IsAvailable()), so on a build without ONNX Runtime the group is dead from the moment the panel appears and no raw is ever decoded for it. The per-sensor case can only be learned by running, so it greys the group on the way back. RawPanel::setDenoiseAvailable is sticky: setDenoiseRunState cannot re-enable the amounts while it is false. NOISE MODEL (k,b): the KSigma normalization needs a per-frame Poisson-Gaussian model (var = k*x + b, white-normalised green plane), resolved by a priority chain (PMRID::resolveKB): (1) a DNG NoiseProfile (tag 51041) baked into the file, read by DngRaw::UnpackCfa -- exact, no calibration; (2) a per-camera-model K(ISO)/B(ISO) table (pmrid_calib_tables.h) -- MEASURED by a photon-transfer sweep (calibrate_pmrid.py) or DERIVED from published sensor data (gen_pmrid_tables.py, photonstophotos/Bill Claff) -- ISO-interpolated; (3) blind estimation from the CFA (var-vs-mean over flat blocks, PMRID::estimateKB) so an uncalibrated body still denoises with its own model; (4) a generic full-frame Sony table as a last-ditch guard. The chosen tier is logged by PMRID::Apply. A TIER MUST BE PLAUSIBLE, NOT MERELY PRESENT (PMRID::kbPlausible): var = k*x + b is in the white-normalised [0,1] domain, so k + b is a small fraction of 1 even for a very noisy frame; a tier whose numbers are outside deliberately loose bounds (k in (0,0.5], b in [0,0.1], both finite) FALLS THROUGH to the next one instead of denoising with them. Checked on the DNG NoiseProfile and the blind estimate -- the two tiers read from the file rather than compiled in. THE 2026-08-30 MAGENTA CAST is why: TiffWalk::Reader::reals() had no case for TIFF type 12 (DOUBLE) and fell to its integer default, and NoiseProfile (51041) is DOUBLE -- so EVERY DNG returned the low 4 mantissa bytes of each value read as an integer, k ~ 3.8e9 instead of 6.9e-5. That gives cvt_k ~ 2.7e-13, so the KSigma forward multiplies the image away to a constant (-0.82 everywhere) before the net sees it and the inverse then divides by 2.7e-13, leaving +/-1e9 that clamps to a near-BLACK mosaic. The visible symptom was not black, though: Develop::BlendRawDenoise adds lum*dY of the correction, so the render base came out flat achromatic (R=G=B ~ 0.011), and stage 0 then applied asShotMul (1.78/1.00/1.59) to a signal that was ALREADY neutral instead of one sitting at R,B ~ 0.59G -- over-boosting red and blue by exactly those factors, i.e. magenta. Two lessons worth keeping: a colour cast can be a LEVELS failure wearing a colour disguise (chase the channel MEANS of each base, which is what the Develop diagnostics' k/b line and a means probe in the blend show at once), and the diagnostics report would have named this in one look -- source = "DNG NoiseProfile" with k = 3.8e9 is self-evidently wrong. Reader::scalar() still has the same DOUBLE gap; no DOUBLE tag is read through it today, so it is left alone deliberately. Only DNG carries a NoiseProfile, so proprietary raws (ARW/NEF/CR2/CR3...) use tier 2 if known, else tier 3. ONE DECODE, TWO BASES + PREVIEW-FIRST: ensureRawDenoise is triggered on image SELECT (MW::fileSelectionChange) AND on entering Develop (MW::setOperationMode), not only on settle, so its progress row (progressRawDenoiseRow) appears immediately in both the "select while already in Develop" and the "in Preview then switch to Develop" flows. Its single decode returns BOTH the PMRID base and the CLEAN base (RawFormat::Decode outClean = the same mosaic demosaiced before PMRID = one UnpackCfa, not two) and publishes the clean base to the cache. The ImageCache decode then REUSES that clean base (ImageDecoder::load short-circuits UnpackCfa+Demosaic when a scene-linear entry is cached), removing the redundant second decode. Meanwhile the loupe shows the embedded JPG immediately (ImageView::loadImageInterim on a develop-raw cache miss) and the developed image pops in when the decode lands. STAGED PROGRESS: the denoise progress bar spans the WHOLE decode, not just the PMRID stage: RawFormat::Decode maps the pre-PMRID (clean) demosaic to [0,25%], PMRID to [25,75%], and the post-PMRID (denoised) demosaic to [75,100%] of one 0..1000 bar (Demosaic::Run gained a per-row progress callback). So the bar moves as the clean demosaic runs and continues through PMRID -- no empty gap. Inert for a normal (non-denoise) decode, which passes no progress sink. ZOOM/PAN KEPT ACROSS THE POP: MW::fileSelectionChange captures the outgoing zoom/pan (ImageView::captureDevelopView -> developCapture*) BEFORE the pixmap is replaced, for every Develop navigation (loadImageInterim also captures if the caller did not); the developed image restores it via applyDevelopCapturedView, called from both the clean-image loadImage and setDevelopPreview, instead of re-fitting -- so a zoomed inspection stays put through interim -> developed. The crop case still re-fits: setDevelopPreview keeps the view only when the aspect ratio is unchanged (a resolution change); an aspect change = crop -> refit. MATCHING PREVIEW MODE (SAME AREA IN THE NEXT IMAGE): in Preview the swap is synchronous, so navigating zoomed lands on the same area and a thumbnail click pans the loupe to the clicked point (IconView::thumbClick -> ImageView::panTo). In Develop the developed image arrives seconds later, so a capture frozen at navigation would undo any pan made meanwhile -- and the thumbnail click always arrives afterwards (selection happens on mouse press, thumbClick on release). ImageView::refreshDevelopCapture therefore keeps the capture in step with the live view (called from panTo, scale and scrollChange) until the developed image consumes it; developCaptureLocked suspends tracking while the view is being (re)framed by loadImageInterim / applyDevelopCapturedView. The interim JPG is a fraction of the sensor resolution, so loadImageInterim scales the carried zoom by the change in fit zoom (interimZoomScale) -- the placeholder frames the same content as the developed image (and stays scrollable, so the pan is not swallowed); tracking divides the factor back out so the capture is always in full size terms. First image in a new folder is not captured (it starts at fit). WHY TWO DEMOSAICS: "Denoise raw" is not all-or-nothing -- it is a BLEND scaled by the luma/chroma amount sliders, so the decode needs BOTH endpoints, and both come from demosaicing the SAME CFA mosaic, differing only in whether PMRID ran first:

  • clean base (outClean) = mosaic demosaiced BEFORE PMRID = the noisy, un-denoised RGB = the 0% endpoint.

  • PMRID base (*work)    = mosaic demosaiced AFTER PMRID = full-strength denoised RGB = the 100% endpoint. PMRID itself is a PRE-demosaic (CFA-domain) denoiser, NOT a joint demosaic+denoise; the demosaic is a separate bilinear pass on each. Develop::BlendRawDenoise then does shown = clean + amount*(pmrid - clean) (luma/chroma split). The progress bar (progressRawDenoiseRow) tracks ONLY the PMRID stage, so it sits empty through the demosaics and fills through the PMRID tiles. Caching both bases is what lets a denoise-slider drag re-blend instantly: the model re-runs only on an image or ISO change. BASE MUST BE SCENE-LINEAR: the denoise blend (Develop::BlendRawDenoise) subtracts the clean cached base from the denoised re-decode, so the two must share geometry + space. The clean base comes from MW::ensureDevelopWork -> ImageDecoder::decodeIndependent, which selects the in-house RAW decoder off ImageMetadata::ext. DataModel::imMetadata() populates m.type but NOT m.ext, so ensureDevelopWork sets m.ext from the file suffix; without it the raw decode is skipped, the base falls back to the display-referred embedded JPG (active-area size, not scene-linear), and the blend silently rejects the size mismatch (denoise appears dead). See the Develop diagnostics report to confirm the base is sceneReferred with matching dims. MATCHING DIMS ARE NOT ENOUGH -- COLOUR STATE MUST MATCH TOO: the display-referred fallback is active-area sized, so it can be the SAME dimensions as the sensor image, and then nothing rejects it. Its pixels are working-space, white-balanced and display-referred (InputTransform::FromImage), while the PMRID base is camera-native scene-linear (RawColor::ToCameraNative, WB and matrix deliberately downstream of the cache) -- blending the two adds a camera-native difference to white-balanced pixels and casts the image (the sensor's green sits far above its red/blue until the white balance runs, so the difference is dominated by green). The WorkingImageCache is keyed by path alone and, despite what its header promises, does hold such entries under a raw's path: Preview mode and the display-referred paths (ImageDecoder::applyDevelop, MW::ensureWorkingImageNow) put one there. renderDevelopPreview and ensureWorkingImageNow already re-decoded rather than trust one; ensureRawDenoise did not, and its two call sites handed it WorkingImageCache::instance().get(fPath) raw. MW::rawDenoiseCleanBaseOk is now the single test (valid + sceneReferred + space == CameraNative): a clean base that fails it is DROPPED, not fixed up, so the decode produces its own (outClean) and that replaces the bad cache entry -- otherwise the next render and the next denoise would go straight back to it. Develop::BlendRawDenoise also refuses a space / sceneReferred mismatch outright (serves the clean image and qWarnings). This is a latent hazard that is now closed; it was NOT the cause of the 2026-08-30 magenta cast -- see the next paragraph, and do not let the two be confused when reading the guard.

- LOCAL luminance NR is a MASKABLE Develop op: an ADDITIONAL luminance-only smoothing applied on top of the baseline, per scope/mask. It is a SPATIAL op (below), operating on the already-decoded WorkingImage -- so it needs no second decode and is independent of which decode engine ran. It is therefore a PORTABLE OpenCV pass (mac + Windows), not Core Image. Algorithm: ratio-preserving luma denoise -- Y = dot(rgb, lumaWeights); Yd = edge-preserving denoise(Y, strength) (bilateral / guided, NLM as a slower higher-quality option); rgb' = rgb * (Yd / Y) so only luminance changes and chroma ratios are untouched.

DATA MODEL (Develop/, planned):

  struct MaskComponent {
      enum Type { Linear, Radial, Brush };
      enum Op   { Add, Subtract };          // union / cut-out WITHIN a scope
      // Linear/Radial: parametric geometry in normalised 0..1 image coords (a few floats)
      // Brush:         a list of stamps {x,y,radius,flow,hardness} -- NOT a bitmap
      bool inverted; bool feather; float featherAmount;
  };
  struct Scope {
      QString name;
      EditParams params;                    // unchanged per-scope unit
      float opacity = 1.0f;  bool visible = true;
      std::vector<MaskComponent> mask;      // empty => global
  };
  struct EditStack {
      EditParams base;                       // global edits (or model as scopes[0])
      std::vector<Scope> scopes;
      int version = 1;
      bool isIdentity() const;
  };

COMPOSITING (sequential-on-accumulator). Each scope develops the result of the stack BELOW it, blended back by opacity x mask alpha:

  acc = Develop(workingImage, base.params)                 // global foundation

for each scope i (bottom -> top):

  a   = opacity_i * maskAlpha_i(x)                     // 0 where the scope doesn't act
  acc = lerp(acc, Develop(acc, params_i), a)           // per pixel

This makes edits sequentially ADDITIVE (each scope sees the result below it) while keeping opacity and masks well defined per pixel. Scope ORDER therefore matters and REORDER changes the result -- that is correct and is surfaced in the UI. Global scope is always opacity 1, no

  mask.

PERFORMANCE (the reason for the masking model over a pixel-scope stack). Ops are split by cost and the cheap ones are FUSED:

- POINT ops -- exposure, contrast, highlights/shadows/whites/blacks, temp, tint, the Colour RGB + HSL sliders, and (for now) texture + dehaze -- are pure per-pixel functions. ALL scopes' point ops run in a SINGLE fused pass over the image: one read, one write, no per-scope intermediate buffers; N scopes cost N cheap evaluations in registers per pixel (bandwidth-bound). This is the slider-drag hot path and is independent of scope/mask count. (DECISION 2026-06-25: texture + dehaze are POINT ops for now to keep the fused engine simple; revisit a SPATIAL local-contrast version later -- see RawDecode_ToDo #15.)

- SPATIAL ops -- local luminance NR (and a future spatial texture/dehaze) -- need a neighbourhood, so they cannot fuse: one pass each, blended by the scope's mask, CACHED and recomputed only when their own param or mask changes (never on a point-slider drag). The pass output depends only on its strength param, not the mask VALUES, so it is computed over the mask BOUNDING BOX + filter-radius halo (not the whole frame) and the cached result is reused while only the blend re-reads the mask alpha.

THREADING. The fused point pass is data-parallel WITHIN one render() (row-tiles), NOT spread across the ImageDecoder worker pool. The decoder pool is task-parallel ACROSS images (one decoder per image, to fill the browse cache); an interactive edit is a SINGLE image, so the pool leaves it on one core -- only splitting that one image's rows uses all cores. IMPLEMENTED (phase 1, Develop::applyPointOps) with QtConcurrent::run over the global QThreadPool -- the same idiom FocusStack already uses (fs.cpp) -- rather than cv::parallel_for_ (which appears nowhere in the tree). The global pool is fixed-size, so a background decoder that also calls render() shares it and oversubscription is bounded automatically (no N x N blow-up, no setNumThreads juggling). Rows split into <= maxThreads chunks of >= 64 rows; small images run flat. (cv::parallel_for_ remains an option if a later spatial op is implemented in OpenCV and wants to stay on OpenCV's own pool.)

MASKS store GEOMETRY, never bitmaps. Parametric masks (linear/radial, ~6 floats) are evaluated analytically inline in the fused loop -- zero storage, resolution-independent. Brush masks are stored as the STROKE/STAMP LIST the user painted (not a rasterised alpha), rasterised to a cached alpha buffer on load and after each edit: tiny sidecar, resolution- independent (rasterise at proxy vs full res), and serialisable as text in the XMP sidecar. A scope's combined alpha (the add/subtract union of its components) is rasterised ONCE and cached, recomputed only when mask geometry changes -- never when sliders move.

CACHE TIERS (most already present):

1. pre-develop WorkingImage          -- Develop/workingimagecache.h (DONE)
2. per-scope combined mask alpha      -- recompute on geometry change only
3. spatial-op outputs (local luma NR ...) -- recompute on their param/mask change only

4. fused point pass                   -- always re-run on a point-slider drag (cheap) Plus a downscaled PROXY developed during an active drag, full-res async on release (now implemented: renderDevelopFullResAsync, see "Load Responsiveness") -- sub-frame with the fused engine.

PERSISTENCE -- XMP SIDECAR, per image. Winnow already writes per-image .xmp sidecars (Metadata/xmp.cpp Xmp::writeSidecar, DOM-based); the EditStack serialises there (parametric geometry + brush strokes as numbers, Lightroom-style). This REPLACES the current global QSettings "Develop/Scopes/<name>/..." persistence (which is per-machine, not per-image -- the scaffold's main correctness gap). EditStack is versioned for forward migration.

BUILD PHASING (supersedes the single-EditParams phasing above):

1. [DONE] Fused point engine, global only: white balance (temp/tint), exposure, contrast and the tone regions (highlights/shadows/whites/blacks) implemented for real (Develop::buildPointCoeffs + applyPointOps, develop.cpp); render() unchanged (still one EditParams) but Develop now does a single parallel per-pixel pass. Wired live: DevelopProperties::paramsChanged -> MW::developParamsChange() pulls the cached pre-develop WorkingImage (WorkingImageCache), re-renders Develop + OutputTransform, and pushes straight to the loupe via ImageView::setDevelopPreview() -- no decode, no file read. A non-raw cache miss builds the WorkingImage once from the displayed image (InputTransform). Orientation: a scene-referred (raw, sensor-native) render has EXIF orientation applied to match the loupe (mirrors ImageDecoder::rotate()). NOT yet persisted or written back to the image cache -- navigating away drops the preview (phase 4). White balance = per-channel SCENE-LINEAR gain, now ABSOLUTE (Kelvin + tint) rather than a relative nudge -- see "White balance (absolute, Kelvin)" below. WB and exposure are both linear multiplies, so buildPointCoeffs folds them into one per-channel gain (PointCoeffs:: channelGain) applied before the perceptual tone curve. Exposure = 2^EV scene-linear gain.

TONE CURVE (contrast + highlights/shadows/whites/blacks) -- all four tone regions plus contrast are pure 1-D functions of a channel's white-normalised value, applied in a PERCEPTUAL (~gamma 2.2) domain, NOT scene-linear (a linear-space curve about 0.18 explodes highlights, which sit several stops above the pivot; encoding to gamma first puts mid-grey near 0.46 so tones shape evenly). buildPointCoeffs BAKES the whole chain into a per-render LUT (PointCoeffs::toneLut, indexed by s=(v/white)^(1/gamma), returning white-normalised linear), so the hot loop does ONE encode pow + one interpolated lookup per channel instead of several pow/exp -- the tone pass is now cheaper than the old contrast-only path. Contrast = gentle slope (1 + 0.4*amount, [0.6,1.4]) about mid-grey; the -100..100 slider is NORMALISED to amount -1..1 (without this the first slider unit already hits the extreme). Each tone region adds a Gaussian-weighted lift in perceptual space (centres: blacks 0.0, shadows 0.25, highlights 0.75, whites 1.0; kToneSigma 0.18, max lift kToneShift 0.20 at full slider; + brightens / - recovers, Lightroom-like). The LUT spans up to ~3 stops above white (kToneLutMaxN) so highlight headroom is shaped, not clipped.

COLOUR (RGB + HSL, under the Color header) -- all seven are POINT ops, so they ride the same fused pass at ~no extra cost. RGB (red/green/blue, -100..100) are per-channel scene-linear gains folded straight into channelGain alongside WB + exposure (kRgbGain 0.5 -> full slider = +/-50% on a channel). HSL (hue/saturation/vibrance/luminance, -100..100) is a cross-channel block applied AFTER the tone curve (it mixes channels, so unlike the tone curve it cannot be a per-channel LUT): hue = a Rodrigues rotation about the neutral (1,1,1) axis so greys stay grey (kHueMaxRad = 60deg at full); saturation = chroma scale about Rec.709 luma (-100 -> grey, +100 -> 2x); vibrance = the same chroma scale but weighted per pixel by (1 - HSV saturation), so muted pixels move

  more than already-saturated ones (protecting skies/skin from clipping);

luminance = uniform gain (-100 -> black, +100 -> 2x). hslActive gates the block; identity sliders leave the pass untouched.

SPATIAL ops (Texture #6, Clarity #6.5, Dehaze #7, Vignette #8, Sharpen #8.5, Grain #9) run AFTER the fused

point pass, each owning a full-image pass (like Denoise) -- they need a neighbourhood (or, for Vignette/Grain, pixel coordinates / a noise field) so cannot join the per-pixel kernel. Texture and Dehaze are ratio-preserving on LUMINANCE (chroma untouched) and scale their blur radius to the image's longer edge so the screen-res proxy and the full-res settle render shape the same frequency band. Texture = mid-frequency local contrast: a Gaussian base (kTextureSigmaFrac ~0.0015*edge) splits a detail band that + amplifies (kTextureGain) and - smooths. Dehaze = APPROXIMATION (not dark-channel-prior; user-chosen): a large-radius local-contrast boost (box blur, kDehazeSigmaFrac ~0.02*edge -- box because its running-sum cost is ~radius-independent) + a contrast pull about a low pivot (kDehazePivot 0.3, deepens shadows) + a saturation lift (kDehazeSat); + clears haze, - adds it. Vignette = a radial exposure falloff about the centre: mask = pow(rn, k), rn the elliptical radius (0 centre, 1 corner), k from the Feather slider (high feather -> low k -> gradual/reaches inward; low feather -> corner-hugging); corner gain = 2^Exposure (- darkens, + brightens), scaled by the mask. Off-centre / shaped vignettes use radial masks, so this global op is just Exposure + Feather. Grain = monochromatic film grain added to perceptual luminance (ratio-preserving, like Texture): a fixed-seed noise field on a coarse grain-cell grid (Size sets the cell, scaled to the long edge so the proxy and full res match) is bilinearly upsampled, its amplitude modulated by a lower-frequency Roughness field (0 uniform, 1 patchy), weighted toward the midtones (fades to 0 at black/white) and scaled by Amount. The fixed seed keeps the pattern stable across re-renders (no shimmer). These are global (no-mask) now; the scope compositor will bound them to a mask bbox later. Costs land only on the full-res settle render (proxy filters ~3MP). The Detail "Denoise" is two 0..100 sliders (mapped to 0..1) into Develop::Denoise: "Denoise" (localDenoiseLuma) = luminance NR via a perceptual bilateral (ratio-preserving), and "Denoise Color" (localDenoiseChroma) = chroma NR via a downscaled opponent-chroma (R-Y, B-Y) blur that keeps luma exact. Luma runs first, then chroma. All ten Basic sliders are now live.

ONE OPERATION AT FOUR RADII (the local-contrast family). Texture, Clarity and Dehaze's first stage are the SAME band operation -- split a frequency band off a blurred base and scale it -- differing only in radius and weighting. Sharpen is the same shape again at ~1px. On an 8640px edge:

  Sharpen  ~1px ABSOLUTE     edge-gated            acutance
  Texture  0.0015 x edge     ungated               fine detail contrast
  Clarity  0.007  x edge     midtone + halo        midtone punch / glow
  Dehaze   0.02   x edge     ungated (+ pivot)     atmospheric veiling

An order of magnitude apart, so they STACK rather than compete. The scalar math is header-only in Develop/localcontrast.h (applyBand / bandFactor / midtoneWeight / haloGuard) and the duplicated perceptual-luma prep and ratio fold-back live in develop.cpp's buildPerceptualLuma + shapeAndFoldLuma. shapeAndFoldLuma is TEMPLATED on the shape functor on purpose: it inlines, so each op still costs ONE parallel pass and no extra buffer. gaussianBase carries Texture's downscale-blur-upscale trick and is shared with Clarity; Dehaze keeps a box blur: at its far larger radius a running-sum blur is cheaper still. Grain borrows only midtoneWeight (it needs no blurred base, so it keeps its own fused loop rather than allocating a cv::Mat it does not otherwise want).

CLARITY (#6.5) is what stops that ladder having a 13x gap in the middle, and is deliberately more than "Texture with a bigger radius": the band is weighted by midtoneWeight (so it cannot crush blacks or flatten highlights) and rolled off by haloGuard where the high-pass excursion is large (the strong edges where a band this wide rings). The weights multiply the AMOUNT, not the result, so they only ever pull the band factor back toward 1 -- they can never invert or overshoot the band. Measured on an M4 at 49.8MP: clarity 61ms, texture 83ms, dehaze 141ms.

REFACTOR SAFETY. Extracting the shared kernel rewrote three ops that already shape saved recipes, so tests/unit/tst_localcontrast.cpp is a CHARACTERIZATION test: its golden values were captured from the build immediately BEFORE the extraction. Texture, Sharpen and Grain came out bit-identical; Dehaze was re-expressed from yp + k*(yp-base) to base + (1+k)*(yp-base) (equal in exact arithmetic) and measured at most 6e-7 different over a full frame -- 1.5e-4 of one 8-bit code value, i.e. 25x below even a 16-bit quantum. If one of those tests fails, a saved recipe now renders differently; do not re-bless the numbers without proving the change is intended.

SHARPEN (#8.5) IS THE ONE SCALE-DEPENDENT OP, and the exception to the rule two paragraphs up. Every other spatial op derives its radius from max(w,h) precisely so the proxy and the settle render shape the same RELATIVE band. Acutance is a property of the PIXEL GRID, so sharpenRadius is an ABSOLUTE pixel radius (0.5..3 px at full res) and cannot be scale-invariant. Instead WorkingImage::renderScale carries this render's long edge as a fraction of the full-res long edge (set in WorkingImageCache::downscaled, carried through assignReusing), and Sharpen::effectiveSigma multiplies the radius by it: the drag proxy shows a scale-honest approximation, the settle render shows the truth, and the panel tells the user to judge at 1:1. renderScale is deliberately NOT an EditParams field -- EditParams is both the sidecar format and the per-scope render-cache key (MW::developCompositeStack), so a render detail there would persist to every sidecar and make the proxy and settle renders miss each other's cache entries.

The op itself is an unsharp mask on perceptual luminance, ratio-preserving like Texture: a small Gaussian base gives hp = yp - base, which Detail soft-thresholds (suppressing the smallest amplitudes as noise, sign preserved) and Masking gates by the gradient magnitude of the BLURRED luma (so noise does not read as an edge). The Masking knee is a CURVE, not a constant times the slider (Sharpen::maskKnee, knee = m*(0.10 + 0.40*m^2), recalibrated 2026-08-21): a linear ramp topped out at 0.10/px, which still passed ordinary texture (~0.05/px) at half strength, so the top of the slider could not quiet a noisy sky. It is now linear at the bottom (the old feel) and cubic at the top, so the last third of the travel is where strong protection lives. The stored key and its 0..1 range are unchanged -- nothing to migrate -- but a sharpenMasking written before this now protects more above the middle. The remaining limiter on protection is the (1 - m) blend in edgeGate, which keeps flat areas at (1 - m) of full sharpening so intermediate settings soften rather than switch; raise its shape too if even more travel is wanted.

Scalar math is header-only in Develop/sharpen.h so it is unit-testable (tests/unit/tst_sharpen.cpp) the way colorgrade.h / tonecurve.h are; develop.cpp owns only the OpenCV blur/Sobel and the parallelFor. The Sobel pair is skipped entirely at Masking 0. Placed after Vignette (darkened corners are not detail-boosted) and before Grain (film grain is never itself sharpened). DISTINCT FROM TEXTURE, which is resolution-proportional mid-frequency (~13px on an 8640px edge, ungated): sharpening is ~1px and edge-gated, so the two occupy disjoint bands and stack without double-counting.

SEEING THE MASK (Opt + drag Masking), Lightroom's Alt-drag. Which pixels the edge gate protects is invisible in the result without hunting around at 1:1, so holding Opt while DRAGGING the Detail panel's Masking slider replaces the photo with the gate itself in GRAYSCALE -- white sharpened, black protected, greys the soft roll-off. Momentary in two inputs: it needs Opt down AND the slider's handle held, so releasing either (or the app deactivating) brings it back. The drag gate, rather than mere row focus, is what lets the preview be this loud -- it covers the photo, so it must not be able to sit there once you have let go.

It is built from the DEVELOPED FRAME ON SCREEN (MW::updateSharpenMaskPreview keeps a copy in developFrame as each render is applied), NOT from inside the pipeline: the true gate lives several stages deep in a worker-thread stack render, and plumbing a buffer back out of it would cost a pipeline seam and land a tick late. The same blur + 1/8-Sobel + Sharpen::edgeGate over that frame's luma reproduces the gate closely enough to set the slider by, and it is always in step with what is on screen. Two approximations, neither material here: the frame carries sharpening (and grain) the real gate is measured before, and its perceptual luma is sRGB-encoded gray rather than the pipeline's own encode.

THE ONE DEVELOP OVERLAY THAT IS NOT IN MASK SPACE. Because its source is the displayed frame, the image is already post-geometry and oriented, so ImageView (setSharpenMaskImage / drawForeground) stretches it straight over the pixmap -- putting it through maskNormToItem, which is right for every mask raster, would put it in the wrong place on a cropped image.

Wiring: DevelopProperties::sharpenMaskPreviewActive (live Opt state + SliderEditor::sliderIsDown) -> syncSharpenMaskPreview (called from MW's event filter on each Opt edge and, QUEUED, on each mouse press/release -- queued because the slider sets isSliderDown in its own handler, so reading it inline would be one event stale -- and on app activate/deactivate) -> sharpenMaskPreviewChanged -> MW::updateSharpenMaskPreview, which also re-runs after every applied render so the mask follows the drag.

SLIDER-DRAG LATENCY: the naive path re-rendered the full image synchronously on the GUI thread every tick. For a 50MP RAW that measured ~520 ms/tick (probe below), dominated by OutputTransform::ToImage (~420 ms) -- it was the only serial stage while Develop::applyPointOps was already parallel. Three fixes (DevelopProperties:: paramsChanged -> MW::developParamsChange -> MW::renderDevelopPreview):

  • PROXY: a drag renders a screen-resolution proxy WorkingImage (WorkingImageCache::downscaled, INTER_AREA in scene-linear, sized ~1.5x the loupe viewport, cached per path in MW::developProxy). Raw orientation is applied while the proxy is still small (cheap). ~6-20x less pixel work per tick. The proxy USED to be FAST-upscaled to the full displayed dimensions so ImageView::setDevelopPreview's "same dimensions" contract held -- a ~200 MB QImage::scaled plus an equally large QPixmap::fromImage per tick on a 50MP file. It no longer is: see PROXY-SIZED PIXMAP below.

  • PARALLEL ToImage: OutputTransform::ToImage now splits rows across the global pool (same idiom as applyPointOps), cutting the per-pixel pow cost ~Ncore.

  • COALESCE + SETTLE: developParamsChange does no rendering itself -- it arms a 0ms single-shot (developProxyRenderTimer) that collapses a burst of ticks into one proxy render, and (re)arms developFullResTimer (~160 ms) so the crisp FULL-res render runs once the slider stops.

  • ASYNC FULL-RES (2026-06): the settled full-res render is ~1.3 s on a 50MP RAW and was run synchronously on the GUI thread, freezing the drag. Worse, the freeze drained queued slider events in bursts whose gaps re-fired the settle timer, so the 1.3 s render ran REPEATEDLY mid-drag. [DevTime] confirmed: proxy ticks ~45 ms (render 25 / rotate+scale 18 / preview 2), but interleaved `full 8640x5760 render 1300-1440 ms`. Fix: developFullResTimer now calls MW::renderDevelopFullResAsync, which drives the render on a dedicated 1-thread pool (developRenderPool) OFF the GUI thread; the proxy keeps the drag live meanwhile. The result is marshalled back via QMetaObject::invokeMethod -> MW::onDevelopFullResReady and applied only if still current. STALENESS GUARD: developParamsGen is bumped on every paramsChange; a render whose captured gen != developParamsGen (or whose path != currentFilePath) is discarded, and onDevelopFullResReady re-arms the settle timer so the latest value still gets a crisp render. IN-FLIGHT GUARD: developFullResInFlight allows only one full-res render at a time. Orientation is computed on the GUI thread (MW::developOrientationDegrees, reads dm->sf) before launch; the worker touches only the const WorkingImage (held alive by a shared_ptr) and value-copied params. Compositing (render + rotate + proxy upscale) is shared by both paths via the file-local developComposite() helper.

  • SPATIAL-OP COST (2026-06, follow-up): with texture/dehaze sliders left non-zero (they carry over between images until per-image params land), the async full-res render was still ~1.45 s. Per-stage [DevTime] (WorkingImageCache::RenderTimings + Develop::StageTimings) pinned it on the spatial ops, NOT the point pass: point 64 / texture 899 / dehaze 399 ms. Two fixes: (a) the per-pixel luminance/ output loops in Develop::Denoise/Texture/Dehaze were SINGLE-THREADED -- now split across the global pool via the file-local parallelFor() (same idiom as applyPointOps/ToImage; identical math, bit-for-bit output). (b) Texture's large- sigma cv::GaussianBlur dominated what remained (~600 ms): the base is low-frequency, so it is now computed on a luminance image downscaled by kDown (=clamp(sigma/3,1,4), INTER_AREA down / INTER_LINEAR up) and the cost drops ~kDown^2. Net: full-res render ~1.45 s -> ~360 ms (texture 899->72, dehaze 399->120) on an M4. Spatial ops still recompute every full-res render because they run AFTER the point pass (an exposure change alters their input); the real win for an untouched image is per-image params so a 0 slider early-returns -- see item 4 (XMP sidecar). GENERAL RULE: any new per-pixel Develop loop must use parallelFor; any new large-kernel blur should use a downscaled base (or an O(1) box/integral approach) -- files keep growing.

  • MASK-DRAG LATENCY (2026-08): with masked scopes the proxy tick was still doing all of the work every time. DevelopProperties::paramsChanged fanned out to TWO direct consumers -- developParamsChange (coalesced) AND updateMaskOverlayTint (NOT coalesced), so the veil's one-or-two full buildMaskBuffer passes at a 1600px cap ran on every mouse-move on top of the render. Inside the render, developCompositeStack re-rasterized EVERY scope's mask and renderStack redid `acc = work` + a Develop::Apply for the base and then a full copy + Apply per scope -- even when only one mask's geometry had moved. And Linear/Radial handle drags emitted maskGeometryChanged on every mouse-move while brush strokes went through a 10 Hz throttle. Five fixes: . COALESCED VEIL: paramsChanged no longer connects to updateMaskOverlayTint. The proxy timer's slot calls it, then renderDevelopPreview -- one veil rebuild and one render per event-loop turn, and ONE loupe repaint instead of two. maskEditBegin/End stay direct (they are one-shot). . THROTTLED HANDLE DRAGS: ImageView::maskEmitLiveGeometry(LiveEmit) is the one throttle for every live mask emit (kMaskStrokeLiveMs, trailing edge); brushEmitLiveGeometry is now a thin caller. A brush stroke and a handle drag are mutually exclusive, so one clock covers both. The grab invalidates the clock so the first move emits at once; both releases emit unconditionally, so the settled geometry is never a throttled sample. . PER-SCOPE MASK CACHE + LAYER CACHE: Develop/developstackcache.h. One entry per scope POSITION (so the dragged scope replaces its own entry instead of thrashing a shared store), keyed on the components' signature; plus the developed intermediates (prefix/layer) for the ONE scope being edited. renderStack takes a StackResume and skips every scope below the edited one, and skips the develop pass for the edited scope itself when only its MASK moved -- so a mask drag collapses to one blend + one OutputTransform. PROXY-ONLY and GUI-THREAD-ONLY (the settle render passes nullptr), which is what keeps it lock-free. Invalidated on image / proxy-size / orientation / GLOBAL-params change; a scope whose reference (AI field, range reference, auto-mask guide) was not registered yet is NOT cached, or the un-confined placeholder would never be retired. Covered by tests/unit/tst_renderstack. . PROXY-SIZED PIXMAP: Views/scaledpixmapitem.h. ScaledPixmapItem keeps presenting the FULL image size to the scene while holding the proxy pixmap and stretching it at paint time, so the per-tick upscale + QPixmap::fromImage at full dimensions is gone. This was chosen over making the ~37 pmItem->boundingRect() sites scene-relative: item coordinates ARE image coordinates as before, so no overlay, hit test or zoom calculation moved. Only code that READS PIXELS out of the pixmap needs ImageView::imageToPixmap (the sample loupe; copyImage stretches back for the clipboard). EXCEPTION: a non-identity geometry still upscales first, because crop is applied on the full-output-dimension image so its dims match the settle render's exactly -- cropping the proxy would round differently and make the arriving settle re-fit the view. . SETTLE-ONLY DIAGNOSTICS: updateDevelopScopes(shown, verifyVsPreview) skips the 4096-sample imageGridDiff on an interactive tick. . BRUSH DAB SPACING (the actual dominant cost, found by the probe above). With the develop stages down to ~3 ms, a brush drag on a 2 MP mask still measured `mask 900 ms` per tick and `tint` the same again. It was not the pipeline at all: BrushStamp::segmentMax computed its dab spacing PER SEGMENT (n = max(1, len/step)), so ANY mouse-move -- even a 3 px one -- stamped two full-radius dab boxes, and each box is (2*radius)^2 pixel evaluations (~105k at a 20% brush on this image). The develop render replays every dab of the whole stroke on every tick, so cost grew with the number of mouse EVENTS rather than with the length of the stroke. Fixed by tracking the residual distance across segments (BrushStamp::kDabSpacing + segmentMax's `carry`), so dabs land at a fixed arc-length spacing however the events arrive; ImageView threads the same carry through maskBrushDabCarry so the live preview and the render still place dabs identically. Both stamp the final/cursor point unconditionally -- a spaced walk can stop up to one step short, and the paint must not trail the brush. GENERAL RULE: brush cost is (arc length / (radius * kDabSpacing)) dab boxes; anything that stamps per EVENT instead of per DISTANCE will scale with mouse polling rate. . DAB AREA (the rest of it). Spacing alone only bought ~1.4x, because a fast drag already delivers events roughly a step apart -- the cost was the SIZE of each dab, not the number. A dab box is (2*radius)^2 clamped to the buffer, so a large brush touches most of the image per dab, and BrushStamp::dabMax was the one per-pixel loop in Develop still running single-threaded. It is now row-parallel (bands own disjoint rows, so the MAX accumulation cannot race; serial below 64k px so ImageView's per-mouse-move dab does not pay a thread hop), and its outside/full-core tests use SQUARED distance so the sqrt is paid only in the feather band -- at feather 0, never. coverage() is unchanged for its other callers. . DAB COUNTERS. BrushStamp::dabStats() adds two relaxed atomics per dab and the [DevTime] mask field reports "(N scopes, D dabs, P Mpx)". This is the only way to tell "too many dabs" from "each dab is the whole image" -- they have different fixes, and reading the code cannot distinguish them (it took two wrong guesses to learn that). Shared by the GUI and settle renders, so a settle landing mid-drag inflates one line. . PER-COMPONENT BRUSH CACHE (what it actually was). Splitting the mask number into raster / setup / fold settled it: on a scope with 21 submasks a tick read `raster 275 / setup 308 / fold 51`. The fold was never the problem and neither were the dabs (185 dabs = 4 Mpx against a 700 ms build). The cost was PER COMPONENT: all ~19 brush submasks were re-rasterized every tick, each re-parsing its JSON and allocating + zeroing its own w*h*4 buffer. g_brushCache exists to prevent exactly that, but its policy was `if (size() > 8) clear()` -- past eight entries it wiped itself on every build, so it could never hit on a busy mask. Now a byte-budgeted LRU (kBrushCacheBudget): entries are ~8 MB at a 2 MP proxy, so a COUNT cap either thrashes on a busy mask or hoards on a big one, while least-recently-USED eviction keeps the components the user is not touching and lets the one being edited churn a new key per tick. . VEIL SHARES THE RASTERS. updateMaskOverlayTint built at its own 1600px cap, so its brush components missed every cache entry the render had just filled and it cost about as much as the render again. It now builds at the PROXY's exact dimensions when one exists, so the two share keys. . LESSON. Three fixes were aimed at this before the split existed -- dab spacing, dab parallelism, the scope layer cache -- and all three were reasoned from the code. Only the first was even a real bug. Sub-timings and COUNTS (dabs, components, strokes) are what found it. Split a number before optimising anything behind it. . RESULT (final). On a 1618x1214 proxy with a ~24-submask scope, a brush drag went from ~1450 ms per tick (mask 709 + tint 750) to 77 ms: mask 46 [raster 15 one live stroke / setup 22 / fold 2, ONE component] + develop 3 + toImage 8, with the veil gated off (tint 0). That is ~19x, and it clears the 100 ms live-emit throttle with headroom, which is what stops the brush cursor lagging. WHAT IS LEFT: setup (22 ms) is now the largest item and is mostly signature work -- every component's paramsJson is hashed twice per tick (once for the scope signature in developCompositeStack, once per component in buildMaskBuffer) plus the 8 MB prefix copy; and the proxy render is still SYNCHRONOUS on the GUI thread, so that headroom is a property of THIS image size and component count, not a guarantee. . PREFIX-FOLD CACHE. The component fold is SEQUENTIAL per pixel, so the running value after components [0..k) is a well-defined image -- a prefix -- and editing one submask leaves it exactly right. buildMaskBuffer now resumes from the deepest still-valid prefix (g_maskFoldCache, keyed on path+dims+orientation, mutex-guarded because the GUI render, the veil and the settle worker all call it). Components inside the prefix are not folded, not rasterized and not even JSON-parsed, so this removes the setup and raster cost too, not just the fold. The cut point is the first component whose signature changed, so a drag on one submask resumes at the same place every tick; at least one component is always left out or a no-change tick would cache everything. INVALIDATION: per-component signatures cover everything read out of a MaskComponent but NOT the references a component SAMPLES, so ensureRangeRef (base-params dependent) calls maskFoldCacheClear(); the AI/object/guide refs are per-image and built once, and a component whose ref is not registered yet is refused entry to the prefix (prefixRefsReady), so nothing else can go stale behind it. . VEIL GATING (2026-08). Two changes, one a bug. (a) MW::updateMaskOverlayTint now asks ImageView::maskTintVisible() and returns before building. maskTintHidden lived entirely in ImageView and was only read by drawForeground, so pressing "O" hid the veil but MW kept compositing it at full proxy resolution every tick -- ~87 ms per tick for something nothing drew. The stored QImage is left in place rather than cleared (it is not drawn) and maskTintVisibilityChanged is wired to rebuild on the way back up, so re-showing cannot flash a stale or empty overlay. (b) The veil is now shown only for the scope's FIRST submask -- where nothing else on screen says the mask exists -- and starts hidden for a second or later one, since the tool's own live preview (brush coverage, gradient handles, range swatches) already shows what is being added. "O" brings it back. ImageView::beginMaskEdit no longer force-shows it; the decision belongs to DevelopProperties, at the two places a submask is added, emitted between buildTree() (whose maskEditBegin drives the overlay) and paramsChanged() (whose tick reads the visibility to decide whether to build at all). . WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THE VEIL: the proxy render is SYNCHRONOUS on the GUI thread. With veil ~87 ms + render ~47 ms against a 100 ms live-emit throttle the GUI thread was over 100% occupied during a brush drag, so mouse-move events queued and the brush CURSOR RING -- which ImageView paints itself -- visibly lagged. That is a saturation problem, not a hot loop: no per-stage win fixes it once the total exceeds the throttle. Gating the veil takes the common drag to ~47 ms. The structural fix, still TO DO, is to run the proxy render off the GUI thread the way renderDevelopFullResAsync already runs the settle render (staleness gen, in-flight guard, invokeMethod back). Note before attempting it: DevelopStackCache is lock-free BECAUSE it is GUI-thread-only, orientation must be read on the GUI thread (developOrientationDegrees reads dm->sf), and ImageView::ensureAutoGuide reads pmItem->pixmap() so it has to run before launch. . PROXY RENDER OFF THE GUI THREAD (2026-08). renderDevelopPreview now does only the work that MUST happen on the GUI thread -- orientation (developOrientationDegrees reads dm->sf), ImageView::ensureAutoGuide (reads the loupe pixmap), the ensure*Mask builders (inference + MW state) and the proxy build -- then hands the composite to developProxyPool, a DEDICATED 1-thread pool. Not developRenderPool: sharing it would let a settled full-res render (0.4-1.3 s) block every interactive tick behind it. The worker captures the source as a shared_ptr and the job/path by value, so a GUI-side reset of developProxy cannot dangle it. WHY: the brush cursor and every mask overlay are painted by ImageView on the GUI thread, so compositing there capped cursor smoothness no matter how cheap the render became -- at veil 87 + render 47 against a 100 ms throttle the thread was over 100% occupied and mouse-moves queued. . PROXY WORKER GUARDS. developProxyInFlight allows one at a time. developProxyPending is what makes it CORRECT rather than merely serialised: a request arriving mid-flight is remembered and re-armed on completion. Relying on developParamsGen instead would silently lose the crop / warp / level callers, which call renderDevelopPreview directly without bumping it. developProxyReqGen discards a superseded frame -- discarded, not shown, because the crop tool alternates geometry-applied and geometry-suppressed renders and flashing the wrong one reads as a glitch. [DevTime] gained `worker` (off-thread ms) and `applied` (0 when the frame was dropped as stale). . PROXY WORKER THREAD SAFETY. DevelopStackCache is now MUTEX-GUARDED with private members -- it was lock-free only because it had been GUI-thread-only, and the worker now reads and writes it while the GUI still calls reset()/clear() on image, folder, proxy and denoise-base changes. Never hand out a reference into `entries` or `hot`: copy the shared_ptr out under the lock (hotAt() does) and work from that, which is safe because everything they reach is const. g_brushCache and g_maskFoldCache were already mutex-guarded. applySpots is now serialised behind g_spotHealMutex: the LaMa/MI-GAN heal engines hold per-process state and were only ever driven by one background render at a time; two pools can now overlap. STILL ON THE GUI THREAD, deliberately: the one-off proxy build (~34 ms per image), the ensure*Mask inference (200-400 ms, once per image), and updateMaskOverlayTint -- the veil is gated off for most drags, and moving it would need the same treatment again. . TSAN COVERAGE FOR THE WORKER (tests/tsan/run_tsan_develop.sh, --devtest -> MW::runDevelopStressTest). Nothing in the suite went near the Develop render before this: run_tsan.sh and run_soak.sh only exercise folder load. The driver simulates a brush drag on a multi-submask scope while switching images, toggling the veil, re-rendering through the crop-style caller and re-selecting the folder, so the worker collides with the GUI thread on DevelopStackCache, g_brushCache, g_maskFoldCache and the pending/gen guards. It found a REAL CRASH on its first run -- see the stale-slot bug below -- which is the whole argument for it existing. . STALE FOLD-CACHE SLOT (crash, found by the TSan driver). buildMaskBuffer picks foldSlot while holding g_maskFoldMutex during the LOOKUP, then re-acquires the mutex to store. Between the two, the cache can be cleared -- maskFoldCacheClear() from the GUI thread's ensureRangeRef, the key cap, or another render -- leaving entries[] shorter than foldSlot, and QVector::operator[] out of range is a SEGV. foldSlot is now treated as a HINT and re-validated against the vector under the store lock. GENERAL RULE for these caches: an index chosen under one lock acquisition means nothing under the next; only the data copied out under the lock is safe to keep. . READING THAT TEST'S OUTPUT. WINNOW_DEVTEST_SERIAL=1 is the DEFAULT and is load-bearing: it caps the global pool at one thread so every parallelFor runs serially. Homebrew Qt is not TSan-instrumented, so QFuture::waitForFinished()'s happens-before edge is invisible to the tool -- a parallel-for's lambdas capture the caller's stack by reference, and once the caller returns and reuses that stack every one of those reads is reported as a race. Measured: 49 reports with the pool parallel, 5 with it serial, and the 44 that vanish are all that artifact (proved by A/B -- the same reports appear with the proxy render forced back onto the GUI thread, where that concurrency cannot exist). The run FAILS only on races naming the Develop render; the folder-load / metadata-reader family is warned about, not failed, and crashes always fail.

  • ADJUSTMENT-DRAG LATENCY (2026-08): dragging a slider was slow even on an image with NO edits and NO masks, and the cause was not pixel maths at all. Split the [DevTime] `develop` number into copy / apply / blend / free and the picture inverted: on a 2175x1447 proxy a 121 ms tick was 87 ms of RELEASING MEMORY -- three ~38 MB WorkingImages at ~29 ms each -- against 1 ms for the scope's develop pass and 7 ms for the mask blend. The tell is `copy 2` next to `free 29`: writing 38 MB takes 2 ms (~19 GB/s on an M4) and releasing the same 38 MB takes fifteen times longer, because every tick handed large blocks back to the OS and the next tick faulted them in again. Four fixes: . BUFFER REUSE. Develop/workingimage.h assignReusing(dst, src) refills a WorkingImage through vector::assign, which keeps the existing allocation when capacity fits, and falls back to plain assignment when there is nothing to reuse. That fallback is load-bearing, not tidiness: forcing a fresh 24 MP destination down the grow path measured 631 ms against 49 ms. DevelopStackCache owns the scratch (accScratch / layScratch / outScratch), released by clear() but NOT by reset() -- reset() fires on every tick of a Global drag, and dropping them there made things WORSE, four allocations per tick instead of three. outScratch() hands back whichever of an A/B pair hot.layer is not serving, so the render never writes a buffer the cache is still handing out as const. WorkingImageCache::render takes the same scratch: an UNMASKED image takes developComposite, NOT renderStack, so fixing only the stack path left the commonest case untouched (62 ms per tick). Result: 121 ms -> 35 ms. . SHOW SUPERSEDED FRAMES. `applied 0` on 6 of 7 ticks. A drag delivers mouse events far faster than a render completes, so every frame but the last was superseded and DISCARDED -- the loupe did not update at all until the user paused. That was the lag itself, and no per-stage win could fix it. Frames are tens of ms old and arrive in order (1-thread pool), so they are now shown. The documented reason for discarding was the crop tool flashing a geometry-suppressed frame; that is about GEOMETRY, not age, so developProxyGeomGen bumps only when the geometry actually moves and gates exactly that case. . TRANSFER LUT. With the above done, toImage was ~90% of the tick (35 of 38 ms). On the sRGB path the per-channel chain is a pure 1-D function: the primaries matrix is identity, and HighlightRolloff cannot fire when the baseline curve ran because BaselineTone Clamp01s its own output. So a 16384-entry table plus interpolation replaces the pow, ~4.8x. NOT BIT-EXACT and deliberately so: ~0.001% of inputs land one level of 255 away, always by exactly 1. A denser table does not remove them (the residue is float rounding at the .5 boundary) and an exact-threshold formulation was measured with the SAME residue at only 2.9x -- so this is the faster of two equally approximate options, not a shortcut past an exact one. Used for the preview AND 8-bit export so the two always agree; ToImage16 keeps the exact maths. Display-referred input can exceed 1, where the rolloff is cross-channel, so those pixels take the exact branch. Bound asserted by tests/unit/tst_outputtransform. . SCOPES SAMPLE IN PLACE. updateDevelopScopes ran convertToFormat(RGB32) over the WHOLE frame to read ~180k strided samples, on the GUI thread. ToImage emits RGB888, so it now samples that directly. Newly urgent once superseded frames are shown: this went from 1 tick in 7 to every tick. . CACHE WIPED UNDER THE WORKER (the one that made a Global drag with a mask slow). developStackCache.reset() ran BEFORE the developProxyInFlight early-return, so every mouse-move arriving mid-render still wiped the per-scope masks -- out from under the worker using them -- and then returned. A drag delivers an event about every 8 ms against a ~120 ms render, so the cache was cleared a dozen times per render and no mask ever survived. The symptom was contradictory on purpose: [DevTime] said `cacheKept` (that tick's reset WAS a no-op) while `maskScopes 1` rebuilt the mask anyway, and the orphaned buffer's last reference died in the compositor, which is all COMPOSEOTHER ever was. Fixed by moving the in-flight check above the reset. . BASE PARAMS NO LONGER INVALIDATE MASKS. Even with the ordering fixed, a Global drag changes the base signature every render, and that was part of reset()'s key -- so every mask still died every tick. Only the content-range tools' coverage depends on the base (they threshold ensureRangeRef's reference); every other reference is keyed by PATH and is never rebuilt when the base moves, so the rest were being invalidated for a dependency the code does not have. reset() now keys on image/dims/orientation only, the base signature is folded into the per-scope MASK KEY for range-mask scopes alone (MW::scopeMaskDependsOnBase), and DevelopStackCache::Hot carries its own baseKey because the prefix IS the developed base. . PREFIX SNAPSHOT SCRATCH. With the mask cached, the next allocator cost surfaced underneath it: setHot 62 ms of a 108 ms tick. The hot PREFIX is a function of the base, so a Global drag correctly rejects it every tick -- and then captured a fresh proxy-sized snapshot every tick, whose predecessor setHot released. It now refills its own A/B scratch pair (DevelopStackCache::prefixScratch), separate from the layer's because the two are captured on different schedules. GENERAL SHAPE of this whole round: every fix uncovered the next allocation down, and the probe field that had been 0 became the largest number. Read the whole line, not the stage you just changed. . TSAN COVERAGE FOR THE ADJUSTMENT PATH. The driver only ever simulated a BRUSH drag, which moves the MASK -- so none of the above (the scratch buffers the worker writes, the A/B alternation keeping it off a buffer the cache serves as const, the reset/in-flight reordering) was raced. DevelopProperties::selfTestNudgeAdjustment is the missing half: write one param, emit paramsChanged, nothing else. Getting real coverage out of it took four runs and two fixes, and the PASS line was worthless until both landed -- read the render count and the `resume` distribution, never the PASS alone.

  • The hook must NOT call noteScopeEdit. Marking the stack dirty persists it, the scope then survives the folder churn, and the driver's re-arm stacks another on top: 21-23 scopes and 3 renders in 180 s.

  • selfTestAddMaskScope had to become IDEMPOTENT. It appended a scope whenever the ACTIVE scope was short of components, and an image switch resets the active scope to Global -- so it appended one per switch (179 of them on a long run): 13-14 scopes, 2 renders in 180 s. Latent since it was written; the 45 s default (~2 switches) hid it. Fixing it took renders 2 -> 79.

  • The nudge CADENCE decides which branches run, because a render spans ~16 ticks. Nudging every tick means params always differ between renders, so the cached LAYER is never reused (`prefix +layer` 0 of 79); alternating scope every tick moves the base constantly, so the PREFIX is never reused (`resume -1` 7 of 7). Nudge in 30-tick bursts with 30-tick brush-only gaps, holding a scope for 120, and all three branches run.

  • RESULT: 1206 ticks, 77 renders -- 56 cold, 10 resuming the prefix, 11 resuming prefix+layer -- no races. That is the first run of this driver that actually exercised the interactive cache's reuse paths. . SETTLE RENDER: ONE LAYER BUFFER, NOT ONE PER SCOPE. The full-res path passes no scratch (it runs once and should give its ~735 MB back), and layLocal was declared INSIDE the scope loop, so an N-scope render allocated and released N of them. Measured on a 9600x6376 file with 2 mask scopes: total 5207 ms, of which `free 1735` + `outFree 572` = 2307 ms (44%) was releasing memory. Hoisted out of the loop and released once. WARNING for anyone re-checking this: an ISOLATED benchmark frees the same 735 MB in ~0 ms and says there is nothing here. The cost is memory PRESSURE, not size -- it only appears with the image cache, the source image and the 245 MB mask buffers all resident -- and a micro- benchmark nearly argued this fix away. Giving the settle render a SCRATCH would recover the remaining ~1400 ms but pin ~1.5 GB at 61 MP; that option is written up with its measured cost, its footprint and the shape it should take if revisited, under "FULL-RES SCRATCH" in Develop/workingimagecache.h. It is deferred, not forgotten: the settle render is background work the user is not waiting on. LESSON, the same one as the brush-drag round above: the plan going in was to bound the develop pass and the blend to the mask's BOUNDING BOX, worked out in detail down to the guard list. Measured, those two stages are 1 ms and 7 ms -- the whole design would have saved 8 ms of 121 while the allocator ate 87. Split the number until the stages SUM to the wall clock before choosing a fix, and check allocation churn before reaching for per-pixel cleverness.

  • IMAGE-SWITCH LATENCY (2026-08): switching image in Develop blocks on a full unpack + demosaic + RawColor before anything can be developed (Develop trims read-ahead to the current image). The DELIBERATE answer is progressive display at ZERO memory cost: keep showing the already-decoded 8-bit image while MW::ensureDevelopWork decodes the scene-linear WorkingImage off-thread, and LABEL it -- MW::updateDevelopRenderingHint raises ImageView's top-right chip ("Decoding raw", "Rendering full size") for the two slow stages only, never for the sub-second proxy tick. It is drawn from paintEvent, not drawForeground, so it survives the crop/spot early returns, and sits top-right because drawMaskLegend owns top-left. NOT DONE, on purpose: caching proxies or reading neighbours ahead. A scene-linear WorkingImage is 12 bytes/pixel (~600 MB for 50 MP, against a 768 MB WorkingImageCache budget) and even a screen-resolution proxy is ~46 MB each. A half-size demosaic (2x2 CFA quad -> 1 px, no interpolation) feeding a proxy read-ahead of +/-2 neighbours is the obvious next step if this is revisited; a disk cache was considered and rejected (only helps revisits, large files, mtime invalidation surface). PROBE: set G::isReportDevelopTime to log [DevTime] lines (proxy/full/full(async), dims, proxyBuild / mask (+scope count) / srcCopy / develop [copy apply blend free OTHER] / outFree / resume / cacheKept|cacheWiped / toImage / orient+scale / setHot / worker / COMPOSEOTHER / applied / superseded / preview / scopes / tint ms. OTHER and COMPOSEOTHER are the REMAINDERS -- develop minus its buckets, and worker minus every reported stage -- and they are the point: while either is large the stages do not sum to the wall clock and any fix chosen from the named numbers is aimed at a fraction of the cost. Both were ~40-80 ms, which is how the allocator churn was found. The TINT COUNT is the diagnostic that matters for coalescing: more than one veil rebuild per render means it has come uncoupled again. The async line adds the develop sub-stage breakdown denoise/point/texture/dehaze. Off in production.

2. Scope object + compositor: add EditStack, run the fused loop over scopes with opacity; convert the dock's "scopes-as-alternatives" into a real stack (no masks yet).
3. Parametric masks (linear/radial): analytic inline + per-scope alpha cache.

4. XMP sidecar: serialise/deserialise EditStack; retire the QSettings per-scope store. DONE (2026-06, Increment 1) -- PER-IMAGE DEVELOP STATE:

  • DATA MODEL: Develop/editstack.h. EditStack { version; QVector<EditScope> }, each EditScope { name; mask (reserved -- empty = global, mask editing deferred); opacity (reserved); enabled; EditParams params }. This is the scope = mask + settings shape so the format does NOT change when masks/compositor land. Serialized to JSON then base64 (raw JSON quotes are awkward in an XML attribute). VERSIONED and FORWARD- TOLERANT: unknown keys ignored, missing keys default to identity. Header-only and free of any render-hot-path include (develop.cpp does not include it).

  • SIDECAR: stored as the winnow:Develop rdf:Description attribute (Xmp::initialize registers definedElements["develop"]; the winnow namespace already existed). Metadata::writeDevelopSidecar / readDevelopSidecar mirror writeOrientation (open sidecar, one Xmp item). An identity stack writes "" which CLEARS the attribute (no sidecar is created just to store nothing).

  • DOCK (Develop/Properties/developproperties.cpp): the sliders now reflect the CURRENT IMAGE's EditStack, not app-global QSettings. setCurrentImage(fPath) flushes the previous image then loads+caches this image's stack and pushes its params into the editors (populateSlidersFromStack, guarded by isPopulating so the programmatic setValue->itemChanged does not re-mark dirty). itemChange writes into the current image's active-scope params, marks it dirty, drives the live preview, and (if gated) arms the debounce write. editParams() returns the ACTIVE scope's params (the renderer shows the active scope -- no mask/opacity compositing yet). SCOPE HEADER (Develop/Properties/scopeheader.cpp): the scope dropdown lives in a gradient band WIDGET above the tree (the same header gradient as TransformPanel; the old in-tree "Core" and "Layers" headers are gone). Layout: > Scope [dropdown v] [...] [E] (the band caption reads "Scope"; it was "Layer"/"Layers" before 2026-07-30). The '>' collapse arrow -> setTreeCollapsed hides/shows ONLY the scope's top items (the Core rows for Global, else the mask-tool rows -- the root rows above BasicHeader; applyScopeItemsCollapsed, re-asserted after each buildTree since a rebuild resets row visibility). The Basic/Color/Effects sections stay visible. [E] -> onScopePreviewToggled (whole-mask eye). The DROPDOWN carries ONLY the scopes (ScopeHeader::setScopes): the image's scopes, the active one marked with a checkmark -> onScopeSelected (switch activeScopeIndex; then reveal the scope's OWN section -- un-collapse its mask rows (setTreeCollapsed(false), bulk-guarded so Solo leaves Basic/Color/Effects as-is) and select its first mask so the coverage overlays the image, selectedMaskIndex 0 (Global has none, -1)). The scope ACTIONS live on a separate ellipsis menu button [...] (scopeMenuBtn) between the dropdown and [E], patterned on EmbelProperties::effectNewBtn -> effectContextMenu: clicking it pops a QMenu (ScopeHeader::showScopeMenu, built on demand) with "New mask" -> newScope (name dialog, default "Mask n") and "Reset all edits" -> resetAllEdits (see "Reset all edits" below); then, below a separator, the per-scope actions "Add to <mask>" -> showMaskMenu, a checkable "Show mask overlay" (shown only while a mask tool is expanded; toggles the red coverage tint, the same action as the "O" shortcut -- maskOverlayToggleRequested -> MW::toggleMaskOverlay, and its check state is pushed back from ImageView::maskTintVisibilityChanged -> setMaskOverlayShown), and Reset/Remove/Rename <scope> -> resetActiveScope / deleteScope / renameActiveScope. The per-scope group is OMITTED for Global (index 0) -- ScopeHeader::setGlobalActive, driven by updateMaskMenuBtn. MASK ENTRY POINTS: a scope's first mask tool is offered automatically when the scope is created (newScope -> showMaskMenu); further tools are added via the dropdown "Tweak <mask> mask" or the [+] glyph on each mask-tool row in the tree (UR_AddBtn, hit-tested in mousePressEvent -> showMaskMenu). The dropdown entry covers the case where the scope has no masks at all (skipped on create, or all deleted). DevelopProperties::bindScopeHeader connects these signals.
