TREE = ACTIVE SCOPE: the tree is REBUILT for the active scope by buildTree() (on image change, scope switch and mask add/remove/select): the scope's top items first (Global => the Edit-source selector + Demosaic + Denoise raw rows [formerly the Core header]; else the mask tool rows) followed by the Basic / Color / Effects sections. EDIT SOURCE (RAW files only, Global scope -- addCoreItems): the first Global top row is "Edit:" hosting an A/B radio pair "Raw" / "Embedded Preview" (a plain QWidget in the value cell of a DT_None row, recreated each buildTree). It is kept in sync with G::useRaw and the status-bar useRaw button BOTH WAYS: picking a radio -> onEditSourceChanged -> useRawRequested signal -> MW::toggleUseRaw (a private slot, hence the hop); the button -> toggleUseRaw -> DevelopProperties::syncEditRaw sets the radio (signals blocked) back. currentIsRaw() (MW::isFileRaw = suffix in metadata->hasJpg) gates the whole Core block -- non-raw files get no Core rows (they ARE the developable image). The Demosaic + Denoise raw rows show only when editing raw: applyCoreVisibility() (hide = !useRaw || scopeItemsCollapsed) runs LAST in buildTree (after applyScopeItemsCollapsed, which would otherwise re-show them) and again from setTreeCollapsed/syncEditRaw. Demosaic/Denoise raw are root leaves, so they carry UR_ExtraIndent (one extra indent level in the delegate's paint + sizeHint) to line up with the Basic sliders ("Temp"), which sit a level deeper as BasicHeader children; "Edit:" is left-aligned (not indented). buildTree close()s + removes all rows then re-adds them, preserving the sections' expand-state within a session (read from the current rows on rebuild). ACROSS SESSIONS the Basic/Color/Effects expand-state also persists in QSettings under "Develop/SectionExpanded/<Header>": the first build (no rows yet) seeds each header's default from sectionExpanded() (first-ever run => Basic open, Color/Effects closed), and QTreeView::expanded/collapsed -> persistSectionExpanded() writes the state whenever the user toggles one of the three headers (mask tool rows, which also expand/collapse, are filtered out by name). SECTION HEADERS: the Basic, Color, Calibrate, Color Grade and Effects headers show their plain section name only ("Basic", "Color") via updateSectionHeaderCaptions() -- the active scope is already shown selected in the scope list, so the old "Global: Basic" prefix was redundant. A header may be INDENTED to line up under the scope name it belongs to, while its child rows are NOT -- the sliders keep their full width. The indent is UR_ExtraIndent on the header CAPTION index, read by PropertyDelegate::paint: a bool means one indentation() level (the legacy dock), an int means that many pixels (the NESTED lab layout uses kMaskPanelIndent = 22, the same inset as the Mask panel; the FLAT and MINIMAL layouts use 0 -- see "EDITS LAYOUT A/B/C" below, where the tree is its own region and has no row above to line up under). Only the header's arrow + caption shift. They carry NO band at all (UR_HeaderFlat): they sit on the panel background, like the rows below them. The gradient marks a PANEL-level band (Raw, Scope, Transform), and a section belongs to the scope selected in the Scope band above it, so it is a tier below -- its grouping comes from the containment rail, not from banding. The fill is SKIPPED rather than drawn in a matching colour, so it stays exact in any theme (the view has already painted the row in the palette base). Set in DevelopProperties::addHeader for any header with a previewGroup; PropertyDelegate::paint reads UR_HeaderFlat from the CAPTION index (both column passes read the same sibling, so a value-column-only repaint does not put the gradient back). TREE INSIDE THE SCOPE LIST: the adjustment tree is not an independent strip under the dock -- it is a DETAIL widget of ScopeHeaderLab (setRowDetail(widget, slot, indent); slot EditsDetail follows slot MaskDetail, the Mask panel), so collapsing the "Edits" header hides the sections with the list. Whether it lands below the whole list, nested under the selected row, or under a single scope bar with no list at all is G::developEditsLayout -- see "EDITS LAYOUT A/B/C" below. The tree's detail indent is 0, so its left edge -- and any rail it paints -- coincide with the list's; the visible nesting comes from the section-header indent above. Since it sits inside the list the tree cannot scroll itself: DevelopProperties::setFitToContentHeight(true) turns off both scrollbars and makes sizeHint() = viewportSizeHint() (the bottom of the LAST VISIBLE row, hidden rows and collapsed sections already excluded) plus the closing rule's band. scheduleContentFit() re-fits, deferred + coalesced, from buildTree, expand/collapse, the setRowHidden callers (applyScopeItemsCollapsed / applyCoreVisibility, which emit no model signal), resizeEvent and a paintEvent backstop. wheelEvent is IGNORED in fit mode so the wheel reaches the dock's QScrollArea (the scope list + the nested tree scroll as one block; the action row, scopes strip, Transform, Replace and Raw panels stay pinned above it). MW::createDevelopDock builds that QScrollArea only for the lab UI; the legacy ScopeHeader path keeps the old stacked layout and fit mode stays off. minimumSizeHint in fit mode is (0, sizeHint().height()): the HEIGHT floor is the point (QAbstractScrollArea's own minimum would hold the block open above its content when every section is collapsed), but the WIDTH must stay free. Returning the content width there floors the scroll body -- which has no horizontal scrollbar -- at its pre-scrollbar width, so expanding enough sections to raise the vertical scrollbar clipped the value column and the eye buttons instead of reflowing. The tree has no intrinsic minimum width: the caption column is fixed and the value column stretches. EDITS LAYOUT A/B/C (G::developEditsLayout, an enum in global.h): HOW THE SCOPE IS PICKED and WHERE the editor block -- the Mask panel + the adjustment tree -- sits, switchable at run time from the Edits [:] menu ("Edits layout" submenu), so the candidates can be compared on real edits without a restart. FLAT (the default): the scope list is a fixed selector -- every row, uninterrupted -- and the editor sits BELOW the whole list, headed by its own band ("Editing: Mask 1"). The sliders therefore keep the same height whatever is selected, the whole list stays visible for A/B eye toggling, and nothing implies a scope owns an expandable subtree (there is only ever ONE tree -- buildTree repopulates it on every switch). NESTED: the editor is inserted directly under the SELECTED row, so it moves with the selection and the rows below it sit under the sections. This is the original lab layout. MINIMAL: no list at all. One bar -- "Scope [Global v] [+] [eye] [:]" -- stands in for BOTH the "Edits" header band and the rows, with the editor directly below it and NO containment rail. The combo carries every scope, [+] promotes New mask out of the menu it is buried in elsewhere, and the [eye] [:] pair acts on whichever scope the combo has selected. The submask list is hidden on Global exactly as in the other layouts (that is syncMaskPanel, not a layout rule). ScopeHeaderLab::rebuild() carries the difference: MINIMAL builds no rows at all, and the editor's insert position is rowsLayout->count() (flat and minimal) or active + 1 (nested). Everything else follows from the flag -- which band is up and which carries the header gradient (applyLayoutMode + paintEvent), the detail wrapper indents (setRowDetail remembers the NESTED indent per slot; applyDetailIndent drops it to 0 otherwise, there being no row above to line up under), the section-header caption indent (addHeader/UR_ExtraIndent: kMaskPanelIndent nested, 0 otherwise), the MaskPanel editor's caption split (maskPanelCaptionWidth) and the rail (see below). The switch is DevelopProperties::setEditsLayout, reached via ScopeHeaderBase::editsLayoutRequested: it re-runs buildTree (the headers' indent) then refreshScopeList (which re-runs rebuild, moving the block), and re-pushes the caption split to any OPEN submask editor. Menu emissions are deferred a tick like every other emission here -- the switch rebuilds the rows (or the bar) the menu is standing on. STICKY: persisted in QSettings as "developEditsLayout" (an int, written in MW::writeSettings next to "combineRawJpg", read in MW::loadSettings beside "decodeRawEngine" with the same validate-before-cast guard -- an out-of-range value falls back to the compiled default, and silently, because a wrong layout is only cosmetic). It is read in loadSettings rather than in the Develop dock because loadSettings runs BEFORE createDocks: the panel and ScopeHeaderLab then BUILD in the remembered layout instead of building in the default and being rebuilt. Drop the key in removeDeprecatedSettings when the flag retires. The scope bar and the editor band are both built ONCE in the ctor and stay in their layouts, hidden and 0 high where they do not apply, so a switch is a show/hide and never a re-parent. The editor band, like every band and row in this panel, ends with [eye] then [:], both acting on the ACTIVE scope. It is painted in the SAME G::selectionColor band the selected row carries, so the eye pairs the highlighted row with the sliders below by colour -- that is the binding the flat layout gives up by separating them. THE MINIMAL BAR'S [:] IS THE ONLY MENU in that layout (there is no header band), so showRowMenu appends "Reset all edits" and the layout picker to it when minimal is active -- otherwise the way back OUT of the layout would be unreachable. Those actions fire through their own connect and carry no data code, so showRowMenu's per-scope dispatch ignores them. The bar carries NO collapse arrow (removed 2026-08-20): the minimal layout has no scope list to hide, and the panel headers below it (Submasks, Basic, Color, ...) are always visible. applyLayoutMode expands a listCollapsed left over from another layout on entry, since nothing in this one could undo it. Retire the flag and delete the losing branches once the comparison settles. CONTAINMENT RAIL: a 3px G::selectionColor line down the panel's left edge (G::scopeRailX / G::scopeRailW; scopeRailW = 0 removes it) ties the sections to the scope they belong to. It brackets the SELECTED SCOPE ONLY -- its row plus its editor -- so an unselected scope row carries no rail: it is just a name until it is picked. In the FLAT layout the row and its editor are no longer adjacent, so ScopeHeaderLab paints TWO segments at the same x -- activeRowRect() and editorBlockRect() (the editor band down to the bottom of the last visible detail wrapper) -- and the unrailed rows between them ARE the statement: these two blocks belong together and the rows in between do not. The MINIMAL layout has NO RAIL, in either half. The rail's whole job is to pick the editor's owner out of a list of candidates, and there is no list: one bar sits directly above one editor. DevelopProperties::paintEvent also reverts its closing rule there to the ordinary panel separator (G::tabWidgetBorderColor, full width) -- with no rail to turn the corner into, a rail-coloured foot starting at the rail's right edge would be a stub of a line going nowhere. Otherwise it is drawn in TWO halves that meet at the widget boundary: ScopeHeaderLab::paintEvent runs it over activeBlockRect() when nested (the active row's geometry extended to the bottom of the last visible detail wrapper -- the Mask panel and the tree -- offset by rowsContainer's position), or the two segments above when flat, and DevelopProperties::paintEvent paints the tree's own stretch. In FIT mode that stretch ends at the BOTTOM OF THE CLOSING RULE -- the foot of the bracket -- and never runs past it, so the kBlockBottomGap clear space below separates this scope's block from the next scope row; in the legacy stacked dock it stops at the bottom of the LAST VISIBLE ROW (indexAt(0,0) then walk indexBelow -- NOT model row 0, which may be a hidden row, and indexBelow of a hidden index is invalid). Since the rail colour is the selected row's own band colour, the stretch crossing that row is invisible: the selection reads as a gap in the rail. NO SELECTION: the tree runs in QAbstractItemView::NoSelection (set in the DevelopProperties ctor, overriding PropertyEditor's SingleSelection). Its rows are ADJUSTMENTS, not a list to pick from -- nothing reads the selection (mask tools track selectedMaskIndex, a focused slider paints its own blue cue) -- and a selected row shows up only as an ARTIFACT: the app QSS "QTreeView::item:selected" background fills the row's INDENTATION strip, which QTreeView paints before the delegate runs and the empty drawBranches override deliberately leaves alone, so a solid blue tab appears left of the caption (over and around the containment rail). Stripping State_Selected in a drawRow override does NOT cure it -- QTreeView::drawRow re-derives the flag per cell from the selection model, so the fix has to be an always-empty selection model. The CURRENT index is unaffected: QAbstractItemView still sets it on click (with NoUpdate), so currentChanged -> onMaskSelectionChanged and keyboard navigation are unchanged. ONE BLOCK: the seam between the scope list and the tree is closed -- rowsLayout has no bottom margin, and DevelopProperties turns OFF the frame (setFrameShape + a widget-level "QTreeView { border: none; }", since the app QSS gives every QTreeView a 1px border). That border was both a seam and a 1px break in the rail; with it gone the viewport shares the widget origin, so both rail halves use the same x. The block's bottom is closed by a full-width rule kBlockCloseGap (0px) under the LAST ROW -- see PANEL SEPARATOR below, so the last section (Effects) is closed like every other panel. In the legacy (scrolling) tree it is drawn only when empty dock remains below it: if the rows fill or overflow the viewport, the panel edge already ends the block and a rule at the last visible row would be a false bottom. In FIT mode the viewport is exactly the rows plus the band sizeHint reserves for the rule AND for the kBlockBottomGap (2px) clear space under it, so it always draws -- starting at the RIGHT EDGE OF THE RAIL (G::scopeRailX + G::scopeRailW), not at x 0, and in G::selectionColor (the RAIL's colour, not G::tabWidgetBorderColor). Nested, that rule is the FOOT OF THE BRACKET around the selected scope, so it reads as the rail turning the corner rather than as a divider cutting across it. PANEL SEPARATOR: every Develop dock PANEL draws a G::panelBorderHeight (2px) rule in G::tabWidgetBorderColor across its bottom edge, so stacked panels read as distinct blocks. G::panelBorderHeight = 0 removes it everywhere. Carried by: the action row under the dock title (a QSS border-bottom on QWidget#developActionRow, ID-scoped so it misses the child buttons, 8px clear of them -- MW::createDevelopDock; the dock TITLE BAR's own 1px rule is switched off there via DockTitleBar::setBottomBorderVisible(false), since two rules that close together read as a mistake -- other docks keep theirs), the Scopes strip (histogram / vectorscope -- its fixed height is 160 + the rule, so the scopes keep their 160px), Raw, Mask, Transform and Fill Replace (each in its own paintEvent), and the tree's closing rule under the last row, which is the Effects section's bottom (DevelopProperties::paintEvent, see ONE BLOCK above). Each panel reserves the space as its outer layout's bottom margin, so the rule never overlaps content. TWO WIDGETS DELIBERATELY HAVE NONE: the legacy ScopeHeader (a band, not a panel), and the scope list (ScopeHeaderLab) -- it and the tree below it are ONE BLOCK joined by the containment rail, so a rule there would cut the rail and re-open the seam that block closes. EDITED MARKER: a section header whose group holds any non-default value reads "Basic *" (Basic / Color / Calibrate / Color Grade / Effects), so an edited panel is obvious while collapsed. The test is EditParams::groupIsDefault, which resetGroup's a COPY of the active scope's params and compares -- it can never drift from the group membership. DevelopProperties::updateSectionHeaderCaptions writes the captions; it is driven by paramsChanged (every adjustment) plus populateSlidersFromStack / refreshScopeList / buildTree, and caches each section's star state (hdrStar[], -1 = unknown after a rebuild) so a slider drag does not run five recursive findCaptionIndex searches per tick. UNIFORM HEADER CONTROLS (2026-08-19): EVERY header and row in the Scope/Edits panel ends with the SAME trailing pair -- an EYE (show or hide that thing's changes) and then a VERTICAL ELLIPSIS menu, with the menu always last. That covers the "Edits" band (its eye is the ACTIVE scope's, so band and selected row read alike), each scope row incl. Global (the eye replaced the leading show/hide checkbox, and Global now has a menu too -- New mask / Reset Global, the only actions that apply to it), the "Submasks" band (its eye hides every submask at once; the [+] add button stays ahead of the pair so the primary action is one click), each submask row, and each section header (Basic/Color/Calibrate/Color Grade/Effects, whose menu repeats the Preview + Reset items previously reachable only by right-clicking; Calibrate adds a third item, "Calibrate help", see the Calibrate panel section). The section pair is queued into the BarBtnEditor column in order (btns: eye, then menu), which right-aligns them, so "menu last" holds everywhere. THE PAIR LINES UP DOWN THE WHOLE PANEL: G::headerBtnGap (6) is the space between the two buttons and G::headerBtnRightInset (6) the space from the panel's right edge, used by the bands, the rows AND the tree's section headers. The tree can carry the gap because BarBtnEditor now reads a btnsSpacing global set alongside btns (drained and reset to 0 with them), so only the rows that ask for spacing get it -- the Embellish dock's button pairs stay tight. The section headers align because the delegate gives the editor the whole value-column rect, so BarBtnEditor's own right inset is measured from the same edge as the bands'. EVERY eye and menu emission is DEFERRED a tick (QTimer::singleShot(0)) because the handler rebuilds the rows or the tree and so destroys the very button the click is inside -- the checkboxes this replaced could emit inline only because the row survived on deleteLater. PREVIEW / RESET: each section header (Basic/Color/Effects) carries an eye BarBtn that toggles that group's Preview flag on the active scope; the whole-mask eye + reset live in the ScopeHeader widget (EditScope::showBasic/showColor/showEffects/showScope, all default true, persisted in the sidecar only when false). Preview is NON-DESTRUCTIVE: effectiveScopeParams() returns a COPY of the scope's params with any previewed-off group folded to identity (EditParams::resetGroup) -- stored values are kept, so re-showing restores them; showScope folds all three. The fold is applied at every render-capture site (EditStack::effectiveParams, DevelopProperties::editParams and stackJob). Right-clicking a header (contextMenuEvent) offers Preview (toggle) + Reset <group>; Reset is DESTRUCTIVE -- EditParams::resetGroup on the STORED params, then repopulate. The same menu (shown anywhere in the tree, not only on a header) also carries the tree-wide items Expand all / Collapse all / Solo mode, mirroring the Embellish dock: Expand all / Collapse all go through DevelopProperties::setAllSectionsExpanded, which calls QTreeView expandAll/collapseAll AND drives the Scope row (its collapse arrow lives in the ScopeHeader band, not the tree -- ScopeHeader::setCollapsed + setTreeCollapsed) and the Raw panel (RawPanel::setCollapsed). Solo mode is a checkable toggle (persisted as Settings "Develop/isSolo", restored in initialize via setSolo) that, when on, collapses the other top-level sections whenever one is expanded (PropertyEditor::collapseAllExcept in mousePressEvent). The two widgets that sit OUTSIDE the tree -- the Scope row and, in the lab UI, the Raw panel -- are Solo peers as well, and the base class cannot reach them, so DevelopProperties::soloCollapseOthers(owner) folds every peer except the one just opened. It is called from onSectionExpanded (a Basic/Color/ Effects header expanded), from setTreeCollapsed(false) (the scope's own items shown) and from the RawPanel::collapseToggled handler bound in bindRawPanel (the user expanded the Raw panel). RawPanel::setCollapsed is programmatic and emits nothing, so a fold cannot bounce back into the panel that triggered it; the state persists as "Develop/rawCollapsed". The Demosaic row is a scope item, NOT a Solo peer, so expanding it never collapses the scope. A bulk guard (isBulkExpandCollapse) keeps Expand all from tripping the Solo handler. For a RAW file being edited as raw (currentIsRaw() && G::useRaw) the menu also offers "Raw demosaic" -- captioned "Raw demosaic and denoise" when the engine is Winnow (that engine adds the raw-denoise rows). It is a navigation shortcut: DevelopProperties::showRawDemosaic activates the Global scope (onScopeSelected, the Demosaic/Denoise Core rows exist only there) and un-collapses the scope items so the rows are visible. In the lab UI those controls live in the Raw panel instead, so showRawDemosaic just un-collapses that panel (and folds its peers away under Solo). Group->keys mapping follows the UI (texture/dehaze are Basic; tone splits are Basic for Reset only; the sharpen* quartet and localDenoiseLuma/ localDenoiseChroma -- the Detail "Sharpening" (key "sharpenAmount") and "Denoise" (local NR, key "localDenoise") -- are in Detail, the denoise pair having MOVED there from Effects on 2026-08-20; denoiseLuma/denoiseChroma are the Global "Denoise raw" (decode-time global NR, key "denoise") and in no group). NOTE the migration: a scope's showDetail, not showEffects, now gates the local denoise pair. Old sidecars carry no showDetail key and paramsFromJson is forward-tolerant, so it defaults to true (visible) -- values are unaffected. The saved PRESET format did not change at all: presets store a flat map of EditParams field names and section titles only group the checklist UI, so no key was renamed or rescaled. eye.png/eye_off.png live in images/icon16. Transform has its own Preview eye + Reset in TransformPanel, wired to MW (previewToggled/resetRequested); its eye is a live result toggle, not a group fold (see the Transform section below). NAMING: index 0 is always the "Global" scope (named "Base" before 2026-07-30; normalized on load in setCurrentImage, so older sidecars re-label themselves).  Every OTHER scope is a MASK in the UI -- added with + and named "Mask 1", "Mask 2", ... (the new scope's number = its index), renameable to anything ("Sky", "Subject").  Global CANNOT be removed -- deleteScope refuses when the active scope is index 0 (popup "Global cannot be removed."). RENAME: the header's [R] button -> renameActiveScope pops a QInputDialog seeded with the current name; uniqueScopeName resolves clashes with a numeric suffix; Global (index 0) is rejected with a popup "Global cannot be renamed." An all-identity stack is never persisted, so empty scopes added to an unedited image are not written (EXCEPTION: a scope that has any mask component counts as non-identity so masks survive navigation). The old QSettings scope helpers (readLayerList / setCurrentLayer / layerRootPath / layerValue) were DELETED in the 2026-07-30 rename -- they had been dead since the stack moved per-image; presets use their own QSettings store.

  • MASK (UI increment; compositor + canvas overlay deferred): each NON-Global scope has ONE mask, built from an ordered list of tools -- editstack.h EditScope.components is now that tool list (MaskComponent: tool/op/inverted/feather + opaque paramsJson for per-tool geometry). Each tool ADDS or SUBTRACTS area; the first tool must Add (nothing to subtract from an empty mask). The combine field is legacy/unused. A scope's first mask tool is offered automatically when the scope is created (newScope -> showMaskMenu); Global (index 0) carries no mask. showMaskMenu pops a menu of "Add <tool> Mask" and (once a tool exists) "Subtract <tool> Mask" entries -- every tool name ends in "Mask" and the AI tools drop the "Select" prefix (maskToolName): Linear/Radial Gradient, Brush, Color/ Luminance Range, Subject/Sky/Background, Depth Range. ADD-MASK FLOW: newScope pops the "New scope" name dialog at the cursor (the [+] button just clicked, clamped to the screen) instead of screen-centre, and on accept immediately calls showMaskMenu -- a new scope exists to carry a masked adjustment, so choosing the mask tool is the next step (Escape leaves an empty scope). ADD-MASK PLACEHOLDER: a non-Global scope with no mask yet (e.g. Escape was pressed) shows a single "Add mask" row at the top of the tree with a [+] glyph -- a header-style UR_LeafSingleLine spanned row carrying only UR_AddBtn (no [-], no expand arrow), added by addAddMaskRow in addMaskItems. Clicking anywhere on it (it has no UR_MaskIndex; mousePressEvent identifies it by UR_Name "addMask") pops showMaskMenu. It disappears as soon as a tool is added -- the scope then shows its tool rows, and adding a tool selects it, so the placeholder is present precisely when no mask is selected. Each tool is a top-level tree row (caption "Add/Subtract <tool>" with a [-] remove button; the tools sit ABOVE the Basic/Color/Detail/Effects sections, added by addMaskItems in buildTree). The rows are header-style for the single-line full-width caption + expand arrow, but flagged UR_LeafSingleLine (new role) so the delegate paints them in the LEAF colour, not category teal. UR_ShowDecoration forces the delegate to draw the expand arrow even when the row has no children (an UNSELECTED tool carries no settings children), so every mask tool shows a closed disclosure arrow (open when selected and its settings show). Each tool row is a single FULL-WIDTH spanned cell (setFirstColumnSpanned) and the [-] remove glyph is DRAWN BY THE DELEGATE at the row's right edge (UR_DeleteBtn role), not a value-column button widget: a column-1 widget over the caption gets clipped/covered on expand (the caption's overflow into column 1 is outside the expand repaint's dirty region, and a covering index widget composites stale until a full repaint). mousePressEvent hit-tests the glyph rect (mirroring the delegate geometry) to delete; clicks elsewhere on the row toggle it. [Delegate general behaviour] PropertyDelegate::paint spans EVERY header caption (UR_isHeader) across the full row (caption + value columns). The delegate painter is NOT clipped to the cell (confirmed by probe: QPainter::hasClipping() is false during item paint), so a single column-0 pass draws the caption straight across both columns on a full repaint. The catch is PARTIAL repaints: dragging the dock splitter resizes the (stretch) value column, which marks ONLY the value-column cells dirty. QTreeView then repaints just those cells' background, erasing the caption overflow that was drawn during column 0's pass (column 0 is not re-run) - so a long header caption truncated back to the column boundary after a resize even though hasClipping()==false and the elided text was the full string. Fix: the caption is drawn in BOTH passes - the value-column pass fills its own background (gradient, or the bottom separator for non-gradient headers) and then redraws the SAME elided caption at the SAME viewport rect, so a value-column-only repaint restores it. capLeft must be identical in both passes; for a decoration row it is the column-0 cell left (view->visualRect(capIndex) .x()), reproducible without column 0's rect. The caption right edge is pulled in and the text elided when the value field is occupied: before the delegate-drawn [-] glyph (UR_DeleteBtn), or before a value-column widget (value sibling's UR_Editor geometry + childrenRect; e.g. the Scopes/Templates -/+ buttons, which are index widgets composited on top and so still show). setFirstColumnSpanned is still used by mask-tool rows (so column-1 is not painted at all) but is NOT required for plain headers. Clicking a tool toggles it: the SELECTED tool expands to show its settings as ITS OWN children (Feather slider, Invert checkbox) -- no duplicate tool-name row; only one tool is expanded at a time. Clicking the tool caption again collapses it (setSelectedMask(-1)), so there is no separate Done row. NB PropertyEditor::mousePressEvent does NOT select rows (only expand/collapse), so DevelopProperties overrides mousePressEvent to read the clicked row's UR_MaskIndex and toggle it (and returns without the base expand/collapse). All mask UI is isolated in developproperties.cpp (showMaskMenu/newMask/deleteMask/rebuildMaskTools/addToolRow/ setSelectedMask).

  • SPATIAL EDITING (Linear + Radial Gradient): edited directly on the loupe, not the dock. Geometry lives in MaskComponent.paramsJson as normalized coords: Linear {x1,y1,x2,y2}; Radial {cx,cy,rx,ry,angle} (cx/cy by W,H; rx by W, ry by H; angle deg). Resolution/zoom independent; survives proxy/full re-render. Handshake (wired in MW, mirroring paramsChanged): selecting a spatial tool fires DevelopProperties::maskEditBegin (tool,op,inverted,paramsJson,feather) -> ImageView::beginMaskEdit; deselecting fires maskEditEnd. updateMaskEdit() (end of rebuildMaskTools) tracks every image/scope/ selection change; developDockVisibilityChange() ends/re-asserts it so the overlay never leaks into the plain loupe. ImageView::drawForeground dispatches by tool to drawLinearMask / drawRadialMask, shown only while the cursor is over the view (maskHover via enter/leaveEvent). Linear: tint ramp + centre line + 0%/100% perpendicular guides + endpoint/centre handles; endpoint drag rotates+scales about the fixed centre, centre/line drag translates. Radial: elliptical tint (a unit-circle QRadialGradient mapped through a brush transform to the rotated image ellipse) + TWO outlines + centre + 4 axis handles; the solid outline is the nominal boundary (through the axis handles) and a dashed ellipse marks the HALF-COVERAGE radius (hidden when feather is 0). There is no "ramp starts here" circle: coverage falls from the centre outward (see MASK FEATHER PROFILE) and runs a little PAST the solid outline, so the tint's gradient brush is stretched to the profile's tMax rather than to the ellipse. Centre handle moves, axis handles resize each semi-axis (centre fixed), dragging the outline rotates. SHIFT + an axis-handle drag resizes PROPORTIONALLY: both semi-axes scale by the one factor the grabbed handle moved through, measured in frame pixels against the shape at grab time, so the ellipse keeps its aspect (and a circle stays a circle). Shift may be pressed or released mid-drag -- the anchor is the shape at mouse-down either way. The SAME proportional resize is also on the wheel / two-finger scroll with the cursor inside the CORE of the ellipse -- everything within the dashed half-coverage ring, not the feathered rim outside it (ImageView::maskRadialCoreContains -> adjustRadialSize: both semi-axes scaled by one multiplicative factor, centre and angle fixed, clamped to a 2 px floor and to four frame diagonals; emits through the shared live-geometry throttle as LiveEmit::Wheel, whose trailing emit carries the settled geometry since a wheel gesture has no release). Over the rim, or outside the ellipse, the wheel keeps zooming the image; Shift + wheel is the feather at any position; Space bypasses both. Tints/handles draw in viewport coords for constant on-screen size; a miss falls through to pan/zoom. Drag emits maskGeometryChanged LIVE (and on release) -> DevelopProperties::setActiveMaskParams (stores into the component, debounce-persists, no tree rebuild). Feather/Invert live-update via maskFeatherChanged / maskInvertChanged; feather is ALSO Shift + wheel / two-finger scroll on the canvas (ImageView::adjustMaskFeather -> maskFeatherRequested -> setActiveMaskFeather, which syncs the panel slider, notes the edit and re-composites -- the reverse of the slider path, so it sends no echo back to ImageView); the tint is drawn in the ONE overlay colour (ImageView::maskTintColor -> G::maskOverlayColor), the same colour as the result veil -- the op is read off the veil's OUTCOME and the op chip, never off the colour. That preview is ALWAYS the SELECTED tool. Invert flips (Radial: effect outside).

  • MASK FEATHER PROFILE (Develop/maskfalloff.h): ONE feather curve for every mask tool. Every tool used to taper with a smootherstep, which is flat-ended by construction -- coverage sits at exactly 1 across a core, ramps, then sits at exactly 0. Measured against Lightroom on the same frame (white image, radial mask, feather 82, exposure -3.0), recovering coverage from both screenshots through Winnow's own perceptual blend:

Winnow  smootherstep from 0.180R (== feather 82)  fit rms 0.002

  LR      GAUSSIAN, sigma 0.450R                    fit rms 0.024
          (best smootherstep fit to LR: k~0.02, still rms 0.086)

So Winnow's render was exactly per spec and the SHAPE was what differed: LR's coverage starts falling at the very centre (no flat core) and still carries ~4% at the boundary, dying by ~1.3R. Feeding a Gaussian through Winnow's blend reproduced LR's whole profile to ~3/255 levels, which also confirmed the blend space (kPerceptualMaskBlend) was already right. The family is a super-Gaussian in t, the normalised distance from full coverage (t=0) to the boundary (t=1):

  m(t) = 2^-((t/h)^p)     h = half-coverage radius, p = roundness
  h = 1 - 0.579f          p = (3.3 - 2.024f)/f       f = feather/100

p=2 is a true Gaussian, large p a near-hard edge, p<2 peaks harder with a longer tail. feather 82 gives (0.525, 2.0) -- the LR measurement -- and the rest of the map is the curve that makes the 10-90% transition width come out PROPORTIONAL to the feather (~0.92f) through that anchor, so the slider still behaves the way it reads and softness rises monotonically to 100. Feather 0 short-circuits to a hard step. THREE entry points, because the tools are not all the same shape: Lut (distance-to-boundary: Radial, Brush -- table-built once per component/dab, so the pow never enters a per-pixel loop), cdf (the Linear Gradient's two-sided S -- a Gaussian CDF, sigma = 0.25f, matched to the old smootherstep's 10-90 width so the ramp keeps its apparent width but gains tails past p1/p2), and taper (the fixed-band tools -- Luminance/Colour Range, Depth, Subject, Sky, Object -- where feather is a TOLERANCE band the caller has already scaled, so only the curve's character is shared: same half-coverage at mid-band, no flat start; NOT calibrated against LR, there is no reference capture for those). CONSEQUENCE: a mask now reaches slightly past its drawn outline, exactly as Lightroom's does, and overlay guides mark the HALF-COVERAGE radius -- the only radius the profile makes a promise about. Changed in place, so existing sidecars re-render with the new curve (the feather key itself is unchanged, 0-100). Still OPEN, tracked separately: LR reads exposure -3.0 on white as only -1.95 EV (its scene-referred tone curve), where Winnow applies a literal linear gain -- that gap is why the two frames still differ in DEPTH once the shape matches.

- TERMS: the MASK is what a (non-Global) scope applies; each SUBMASK is a building block folded into it. In code a submask is still a MaskComponent and the sidecar keys are unchanged -- Mask/Submask is UI + docs vocabulary only.

- RESULT VEIL (composite overlay): while a submask is being defined (selectedMaskIndex >= 0 on a non-Global scope), the loupe shows the ENTIRE mask -- all the scope's submasks composited -- as a single flat coverage tint in the ONE overlay colour (G::maskOverlayColor), and hides it when the panel closes. This is the TRUE resulting mask: alpha is the composite coverage ALONE (up to 150), so Subtract holes stay holes and the veil by itself answers "what does this scope affect?". MW::updateMaskOverlayTint (wired to maskEditBegin/End + paramsChanged) reuses the render-path buildMaskBuffer (so the tint is pixel-consistent with the render) at a capped resolution (~1600 px long edge), builds the AI/range refs it needs (cached), converts coverage -> premultiplied overlay colour, rings the pending submask's footprint (below), orients it to the displayed pixmap, and hands it to ImageView::setScopeMaskTint (clearScopeMaskTint when nothing is being edited). drawForeground paints the veil UNDER the active submask's handles, NOT hover-gated; when present the per-submask draw takes drawTint=false (its own tint would double up). The veil reflows live during a geometric drag. "O" (toggleMaskOverlayAction -> MW::toggleMaskOverlay -> ImageView::toggleMaskTint) HIDES/SHOWS the whole overlay -- flips maskTintHidden, gating BOTH veil and per-submask preview (handles/cursor keep drawing); reset to shown on each beginMaskEdit. Changing any adjustment slider while shown also hides it (maskTintHideRequested -> hideMaskTint) so the effect is visible; re-shown by "O", the scope menu's "Show mask overlay" row, or re-selecting a submask. Mask-only edits (Feather/Invert/Range/Brush) keep the overlay shown. COMMITTING A SUBMASK HIDES THE TINT: commitPendingMask emits maskTintHideRequested after paramsChanged, so the coverage clears the moment the submask is folded in and the user sees the developed result. The overlay stays LATCHED (maskLatched -> maskOverlayActive), so it is only hidden, not gone: "O" or the scope menu row brings the combined mask back. maskTintHidden is sticky, so selecting another scope with a mask re-shows it explicitly (onScopeSelected -> maskTintShowRequested -> ImageView::showMaskTint).

- DISABLED SUBMASKS ARE NOT VEILED: a submask whose row eye is off (MaskComponent::enabled == false) is dropped by the render (stackJob skips it), so updateMaskOverlayTint filters it out of the vector it hands buildMaskBuffer -- which composites what it is given and never reads the flag. Before the fix the veil advertised coverage the developed image did not have. The filter also drives the ensure*() reference builds, so an unchecked AI/range submask costs nothing, and the pending index is REMAPPED onto the filtered vector (it is an index into the unfiltered components). Hide the pending submask itself and it leaves the veil too -- no footprint ring, no op chip -- since it then contributes nothing; the tool's own live preview still draws, so editing it is not blind. Toggling the eye emits paramsChanged, which rebuilds both render and veil.

- ONE COLOUR, OUTCOME PREVIEW (how the op is shown): the overlay does NOT colour-code Add/Subtract/Intersect. Instead the PENDING submask (the one being defined, DevelopProperties::pendingMaskIndex) is composited into the veil with the op the held modifier is previewing (pendingMaskOp), so the veil shows the RESULT of what [Update]/Return would do: hold Opt and the veil retreats where that submask would be removed; hold Shift+Opt and it collapses to the overlap. That is strictly more informative than tinting the operand, and it is why one colour suffices. The op is MOMENTARY -- it is never written into the MaskComponent until commit. One ring is still drawn: a thin outline of the pending submask's footprint, in the overlay colour, painted ONLY where the result is absent (buf <= ~0.12), because a Subtract whose footprint misses the mask would otherwise change nothing on screen. The veil winning everywhere it covers also keeps a noisy content submask (Color/Luminance Range), whose per-pixel "edges" are everywhere, from fogging the subject.

- OVERLAY COLOUR: G::maskOverlayColor (default 220,40,40) is the single colour of the veil, the per-submask live preview (ImageView::maskTintColor) and the pending footprint ring. The action-row tint button's CONTEXT MENU picks it (red / green / blue / yellow / magenta / white -- red reads badly over a red subject) via DevelopProperties::setMaskOverlayColour, which writes "Develop/maskOverlayColor" and asks for the veil rebuild. The DevelopProperties ctor seeds G:: from that key. The palette itself is MaskPanel::overlayColours() (2026-08-15: the Mask panel's own swatch row was removed -- the overlay's appearance describes the veil, not a submask, so it belongs with the tint button, not inside the submask editor).

- OVERLAY GRAYSCALE (same context menu): no overlay colour reads well over every subject, so the other half of the answer is to take the colour out of the PICTURE. G::maskOverlayGrayscale (persisted as "Develop/maskOverlayGrayscale", seeded by the DevelopProperties ctor) makes the IMAGE ITEM paint itself grey: Qt has no saturation blend mode, so it cannot be laid over the top in drawForeground. ImageView::paintEvent evaluates maskGrayscaleActive() and pushes it to ScaledPixmapItem:: setGrayscale (no update() from inside a paint pass -- the view has already scheduled the repaint); the item blits a grey copy cached by pixmap().cacheKey(), so a Develop tick pays one Grayscale8 conversion at PROXY resolution and pans/zooms pay none. The veil, handles and legend are drawn after and keep their colour. VIEW effect only -- the render, the sidecar and the export are untouched. It follows the veil (hidden by "M"/"O" -> colour returns) and emits maskOverlayRepaintRequested, a repaint with NO veil rebuild (cheaper again than maskOverlayRefreshRequested). Mask tool ROW captions are NOT role-tinted: the caption already reads "Subtract Brush Mask", and a green/blue caption would re-introduce the colour key this design removed.

- COMBINE MODIFIERS (Opt = subtract, Shift+Opt = intersect): the three [Add]/[Subtract]/[Intersect] buttons are gone -- the MaskPanel has ONE commit button whose LABEL follows the held modifier (Update / Subtract / Intersect), so the words stay visible instead of hiding behind undocumented keys. Return commits too (MW::eventFilter, mirroring the Transform panel's Enter-commit); Esc still discards. ONE MAPPING, ONE PLACE: DevelopProperties::maskOpFromModifiers() reads the LIVE keyboard state, and everything goes through it -- the button label, the veil preview and the commit itself -- so they cannot disagree. MaskPanel::committed() carries no op; commitPendingMask() uses pendingOp -- WHAT THE BUTTON SAYS. It used to re-read the live modifiers at the instant of the commit, which meant releasing Opt to reach for [Update] committed an Add: the user painted a subtract, let go of the key, clicked the button and nothing changed. THE OP LATCHES ON ACTION (latchMaskOp, 2026-08-08): a modifier PRESS still previews momentarily, but starting to SHAPE the submask -- a brush/object stroke or a handle drag (ImageView::maskOpActionStarted) -- freezes the op held at that instant into latchedMaskOp, and a later key release falls back to THAT, not to Add. So the subtract you painted stays a subtract with your hands off the keyboard. Reversible by the same gesture: paint or drag again with nothing held and the latch re-reads as Add. Reset per submask. MW's filter tracks Key_Alt/Key_Shift press AND release (the veil must follow the key with the mouse stationary and the dock usually holds focus, the same reason Space and Esc are driven from there) and calls MW::syncPendingMaskOp -> setPendingMaskOp, which relabels the button and re-composites. THE OP DRIVES THE RENDER, NOT JUST THE VEIL: the pending submask is stored with op = Add until commit, so stackJob() substitutes pendingOp into its component (local copy -- nothing is written into the stored stack before the commit), exactly as updateMaskOverlayTint does for the veil, and setPendingMaskOp emits paramsChanged rather than a veil-only refresh. Without that the image kept showing an Opt submask being ADDED while the veil showed it being subtracted -- and over an already-masked area that is NO VISIBLE CHANGE, which reads as "subtract brush is broken". The Alt KeyPress is accepted so Windows cannot open the menu bar under us; ApplicationActivate re-syncs so a modifier released while the app was inactive cannot stick. The Return branch deliberately has NO value-editor bail (only a QLineEdit keeps the key): while a submask is being defined the focus is almost always on one of the panel's own sliders, which is exactly when Return must commit -- the same reasoning that puts Esc ahead of the editor bail in developShortcutIntercept. ERASE STROKE CANCELLED (G::useBrushEraseStroke, false, 2026-08-08): Opt now means Subtract EVERYWHERE, painting included -- one key, one meaning. The erase stroke was there because the effect only appeared on stroke release, so erasing inside the submask was the only way to fix a stroke you could not yet judge; the effect now re-renders DURING the swipe, so a mis-paint is visible as it happens (correct it with Cmd+Z, or commit and subtract). The whole erase path is intact behind the flag -- maskBrushErase, the per-stroke "erase" JSON flag, BrushStamp's erase composite, the pink cursor -- so set it true to restore; delete both once this proves out. THE RULE IT REPLACES, still accurate with the flag TRUE: Opt takes away -- from the STROKE while painting a Brush or Object submask (maskBrushErase), from the MASK otherwise. WITH ONE CLAUSE: a stroke with nothing to erase is not an erase. While the submask has NO coverage yet (maskBrushStrokesJson empty) Opt keeps meaning subtract and the stroke paints normally -- otherwise the natural gesture for a subtract brush ("hold Opt, paint what to remove") would paint nothing, leaving an empty submask whose Subtract is arithmetically a no-op (m *= 1 - 0) and a commit that silently changes nothing. That was a real bug, found by probe: the whole op/preview chain was correct and the veil simply could not change. From the second stroke on, Opt erases as it always has. Consequently only an ERASE stroke owns Opt: ImageView::maskStrokeIsErase() (not merely maskStrokeInFlight) is what drops the previewed op back to Add for the stroke's duration, so an Opt paint on an empty submask keeps previewing the subtraction while it is drawn. ImageView emits maskStrokeStateChanged on both edges of a stroke (AFTER maskBrushErase is decided) and MW re-reads the modifiers there. The FIRST submask has nothing to combine with, so it is pinned to Add and the hint is hidden.

- OP INDICATOR (on-canvas chip): while the overlay is shown ImageView::drawMaskLegend paints a compact chip top-left naming what the commit button/Return will do -- "Add: Brush Mask", "Subtract: Brush Mask", "Intersect: Brush Mask" -- over a dim reminder line "Opt subtract . Shift+Opt intersect" (suppressed on the first submask). It replaces the old colour-key legend, which named a RED/GREEN/BLUE scheme that no longer exists. MW pushes it via ImageView::setMaskLegend(submaskName, op, showHint) (name from DevelopProperties::maskToolName, public for this).

- SUBMASK BUILD-UP (MaskPanel): every submask -- including the first -- is PENDING from the moment its type is picked (beginMaskTool appends it and sets pendingIdx) until the commit button/Return folds it in with the op the BUTTON shows (commitPendingMask) or Esc/[x] removes it (cancelMaskTool). The button is labelled by that op plus the act -- "Add and Commit", "Subtract and Commit", "Intersect and Commit". It was once just "Update", which stopped making sense when the render went live: with the outcome already on screen the word read as "refresh the view" rather than "fold this submask in". The button is NOT a render trigger; it is the commit boundary -- it writes the op into the component, ends the pending session, names the history entry and makes the submask persistent (a pending submask is discarded when the image is left, so it never reaches the sidecar). There is ONE commit path: the old "first submask commits immediately / later ones preview" split, and finishFirstMask with it, are gone -- with outcome preview in one colour the first submask looks identical either way.

- THE MASK PANEL IS NESTED UNDER ITS MASK (2026-08-13): the MaskPanel is no longer a transient strip above the Scopes list. It is a CHILD of the active mask's row in the Edits list (ScopeHeaderLab::setRowDetail, indented by kDetailIndent = 22, the same inset the scope captions now carry) and is up whenever a MASK scope is active, not only while a submask is being built:

  Mask 2                      [eye] [:]     <- the scope row
  |  v Mask                        [:]      <- MASK level band (collapses)
  |    Edge      -----o-----                  <- levelEditor
  |    Halo      -----o-----
  |  v Submasks           [+] [eye] [:]     <- SubmaskList (collapses)
  |    (+) Linear Gradient     [eye] [:]
  |    (-) Brush           *   [eye] [:]     <- open for editing
  |    Feather   -----o-----                  <- embedded MaskEditor (submask)
  |    Invert    [ ]
  |    Edge      -----o-----
  |    [    Add and Commit    ]               <- "Done" when re-editing

  ScopeHeaderLab::rebuild() destroys and re-makes every row on each

setScopeRows, so it SKIPS the detail wrappers when it drains rowsLayout (QLayout::takeAt removes a widget from the layout without re-parenting it, so they stay children of rowsContainer) and re-inserts them, in slot order, after the active row -- the details are owned by the caller and must never be taken down with the rows. They are deliberately NOT re-parented on the way through: the adjustment tree is a detail too (slot EditsDetail), and re-parenting a QTreeView full of setIndexWidget editors on every scope switch is the flicker-prone, expensive path. The panel's own "Mask: <tool>" header band is gone: the scope row above it and the selected submask row already say it. The tree carries NO mask rows in the lab UI at all (addMaskItems returns immediately) -- the old "Add mask" placeholder row is replaced by the Submasks section's own [+].

- SUBMASK LIST (Develop/Properties/submasklist.h, SubmaskList): the mask's components IN FOLD ORDER, and the only way back into one. Built on the ScopeHeaderLab pattern -- a gradient header band, a rows container rebuilt wholesale by setSubmasks(rows, selected), and EVERY emission DEFERRED a tick (QTimer::singleShot(0)) because the handler loops back through setSubmasks and deletes the very row widget the click is inside. Per row: an OP CHIP that cycles Add/Subtract/Intersect, the tool name, then the standard trailing pair -- an EYE (MaskComponent::enabled -- the fold skips a disabled component, so it is a one-click A/B) and a [:] menu (Edit / op / Invert / Move up / Move down / Duplicate / Delete). The band carries the same pair: its eye reads "shown" while ANY submask still contributes and hides them all in one click (emitted per row, since the owner has no bulk slot), and its menu offers Add submask / Show all / Hide all. DevelopProperties owns the model side: reopenSubmask, setSubmaskEnabled, setSubmaskOp, toggleSubmaskInverted, moveSubmask, duplicateSubmask and deleteMask -- each mutates, fixes up selectedMaskIndex/pendingIdx, noteEdit()s, buildTree()s and emits paramsChanged(). syncMaskPanel() at the end of buildTree() is the single refresh point, so the list tracks image, scope and mask changes without any caller remembering to update it. COLLAPSING the section (header arrow or a click on the band) hides the selected submask's SETTINGS as well as the rows: SubmaskList emits collapsedChanged and MaskPanel::syncAttrVisible() takes the whole attribute block (scope label, MaskEditor, commit row) down with them, since those settings belong to a submask inside the collapsed list. Starting a new submask (beginPending) re-expands the section, so a pending edit can never be stranded behind a closed header. The section STARTS COLLAPSED (SubmaskList::collapsed defaults true, honoured in buildUi): most masks hold one or two submasks, so the list is noise until the user goes looking for it, and the one path that needs it open -- adding a submask -- opens it.

- EDGE (grow/shrink, 2026-08-23): TWO sliders, both -100..100 and both "1 unit = 1 pixel OF THE FULL-RESOLUTION IMAGE" -- MaskComponent::edge on each submask, and EditScope::maskEdge on the mask as a whole. Positive dilates the boundary, negative erodes it. WHY IT IS NOT FEATHER: feather SOFTENS an edge, it does not MOVE one, and it is not a pixel distance for any tool (a fraction of a radius on Radial, of the p1->p2 length on Linear, of a value band on the Range tools, of an alpha threshold band on Subject/Sky/Object). So there was no way to say "this AI cutout lands two pixels inside the subject, push it out two pixels" -- Photoshop's Select > Modify > Expand/Contract. On the Range tools a small negative Edge is also the cheapest way to kill the speckle content thresholding always produces, and a small positive one closes pinholes. The two compose: submask +5 inside mask -3 nets +2.

RESOLUTION INDEPENDENCE is the whole contract, so buildMaskBuffer takes a pixelScale (this render's long edge / the FULL-RES long edge) and converts before doing anything: src.renderScale at the composite call, developProxy->renderScale at the veil (renderScale COMPOUNDS against the original full res, so deriving bw/mw instead would disagree whenever the cached WorkingImage is itself capped), and sc*work->renderScale on the veil's 1600-cap branch. At a ~0.3 proxy a 1-unit nudge is a 0.3 px radius, which is why MaskEdge interpolates fractional radii instead of rounding them (a rounding kernel would make the slider move in visible jumps of 3 and the preview disagree with the settle).

MECHANISM (Develop/maskedge.h, header-only, tests/unit/tst_maskedge.cpp): grayscale morphology, because dilating by a flat structuring element TRANSLATES a soft edge rigidly -- the feather profile rides along instead of being reshaped. The structuring element is an OCTAGON, square(a) (+) diamond(b) with a = r(sqrt2-1), b = r(2-sqrt2), decomposed into FOUR separable 1-D passes (horizontal, vertical, and the two diagonals at strides w+1 and w-1, the diamond being the Minkowski sum of its two diagonal segments). A plain separable square would have been half the cost and was rejected: it displaces a 45-degree boundary by r*1.414, a 41% error against a slider whose contract is "1 unit = 1 pixel", and it would have put the analytic tools and the buffer tools 41% apart on the SAME diagonal edge. The octagon is exact on the axes AND the diagonals and overshoots at most 8.2% at its vertices. Each pass is van Herk / Gil-Werman (forward prefix + backward suffix extremum over blocks of the window width), so cost is O(1) per pixel REGARDLESS of radius -- Edge +-100 costs what +-1 costs. Samples are uint16 (MaskFalloff::kCutoff is 1/512, so 16 bits is ~7 bits finer than the profile promises) and every pass is IN PLACE: a line is gathered into a per-thread scratch, processed and written back, so no image-sized temporary is ever needed.

TWO ANALYTIC TOOLS, EIGHT BUFFER TOOLS. Linear Gradient and Radial Gradient fold Edge into their GEOMETRY in parseMaskComp and never touch a buffer. Linear precomputes tShift = edgePx * |grad t|, which is EXACT (dilating a ramp monotone along its normal by a disc is precisely a translation). Radial adds edgePx to both semi-axes -- exact for a circle, the standard ellipse approximation otherwise (zero error at the four vertices) -- and its feather rides the radius rather than translating, deliberately: a radial's feather has always BEEN a fraction of its radius, so Edge behaves exactly like dragging the radius handle, which is the least surprising thing it can do there. The other eight (Brush, Luminance/Colour Range, Depth, Subject, Sky, Background, Object) are materialized and morphed. BRUSH DELIBERATELY DOES NOT take the "free" route of growing each dab radius (dilation distributes over union, so it would be exact for grow): it changes the brushRasterCached key, so every tick would re-run BrushStamp::rasterize over every stroke -- the exact 275 ms the LRU exists to avoid; it grows INSIDE an auto-mask stroke's confinement when the user means to push past it; and dab feather is a fraction of dab radius, so +1 then -1 would not return. The AI tools morph their per-component w*h buffer, never the ref: Subject/Sky/Depth refs are SHARED per-path across components, so morphing one would corrupt every component sharing it.

THE FOLD IS SEGMENTED, not restructured. buildMaskBuffer's fold is a per-pixel scalar loop with no neighbourhood access, so an Edge-bearing component cannot be evaluated inline. Maximal runs of edge==0 components keep the ORIGINAL interleaved fold verbatim (a mask nobody has run the slider on costs exactly what it did before); each edge!=0 component becomes its own single-component segment -- materialize (its `inverted` applied, which is what puts invert BEFORE the morphology so growing an inverted mask shrinks the original), morph, fold with its op. ONE uint16 scratch serves every such component AND the mask-level pass: at 45 MP that is 90 MB held once instead of 180 MB of float PER SUBMASK, which on a 22-submask scope is the difference between a transient allocation and an OOM. Turning the whole fold into one-pass-per-component was rejected for the opposite reason: 22 read-modify-write passes over 45 MP is ~8 GB of traffic where there is 360 MB today. The per-tool eval switch was hoisted into evalCompAt() so the inline fold and the materializer cannot drift.

THE MASK-LEVEL PASS RUNS AFTER THE PREFIX SNAPSHOT, never before -- the prefix is a PARTIAL fold, and morphing it would hand every later resume a shape that is not a prefix of anything. It also has to run on the EMPTY-comps early return, because dragging that slider changes no component signature, so a fully-resumed prefix is exactly the path it takes every tick. CACHE KEYS: MaskComponent::edge goes into maskComponentSignature (which keys both the fold-prefix cache and the per-scope mask cache), and EditScope::maskEdge is appended by maskComponentsSignature(components, scopeEdge) -- it belongs to no component, so leaving it out would make the mask-level slider move nothing at all.

UI: the per-submask row is in MaskEditor::showTool (every case including default:, so all ten tools have it) handled by onMaskEditorSetting; the legacy tree mirrors it in addToolRow / itemChange. The MASK-level row is a SECOND MaskEditor (MaskPanel::levelEditor(), populated by showMaskLevel(), key "scopeMaskEdge", handled by onMaskLevelSetting) sitting ABOVE the Submasks row, directly under the scope row -- not a row inside the first editor, because that one is rebuilt per selected submask and hides when none is, whereas this must persist. Above the Submasks HEADER rather than under the list because it acts on the whole mask, so it reads as a property of the mask itself rather than of anything in the list; that also puts it OUTSIDE the collapsible section, so unlike the submask settings block it is NOT hidden when Submasks is collapsed, and it stays anchored under the scope row instead of shuffling as the list grows. Its only visibility rule is that the mask has at least one submask (nothing to grow otherwise). History labels are "Edge" and "Mask edge" so the two levels are distinguishable.

THE MASK-LEVEL ROWS SIT UNDER THEIR OWN "Mask" BAND (2026-08-24, MaskPanel::buildMaskLevel): collapse arrow + caption + [:], built from the same idiom as the Submasks band below it (translucent band, gradient painted by the parent's paintEvent, band click toggles collapse) so the two read as sibling sections. Bare, the sliders read as belonging to whichever neighbour the eye landed on first, and EDGE APPEARS TWICE IN THIS PANEL -- once here for the folded mask, once in a selected submask's settings -- with nothing on screen to say which was which. The band's paintEvent lives on MaskPanel rather than on a SubmaskList-style widget of its own and maps the band rect with mapTo(), because the band is a GRANDCHILD here (levelWrap > levelBand) where SubmaskList's is a direct child; levelWrap, levelBand and levelBody are all WA_TranslucentBackground or the app stylesheet's opaque QWidget fill would paint over that gradient. It opens by default (the two sliders ARE the section, unlike the Submasks list, which is noise until it is wanted) and its collapse is view state only -- nothing about it is persisted. NO BAND EYE, breaking the panel's usual eye-then-[:] pair on purpose: Edge and Halo reshape the mask rather than contribute to it, so there is nothing to show or hide, and both already have a neutral value reachable from the [:] menu ("Reset mask Edge and Halo" -> DevelopProperties::resetMaskLevel, which zeroes the pair on the active scope and leaves each submask's own Edge alone -- flattening the parts would be a far bigger edit than the menu says). The menu's last item is "Mask help", opening the SUBMASKS page (submasksHelp): Edge and Halo are documented there, and one mask page beats two half ones. The footprint ring (updateMaskOverlayTint's second buildMaskBuffer call) is passed scopeEdge 0 on purpose: it tracks the submask being dragged, not the assembled mask. KNOWN GAP: the loupe's gradient/radial HANDLES draw from the stored geometry, so a Radial at Edge +40 renders larger than its drawn outline.

- HALO (halo suppression, 2026-08-24): ONE slider, 0..100, at MASK level only (EditScope::maskHalo, panel key "scopeHalo"). It answers the complaint Edge and Feather cannot: when a mask boundary does not land on the subject's real edge, a masked tone/colour adjustment lifts a RIM OF BACKGROUND and reads as a glow around the subject. Feather sets the WIDTH of the transition band, not its position; Edge slides the whole boundary a uniform distance, so wherever the mask's error is not uniform it trades a halo on one side for a bitten edge on the other. Halo instead FADES THE ADJUSTMENT OUT ACROSS the picture's edge, leaving the boundary where the user put it. Mask level only because a halo is a property of the FINAL folded boundary, and because it costs one pass per scope rather than per submask. DISABLED, with the reason in the row's tooltip, on an all-gradient mask (maskHasRealEdge in developproperties.cpp): the two gradients are pure geometry with no relationship to anything in the image, so there is no edge to work from.

IT IS AN ALPHA OPERATION, which is why it lives in buildMaskBuffer and not in the compositor. Compositing is a per-pixel lerp (acc = acc*(1-m) + scope*m, WorkingImageCache::renderStack), so "weaken the adjustment near the edge" is arithmetically identical to "multiply the alpha near the edge". Doing it on the alpha keeps a mask a PURE FUNCTION OF ITS COMPONENTS -- which is exactly what the per-scope render cache assumes.

THE GUIDE IS THE UNDEVELOPED WORKING IMAGE (buildHaloGuide in mainwindow.cpp), perceptual luma (pow 1/2.2, the same encoding the mask BLEND uses), built ONCE per render tick and shared by every scope. Two deliberate choices. It is the working image and not the developed result because a guide derived from the develop output would move every time a slider moved -- the mask would reshape itself in response to the very adjustment it is masking, which is the oscillation the content-range masks avoid by keying off the base only. It is not RangeMask::RangeRef because that is fixed at ~1024 px: at full-res settle and export the alpha would snap to a blurry guide and the preview would stop matching the render. developCompositeStack already holds the WorkingImage it sizes the mask from, so the guide is on EXACTLY the mask's grid -- no mapping, no resampling.

MECHANISM (Develop/maskhalo.h, header-only, tests/unit/tst_maskhalo.cpp). The mask's own boundary NEIGHBOURHOOD is found by blurring the alpha and taking 4*b*(1-b) -- 1 where the blurred mask is halfway (the boundary), 0 into the solid interior and the clean exterior. "Wherever 0 < alpha < 1" was tried first and does nothing at all on a crisp mask, where that band is one pixel wide. The guide's Scharr gradient is then spread over the reach radius by a LOCAL MAX, not a blur: Scharr responds over three pixels, so the raw magnitude is a hairline that cuts a two-pixel notch at the boundary and leaves the whole rim beyond it fully adjusted, which is not a halo fix; dilating answers the question that actually matters, "is there a committed edge within reach of this pixel", without a blur's attenuation of the peak. Then alpha *= 1 - amount*edge*band. THE SLIDER IS REACH: how far out a halo can be and still be found, which is what varies between pictures. The damping BAND width is FIXED (kDampBandPx), not driven by the slider, because widening it would eat further and further into the subject -- the failure this approach is prone to.

REFINE WAS BUILT, TESTED AND REJECTED (2026-08-24), and the alternative is recorded because it is the obvious thing to reach for again. Instead of fading the adjustment, MOVE the alpha: guided-filter the folded mask against the picture (He et al. -- SegRefine::guidedFilterGray, Utilities/segrefine.h, which the Subject/Sky/Depth/Object cutouts still use) so the boundary snaps onto the real edge, then pinch about 0.5 to kill the residual fringe. It is the more principled fix on paper and it passed its displacement tests: a boundary 6 px out was pulled to within 2 px, monotonically in the slider, with preview matching render across a 0.4 proxy. IN THE LOUPE IT BLURRED THE HALO RATHER THAN REMOVING IT -- the rim softened and spread instead of going away. That fits the mechanism: a guided filter reconstructs the alpha as a local linear function of the guide, and a wide window over a soft, low-contrast boundary (fur, hair, foliage -- exactly where masks miss) has no sharp answer to converge on, so it returns a smooth ramp. Damping never has to decide where the edge IS, only that one is near, which is a far easier question of the same data. NOTE FOR THE TEST SUITE: the synthetic step edge the unit tests use is precisely the case Refine was good at, which is why the tests passed and a photograph caught it. Do not re-add the guided-filter path without a real picture it beats damping on.

ORDER, CACHE, VEIL. Halo runs immediately AFTER applyScopeEdge on both of buildMaskBuffer's exit paths: Edge is a deliberate uniform offset and Halo then works from the picture's edges against that offset boundary, whereas morphing afterwards would smear the result. Like Edge it runs after the fold-prefix snapshot, never before. maskComponentsSignature takes the amount alongside scopeEdge -- leave it out and the slider moves nothing, because the cached buffer is still keyed as current. The loupe veil passes the same value (activeScopeMaskHalo) and builds its guide from developProxy, so the tint shows the mask the render composites; on the capped fallback path (no proxy for this file yet) there is no working image at the veil's dimensions, so it draws the UNSUPPRESSED mask for that moment rather than a guide resampled off the wrong grid.

RETIRED SIDECAR KEY. Both algorithms shipped briefly behind a method combo, so sidecars written on 2026-08-24 may carry "maskHaloMode" (0 = Refine, 1 = Damp, 2 = Both) on a scope. It is no longer read or written and fromJson ignores it, but the NAME must never be reused for anything else -- see "Stale Presets and History (Schema Drift)". A maskHalo value dialled in while Refine was selected now renders as damping, which is a deliberate one-off break confined to that day's test images.

A DEAD BUG SURFACED BY THIS: parseMaskComp returned valid=false for a zero semi-axis and the caller does `if (!g.valid) continue`, DROPPING the component. Dropping is not the same as an empty component -- under Intersect an empty component must zero the mask, under Subtract it must be a no-op. Authoring rx=0 is unreachable from the UI so the path was dead; with Edge, shrinking a small radial to nothing is an ordinary drag. Now clamped to a 1e-3 px epsilon instead.

- COMMITTED SUBMASKS RE-OPEN (2026-08-13): commitPendingMask() used to set selectedMaskIndex = -1 ("flatten: committed submasks don't re-open"), which made the mask a WRITE-ONLY PILE: a committed submask could not be re-shaped, re-tuned, disabled, reordered, re-op'd or even deleted -- deleteMask(int) existed and was correct but had no reachable caller. The data model always supported all of it (enabled/op/inverted/feather/ paramsJson per component, order = array order, all already in the sidecar); only the UI threw it away. Now commit ends the PENDING session and nothing else, and clicking a row calls reopenSubmask(): selectedMaskIndex moves, pendingIdx goes to -1, the settings load into the panel's MaskEditor and buildTree()'s updateMaskEdit() re-arms the ON-CANVAS overlay (gradient handles, brush buffers, range swatches) -- that last part needed no new ImageView work, because maskEditBegin already fires for any selectedMaskIndex across all ten tools. Every mask mutator (setActiveMaskParams, setActiveMaskFeather, setActiveBrushSize, onMaskEditorSetting) already writes through selectedMaskIndex, so they work on a re-opened submask unchanged. TWO STATES, because a submask is only fragile while it is NEW: . PENDING (pendingIdx >= 0): discarded by Esc/[x] or by leaving the image; op comes from the held modifier (latched); the button reads "<Op> and Commit"; leaving the scope prompts (confirmPendingMask). . EXISTING (pendingIdx < 0, selectedMaskIndex >= 0): its edits are already real, so the button reads "Done" (deselect), the [x] is hidden, Esc just closes it, and MODIFIERS ARE INERT -- setPendingMaskOp returns early, since a stray Opt press rewriting a committed submask's op would be an invisible edit. Its op is changed on its own row instead.

THE ROW IS A TOGGLE (2026-08-24): clicking the row of the submask that is ALREADY open closes it -- reopenSubmask's early `if (index == selectedMaskIndex) return` now calls closeSubmaskEditing() instead, the block lifted verbatim out of commitPendingMask's pendingIdx < 0 path (deselect, keep the mask latched, buildTree to take the handles off the canvas, hide the tint), which "Done" still uses. The row is the list's only disclosure affordance, so a row that opens must also close, like every other disclosure in the dock. PENDING IS EXEMPT: closing a submask that is still being built would take away the commit button it needs. The click does not silently do nothing there -- a dead row reads as broken -- it pops up "<tool> has not been committed yet, so its settings have to stay open. Return (or the button below) folds it into the mask; Esc discards it." (G::popup, 3 s). That is a deliberate exception to the dock's usual "grey the control and say why inline" rule: the row is not a control that can be greyed, and there is nowhere inline to put the reason. The row [:] menu emits the SAME signal, so its first item is relabelled "Close <tool> settings" when that row is the open one -- an "Edit" that closed the settings would be a lie -- and it stays ENABLED while pending so it reaches the same explanation rather than greying out unexplained.

- BRUSH ATTRIBUTES HAVE THREE SCOPES, AND THE PANEL SAYS WHICH: size, feather, flow and auto-mask are BAKED INTO EACH STROKE as it is drawn (ImageView writes them into the stroke object, BrushStamp reads them back per stroke), which is what lets one submask hold strokes of different sizes. The cost is that the same slider can mean three different things, and a slider whose effect you cannot predict reads as broken:

. ADDING (pendingIdx >= 0, the submask is still being built) -> the NEXT stroke (brushWith), the original behaviour; it is how a submask gets strokes of different sizes. . ADDING + SHIFT, with a stroke already painted -> the LAST STROKE only (brushLastStrokeWith). "I just painted that, make it softer" -- the common correction. The component's next-stroke default is deliberately NOT touched, so the next stroke still comes out as armed. . SELECTED (pendingIdx < 0, re-opened from the submask list) -> EVERY

stroke (brushStrokesWith). Nothing is being painted, so there is no next stroke to arm and no "last" one to single out; a slider that only armed would move and change nothing at all. Invisible while a committed submask could not be re-opened; a dead end now that it can. SHIFT IS DELIBERATELY INERT HERE -- it belongs to the act of painting.

ATTRIBUTE SCOPE HEADER (MaskPanel::setAttributeScope, text from DevelopProperties::maskAttributeScopeText): a dim caption row just above the settings naming the state in force, in exactly those words -- "Attribute adjustment applies to 7 strokes" (SELECTED, singular at 1), "... to next stroke" (ADDING), "... to last stroke" (ADDING + Shift), or for a non-stroke tool "Attribute adjustment applies to this Linear Gradient submask". It is LIVE: MW's modifier filter calls syncAttributeScopeLabel() on every Shift press and release, so the header names the scope the next drag will use BEFORE the drag happens. A stroke tool with nothing painted yet always reads "next stroke" -- naming a last stroke that does not exist would be a lie.

The three-way split is applied in onMaskEditorSetting (the panel rows). The CANVAS gestures -- setActiveBrushSize ([ ]), setActiveMaskFeather (Shift+wheel) and setActiveBrushAutoMask -- take the pending/existing split but NOT the Shift branch: Shift+wheel already means feather there, so a second meaning for the same key could not be resolved. Per-stroke VARIATION is lost the moment a SELECTED brush's slider is touched -- every stroke takes the new value. That is the trade accepted for a committed brush being tunable at all; Cmd+Z restores it, and Shift while still painting is the way to fix one stroke without flattening the rest.

MW's modifier/commit block is gated on isSubmaskOpen() (any open submask), not isMaskPanelOpen() (a PENDING one), so Return/Esc and this header follow a re-opened submask too; syncPendingMaskOp keeps the op preview itself

  pending-only.

ORDER IS THE RESULT (moveSubmask): components fold sequentially, so a Subtract placed before the Add it was meant to cut into does nothing. The fold-prefix cache resumes at the longest matching prefix of per-component signatures, so a move/delete/toggle at index k invalidates [k..n) and keeps [0..k) with no cache work at all. No sidecar change either: enabled, op, inverted, feather, params and the array order were always written.

  • LEAVING A PENDING SUBMASK (confirmPendingMask): picking another scope, or adding one, used to discard the pending submask SILENTLY -- minutes of brushwork gone with no warning, the MaskPanel being the only cue that anything was outstanding. Both paths now route through DevelopProperties::confirmPendingMask(). It names the tool and the scope ("The Brush submask on "Mask 2" has not been committed.") and offers Cancel / Commit / Discard -- one word each, so the body text spells out what all three DO, the Commit line carrying the previewed op ("Commit -- subtract it from the mask, then continue. / Discard -- throw the submask away and continue. / Cancel -- stay on "Mask 2" and keep building it."). Discard and Cancel are the pair a user can read backwards and lose the work, which is why neither stands on its label alone. It is a hand-built QDialog, NOT a QMessageBox: a message box orders its buttons by ROLE, per platform, and pads them unevenly (mac banishes the destructive one to the far left). Here the order is fixed left-to-right, the three buttons are equal width (Expanding) with one 12px spacing between them, Discard is the default (Return) and Esc leaves the choice at Cancel. Cancel abandons the transition and the caller re-asserts the header selection (refreshScopeList), since the clicked row has already moved. It asks only when there is WORK TO LOSE: pendingMaskIsUntouched() drops the submask silently (as leaving always did) when committing it would achieve nothing -- it covers no pixels (Brush or Object with an empty stroke list, Color Range with no samples, so picking the tool or dragging its size slider does not count) or it is still byte-identical to what beginMaskTool built (pendingPristine), which is the centred-gradient case: a real mask, but not one the user made. Anything that misses both tests prompts -- the safe direction. Two paths deliberately do NOT prompt either: deleting the scope (there is nothing left to commit into -- it just closes the panel) and leaving the IMAGE (setCurrentImage still drops it, so a half-built submask can never reach the sidecar). ScopeHeaderLab's row menu emits scopeSelected then acts on the active scope, so it re-checks currentScopeName() after the emit -- a refused switch must not let "Remove Mask 2" remove Mask 1.

  • RENDER COMPOSITING (Stage 4b + N-scope stack): the render composites the WHOLE stack in SCENE-LINEAR before the output transform, INDEPENDENT of which scope is active for editing -- so a saved mask is visible the moment the image loads (no need to select its scope). DevelopProperties::stackJob() captures it as plain values (Global params + each enabled non-Global scope's params/masks/combine) so it is safe for the off-thread full-res render. WorkingImageCache::renderStack starts from Global (whole image), then for each scope develops the RUNNING ACCUMULATOR `acc` with that scope's params (an identity side aliases acc -- no copy/Develop) and blends back over acc by its mask (acc = acc*(1-m)+scope*m; an empty/global mask applies the params globally on top); one final OutputTransform. MASK BLEND SPACE is an A/B flag (kPerceptualMaskBlend in workingimagecache.cpp): A = lerp in scene-linear (physically faithful, but a STRONG masked adjustment's visible falloff is front-loaded -- display gamma steepens it near m=1, so a linear feather reads abrupt at the size edge); B (current) = lerp in a PERCEPTUAL (gamma 2.2) space so DISPLAYED brightness tracks m ~linearly and a feather reads evenly (== the coverage tint). Only feather pixels (0<m<1) pay the pow; m<=0 skip, m>=1 copy. Flip the flag to compare/revert. Because each scope develops the accumulated result (NOT the original work), where two scopes' masks OVERLAP their adjustments COMPOUND rather than the top scope replacing the one below -- e.g. two overlapping +1-stop scopes give ~+2 stops in the overlap (in scene-linear the per-op composition is natural: exposure multiplies, etc.). Scope ORDER therefore matters for non-commuting ops. buildMaskBuffer (mainwindow.cpp) rasterizes a scope's component(s) at WorkingImage (pre-orientation) resolution; each pixel maps work-normalized -> output-normalized (output = work rotated CW by degrees) so geometry edited on the oriented loupe lines up after the EXIF rotation. parseMaskComp/evalMaskComp branch by tool: Linear = projection ramp; Radial = distance in the ellipse's local frame (computed in OUTPUT-PIXEL space so the ellipse keeps aspect + rotation), which IS the MaskFalloff t: 1 at the centre, falling to ~8% at the boundary and dying out past it (see MASK FEATHER PROFILE below). The BRUSH component is not analytic: its strokes are RASTERIZED (Develop/brushstamp.h, shared with the ImageView preview so they match) into a work-space coverage buffer, cached (brushRasterCached, paramsJson+dims+degrees key, mutex-guarded since the GUI proxy and full-res worker both build it; only proxy-size buffers are cached -- full-res is ~w*h*4 bytes and built once per settle). Components combine in order: brush samples its buffer (then component Invert), parametric uses evalMaskComp. Falloff = the shared MaskFalloff profile, Add=union(max), Subtract=*(1-c), Invert flips. developComposite remains the fast single-pass path when there are no masks. Mask add/delete/geometry/feather/ invert and a brush stroke (live DURING the swipe, throttled, and again on release) emit paramsChanged() to re-render.
