Deferred: true STACKED adjustments (each scope builds on the result below, not the original), the combine field (Union only today), and AI tools.

  • BRUSH (freehand mask): vector strokes in paramsJson {size,flow,autoMask, strokes:[{pts,size,feather,flow,erase,autoMask}]} (each stroke snapshots its settings; current settings drive the next stroke).  autoMaskMode ("lum"|"ai") is a RETIRED key -- old sidecars still read, the value is ignored. DEFAULTS: feather 0 (crisp), flow 100. Painted on the loupe (ImageView): a two-ring cursor -- the OUTER ring is the brush SIZE (the total extent; the dab is clipped there), and when feather>0 an INNER ring at the HALF-COVERAGE radius (MaskFalloff::Shape::h) marks the feather, matching Lightroom's cursor and the Radial mask's dashed ellipse. left-drag paints, Alt-drag erases; dabs accumulate per stroke by MAX then are composited with flow (build-up across strokes). FEATHER = LIGHTROOM MODEL (softens INWARD) through the shared MaskFalloff profile -- feather does NOT change the brush footprint (size = outer extent, fixed); it pulls the half-coverage radius inward with a rounder falloff and no flat core (feather 0 = hard edge at the size ring, feather 100 = fully soft). Pairs with the perceptual mask blend (kPerceptualMaskBlend) so the displayed effect tracks the coverage the way Lightroom's does. Live red/blue tint preview rebuilt only on the dirty segment bbox. The DEVELOP EFFECT follows the swipe: the in-progress stroke is handed to the dock mid-stroke (brushEmitLiveGeometry -> maskGeometryChanged -> setActiveMaskParams -> paramsChanged), THROTTLED to kMaskStrokeLiveMs (100ms, ~10Hz) because each tick costs a proxy develop render of the whole stroke set. The throttle has a TRAILING edge (one scheduled catch-up emit), so a stroke that pauses mid-swipe still settles on the mask as painted, not as of 100ms ago; the release emit is unconditional, so the settled render comes from the finished stroke, not the last live tick. brushParamsJson(withLiveStroke) is the single builder for the live, release and undo emits. WHILE AN ADD STROKE is swiped the whole-mask composite tint (scopeMaskTint) is SUPPRESSED (drawForeground brushStroking guard, = ImageView::maskBrushOwnsTint) so the per-brush live preview shows the stroke building up in real time, and MW::updateMaskOverlayTint returns early while that stroke is in flight (maskStrokeInFlight), so live ticks never rebuild a veil nothing draws.  The stand-in HOLDS PAST THE RELEASE: mouse-up sets maskBrushVeilStale, which setScopeMaskTint clears when MW pushes the veil rebuilt from the finished stroke.  Without it the coverage blinked back to the PRE-stroke veil at mouse-up and only caught up a render tick later -- precisely when the user is reading the mask to place the next stroke.  The hold is dropped when the local preview would not be drawn anyway (the per-tool draw is hover-gated), because off the image a stale veil beats no coverage. A SUBTRACT/INTERSECT stroke inverts both gates (maskLegendOp > 0): the local preview paints the overlay colour exactly where the mask is being REMOVED -- it reads as adding -- so the real veil stays drawn and IS rebuilt on the live ticks, retreating under the brush in step with the develop render. Dock rows: Size/Feather/Flow/Auto mask/Invert (ONE auto-mask checkbox -- see AUTO-MASK below). Size also via [ / ] keys or a two-finger drag (ImageView wheelEvent, gated to brush+hover) -> maskBrushSizeRequested -> setActiveBrushSize syncs the dock.  SIZE RESOLUTION: size is a % of the long edge at 0.1 resolution (ImageView::kBrushSizeStep), not whole percents -- the dock slider is a div-10 row (1..1000 = 0.1..100.0) and every path (canvas gesture, slider, sidecar) quantises to the same step through ImageView::setBrushSize / DevelopProperties::setActiveBrushSize, so they cannot disagree.  The floor is ImageView::maskBrushSizeMin() = a ~3px diameter on the image (the spotSizeMin rule), never coarser than one step; the old whole-percent round made the smallest brush ~1% of the long edge (60px on a 6000px file), too blunt for fine work.  BOTH the wheel/two-finger gesture and [ / ] are RELATIVE (ImageView::scaleBrushSize: x1.15 per key, ~x1.0075 per wheel pixel) -- a fixed delta crawls at 80% and jumps from 0.5 to 5 -- falling back to one step when the proportional move is below the quantum; Feather also via Shift + wheel / two-finger drag -> maskFeatherRequested -> setActiveMaskFeather, which is the NEXT stroke's feather (committed strokes keep the feather they were painted with, so this only refreshes the cursor rings); "A" toggles auto-mask (-> maskBrushAutoMaskRequested -> setActiveBrushAutoMask); Cmd/Ctrl+Z removes the last stroke. ImageView needs focus (set on brush enter) and claims [ ] A from their global action shortcuts via QEvent::ShortcutOverride. Each cursor ring is drawn twice -- a dark halo under the coloured line -- so it stays visible on light AND dark images (Level-tool halo idiom). Cursor hint: green = auto-mask, pink = erase, white = plain. A CENTRE GLYPH says what the pending work will do: a plus in the ring colour when adding, a RED MINUS when subtracting, a RED CROSS when intersecting (skipped when the brush is smaller than the glyph). It polls the HELD MODIFIER (QGuiApplication::queryKeyboardModifiers, the same read DevelopProperties::maskOpFromModifiers uses) ORed with maskLegendOp, so it answers "what does Opt do right now?" even on the FIRST submask -- where the pending op is pinned to Add and the legend chip stays "Update" -- while a LATCHED subtract keeps the minus after the key is released. The Alt/Shift branch of MW::eventFilter repaints the loupe, because it eats the Alt KeyPress and an unchanged op returns early without rebuilding the veil. If the erase stroke is ever restored (G::useBrushEraseStroke) the glyph covers it too: maskBrushErase is latched at mouse-press, so on hover the cursor PREDICTS it from the Opt key held right now. AUTO-MASK is ONE checkbox and one algorithm (the "A" key toggles it). It attenuates each dab by an acceptance field evaluated in the SHARED rasterizer, so preview == render:

  • AUTO-MASK (BrushStamp, per DAB): the guide is a small YCbCr COLOUR map of the displayed image (BrushStamp::Guide, output-oriented), built from the loupe pixmap when the brush activates (ImageView::ensureAutoGuide) and registered by path (putGuide); the render samples the SAME guide (getGuide(fPath), threaded through developCompositeStack/buildMaskBuffer/brushRasterCached), so the confinement is identical in the overlay and the render.  For each DAB, in guide space (BrushStamp::buildDabField): (1) the REFERENCE is the median colour of a small window at the dab centre, re-sampled as the brush travels; (2) the TOLERANCE adapts to that window's own spread (75th percentile of the distance to the reference x kTolK, clamped to [kTolFloor, kTolCeil]); (3) the DISTANCE is Y plus double-weighted chroma (kAutoChromaWeight); (4) a FLOOD FILL from the dab centre keeps only the accepted region the centre can reach, then a 3x3 blur softens the guide-resolution staircase.  The dab's pixel loop bilinearly samples that 0..1 field (DabField::at) through an AFFINE target->guide map solved once per dab (guideMapFor -- degrees is the same rotation buildMaskBuffer uses), and the field is cached and reused while the centre has not moved a guide pixel.  Cost measured at ~32ms for a long stroke replayed on a 24MP buffer.

  • WHY THAT SHAPE (the 2026-08 rewrite): the first auto-mask compared each pixel's LUMINANCE against a single reference sampled at the STROKE START, within a fixed +-0.15 band, INDEPENDENTLY per pixel. All three parts failed on the same photo (a brown cone on a grey background of the same lightness): equal luminance means the background matched, so it painted it; a fixed band cannot cover a textured surface, so the object came out speckled; and a lone pixel needs no route back to the brush, so matching pixels on the FAR side of the edge were painted too. Chroma answers the first, the adaptive tolerance the second, the flood fill the third -- validated offline against that photo before the port. THE OTHER HALF was resolution: the guide was a fixed 1024px map of the whole FRAME, so a small brush on a big file decided everything from ~10 guide pixels of mush. ensureAutoGuide now sizes the guide so the brush DIAMETER spans at least kGuidePxPerDab (96) guide pixels, clamped to [1024, 3072] -- sized against the smallest brush it must serve, the loaded one AND every committed auto-masked stroke, so re-opening a submask re-rasterizes its strokes on a guide at least as fine as the one they were painted with -- and rebuilds (only ever finer) when a smaller brush needs it (the guide dims are part of brushRasterCached's key, so the cache cannot serve a raster made against the old one).

  • AI AUTO-MASK (RETIRED, 2026-08): a second "AI edge (SAM)" mode used to confine a stroke to the SAM 2 object under its seed point (per-stroke SAM field, ~1s encode + 40ms decode). It was dropped with the rewrite above: two auto-mask checkboxes is one too many ("just works" is the requirement), and on real edges it was no better than the colour algorithm at a far higher cost. Gone with it: the BrushStamp SAM-field store, MW::ensureBrushSamField(s)/onBrushSamFieldRequested/warmBrushSamEncoder, ObjectMaskPredictor::refinePoint and the maskAutoMaskAi row. The OBJECT MASK tool keeps the whole SAM 2 stack (encoder cache included) -- only the brush's use of it is gone.

  • CONTENT-RANGE MASKS (Luminance Range + Color Range): coverage depends on the pixel VALUE, not its position, so unlike the geometric tools these need the image's pixels. Both measure on a SHARED display-referred reference (Develop/rangemask.h, RangeMask::RangeRef -- interleaved RGB 0..1, output-oriented, capped ~1024), built and registered by path (putRef/getRef, mirroring BrushStamp's guide) so the loupe overlay and the develop render sample the IDENTICAL map (preview == render). The reference is the developed GLOBAL scope only (Global params + OutputTransform + orientation, MW:: ensureRangeRef via developComposite on a downscaled work) -- "what you see", so the selection tracks the base tones -- and BASE-ONLY on purpose: measuring on the composite would let a range mask that shifts its own tones feed back on its own selection and oscillate. ensureRangeRef is cached (keyed on path + a base-params signature; run from renderDevelopPreview and renderDevelopFullResAsync only when a range mask exists), so a range-slider drag or a colour sample never rebuilds it -- they just re-threshold the cached reference. buildMaskBuffer gains a third component kind (isRange) beside brush / parametric: it fetches getRef(fPath) once (absent -> the component yields 0, like a brush with no guide) and per pixel calls RangeMask::lumCoverage / colorCoverage at the mapped output coords, combining with the same Add=max / Subtract=*(1-c) / Invert logic. LUMINANCE RANGE: paramsJson {lo,hi} (0..1 display luminance, Rec.601); coverage 1 inside [lo,hi] with a smootherstep band from MaskComponent.feather. Dock rows Range Min / Range Max / Feather / Invert; lo/hi changes emit maskRangeChanged -> ImageView:: setMaskRangeParams (live tint). No canvas interaction. COLOR RANGE: a HUE + SATURATION band selection in HSV (value ignored -- brightness stays with Luminance Range). paramsJson {hueLo, hueHi (degrees 0..90), satLo, satHi (0..100 -> sat fractions 0..1), samples:[[r,g,b],...] 0..1 display}. Each sample fixes a centre (hue angle, sat radius; ColorSample via RangeMask:: rgb2hs); coverage is 1 where a pixel's HUE lies in [centre-hueLo, centre+ hueHi] AND its SAT in [centre-satLo, centre+satHi] of ANY sample (union), each edge smootherstep-feathered, a tiny sat floor dropping near-neutral pixels. hueLo/hueHi/satLo/satHi widen the band INDEPENDENTLY in four directions -- this replaced the earlier symmetric "refine" luma/chroma distance tolerance. WHEEL: a spanned ColorRangeWheel index widget above the sliders (mirrors the Color Grade wheel embed) paints the HSV disc, a dot per sample, the band SECTOR per sample, and four GRAB HANDLES on the anchor (first) sample -- two hue edges (on the rim, each labelled with its ABSOLUTE hue in degrees) + inner/outer sat arcs. Dragging a handle changes the matching GLOBAL bound; wheel<->sliders stay in sync (onColorRangeWheelChanged writes params + guarded setSliderReal; slider drags call wheel->setBounds). The disc/dots/sector use the SAME HSV as the coverage, so the wheel is a faithful picture of the selection. EYEDROPPER: with the tool active a loupe click samples RangeRef under the cursor (PIPETTE cursor -- the shared dropperCursor, same as the WB pick), shift-click ADDS another sample; sampled colours draw as on-image swatches (bottom-left, viewport coords) and clicking a swatch removes it (pointer cursor). A SAMPLING LOUPE (ImageView::drawSampleLoupe -- the WB "pick a target neutral" panel generalised, shared by both) follows the tip while hovering the image: a magnified 5x5 grid of the pixels under the tip + their RGB and HSL readout. Sample edits go back through the existing geometry handshake (ImageView::maskGeometryChanged -> DevelopProperties::setActiveMaskParams, which also refreshes the wheel dots), so no new persist path. Dock rows: the wheel, then Hue Lo / Hue Hi / Sat Lo / Sat Hi / Feather / Invert. The tint (ImageView::drawRangeMask / buildRangePreview) is red (Add) / blue (Subtract) like the other tools and rebuilds on any dock param change (maskRange Changed / setMaskFeather / setMaskInverted) or eyedropper edit; it heals if the reference lands after the overlay began (buildRangePreview on a null preview). Deferred: a dual-handle luminance range slider, per-scope "image below" reference for stacked range masks, and an in-dock swatch strip (swatches live on the loupe for now).

  • INFERENCE LAYER (Utilities/inference/): the unified model-inference seam that new neural features use. InferenceSession (front door) owns an InferenceBackend; the only concrete backend today is OrtBackend (ONNX Runtime). Per-platform execution providers are selected at load: CoreML EP on macOS (dispatches to the Apple Neural Engine / GPU), DirectML on Windows, CPU everywhere as the fallback. All ORT contact is confined to ortbackend.cpp behind a PIMPL, and the whole scope is guarded by WINNOW_HAVE_ORT: built without ORT (CMake option WINNOW_ENABLE_ORT=OFF, the default until the runtime is vendored on both platforms) it compiles to a stub -- IsLoaded()==false, Run() no-ops -- so features degrade like a missing model. This DELIBERATELY supersedes the old cv::dnn / "no onnxruntime" approach for NEW models (it reaches the ANE and loads architectures cv::dnn cannot, e.g. attention/diffusion); the existing cv::dnn predictors (u2net/skyseg/ midas/focus/SAM2, below) keep working unchanged and migrate opportunistically. The InferenceBackend abstract class is an ESCAPE HATCH: a native CoreMLBackend can be added for a model that needs maximal ANE utilisation (generative fill) without touching callers. FUTURE: we may move to a full native-per-platform solution (native Core ML on Mac + DirectML/native on Windows) if ORT's CoreML EP underuses the ANE. DEVICE SELECTION (InferenceDevice pref, ortbackend.cpp): the pref biases WHICH compute units the CoreML EP targets. Auto/NPU append the CoreML EP with flags 0 = the full set (ANE -> GPU -> CPU), the ANE-first default. GPU appends it with COREML_FLAG_USE_CPU_AND_GPU | COREML_FLAG_CREATE_MLPROGRAM, i.e. Apple GPU (Metal / MPSGraph) with the ANE EXCLUDED -- for graphs whose ops the ANE cannot service (e.g. FFT-based FFC blocks in LaMa-style generative fill); BackendName() then reports "CoreML/GPU" so a log line confirms the GPU units took. CPU forces the CPU EP. CAVEAT: this only picks the target DEVICE -- ops the CoreML EP cannot map are still partitioned back to the CPU EP regardless of the flag, so an end-to-end-GPU LaMa also depends on the ONNX export (FFT decomposed to matmul); the GPU flag alone does not guarantee it. Note there is NO ONNX Runtime "MPS execution provider" -- MPS-as-a-backend is a PyTorch concept; on ORT the GPU is reached THROUGH the CoreML EP's CPUAndGPU compute units as above. Only PMRID requests a device today (Auto), so the GPU path stays inert until a caller asks for it. Active consumer: PMRID "Denoise raw" (ImageFormats/Raw/pmrid.cpp, see "DENOISE RAW" above) -- it took over when the earlier RawRefinery "TreeNet" denoiser was removed 2026-07-12. Models resolve via applicationDirPath() + a ReleaseExtras/ .onnx, warn-if-absent, like the cv::dnn models.

  • SELECT SUBJECT MASK (AI): an AI salient-object mask that selects the main subject. Like the content-range masks it is CONTENT-derived and shares their preview==render design, but the coverage is a FIXED saliency map produced once by the U^2-Net ONNX model rather than a per-tick threshold. The model runs through OpenCV's DNN module (cv::dnn::readNetFromONNX -- the SAME runtime FocusPredictor uses; no onnxruntime dependency), wrapped in Utilities/subjectpredictor (SubjectPredictor): U^2-Net preprocessing (RGB, 320x320, /maxpixel, per-channel ImageNet mean/std). GOTCHA: U^2-Net has 7 outputs (d0..d6) and OpenCV's forward() with no name returns the LAST- registered layer, which here is a COARSE side output (d6 -- a fuzzy blob, the original "rough selection" bug); we fetch outputs by name (getUnconnectedOutLayersNames) and use outs[0] = d0, the fused full-res saliency, min-max normalized to a 0..1 map. The model file u2net.onnx (~176 MB) sits next to focus_point_model.onnx (executable dir on Windows, .app/Contents/MacOS on mac; CMake bundles/copies it, warning rather than failing if absent so the mask just no-ops). MW::ensureSubjectMask (mirroring ensureRangeRef) develops the downscaled BASE scope (output-oriented, "what you see"), runs the model synchronously on the GUI thread with a busy cursor (~200-400 ms, one-shot per image on the user's add/select action), and registers the coverage by path in Develop/subjectmask.h (SubjectMask::SubjectRef, single-channel). The raw 320x320 saliency is EDGE-REFINED before storage: upsampled to the reference (~1024) size and guided-filtered (He et al.) against the developed base's luminance so the cutout snaps to the real subject boundary instead of a blocky low-res ramp (subjectpredictor guidedFilterGray -- ~9 box filters, OpenCV core only, no ximgproc). It is CACHED BY PATH ONLY -- subject detection is independent of the develop sliders, so slider drags never re-run inference. buildMaskBuffer gains an isSubject component kind: it fetches getRef(fPath) once (absent -> yields 0, like a range mask with no reference) and per pixel calls SubjectMask::coverage at the mapped output coords, combining with the same Add / Subtract / Invert logic. paramsJson is empty (content-derived, no geometry); the dock shows only Feather (a smootherstep threshold band around the 0.5 saliency cutoff -- softens the cutout edge) and Invert (selects the background instead). The saliency is edge-tuned in subjectpredictor (small guided-filter radius = long/256 + eps 1e-6, then a contrast around 0.5) so the matte hugs the subject without a halo; Feather softens from that tight base. The loupe tint (ImageView::buildSubjectPreview, sharing drawRangeMask / the maskRangePreview buffer via rebuildContentPreview) is red (Add) / blue (Subtract) like the other tools, rebuilds on Feather / Invert changes, and heals if the saliency lands after the overlay began. CONTENT-MASK TINT PERSISTENCE: unlike the geometric tools (hover-gated -- their handles are pointer-relative), the Range/Subject coverage tint stays drawn as long as the tool is active (maskEditMode), even when the pointer leaves the view, so it is visible while adjusting Feather/Refine in the dock (drawForeground content branch + the setMaskFeather/setMaskInverted repaint no longer require maskHover). ensureSubjectMask is called from renderDevelopPreview / renderDevelopFullResAsync only when a Subject mask exists (stackHasSubjectMask).

  • SELECT SKY MASK (AI): the sky twin of Select Subject, built as a PARALLEL of it (Utilities/skypredictor SkyPredictor, Develop/skymask.h SkyMask store, MW::ensureSkyMask / stackHasSkyMask, an isSky component in buildMaskBuffer, ImageView::buildSkyPreview, tool==6). It runs a single-channel sky-segmentation ONNX (skyseg.onnx, next to u2net.onnx; CMake bundles/copies it, warning rather than failing if absent so the mask no-ops) through the SAME OpenCV DNN runtime. Preprocessing is the generic seg-net convention (RGB, /255, per-channel ImageNet mean/std); output is [1,C,H,W] with the sky channel taken (C==2 -> channel 1, else 0). The coarse map is edge-refined by the SHARED SegRefine pipeline (Utilities/segrefine.h -- min-max -> upsample -> guided filter vs the photo's luma -> contrast), the identical matting the Subject mask uses (that code was extracted into segrefine.h so both stay in sync). A SEPARATE path store from SubjectMask so a Subject and a Sky mask can coexist on one image. Dock rows Feather + Invert; the loupe tint, persistence and add/select build (MW::onAiMaskEditBegin handles the AI tools) all mirror Subject.

  • SELECT BACKGROUND MASK (AI): the INVERSE of Select Subject -- no new model or store. It reuses the SubjectMask saliency (ensureSubjectMask; stackHasSubjectMask + the subjRef fetch + onAiMaskEditBegin all treat Subject and Background the same) and simply selects 1 - subject. In buildMaskBuffer the subject CompDesc gains subjectBaseInvert (true for Background), and coverage is sampled with (inverted ^ subjectBaseInvert) so Background with Invert-off = background, Invert-on = subject; the loupe tint (buildSubjectPreview) XORs the same way (maskIsBackground, tool==7). Dock rows Feather + Invert.

  • DEPTH RANGE MASK (AI): a HYBRID -- the MODEL produces a depth field (like Subject/Sky) but the SELECTION is a [near,far] BAND (like Luminance Range over depth). Model is MiDaS v2.1 small (midas.onnx, ~64MB, conv-based -- an official OpenCV DNN sample; ViT DPT / Depth-Anything won't load) via Utilities/depthpredictor: input [1,3,256,256], /255 + ImageNet mean/std, single output [1,256,256] inverse depth. The map is min-max'd and INVERTED to 0=near..1=far, then guided-UPSAMPLED against the photo (SegRefine:: guidedUpsample -- continuous field, guided-filter joint upsampling, NO binary contrast). Stored in Develop/depthmask.h (DepthMask::DepthRef, its own path store) and built by MW::ensureDepthMask (stackHasDepthMask + a depthRef fetch + onAiMaskEditBegin, all mirroring Sky). buildMaskBuffer's isDepth CompDesc carries the band [rlo,rhi] from paramsJson {lo,hi}; per pixel it calls DepthMask::coverage = a smootherstep band over the sampled depth (the exact analogue of RangeMask::lumCoverage). Dock rows Near / Far / Feather / Invert (Near/Far reuse the maskRangeLo/maskRangeHi sources -> maskRangeChanged -> ImageView::setMaskRangeParams, which now accepts Depth; live tint buildDepthPreview). paramsJson is non-empty {lo,hi} so it is NOT content-derived (survives stackJob's empty- params skip normally). AI masks Subject/Background/Sky/Depth are fully implemented; Object Mask (below) is the one AI mask currently at scaffold stage. KNOWN LIMITATION (validated, not a bug): skyseg is strong on horizon-type skies (offline test on a wide winter forest/sky photo gave a near-perfect mask, bright snow correctly rejected) but weak on sky FRAGMENTED through dense canopy behind a large subject + bokeh -- the raw model output itself only fires on scattered patches there, so no post- processing recovers it. Workaround for such shots: a Luminance/Color Range mask + brush. FUTURE WIP (deferred): benchmark alternative sky models offline (onnxruntime) and swap only if one clearly beats skyseg on the hard case AND loads in OpenCV DNN.

  • OBJECT MASK (AI, PERIMETER-PAINT): a PROMPTABLE mask. The user traces the object BOUNDARY with a solid brush (multiple strokes; Opt/Alt erases); when the traced perimeter forms a CLOSED loop the enclosed region is filled and that silhouette is refined to the edge by a SAM 2 encoder+decoder pair via OpenCV DNN (NO onnxruntime -- feasibility proved by an offline spike: cv::dnn 4.11 loads+runs both graphs). Unlike Subject/Sky (one-shot per image) it is TWO-PHASE, matching SAM's heavy-encoder / light-decoder split: Utilities/objectmaskpredictor ObjectMaskPredictor:: setImage() encodes the developed base ONCE (~1s CPU, caches image_embed + high_res_feats + guide), then refine() runs the decoder PER brush edit (~40ms) feeding the painted silhouette as the dense mask_input prompt (has_mask_input=1) plus the stroke bbox as a box (labels 2/3), picks the best of 3 candidate masks by predicted IoU, morphologically cleans it (open + keep-largest-CC + hole-fill, kills occluder specks), then shares SegRefine::refine for the edge matte. Preprocessing = SAM 2 reference: RGB, 1024^2, (px/255-mean)/std, ImageNet stats; point_coords scaled to the 1024 space. DEPLOYMENT: the DECODER must be the FIXED-SHAPE export -- cv::dnn's importer rejects the stock samexporter decoder's dynamic num_labels/num_points ("parseShape: dynamic 'zero' shapes"); pin them (1 label / 2 points) + onnxsim constant-fold offline (build-time tool only, nothing extra ships). Encoder loads as-is. Models (sam2 encoder/decoder .onnx) live next to u2net.onnx in the executable dir; warn-if-absent like the other AI masks. WIRED: MaskTool::Object (appended to the enum); MW::ensureObjectMask (two-phase -- encode cached by path+ROI in objectMaskPredictor, decode the component brush + putRef under objectRefKey = path+brushHash so multiple object masks coexist); stackHasObjectMask; and the four triggers (onAiMaskEditBegin warms the encoder + decodes if a stroke exists; updateMaskOverlayTint per-component; renderDevelopPreview + renderDevelopFullResAsync loop the job's object components). Perimeter-paint contract (the UI emits it): paramsJson {"size":N,"strokes":[{"pts":[x,y,...], "size":N,"erase":bool},...]}, output-normalized 0..1 boundary strokes (like the Brush tool). parseObjectBrush rasterizes the strokes with BrushStamp::rasterize, then ObjectMask::fillEnclosed (Develop/objectmask.h) does a morphological close + border flood- fill: it thickens the wall by bridgePx (a fraction of the long edge, so preview/render agree), floods the background inward from the border, and any unreached non-wall pixel is ENCLOSED. Returns the filled silhouette ONLY when a closed loop is found; an open trace -> no coverage (matches the amber/green UI). No opencv in mainwindow. buildMaskBuffer WIRED: CompDesc.isObject + a PER-COMPONENT objRef (object refs are per-brush, not shared by path like Subject/Sky/ Depth, so each CompDesc carries its own ObjectMask::getRef( objectRefKey(fPath,paramsJson))); the per-pixel loop calls ObjectMask::coverage. objectRefKey lives in the first anon namespace (before buildMaskBuffer) so both it and ensureObjectMask share it. Absent ref => no effect. PERIMETER-PAINT UI WIRED (ImageView): the Object tool is a solid BOUNDARY brush (NOT a lasso, NOT a fill brush). It REUSES the Brush buffers: strokes accumulate into maskBrushMain (the perimeter wall), maskBrushStroke holds the in-progress stroke, maskBrushSize is the diameter, maskBrushStrokesJson the committed list. Press begins a stroke (Alt = erase); move stamps segments (BrushStamp) + folds them into maskObjPerim (wall+stroke) and runs objRecomputeFill (ObjectMask::fillEnclosed) -> maskObjClosed + maskObjFill; objRebuildPreview tints the overlay AMBER while open, GREEN + a faint fill once closed. Release composites the stroke into the wall and emits {"size","strokes"} via maskGeometryChanged -> setActiveMaskParams -> paramsChanged, which runs ensureObjectMask (encode once + fill + decode) when closed and refreshes scopeMaskTint. Size: [ / ] keys, a two-finger drag / wheel (scaleBrushSize -> maskBrushSizeRequested -> setActiveBrushSize), or the dock Size slider -- same 0.1% step, relative gestures and ~3px floor as the Brush (see BRUSH, SIZE RESOLUTION); Cmd/Ctrl+Z = objUndoStroke. State: maskObjPerim/maskObjFill/maskObjClosed/ maskObjDrawing (imageview.h); helpers objEnsureBuffers/ objRecomputeFill/objRebuildPreview/objUndoStroke; beginMaskEdit restores strokes+size, endMaskEdit clears; maskHitTest returns -1 for Object; drawMask suppresses the stale scope tint while a stroke is in flight (brushStroking) so the amber/green shows live. Object is NOT in maskIsContent() and needs no content preview. DOCK ROWS WIRED (developproperties.cpp): maskToolName/FromName know Object; showMaskMenu offers Add/Subtract Object Mask (loops bound to MaskTool::Object); the maskEditBegin dispatcher includes Object so selecting it activates the perimeter brush AND fires onAiMaskEditBegin (warms the encoder); the settings rows are Size + Feather + Invert (its own row-builder branch); setActiveBrushSize accepts Object; defaultMaskParams returns {"size":8,"strokes":[]}. NOT added to the stackJob contentDerived list on purpose -- an undrawn/open Object mask yields no coverage and is dropped from the render like an unplaced gradient; once a loop closes it fills and composites. Full loop closes via existing signals: stroke release -> maskGeometryChanged -> setActiveMaskParams -> paramsChanged -> {updateMaskOverlayTint -> ensureObjectMask -> buildMaskBuffer tint} + {developParamsChange -> masked render}. MODELS + EXPORT WIRED: ReleaseExtras/tools/export_sam2.{py,sh} produce sam2_encoder.onnx (as-is) + sam2_decoder.onnx (fixed-shape: pins prompt dims 1 label/2 points + onnxsim constant-fold; prints Shape=0 on success). CMake stages both like u2net/skyseg/midas (warn-if-absent; bundled into MacOS/ on mac, copied beside Winnow.exe on Windows). The produced pair was verified to load+run under cv::dnn (enc ~1s, dec ~43ms). See ReleaseExtras/tools/README.md. Loader expects the names sam2_encoder/decoder.onnx. PENDING: full-app build verify; live end-to-end test; Windows confirm. NOTE buildMaskBuffer runs off the GUI thread; ObjectMask get/putRef are QMutex-guarded. Model quality validated offline (box + brush) on a real photo; the brush dense-prompt path fixes the box-only occluder weakness (IoU 0.92->0.99).

    THREE-LEVEL DENSE PROMPT (2026-08-28): the silhouette is NOT prompted as uniform foreground. The traced perimeter straddles the true boundary, so the painted band is ~half background; prompting it at +8 logits baked the brush dabs into the mask (the visible defect was soft dab-shaped blobs spilling onto the background, with SAM given no way to disagree). refine() therefore takes fill and band SEPARATELY -- interior +8, exterior -8, and the band FADED TO 0 = "no opinion, read the image", continuous after the 256^2 downsample so confidence ramps off smoothly. ObjectMask::fillEnclosed gained an optional bandOut (the bridge-thickened wall) to produce it; parseObjectBrush returns both. Output ObjectRef (Develop/objectmask.h, its own path store).

    OBJECT MASK RESOLUTION (ROI-CROPPED ENCODE, 2026-08-28): the encoder input is a fixed 1024^2 whatever it is fed, so encoding the WHOLE frame spends that budget mostly on background -- an object covering a third of the frame got ~340 px, and SAM's 256^2 mask head resolved it at ~85, i.e. one mask pixel per ~23 image pixels. Fine structure (fur, hair, foliage) was never REPRESENTABLE, so no downstream refinement could recover it. ensureObjectEncoder now takes an output-normalized ROI: objectBrushRoi computes the trace's extent straight from the stroke JSON (points expanded by the brush radius, which is a % of the LONG edge and therefore a different normalized amount per axis), pads 12% of the ROI long side for context (a soft fringe extends past the trace), and quantizes OUTWARD to a 1/16 grid. The guide long edge is then chosen so the ROI lands at ~1024 px (closed-form: gw/gh scale linearly), clamped to [1024, min(4096, native)], and ONLY the crop is encoded. Encode cost is unchanged (still one 1024^2 forward); only the crop's detail rises. CACHING: re-encoding is the ~1s step and must not fire per stroke, so the cached embedding is reused whenever the requested ROI still FITS INSIDE it (developObjectRoi/GuideW/GuideH/Crop members) and is not >2.5x larger in area than needed; the outward quantization is what makes a growing trace keep hitting that path. The Brush "AI" auto-mask passes the FULL frame (a point prompt has no traced region), which also guarantees the cached guide is un-cropped so refinePoint's normalized seed maps directly onto it. ObjectRef is stored CROP-LOCAL (x0,y0,x1,y1 = the crop's normalized extent, coverage 0 outside), which is what lets the higher resolution be kept in the same ~4 MB rather than being flattened back into a whole-frame map. cropCoverage expresses the prompt in the crop's frame.

    OBJECT MASK MATTE (SegRefine::Mode, 2026-08-28): SegRefine::refine gained a Mode. Saliency (Subject/Sky, unchanged default) min-max stretches the net's sigmoid-ish map then CONTRASTS it around 0.5 to crush the halo into a clean 1-2 px cutout. AlphaLogits (Object Mask) instead takes the SIGMOID of the signed logits -- their zero crossing IS the decision boundary, so min-max was putting the 0.5 level wherever the extremes happened to land, systematically growing or shrinking the mask -- and SKIPS the contrast, because on fur/hair/foliage the fractional alpha in the fringe IS the result and stretching it to near-binary is what produced a blobby cutout. ObjectMask::coverage matches: feather 0 now passes the stored alpha through UNCHANGED (it is a genuine matte, unlike the coarse Subject/Sky saliency refs that must be thresholded to mean anything -- a hard 0.5 threshold there was the second half of the fur defect, killing the edge detail at the last sampling step); feather > 0 still SOFTENS via the Gaussian MaskFalloff::cdf band.

  • LIFECYCLE / FLUSH: MW::fileSelectionChange calls setCurrentImage; after the loupe pixmap is shown (both the cache-hit path and MW::refreshViewsOnCacheChange) MW:: applyDevelopPreviewIfEdited renders the saved edits over an edited image (no-op for identity, so an unedited image stays the normal decode -- and is never written). Sidecars are flushed on navigate-away (setCurrentImage), quit (closeEvent), and before ingest/copy (MW::ingest / copyFiles); NEVER during a drag. Writes are synchronous (a few-KB text file). The OPTIONAL debounce-while-editing write (~2 s after edits settle) is gated by G::isDevelopDebounceWrite (default true); the other flushes always run.

  • VIDEOS: the Develop module operates on decoded still frames, so it is gated off for video selections. MW::currentIsVideo() (the sf VideoColumn flag) guards every entry point: fileSelectionChange hands developProperties->setCurrentImage an EMPTY path for a video (still flushing the image being left, then pointing the dock at nothing), and the three render entries -- applyDevelopPreviewIfEdited, renderDevelopPreview, renderDevelopFullResAsync -- early-return, so armed proxy/settle timers or Transform refreshes never try to develop a video. MODE IS NOT CHANGED. Selecting a video in Develop leaves G::operationMode alone -- Preview/Develop is the user's deliberate choice, and auto-flipping it would churn G::useRaw and rebuild the image cache on every video/still crossing in a mixed folder. The video plays on the VideoTab as in Preview. WHAT THE USER SEES: the panel greys (syncDevelopPanelEnabled) and a RED show-stopper alert row says why -- see "Develop alerts and the greyed panel" below for the alert severities, the mixed video+stills case and the disabled-state rules every Develop widget must follow. D STILL WORKS on a video (operationModeAction is never disabled) -- disabling it would strand the user in Preview-only navigation, and entering Develop on a video is harmless. setOperationMode does skip the forced re-decode / read-ahead re-target for a video current item (no loupe to refresh, ImageCache does not decode videos); the useRaw flip itself is NOT skipped, as it is a mode-level setting that must track the mode so the next still decodes correctly.

  • PERF NOTE: setCurrentImage reads the sidecar synchronously on first touch of each image (cheap: no sidecar => fast empty read). Folding the develop blob into the existing metadata sidecar read (a datamodel column) is a fast-follow if navigation over many edited images shows latency. Increment 2 (presets): snapshot an EditStack (or subset) to a QSettings presets collection; apply to a selection (write each image's sidecar). Then masks/compositor.

5. Brush masks (stroke list + rasterise) -> spatial ops with cache tier 3 -> proxy-on-drag.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Fill Replace (Spot / Fill / Object Heal)

"Fill Replace": heal a clicked spot, a brushed area, or a brushed-over object, parametrically and non-destructively. SHIPPED SCOPE (decision 2026-07-17): SPOT CLEANUP ONLY -- the spot button / "F" arms a click-to-heal spot tool; the Fill (paint an area) and Object (remove an object) modes and the Fill Replace panel are SHELVED behind G::useReplaceFillModes=false (global.cpp). All the code below is kept and working; flip the flag to bring the panel + modes back. The primary engine is an exemplar CLONE heal (real neighbouring pixels, Lightroom heal-brush style -- see CLONE HEAL below); clone fallbacks (and Object-kind spots) use LaMa regenerative inpainting via the unified inference layer on the Apple GPU (see ENGINE SELECTION / LAMA SPOT-FILL below).

------------------------------ WHAT WAS TRIED ------------------------------------- Intent: grow "spot removal" into a full Lightroom-class Fill Replace tool -- Spot (click), Fill (paint an area, clone-filled) and Object (brush an object, generative fill) -- entirely offline. Chronology and honest results:

1. MI-GAN inpaint (27 MB, ~76 ms/512-tile on the ANE): fine on tiny spots; on high-ISO / noisy or defocused content the fill is a coarse colored mosaic that reads as a smudge. Kept only as the G::useLamaSpotFill=false fallback.

2. LaMa inpaint (208 MB, ~272 ms/512-tile on the Apple GPU via CoreML CPUAndGPU; the ANE cannot run its FFC blocks): better than MI-GAN, but any 512-net fill upscaled to a large tile is soft and its low-frequency level drifts (measured +5 luma dome). Corrections (Poisson / low-freq transfer / harmonic membrane) plus grain matching (quilted real residual -- synthetic RNG noise measured wrong in sigma AND character) narrowed but never closed the gap; a ground-truth bench (heal clean areas, compare to the untouched original) proved any generative fill leaves a visible patch on grainy/mottled surrounds.

3. Exemplar CLONE heal (no model; masked template match + Poisson blend + chunking for long strokes + pinned sources + thickness-based feather + coverage-aware source rules): EXCELLENT on spots and thin-stroke removals (branch tests healed invisibly, user-confirmed) -- this is the shipped spot engine. On LARGE fills it hits an information limit: a 200px-wide region's true content is not inferable from its surround (ground-truth RMSE ~15 for every method tried), and patch- scale colour drift remains perceptible; candidate fixes (low-freq harmonization, smaller chunks) benched WORSE and were rejected.

4. Diffusion inpaint (DreamShaper-8-inpainting, SD1.5-class, ~2 GB fp16, ~15 s per 512 fill via torch/MPS on the M4): clearly the best large-area quality (bench: fat-fill RMSE 9.1 vs 15.8 for clone; stalk+leaf removal visually superior), but only with ALL of: a complete object mask + margin (else it REGENERATES the object from leftover fragments -- this is what SAM2 selection would be for), a background prompt plus a negative prompt (else worse than clone; the word "bokeh" literally paints bokeh discs), and the grain quilt on top. Integration would demand a ~2 GB model, a new runtime (stable-diffusion.cpp or Core ML), heal-result caching (15 s cannot run per render), and prompt plumbing.

CONCLUSION (2026-07-17): spots are solved and shipped; large-area fill is not worth the model size / runtime / complexity for Winnow's scope. Fill + Object modes shelved, code retained. If revisited, the path is: SAM2 click-select (full-object mask) -> diffusion tier with negative prompt + grain quilt + cached results.

HISTORY (detail): the tool began as MI-GAN "spot removal" (MI-GAN beat LaMa in the first bake-off: 27 MB and ~76 ms on the ANE vs 208 MB and seemingly CPU-bound; scope was SPOTS only). 2026-07: LaMa was found NOT CPU-bound after all -- it runs on the Apple GPU via the CoreML EP's CPUAndGPU compute units at ~272 ms/tile, 4.2x faster than CPU; the ANE genuinely can't take its FFC blocks but the GPU can (see project_lama_coreml_gpu). Then measurement showed ANY generative fill reads as a patch on grainy/mottled surrounds, so the clone heal became the primary engine and LaMa the regenerative path; MI-GAN remains only as the G::useLamaSpotFill=false fallback engine.

DATA MODEL (Develop/editstack.h): per-image like Geometry -- EditStack::spots is an ordered list of FillSpot{enabled, paramsJson}. paramsJson is opaque brush geometry (FillSpotGeom: feather 0..100 + "kind" = the Replace mode, and EITHER the single-stroke form -- size = diameter/long-edge, pts = output-normalized stroke points (Spot click / Object drag / legacy spots) -- OR the painted-area form "strokes": an ordered add/erase stroke list, each with its own brush size, from Fill mode; rasterize() composites them in paint order, add = max, erase = multiply by (1 - alpha), per-segment so long painted strokes stay cheap), serialized under the "fill" key (forward-tolerant). PARAMETRIC and non-destructive: only the strokes are stored; the heal is re-synthesized at render (the engines are deterministic) and is stable under later slider changes (no re-inference on an exposure tweak).

RENDER: heals run BEFORE the geometry stage, on the developed oriented full frame, so spots stay glued to content when it is later cropped/straightened. developCompositeStack (Main/mainwindow.cpp) calls MiganFill::apply(out, job.spots) just before CropTransform::applyGeometry; StackRenderJob.spots is filled by DevelopProperties::stackJob. Engine (Utilities/inference/miganfill.*): per spot, take a square context tile (~4x the spot), resize to 512, pack [keep-0.5, im*keep] (keep=1 outside the hole), run, composite im*keep + out*(1-keep). The hole is DILATED a few px for the model (hard edges seam); the ORIGINAL soft brush alpha composites the patch back, so the seam is feathered. Spots apply in order (each heals over prior ones). Shared FillSpotGeom rasteriser (Develop/fillspot.h) is used by BOTH the ImageView preview and the render, so preview == render (cf. objectmask.h).

UI (discoverability): a spot tool BarBtn in the Develop dock action row (initialize.cpp, images/icon16/spot.png; dims off, brightens armed) arms the mode. It calls DevelopProperties::onSpotToolToggled; DevelopProperties owns the armed state (spotMode) and drives the button icon back via the spotActiveChanged signal. Armed, ImageView shows a brush-size circle cursor ([ / ] resize); a swiped stroke

  paints one spot on release;

Escape disarms. Each spot drops an on-canvas PIN; click a pin to remove it. Stroke -> spotStrokeCommitted -> DevelopProperties appends a FillSpot -> re-render (heal); pins come from spotPinCenters via spotPinsChanged. Model: ReleaseExtras/migan.onnx (gitignored, EXISTS-guarded bundling); warn-if-absent -> the tool no-ops like the AI masks. FUTURE: the "Fill" pseudo-scope can be promoted to a real EditScope (format is forward-tolerant); larger creative fills need a diffusion model behind the InferenceBackend seam.

FILL REPLACE PANEL (Develop/Replace/replacepanel.* -- SHELVED: hidden while G::useReplaceFillModes is false, see WHAT WAS TRIED; the description below applies when the flag is re-enabled): the tool's UI surface, a

TransformPanel-style strip below the Transform panel ("Fill Replace" gradient header with [?] tips and [E] preview eye; the eye renders with/without every committed heal -- non-destructive bypass via DevelopProperties::spotsShown gating stackJob's spots). Three mode rows, each shortcut letter bold in G::header2Color:

- Spot — (S)  click a blemish -- single click, no dragging; clone heal

- Fill — (F)  PAINT the area to replace (resizable brush; strokes accumulate as a teal tint, Opt/Alt strokes ERASE from it), then Enter commits the painted area as ONE heal; Esc clears the pending paint (a second Esc exits the tool). Uncommitted paint is discarded on mode switch / tool exit. Clone heal.

Object (O)  brush over an object to remove; regenerative model fill

The brush has NO user-facing feather: the cursor is one ring (= exactly what gets replaced), the tint shows exactly what was brushed, and rasterization is HARD (1px anti-alias edge). The seamless transition is the ENGINE's job: at composite time the alpha is feathered by a band sized from the stroke THICKNESS (featherFromHole = clamp(0.7 x max inscribed radius, 2..12px) -- NOT the bbox: a thin diagonal stroke has a huge bbox, and a bbox-sized feather smeared content far outside the brushed area; the clone extent is dilated by the same band so the ramp blends cloned content over its whole width). The stored per-spot "feather" JSON field is legacy metadata, no longer rasterized.

The action-row spot button or "S" toggles the panel; visible == armed (DevelopProperties::spotMode, panel visibility tracks spotActiveChanged so Escape closes it too). S is a Develop mode local shortcut (Slideshow in Preview mode) -- see DEVELOP MODE LOCAL SHORTCUTS. The tool must NOT claim S back from that table while armed, or S could arm it but never disarm it.

S/F/O select the mode while the panel has focus (ReplacePanel::eventFilter, gated on G::useReplaceFillModes). The loupe's S/F/O mode keys, and the A/B "F" tool toggle, "B" correction cycle and "N" grain toggle, were RETIRED when S became the Develop mode spot toggle -- G::spotFillCorrectMode / G::spotFillGrain keep their defaults and are no longer keyboard-settable. The mode is

stored per stroke as "kind" in FillSpotGeom paramsJson (legacy spots parse as fill), so each heal re-renders with the engine it was made with; mode persists to QSettings "Develop/Replace/mode".

ENGINE SELECTION (lamafill.cpp LamaFill::apply, per spot by kind): spot/fill heal by the exemplar CLONE (cloneHealOne, below) and fall back to the model path when no clean source region exists (e.g. the hole touches a frame edge); object goes straight to the model path (a removed object leaves no clean source to clone). Clone heals need NO model, so lama.onnx-absent builds still heal spots/fills; only the model path no-ops warn-if-absent.

LAMA SPOT-FILL (Utilities/inference/lamafill.*, LamaFill::apply, mirrors MiganFill's tile-context heal + soft-alpha composite). developCompositeStack uses it when

  G::useLamaSpotFill (default true), else falls back to MiganFill;

flip the flag in global.cpp to revert. Runs on the GPU (InferenceDevice::GPU -> CoreML CPUAndGPU) because the ANE can't service LaMa's FFC. LaMa I/O differs from MI-GAN: two inputs image[1x3x512x512] RGB in [0,1] + mask[1x1x512x512] where 1 = inpaint region; output[1x3x512x512] RGB in [0,255] (verified by probe). Model is ReleaseExtras/lama.onnx (gitignored, EXISTS-guarded mac bundling like migan.onnx -- Windows deploy copy not added, matching MI-GAN). The lama.onnx export MUST be the fixed-shape (1x3x512x512) + Einsum("ij,jk->ik")->MatMul rewrite, which cut CoreML partitions 405->76; a raw dynamic-batch export is far slower. See project_lama_coreml_gpu for the export recipe.

HEAL LUMINANCE-MATCH A/B (large fills leave a low-frequency "patch" offset against a smooth surround, since the alpha composite feathers only the SEAM, not the interior level): applyFillCorrection (lamafill.cpp) corrects the model fill at net-res before compositing, selected by G::spotFillCorrectMode -- 0 none (raw model), 1 Poisson (cv::seamlessClone NORMAL_CLONE, boundary-gradient match; no-ops if the hole is empty or touches a frame edge; preserves the model's WRONG gradient so it still patches), 2 membrane transfer (model texture + push-pull surround level; INERT for this lama.onnx -- probe-verified 2026-07-16 that the export composites the input over the keep region IN-GRAPH, so orig == model outside the hole, the membrane of their difference is identically zero and mode 2 == mode 0 byte-for-byte; any correction anchored on the keep-region model-vs-orig difference is dead for this export), 3 pure harmonic (push-pull surround only, discard the model). The surround is reconstructed by pushPull() -- a Gaussian-pyramid push-pull (Gortler et al.) that fills the hole with a smooth harmonic membrane; this REPLACED a single-Gaussian normalized convolution that collapsed to a flat average deep in a large hole (leaving a residual luminance blob). Mode 1 Poisson is the DEFAULT: the model path serves Object fills (and clone fallbacks), which must keep the model's content, and Poisson keeps it with the level solved from the boundary; mode 3 discards the model, only right for smooth surfaces. **B** cycles the mode while the replace tool is armed (ImageView::keyPressEvent -> spotFillModeChanged -> MW::developParamsChange re-heals the same image live -- proxy tick + full-res settle; a bare

renderDevelopPreview(false) left the loupe stuck on the upscaled PROXY, whose grainless surround invalidated the first A/B round; status bar shows the mode). TESTING-only key, not in shortcutsform.ui.

CLONE HEAL (cloneHealOne, Lightroom heal-brush style, the spot/fill engine): a masked template match on the context ring finds the best source patch near the spot -- coarse-to-fine (match at reduced resolution so the hole is ~<=48px, refine +-q px at full res), search window 3x the hole so lighting agrees -- then cv::seamlessClone (NORMAL_CLONE) Poisson-blends the patch over the hole (texture AND gradients from the source, level solved from the destination boundary) and the engine-feathered alpha blends it in. WHY POISSON: ground-truth benchmarked (scratch heal_bench.py -- heal strokes over CLEAN background, compare against the untouched original) it beat every alternative on every content type (trunk RMSE 5.8 vs 7.5 membrane-split / 5.9 xphoto SHIFTMAP / 7.0 pure membrane; bokeh 5.4 vs 10.3 / 8.7 / 7.4). Harmonic membranes obey the maximum principle and can NEVER reconstruct a local brightness maximum, so any membrane-based low-freq dims the bokeh blobs a stroke crosses (a visible "dark sausage"); SHIFTMAP shows graph-cut patch seams on defocused backgrounds; Poisson keeps the source's own blobs and only tilts them to fit the boundary. No model inference, full resolution; copied pixels carry the surround's true texture at EVERY frequency (grain, mid-freq mottle), which was the remaining visible tell of every generative fill. Holes reaching a FRAME EDGE still clone: seamlessClone needs its mask strictly inside the patch, so the tile is REFLECT- padded 8px for the solve and cropped back. Returns false when no clean source exists -> the caller falls back to the model path.

PINNED SOURCES: the template match runs on DEVELOPED pixels, so re-matching every render could flip the chosen source mid-slider-drag (heal-content shimmer). The first successful match per (image path, render size, spot geometry, chunk) is pinned in a small mutex-guarded cache (full-res renders run off the GUI thread) and reused; the Poisson blend still re-runs on the current pixels, so exposure/colour edits track while the heal GEOMETRY stays stable. "No source" is pinned too, so failing chunks don't re-search every render. Also removes the per-render match cost. Keyed by qHash(paramsJson), so editing a spot re-matches naturally.

CHUNKED CLONE (large/long painted regions): ONE source offset for a whole stroke leaves a stroke-shaped ghost when the background structure doesn't repeat at a single shift (measured on the branch-removal test), so when the coverage bbox exceeds ~1.5x the chunk size the heal splits into a grid of chunks: each chunk clones its own windowed share of the coverage (ChunkWin: weight 1 on the core cell, linear ramp over core/4 px; cores tile the bbox so every covered pixel is at full weight in exactly one chunk) from its own LOCAL source, and the ramps blend neighbouring chunks' different sources into each other. The chunk core = 3x the painted THICKNESS (6 x max inscribed radius, clamped 96..384 px) -- like the feather, NOT the brush diameter: a big brush previously made 640px chunks whose interiors sit far from any boundary, and nothing matches a smoothly varying background at that scale (stalk test: colour drift, 2000px source offsets). The coarse match also carries a DISTANCE PENALTY (score x (1 + (d/R)^2)): a source at the search edge must be ~2x better than a near one, keeping heals inside the local colour/lighting zone. Two coverage-aware rules keep sources clean, both against the WHOLE blemish, not just the chunk's hole: (1) the template-match ring ignores all coverage (else it seeks sources that LOOK like the remaining blemish), and (2) a candidate source is rejected if the pixels it would copy -- the chunk's hole SHAPE, padded past the feather dilation -- overlap ANY coverage; the overlap for every offset comes from ONE cross-correlation (cv::matchTemplate TM_CCORR) of the coverage with the hole shape. The shape matters: a bounding-RECT test rejected nearly every candidate for a diagonal stroke (its bbox is a huge, mostly-empty square), silently sending all chunks to the model path -- which WAS the visible stroke-shaped smudge (lab-verified on the branch image). A chunk with no valid source falls back to the model fill for just that chunk.

GRAIN MATCH (applyGrainMatch, G::spotFillGrain, default on, **N** toggles): measured root cause of the residual "patch" on smooth walls is NOT luminance (level corrections land within ~1/255) but MISSING sensor grain -- a smooth fill reads as plastic against a grainy wall (harmonic fill measured at 0.17x the surround's grain). Synthetic RNG noise also measured WRONG in character (white, lag-1 autocorrelation ~0.05 vs the real grain's ~0.38; channel-independent ~0.04 vs ~0.92 -> chroma confetti that attenuates more than real grain under display resampling), so the grain is QUILTED: random high-frequency residual (sigma-4 highpass) blocks from the tile's kept surround, screened to near-median energy so structure is never copied, overlap-blended across the hole with random flips -- copied real grain carries sigma, spatial and channel correlation and fine mottle for free. Per channel the amplitude tops the fill's existing grain up to the surround's in quadrature; falls back to channel- and spatially-correlated synthetic noise when the surround has too few clean source blocks (fixed RNG seed either way so re-renders don't shimmer). MODEL-path only -- clone heals carry real grain already. Diagnosis was instrument-first (measured the A/B outputs in Python) after level-only fixes kept failing.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop Dock (UI)

The Develop panel is a dock (View menu > Develop Editor Panel, shortcut F9), built like the Embellish dock: MW::createDevelopDock() in initialize.cpp wraps a DevelopProperties widget (Develop/Properties/), a PropertyEditor subclass mirroring EmbelProperties. It is tabbed with the Embellish dock in the right dock area and its visible / collapsed state persists exactly as the other docks (settings isDevelopDockVisible + DevelopDock, plus the workspace struct field isDevelopDockVisible).

The dock's widget is a container, top to bottom: the ACTION ROW, a fixed-height SCOPES strip, the TRANSFORM (crop + perspective) panel, then the DevelopProperties tree taking the remaining (stretch) space. The scopes and transform strips are each independently toggled from the action row.

The ACTION ROW is the dock's tool bar: the Scopes, Transform (crop), Spot, Mask tint, Preset and Export BarBtns, in a centered QHBoxLayout (a stretch at each end) below the dock title bar. It sits INSIDE the dock body, not in the title bar -- the title bar keeps only the ? (how this works), collapse and X (close) buttons, so the title text is not squeezed by the tools. Collapsing the dock hides the row with the rest of the body (DockWidget::setCollapsed clamps the dock to the title bar height).

TRANSFORM AND SPOT ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE. Both tools drive the loupe (the crop / warp overlay vs the spot brush + pins), so arming either one closes the other -- whether it is armed from the action row, the Develop menu or the R / S keys:

- toggleDevelopTransform disarms Spot (onSpotToolToggled(false)) BEFORE showing the panel, so the spot tool has released the canvas before enterDevelopCrop starts the overlay.

- toggleDevelopReplace calls toggleDevelopTransform when a Transform session is open, re-using its hide path rather than duplicating it -- which means the crop COMMITS, exactly as it does when the user closes the panel with R / the button. Esc (cancelDevelopTransform) remains the only way to discard a crop session.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop Alerts and the Greyed Panel (Severity Rows, Disabled States)

Two halves of the same job: tell the user when Develop is not doing what the panel appears to promise (ALERT ROWS), and make the controls LOOK as dead as they are (the greyed panel). Both are driven from the selection, so both refresh on every selection change.

ALERT ROWS. developAlertRows sits directly BELOW the Develop action row -- coloured text on the panel background (no filled banner), one row per live condition, filled by MW::setDevelopAlerts from MW::updateDevelopSelectionWarning. Conditions STACK: each gets its own row when several hold at once.

TWO SEVERITIES (MW::DevelopAlert), and they say different things:

- AlertShowStopper — Develop cannot run on this selection at all -- a video. RED #FF4040. The panel is greyed alongside, so the row is what explains the dead controls. Text: "First image selected is a video."

- AlertWarning — Develop runs, but not on what the user thinks: with a multi-image selection every edit and every Paste Settings lands on ALL of them (DevelopProperties::flushPropagation), off-screen and invisible. AMBER #FFB340 -- proceed, knowingly.

setDevelopAlerts stable-sorts show stoppers to the TOP, so severity reads from position as well as colour (colour alone fails a colour-blind user). It also re-applies the colour to every REUSED row: the label pool means a row that held a show stopper last time may hold a warning now.

The same text also goes to the STATUS BAR (the longer "Video selected - Develop applies to still images only", which has to explain itself), because the dock may be tabbed away behind History or Presets where the rows cannot be seen.

MIXED SELECTION (video CURRENT + stills selected) IS ITS OWN CASE. MW empties developProperties' current path for a video, so selectedEditCount is 0 and the multi-image row would say nothing while a dozen JPGs are selected. DevelopProperties::selectedStillCount counts the developable stills WITHOUT going through the current path, and the alert block adds a second row -- "N still images are also selected - make one of them current to develop the selection". Nothing can be edited from a video current item (Develop edits the current image and fans the change out from it), so the row says how to reach the stills rather than promising a fan-out. When the current item is a STILL, videos elsewhere in the selection need no special case: selectedEditCount goes through otherSelectedPaths, which already skips them, so the count is of stills only.

GREYING THE PANEL. syncDevelopPanelEnabled() greys the panel for a video, so the sliders are visibly dead instead of live-looking and inert. setDevelopPanelEnabled takes TWO flags: VISIBLE (developAction checked AND Develop mode) and USABLE (the selection can be developed). The dock itself is NOT disabled in the unusable case -- a disabled parent greys every child, and a child cannot override a disabled ancestor -- so each piece is greyed individually, leaving the alert rows readable: developProperties (setPanelEnabled also reaches its sibling ScopeHeader and RawPanel), the developActionRow, and the pinned Transform and Fill Replace strips, which are siblings of the property panel and so are NOT covered by setPanelEnabled -- miss them and a video selection leaves a live crop/heal strip over a dead panel. History and Presets grey with it. setDevelopPanelEnabled raises developDock only on a hidden -> visible TRANSITION; it runs on every selection change, and an unconditional raise() would yank Develop to the front tab whenever the user navigated with History or Presets selected.

EVERY ITEM MUST HAVE A DISABLED STATE. setEnabled() only greys what the style paints, and a Develop panel overrides the style in four ways that each defeat it -- all four now carry a disabled form:

- PER-WIDGET STYLESHEETS. A widget stylesheet beats the app one, including its "QLabel:disabled" rule, so a hand-rolled "color: x" stays at full strength on a greyed panel. G::labelCss(colour, ptSize) emits the normal AND the :disabled rule -- use it for every explicitly coloured label (scope header, scope + submask rows, Raw captions, Transform / Fill Replace titles and hints). Same for non-label rules: the Transform mode buttons and the Color Range hue chips spell out :disabled (and :checked:disabled) themselves.

- INLINE HTML. An accented shortcut letter ("<b>C</b>rop", "<b>S</b>pot") colours the letter inside the text, where no pseudo-state reaches. G::setAccentCaption stores the two halves on the label and G::restyleAccentLabels re-renders them; the panel calls it from changeEvent on QEvent::EnabledChange.

- CUSTOM PAINTING. paintEvent and item delegates draw with literal colours. The scope rail (ScopeHeaderLab and DevelopProperties each paint a segment) tests isEnabled(). PropertyDelegate paints the tree from PENS, not from the widget state: the leaf branch already honoured UR_isEnabled, and the HEADER branch now does too (the section captions Basic / Color / Calibrate / Color Grade / Effects stayed teal over dead rows). It is tested only when the role IS SET, so trees that never set it (Preferences, Embellish) keep their normal colour.

- SHARED / APP-WIDE QSS THAT FORGOT :disabled, or greyed to the WRONG grey. BarBtn::setActive paints the armed tool's accent border white and adds a G::dimmed(white) ":disabled" border, so the armed tool stays legible on a greyed action row without looking live. SliderEditor's value field greyed to a literal "gray", brighter than every caption beside it; it uses G::disabledColor like everything else. WidgetCSS::radioButton set ":checked" (cadetblue) and ":unchecked" with no ":enabled" qualifier, so a disabled radio kept full-strength text -- the Raw panel's "Raw / Embedded Preview" pair; its ":disabled" rule is added LAST (same specificity, later rule wins).

Colour that carries MEANING (selection band, rail, hue chip) dims via G::dimmed() -- blended halfway into the panel background, so it stays identifiable but visibly dead. TEXT uses G::disabledColor.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop Scopes (Histogram + Vectorscope)

A live histogram (left) and vectorscope (right) sit above the property tree, in Develop/Scopes/: HistogramView (256-bin R/G/B with additive blend + a faint luma outline), VectorscopeView (YCbCr Cb/Cr 2-D accumulation on a circular graticule, log-scaled and hue-coloured), wrapped by ScopesView (a QHBoxLayout, histogram given the larger stretch).

They reflect the image CURRENTLY SHOWN in the loupe. MW::updateDevelopScopes(const QImage&) does ONE strided sample pass (~180k-pixel budget, so a 50MP RAW costs the same as the proxy; constScanLine so no detach) that fills both scopes from the same downsample. It is called after each develop preview render (the post-Develop `out`, both setDevelopPreview sites) and, for an unedited image, from the decoded image in applyDevelopPreviewIfEdited; the scopes are cleared at the imageView->clear() sites. No new timers -- it piggybacks the existing coalesced render path. The pass is a cheap no-op while the scopes are hidden.

A button on the Develop action row (graphic.png) shows/hides the strip (MW::toggleDevelopScopes -> setDevelopScopesVisible); the choice persists as Settings "Develop/scopesVisible". (A standalone widget above the tree was chosen over an in-tree spanned row so the scope stays pinned rather than scrolling away; see notes/Develop_Scopes_Plan.txt for the rejected alternative.)

Layout choice (was "G" cycling both -> histogram -> vectorscope -> hidden until 2026-08-22; the key and MW::cycleDevelopScopes are gone). The four states are now PICKED from two places showing the same four exclusive checkable actions (developScopesBoth/Hist/Vect/HideAction, one QActionGroup, no shortcut):

- right-click ANY scope in the strip. The layout section is identical everywhere, so it is built in ONE place and each scope contributes only its own items:

- scope widget contextMenuEvent — builds a stack QMenu with its own items HistogramView                   none yet -- add histogram-only entries (channel visibility, log scale, the neutral-core opacity) here VectorscopeView                 100/150/200% zoom + skin-tone line ScopesView                      nothing: the fallback for right-clicks no scope claimed (tone slider, margins) | | menuRequested(QMenu *menu, QPoint globalPos), relayed by ScopesView v

- MW::showDevelopScopesMenu — appends a separator (only if the scope added items) + the four layout actions, then execs

The QMenu is on the EMITTING scope's stack, so the receiver must handle it synchronously and keep no pointer to it; the actions belong to MW and a QMenu does not own actions added to it, so they outlive the menu.

- the Develop menu's "Scopes" submenu (developScopesMenu), Develop-mode gated like the rest of the menu and ticked from the strip's current state on aboutToShow (MW::syncDevelopScopesMenu).

Both routes call MW::setDevelopScopesChoice(int), where the choice is a ScopesView::ScopeLayout or MW::kDevelopScopesHidden (-1). ScopesView::setScopeLayout hides the scope(s) not wanted -- a hidden widget is an empty layout item, and the stretches are re-set to 0 as well -- so the surviving scope expands to fill the strip. The tone-region slider shares the histogram's x-axis and so follows the histogram. The layout persists as Settings "Develop/scopesLayout" (the ScopesView::ScopeLayout values 0/1/2, so do not renumber the enum) and is re-applied in createDevelopDock, i.e. the strip returns in the state it was left in. Picking a layout while the strip is hidden also shows it, and showing it repopulates the scopes, which go stale while hidden.

Tone-region slider (ToneRegionSlider, under the histogram, sharing its x-axis): a black->white track with three draggable handles that split the tonal range into blacks | shadows | highlights | whites (like the Lightroom tone-curve region splits). The handles move WHERE the shadows/highlights tone controls act and how far each reaches; blacks (0) and whites (1) stay pinned. They map to three EditParams fields (toneShadowCenter / toneCrossover / toneHighlightCenter, perceptual 0..1, defaults 0.25/0.50/0.75). In Develop::buildToneLut the shadows/highlights Gaussian centres come from the two outer handles and each region's sigma is derived from its gap to the crossover (kToneSigmaSpan, so the default gap reproduces the old fixed kToneSigma) -- i.e. the defaults are an exact no-op. A drag writes the active scope's params and emits paramsChanged() (live re-render, coalesced like the sliders); the splits only change pixels when a tone slider is non-zero, so they are NOT part of EditParams::isIdentity(). DevelopProperties::bindToneSlider() connects the slider (drag -> onToneSplitsChanged) and populateSlidersFromStack() pushes the saved positions back on image switch.

THE SAME HANDLES ALSO APPEAR IN THE CURVES PANEL, under its plot in Parametric mode -- one set of params, two ToneRegionSlider instances. onToneSplitsChanged cross-pushes to whichever instance did not originate the change (setPositions emits nothing, so it cannot loop). They stayed here as well as there because this is where they align with real histogram data, and G can cycle this strip away entirely -- which would otherwise leave the Curves panel's parametric bands with no way to move their boundaries. See "Curves panel (tone curve)".

Cursor readout: hovering the loupe marks the pixel under the cursor on both scopes -- vertical R/G/B ticks on the histogram, a ring at its Cb/Cr on the vectorscope. ImageView (mouse tracking on) emits cursorImagePos (normalized) / cursorLeftImage; MW::onImageCursorPos samples that one pixel from developShownImage (the cached QImage last handed to updateDevelopScopes == what the loupe shows; implicitly shared, so O(1) per move, no re-bin, no pixmap->image copy) and calls ScopesView::setMarker / clearMarker. Cleared when the cursor leaves the image. No-op while the scopes are hidden.

Vectorscope zoom: a right-click menu (100/150/200%) magnifies the plotted trace + marker outward from the centre to reveal low-saturation detail; the graticule circle stays as the 100% reference and a small "%"-label shows in the BOTTOM RIGHT corner when zoomed. VectorscopeView::setZoom + zoomChanged; ScopesView re-emits as vectorscopeZoomChanged; MW restores/persists it as Settings "Develop/vectorscopeZoom".

Strip close button: ONE [X] for the whole strip, owned by ScopesView rather than by either scope. It is a BarBtn (close.png, the same chrome button as the dock title bars) parented to ScopesView and FLOATED over the top right corner -- not placed in the layout, which the scopes fill; a layout slot would steal width from whichever scope is on the right. ScopesView::resizeEvent pins it to that corner and raise()s it (setScopeLayout raises it too, since the scope underneath changes with the layout), so it sits over the histogram or the vectorscope, whichever is there. Its click emits ScopesView::closeRequested -> MW::closeDevelopScopes -> setDevelopScopesVisible(false): the strip hides and the LAYOUT IS LEFT ALONE, so "G" or the action-row button brings it back showing the same scope(s) as before.

Skin-tone line: the same right-click menu toggles a flesh-tone reference line -- the YIQ I-axis, which from our BT.601 Cb/Cr coefficients is (Cr-128) = -1.946*(Cb-128); skin of every ethnicity falls along it (hue is constant; only saturation/brightness vary). Drawn centre -> edge into the warm upper-left ray, independent of zoom (it is an angle guide). setSkinLine + skinLineChanged -> ScopesView::vectorscopeSkinLineChanged -> Settings "Develop/vectorscopeSkinLine".


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Detail 1:1 Preview (Live Full-Resolution Patch)

WHY IT EXISTS. Sharpening is the only Develop op with an ABSOLUTE pixel radius (see Develop/sharpen.h), which makes it the only op the interactive proxy renders wrongly BY DESIGN: at a typical proxy scale (~0.3-0.4) Sharpen::effectiveSigma collapses to the kMinSigma 0.4 floor and the whole 0..1.5 amount range moves acutance by ~5%, against ~70% at renderScale 1.0 (measured with Develop::Apply on a synthetic detail field). A fit-to-window loupe hides it for the second, independent reason that the VIEW downsamples the settle render. Net effect before this preview: the Sharpening slider looked dead at every zoom except a 1:1 loupe on a landed settle render, which is what prompted it.

WHAT IT IS. A square window at the head of the Detail section (Develop/Properties/detailpreview.h) showing one patch of the image at one image pixel per LOGICAL pixel -- the loupe's own definition of 100%, so the two agree. It is LIVE: the patch is re-rendered on every proxy tick, so it tracks a slider drag with no settle wait, and it is independent of the loupe's zoom.

THE RENDER (MW::renderDetailRoiImage, mainwindow.cpp). Carried as a DetailRoiJob into developCompositeStack, which is where this tick's per-scope mask buffers already exist. The patch is cut from the FULL-RES base (cutDetailRoi, renderScale forced to 1.0 -- copyMetadata would otherwise carry the base's own value) and run through WorkingImageCache::renderStack, the same function the main composite uses, then EXIF-rotated. Cost is a few hundred px square against a 50 MP settle render, so it rides along with the proxy tick rather than earning its own path.

TWO DELIBERATE APPROXIMATIONS, both documented at their call sites:
  • A masked scope's coverage is sampled BILINEARLY from the mask already rasterized at proxy resolution (sampleMaskRoi), not rebuilt at full res. Coverage is a smooth field, so the cost is a slightly softer mask EDGE inside the patch; rebuilding a 50 MP mask per tick to feed a 170 px window would cost orders of magnitude more than the render it feeds.
  • Geometry (crop / warp) is NOT applied. Crop does not resample, so 1:1 pixels are identical either way; a warp does, but reproducing it for one patch buys less than it costs. The sample point is normalized over the geometry-INPUT (oriented) frame, which is the space ImageView::maskItemToNorm already reports.

COORDINATE MAPPING (Develop/detailroi.h, tests/unit/tst_detailroi.cpp). Three spaces: source (un-rotated WorkingImage), oriented (source turned by the EXIF degrees -- where masks are normalized and where the pick arrives), and displayed (oriented then geometry). The header maps an oriented normalized point to a SOURCE pixel rect, undoing the rotation; the rect inverse is where the sign errors live (at 90 the oriented rect's LEFT edge comes from the source's BOTTOM edge, and the axes swap), so the test checks every mapped corner against an independently written FORWARD mapping rather than against copied numbers, and asserts the rect always lies inside the pixel buffer.

THE PICKER. The target button beside the "1:1" caption arms ImageView's detailPickMode -- the WB dropper's contract exactly: ImageView reports a normalized point (detailPointPicked), the dock disarms, and MW stores it. It joins developToolActive() so an armed click cannot also toggle the loupe zoom, and it sets suppressClickZoom for the release (the dock disarms synchronously, so the gate is already false by then). Esc is handled in MW::developShortcutIntercept, not ImageView, because the tool is armed from the dock and focus is there. A marker is drawn on the image at the sample point, and dragging inside the preview slides it (the drag is resolved by MW, which owns developOrientationDegrees).

VIEW STATE, NOT AN EDIT. The sample point is not in EditParams, not in the sidecar, and not in a preset -- it is which patch you are looking at, not what the image looks like. It resets to unpicked on image change (MW::resetDetailPoint), which is why the preview opens on a "pick a location" prompt rather than a guess.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop Transform Panel (Crop + Perspective)  [UI PROTOTYPE]

TransformPanel (Develop/Transform/) is a compact control strip below the scopes and above the property tree -- the sibling of ScopesView. It is the UI surface for a non-destructive crop/straighten/perspective edit; it carries NO geometry or pixel state. The crop rectangle, straighten angle and perspective homography will live in the edit stack and apply LAST in the pipeline (after the develop ops), so they stay non-destructive and masks map through them. The image-side overlay and the warp engine are wired to the panel's signals in a later increment; for now each control persists its own state and emits a signal (the integration hook).

Layout (redesigned): a property-style gradient HEADER band ("Transform", drawn by an internal GradientHeader QWidget using the same backgroundShade +5 -> -15 gradient as PropertyDelegate) with four trailing BarBtns: [?] tips ("Crop and transform tips"), [E] preview eye (show/ignore the whole transform, previewToggled), [R] reset (clear the crop, straighten and warp to identity, resetRequested; icon reset.png -- "chasing arrows", replacing the old refresh.png) and, at the extreme right, [X] close (close.png, closeRequested). The [X] runs MW::toggleDevelopTransform's hide path, so closing from the header commits the crop session and clears the tool exactly like the action-row Transform button or "R" (Esc still CANCELS via MW::cancelDevelopTransform).

Below it a three-way MODE toggle down the left (Crop / Level / Warp -- a mutually exclusive QButtonGroup; the selected button gets a teal (#1b8a83) background + border). Each mode row carries that mode's controls and its own [R] reset (resetModeRequested, all reset buttons kept

  in the last grid column so they align vertically):
      Crop : an Aspect combo (As shot / 1x1 / 3x2 / 4x3 / 5x7 / 4x5 / 8.5x11 / 16x9 / 16x10 /
             persisted custom aspects / "Add custom aspect..."; userData per item is the w/h
             ratio, 0.0 = As shot/free) + a padlock that locks the ratio + a flip button
             (swap.png, "F") that swaps the crop between landscape and portrait. Locking
             "As shot" constrains drags to the image's native aspect
             (ImageView::cropLockedAspect); flipping honours 1/aspect.
             Flip (ImageView::setCropAspectFlip) rotates the crop box 90 deg by swapping
             its pixel width and height (new width = old height, etc.), keeping the
             centre and scaling down only if the rotated box would not fit the image; a
             locked crop already sits at the ratio so the swap lands on the inverted
             ratio, a free crop just swaps its own dimensions.
             Flip STASH: a straight flip-back must restore the exact pre-flip rect,
             else the scale-to-fit clamping shrinks the crop a little on every
             landscape<->portrait round trip. cropFlipPrevN holds the rect from before
             the last flip and cropFlipResultN what that flip produced; if the crop is
             unchanged since (cropN still == cropFlipResultN, epsilon compare) the next
             flip restores cropFlipPrevN verbatim instead of recomputing. Any manual
             crop edit (resize/draw/pan) moves cropN off cropFlipResultN, so the next
             flip recomputes from the edited rect. The stash is discarded in
             beginCropEdit (new session) and setCropAspect (aspect/lock change breaks
             the pairing).
      Level: a straighten-angle field (-45..45 deg, editingFinished -> levelAngleEntered, applied
             as an absolute straighten). Selecting Level also arms the draw-a-level-line tool.
      Warp : selecting it seeds the 4-point quad (ImageView::beginWarp) for corner dragging.
             Commit the traced quad with Enter/Return or a double-click (ImageView claims those
             keys/clicks via ShortcutOverride while cropWarp is active, emits warpCommitRequested
             -> MW::rectifyDevelopCrop, which stores the warp + suggested crop and returns the
             toggle to Crop). There is no separate Rectify button.

The Aspect combo/lock/flip are enabled only in Crop mode, the angle field only in Level mode.

MW wiring: modeChanged -> setDevelopTransformMode arms the matching ImageView tool (beginCropEdit / beginLevel / beginWarp); levelAngleEntered -> setDevelopLevelAngle; resetModeRequested -> resetDevelopTransformMode clears just that mode's geometry contribution.
