The CROP reset (and the header reset) also calls setAspectAsShot() first: beginCropEdit re-fits a full-frame crop to a LOCKED aspect (cropAspect > 0), so without freeing the aspect the reset to the full frame would just re-inscribe the locked ratio and appear to do nothing.

Shortcuts: "R" toggles the panel (MW::toggleDevelopTransform, developTransformAction, editor- bar button rectangle.png; persists Settings "Develop/transformVisible"). R is a Develop mode local shortcut and does nothing in Preview mode -- see DEVELOP MODE LOCAL SHORTCUTS. Closing the panel has two OPPOSITE outcomes: R / Enter / the action-row button all COMMIT (via toggleDevelopTransform -> exitDevelopCrop: write the overlay crop into the edit stack, show the cropped result); Esc CANCELS via MW::cancelDevelopTransform -- restores the Geometry snapshot taken at enterDevelopCrop (developCropGeometryBackup), drops the overlay WITHOUT reading its rect, and hides the panel. The snapshot/restore is needed because straighten and warp are written to geometry LIVE during a session (applyDevelopLevel / rectifyDevelopCrop), so a cancel must revert them, not merely skip the pending crop. (Enter is special-cased while a WARP quad is being traced: there it commits the quad -- rectify -- not closes; see the contextual-keys note in DEVELOP MODE LOCAL SHORTCUTS.)

Whenever Transform is active, single letters select/act: "C" Crop, "L" Level, "W" Warp, "A" toggles the aspect lock, "F" flips the aspect between landscape and portrait -- and must beat the window-level shortcuts on those keys (e.g. "A" Run Droplet, "C" Compare, "F" the spot/fill tool, which stays available outside a transform session). Because the crop overlay

(ImageView), not the panel, usually holds focus during a transform, these are dispatched by MW::developShortcutIntercept (the qApp-level Develop-mode arbiter that also routes "S" to the spot tool), gated on developCropEditing: it accepts the key's ShortcutOverride (freeing it from the global QAction) and, on the following KeyPress, calls TransformPanel::handleTransformShortcut(key). It runs before Qt's shortcut system and independent of focus, so it works wherever focus sits; the arbiter's value-editor guard excludes text fields (aspect combo, angle field) so typing an aspect/angle still works. TransformPanel::eventFilter still claims the same keys when the panel or one of its non-text controls holds focus (the same handler). A panel-scoped letter QAction is not used because it would be an ambiguous overload with the window-level shortcuts. Custom aspects + lock state + selected mode persist under Settings "Develop/Transform/".

Crop rubber-band tool (ImageView) -- IMPLEMENTED:

Showing the Transform panel (R / button) calls ImageView::beginCropEdit; hiding it calls endCropEdit. KEY RULE (user-specified): the crop tool NEVER changes the view transform -- no zoom, no auto-pan. The user keeps full control of zoom/pan; begin/end only toggle the overlay and leave the image exactly where it is. cropN (normalized image coords) is the source of truth; cropFrameVp (the on-screen frame, viewport px) is DERIVED from it (cropNToVpRect) and tracks the image under the current zoom/pan. Interaction (all guarded by cropActive()):

- drag a HANDLE (cropHitTest 0..7: 4 corners + 4 edge mids) -> resizes the frame over the STATIC canvas (cropResizeFromHandle anchors the opposite corner/edge, clamps to the image's on-screen rect); cropN := cropVpRectToN(frame). No re-stage, no zoom change.

- while the crop is still the WHOLE frame (cropIsFull), the cursor is a crop crosshair and a drag on empty area rubber-bands a brand-new crop (cropDrawNewFrom, cropDrag==kCropDrawNew) -- so you draw the crop directly instead of shrinking the full frame by its handles. Over a handle it reverts to the resize cursor/behaviour.

- Alt/Opt over a rectangle crop shows the "transform" rubber band (red border, circular corner handles, no side handles); an Alt-drag of a corner pulls the free perspective quad.

- drag elsewhere (a sub-crop exists) -> a NORMAL canvas pan (the existing scrollbar pan); the frame stays fixed on screen and the image slides under it, so cropN is recomputed from the unchanged frame. This is how the crop is repositioned -- you move the canvas, not the crop rectangle.

- aspect lock (from the panel, setCropAspect) constrains the frame ratio; "As shot"/ratio 0 is free.

- when the USER zooms (scale()) or the window resizes, the frame is re-derived from cropN (cropSyncFrameFromN) so the crop stays on the same image content. Overlay (cropDrawOverlay, viewport coords in drawForeground): dimmed exterior, rule-of-thirds grid, frame border, corner/edge handles. ImageView emits cropChanged(x,y,w,h) for persistence. The wheel is swallowed during crop (wheel = image navigation, which would strand the overlay).

Warp (4-point perspective) sub-mode -- IMPLEMENTED (interaction only): Holding Alt/Option while dragging a crop CORNER breaks the rectangle into a free quadrilateral (cropWarp=true; cropEnterWarp seeds cropQuadN/cropQuadVp from the rectangle, corners TL,TR,BR,BL). In warp mode only the 4 corners are handles (each drags independently, clamped to the image); cropHitTest / cropResizeFromHandle / cropDrawOverlay / cropSyncFrameFromN all branch on cropWarp. The overlay draws the quad with a bilinear 3x3 guide grid + 4 corner handles and dims outside the polygon. Panning still works (the quad is fixed on screen, recomputed from the moving image; the pan-clamp uses the quad's bounding box, cropFrameBBoxVp). Choosing an aspect, re-entering, or Rectify leaves warp mode.

Perspective-warp PIXEL engine -- IMPLEMENTED (Develop/Transform/croptransform.{h,cpp}): CropTransform::rectifyPerspective(src, quadPx[4], &outCropNorm) warps the whole image so the quad (TL,TR,BR,BL image-pixel coords) becomes an axis-aligned rectangle and returns the corrected QImage (transparent where the warp leaves the original) plus the largest inscribed axis-aligned rectangle (normalized) as the default crop. Math: cv::getPerspectiveTransform (quad -> WxH rect sized by averaged opposite-edge lengths) -> translate into a positive canvas bounded by the warped image corners -> cv::warpPerspective (BGRA, transparent border) -> validity mask (alpha>=250) -> largest-rectangle-in-binary-matrix (histogram+stack, on a downscaled mask). Self-contained (OpenCV only) so it can later be called from the render pipeline. Validated offline vs the same algorithm in cv2 (keystone quad -> exact axis-aligned rect; inscribed rect avoids the transparent corners). ImageView::rectifyCrop drives it on the DISPLAYED image: warp -> setPixmap -> resetFitZoom -> re-enlarge scene -> crop continues on the rectified image (cropN = outCropNorm). This is a DISPLAY-LEVEL preview (the develop pipeline still holds the original; a develop edit re-renders un-warped).

Persistent crop (geometry applied in the render pipeline) -- IMPLEMENTED (Phase A, rectangle crop):  EditStack now carries a per-image Geometry (cropX/Y/W/H, straighten, hasWarp+quad[8], fillCanvas, show), serialized in the sidecar (toJson/fromJson, folded into isIdentity). The Transform panel's Preview eye is a LIVE RESULT TOGGLE (the panel is only on-screen while the crop tool is active, and that view deliberately shows the full frame + overlay, so a plain show/ignore flag would have nothing to toggle): eye ON commits the overlay's crop, drops the overlay (ImageView::endCropEdit) and renders with the geometry applied (MW::developCropShowResult gates the crop-suppression in renderDevelopPreview + the async full-res path); eye OFF re-renders the full frame and re-arms the overlay (beginCropEdit). enter/exitDevelopCrop reset the flag + setPreviewShown. Transform Reset writes an identity Geometry and returns to editing. (Geometry:: show is retained in the model/sidecar for a persistent ignore but is not what the eye drives.) The renderer

applies it LAST -- developCompositeStack runs CropTransform::applyGeometry(out, job.geometry) after the develop ops + EXIF orientation, on the full-output-dimension image, in both the proxy and async full-res paths (StackRenderJob.geometry from DevelopProperties::stackJob). Commit model (Lightroom): MW::developCropEditing gates it -- while the crop tool is being edited the render NEUTRALIZES the geometry so the FULL frame shows for the overlay; committing applies it. MW::enterDevelopCrop (set flag -> render full -> ImageView::beginCropEdit(storedCrop)) and MW::exitDevelopCrop (read cropRect -> endCropEdit -> DevelopProperties::setCurrentGeometry -> clear flag -> render cropped) are driven from toggleDevelopTransform + developDockVisibilityChange. ImageView::setDevelopPreview re-fits when the image dimensions change (crop applied/removed); the same-size path (a develop-slider drag) still just swaps pixels.

Persistent warp (Phase B) -- IMPLEMENTED: the 4-point perspective is a STORED geometry param (hasWarp + quad[8]), non-destructive (no pixmap bake). Two-phase display via the render gate: while the crop tool is active the render suppresses ONLY the crop (resets it to full) but KEEPS the warp, so before Rectify the full frame shows (trace the quad) and after Rectify the corrected canvas shows (set the final crop). MW::rectifyDevelopCrop stores g.hasWarp + g.quad (= the traced corners, normalized in the developed+oriented frame = applyGeometry's input space) + g.crop (the suggested largest-inscribed rect from ImageView::computeRectifyCrop), then re-renders the corrected canvas and restarts the crop overlay on it. exitDevelopCrop keeps the stored warp and updates only the crop. KNOWN LIMIT: re-warping an already-warped image is wrong (the new quad is traced on the warped canvas, but applyGeometry warps the un-warped frame) -- there is no warp-reset UI yet, so fresh-image warp is the supported path.

Level / straighten tool -- IMPLEMENTED: the panel Level button arms ImageView::beginLevel (draw a line along something that should be horizontal/vertical). On release the line's tilt is reduced to the nearest H/V and emitted (levelAngleChanged); MW::applyDevelopLevel adds it to g.straighten (clamped +/-45), auto-fits the crop to the rotation wedges via CropTransform::straightenCropNorm (analytic rotatedRectWithMaxArea, pixel-validated to sit inside the valid region), then re-renders the straightened canvas and restarts the crop overlay -- same two-phase pattern as Rectify. applyGeometry's straighten converts to ARGB32 so the wedges are transparent.

NOT yet done (next increments): a warp-reset (to re-warp); confirm export applies the geometry; content-aware fill. The panel's Crop button is unwired.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Overlays over a Transformed Image (Mask Space vs Displayed Space) -- IMPLEMENTED

Geometry is the LAST render stage, but every mask and spot coordinate is normalized in its INPUT -- the developed, EXIF-oriented, UNCROPPED frame ("mask space") -- so an edit stays glued to the photo content when the crop later changes. The loupe shows the OUTPUT. Everything the overlays do has to cross that gap, and before this was in place a cropped image painted its submasks (and drew its veils, gradients, ellipses, brush cursor and auto-mask guide) in the wrong place -- normalized against the visible edges instead of the full frame.

ONE map resolves it. CropTransform::geometryTransform(srcW, srcH, g) is the POINT twin of applyGeometry: same straighten -> warp -> crop order, in QTransforms instead of pixels (the warp reuses QTransform::quadToQuad for the homography applyGeometry gets from cv). Only the input frame's ASPECT matters, so a proxy-sized frame maps identically. MW pairs each render with the geometry that produced it -- imageView->setDevelopGeometry(mj.geometry, orientedSize) right before setDevelopPreview, in the async completion so the pairing is never a tick stale; a plain ImageView::loadImage resets it to identity. Cheap on repeat: an unchanged geometry early-returns, because the setter invalidates the overlay buffers and it is called on every drag tick.

ImageView::maskNormToItem() (+ maskItemToNorm) is then the ONLY place the two spaces meet: mask-normalized -> geometry-input px -> geometry-output px -> displayed px. With an identity geometry it collapses to a scale by the pixmap size, which is what each overlay used to do inline. Consequences worth knowing:

o maskViewportToNorm / maskNormToViewport go through it, so hit-testing, dragging, brush stamping, the range eyedropper and the WB dropper land in mask space. o The overlay RASTERS (brush + object tint, content/AI coverage tint, MW's whole-mask veil) are built in mask space, sized from maskNormFrameSize(), and painted by maskDrawSpaceImage() -- through the map and CLIPPED to the photo, since the buffer spans the uncropped frame. o The radial ellipse and its handles are computed in mask-frame px (where the render defines them, and where the rotation angle is meaningful), then mapped. o Brush / spot radii are fractions of the mask frame's long edge, measured on screen via maskNormLongEdgeItemPx(), so a crop magnifies the cursor exactly as it magnifies the render. o The luminance auto-mask guide is sampled in mask space, so ensureAutoGuide draws the displayed image back into that frame through the inverse map (what the crop threw away is left mid-grey).

Under a perspective warp the gradient RAMPS (linear/radial) are positioned correctly but their spacing stays linear on screen; the tint rasters, which go through the full projective map, are exact.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > White Balance (Absolute, Kelvin) -- IMPLEMENTED

The Basic panel's white balance is ABSOLUTE, like Lightroom's: Temp is a colour temperature in KELVIN and Tint a green/magenta offset, both resolved against the image's own colour characterisation rather than applied as a blind per-channel nudge. "5500 K" therefore means the same light on every camera body.

COLOUR SCIENCE (Develop/whitebalance.h/.cpp) -- self-contained, no Qt widgets, unit tested by tests/unit/tst_whitebalance.cpp. The model: a white balance names the illuminant the scene was lit by. For a target (Kelvin, tint) the forward chain is

  (K, tint) -> xy chromaticity on the locus, tint displacing it perpendicular
            -> XYZ -> camera RGB (cam.xyzToCam) -> the as-shot WB the decode
               applied by Develop's stage 0 (cam.asShotMul) -> working space (cam.camToWorking)
            = s, the rendered colour of that illuminant's neutral

gains() returns (s.g/s.r, 1, s.g/s.b): the scene-linear gains that make s neutral again. Green is PINNED to 1 so exposure does not drift with temperature -- which is why a tint change moves the red and blue gains, not the green one.

TWO LOCI, crossfaded 4000..5000 K (smoothstep): the PLANCKIAN (blackbody) locus below, because tungsten and candlelight really are incandescent, and the CIE DAYLIGHT locus above, because daylight is NOT a blackbody and the standard D illuminants are defined on it. Using Planckian throughout puts D65 about 550 K and 22 tint units out, so an untouched sRGB file would open reading "7050 K, -22" instead of "6500, 0". The crossfade exists so dragging the slider through 4000 K shows no colour step.

SIGN of both controls: they describe the LIGHT, so the image moves the opposite way. A higher Kelvin says "the light was bluer" and renders the image WARMER; a positive tint assumes a GREENER light and renders the image MORE MAGENTA. This matches the Lightroom sliders under the hand.

THE INVERSE, solve(): given a colour in linear sRGB, find the (K, tint) whose illuminant renders to that chromaticity. NESTED bisection -- for every candidate K the tint is solved exactly first, then that K is judged on the warmth it produces with its own matched tint. Alternating the two searches instead (bisect K, bisect tint, repeat) leaves each K step judged against a stale tint and drifts badly at strong tints: a +80 tint at 3400 K came back as 2742 K / +42. Cost is ~1600 evaluations of a 30-flop function, irrelevant because solve() runs on a dropper click, an Auto pick and once per decode -- never per rendered pixel. ONE function serves three callers: the as-shot estimate (solve for neutral -- the as-shot render IS neutral by construction, so the illuminant that renders neutral is the shooting illuminant), the dropper (solve for the sampled pixel), and Auto.

WHAT CARRIES IT: WorkingImage::cam (struct CameraColor, workingimage.h) holds xyzToCam / asShotMul / camToWorking plus the solved asShotK + asShotTint. RawColor::ToCameraNative fills it from the model matrix and the as-shot multipliers -- which it computes but no longer APPLIES, since cam is now also the input profile Develop's stage 0 reads. InputTransform fills a SYNTHETIC camera for non-raw whose response IS linear sRGB (asShotMul = 1, camToWorking = the sRGB->working matrix it actually applied, xyzToCam = XYZ->sRGB), so a display-referred file resolves to its own white point, D65 ~6500 K, and the sliders behave uniformly. WorkingImageCache::downscaled COPIES cam -- otherwise the interactive proxy would resolve white balance differently from the full-res render.

STORAGE (EditParams): temp is Kelvin and tint the offset, both ABSOLUTE, with temp == 0 the sentinel for "as shot" (NOT 0 K) so an untouched image reads as identity and never marks itself edited. The panel always writes the pair together -- moving Tint alone still commits the resolved Kelvin -- so temp == 0 only ever means a pristine image and the two can never disagree about their reference. Develop uses relativeGains(), which divides by the gains at (asShotK, asShotTint) and so is an EXACT no-op at as-shot even though asShotK came from a bisection fit. wbPreset (a WhiteBalance::Preset as int) is stored only so the dropdown can show what was picked; temp/tint are what render.

THE WB ROW (DevelopProperties::addWhiteBalanceRow, above Temp) -- a DT_None row owning both cells, the same idiom as the Core rows: a dropper button in the caption cell, the preset dropdown in the value cell. The button is a BarBtn -- the SAME class as the Develop action-row tools (Scopes / Transform / Spot), so it inherits their chrome for free: G::css draws it transparent with no border, and BarBtn::setActive() adds the white accent border when armed. Do not hand-roll a stylesheet here; it drifts from the action row the moment that chrome changes. The glyph is DRAWN (dropperIcon) rather than shipped as a png + qrc entry, at G::iconOpacity when idle so the row has no conspicuously brighter icon in it. The ARMED ghost is drawn at FULL opacity in the accent colour: dimming it too would leave the empty socket indistinguishable from the idle glyph.

- DROPPER — armed from the row's button, the Develop menu, or "W" (a Develop mode local shortcut -- New Workspace in Preview; an active Transform owns W for Warp, so W reaches the dropper only when no Transform is up). All three go through DevelopProperties::toggleWbDropper, which refuses to arm with no current image so the key cannot arm a dropper that can only fail. It toggles ImageView's wbPickMode (beginWbPick/endWbPick). Arming it LIFTS THE DROPPER OUT OF THE BUTTON: the mouse cursor becomes a pipette (ImageView::dropperCursor, buildDropperCursor, hotspot at the TIP so the sampled pixel is the one under the point) and the button's icon becomes a faint dashed ghost of the same glyph -- the empty socket. The ghost rather than a blank keeps the button's size so the row does not twitch. TWO gates are needed against the loupe's click-to-toggle-zoom, which fires on mouse RELEASE. wbPickMode joins developToolActive() (as every canvas tool must), but that alone is NOT enough here: the dropper AUTO-DISMISSES, so the dock clears wbPickMode synchronously while handling wbSampled during the PRESS, and by the release developToolActive() is false again. mousePressEvent therefore also latches ImageView::suppressClickZoom, a one-shot consumed at the top of mouseReleaseEvent (consumed there, not at the gate, so an early return cannot leave it set and swallow the NEXT click). Any future disarm-on-press tool needs the same pair. The cursor is likewise RE-ASSERTED from mouseMoveEvent, not just set in beginWbPick -- the base view resets it as the pointer moves, which is why spot/level/crop/ mask all re-apply their cursors there too.

- LOUPE — while the dropper is armed and over the image, ImageView::drawWbLoupe paints a "Pick a target neutral:" panel that follows the tip, modelled on Lightroom's: a 5x5 grid of the pixels under the tip, one image pixel per cell, a crosshair on the centre cell, and an R/G/B readout in PERCENT. The grid is EXACTLY the 5x5 patch onWbSampled averages (kWbLoupeCells keeps them in step) and the readout is that average -- so the panel is a preview of the measurement, not a decorative magnifier. Swatches/readout are DISPLAY values (the developed pixels on screen); the balance itself is solved from the pre-develop scene-linear buffer, which is the right input but not what the user is looking at. While Opt/Alt is held the title swaps to "Pick a skin tone:" and the panel border picks up the accent colour, so which sample the click will take is unmistakable; a permanent dim hint line advertises the modifier the rest of the time. wbAltHeld is read from the MOUSE events (ImageView has no reliable keyboard focus in Develop), so the title catches up on the next movement -- the CLICK always uses its own modifiers, so the action is correct regardless. Pixels are read from the displayed pixmap PER PAINT over the clamped 5x5 rect -- caching the whole pixmap as a QImage would cost ~180MB on a 45MP raw for 25 pixels of data and would go stale when a develop edit re-rendered the preview. It is painted from an overridden paintEvent (after QGraphicsView::paintEvent) rather than drawForeground, whose many early returns cannot guarantee the panel lands on top. The button is enabled whenever there is a current image and deliberately NOT gated on the cached WorkingImage -- buildTree can run before the decode populates the cache, which used to leave the dropper dead for the session. A left click is checked FIRST in mousePressEvent -- while armed the dropper owns the click, ahead of any crop or mask tool -- and emits wbSampled(nx, ny); Esc emits wbPickExited. ImageView only reports WHERE: DevelopProperties::onWbSampled reads that point out of the PRE-DEVELOP WorkingImage (the same buffer Develop's WB acts on, so the solve is exact and independent of any exposure/tone already dialled in), averages a 5x5 patch (one pixel is a poor estimate on a noisy raw), and solves. It auto-dismisses on use, like Lightroom. A black or clipped sample is refused with a popup rather than silently mis-balancing. ON-DEMAND BUFFER: the dropper and Auto WB are the only Develop actions that need the pre-develop WorkingImage BEFORE any edit exists, and for a DISPLAY-REFERRED file nothing has built one: ImageDecoder::applyDevelop skips identity params and MW::applyDevelopPreviewIfEdited skips an unedited image, so a freshly opened JPEG had no cache entry and the dropper answered "not ready to sample yet" until a slider was touched. Both now go through DevelopProperties::ensureWorkingImage, which on a miss emits workingImageNeeded -> MW::ensureWorkingImageNow: a display-referred file is built THERE AND THEN (one InputTransform pass, direct connection, so the value is ready when emit returns); a raw missing its scene-linear image hands off to the async ensureDevelopWork and the caller reports "not ready" as before. Deliberately not eager on selection -- that would pay a full-res float conversion per navigation. ESC disarms it, and that MUST live in MW::developShortcutIntercept (rule 1b), not ImageView: the dropper is armed from the Develop dock, so focus is in the dock and ImageView's keyPressEvent never runs -- the same reason Transform's Esc is arbitrated there. ImageView keeps an Esc handler only as a fallback for when it does hold focus. The dropper is checked BEFORE the Transform block, so the more recently armed tool owns Esc.

- SKIN — Opt/Alt-click samples SKIN instead of a neutral (WhiteBalance:: solveSkin) -- for portraits, where there is often no neutral in frame but always a face. It does NOT drive the sample to grey; it drives it back onto the skin HUE LINE, keeping the skin's own saturation and lightness. Measured skin of very different depths agrees on hue to 0.3 deg in the (warmth, green) plane while chroma magnitude differs 23% (ColorChecker light vs dark skin patches), so hue is the part a cast corrupts and the part safe to correct; a fixed reference chromaticity would desaturate deep skin by about a quarter. GEOMETRY: the same (warmth, green) log axes solve() bisects on, where NEUTRAL IS THE ORIGIN -- so hue is a direction and saturation the distance. The sampled chroma is projected onto the skin direction (keep the along-line part, zero the perpendicular error); that projection is the target T, and the correction is the one carrying the sample to it. It reduces to the neutral case exactly: solving for the component-wise ratio s/T neutralises it, and a neutral pick is just T = (1,1,1) -- so both dropper modes share one solver and cannot drift apart. Exact in one shot; the corrected sample lands ON the line by construction, no iteration. GUARD: a sample more than kSkinHueToleranceDeg (60) off the line, or with almost no chroma, is REFUSED with a message naming the reason rather than applying a wild correction. 60 deg is deliberately generous: skin under a strong tungsten cast is already 14 deg off and a tighter limit would refuse exactly the images the tool exists for. It rejects foliage (67 deg) and blue sky (167). It CANNOT reject a red or orange object -- a red shirt is only 18 deg off, because skin hue genuinely IS orange. The guard catches gross mis-clicks, not subtle ones. An extreme blue cast can push skin past the tolerance; the neutral dropper is the fallback there.

- PRESETS — As Shot, Auto, Daylight 5500/+10, Cloudy 6500/+10, Shade 7500/+10, Tungsten 2850/0, Fluorescent 3800/+21, Flash 5500/0, Custom (Adobe's standard values). As Shot writes the 0 sentinel -- the canonical, exact way back. Auto is a robust neutral estimate (autoWhiteBalance: average the BRIGHT, unclipped pixels above the 80th luminance percentile, so a large saturated subject cannot drag the balance) then solved. Custom is a readout, not a command -- picking it changes nothing; any manual slider move demotes the selection to it.

DBL CLICK a double click on the WB row is As Shot -- the row's equivalent of the double-click-resets-to-default every slider row has (the base class has no default to restore for a DT_None row, so DevelopProperties:: mouseDoubleClickEvent handles it before falling through). The "WB" label carries WA_TransparentForMouseEvents so its clicks reach the tree; the attribute is set on the LABEL, never the cell widget, as it also covers a widget's children and would deafen the dropper. A double click on the Temp or Tint CAPTION resets THAT axis to its as-shot value (resetWbAxisToAsShot), not to the slider's 0 default: 0 is the untouched sentinel, not a temperature, and the base reset would slam Temp to the 2000 K floor. The other axis keeps its value and is rewritten as resolved absolute Kelvin/tint, since the pair always commits together; when both land back on as shot the row restores the 0 sentinel and the As Shot preset, so the image reads as unedited.

  NON-RAW   only As Shot / Auto / Custom are offered, matching Lightroom: a
            rendered JPEG has no camera-neutral reference for the named
            illuminants to mean anything against.

THE KELVIN SLIDER: 2000..50000 K on a LOGARITHMIC ramp (ItemInfo::logScale -> UR_LogScale -> SliderEditor). Linear would bury every useful temperature in the first sixth of the track. The slider's integer position is a point on the ramp, not the value; valueFromPos/posFromValue convert and everything outside SliderEditor still sees real Kelvin, rounded to 10 K (finer resolution is meaningless and reads as noise). Above 25000 K the daylight branch is extrapolated -- well behaved, but no real illuminant lives there. Temp's gradient runs blue -> yellow WITH the value, which is correct: a higher Kelvin renders the image warmer.

Sections (built via PropertyEditor::addItem):

- Scope — the band above the tree (scopeheader.cpp / scopeheaderlab.cpp): Global plus one row per mask, with -/+ and the per-row [v] menu. Selecting a row only chooses which scope the sliders EDIT -- every enabled scope is composited, per the "Scope & mask model" subsection above, and the whole stack persists per-image in the XMP sidecar. HISTORY: an early SCAFFOLD stored "scopes as ALTERNATIVES" per-machine under QSettings "Develop/Layers/<name>/..." (only the selected one active). Its helpers (readLayerList / setCurrentLayer / layerRootPath / layerValue) were deleted in the 2026-07-30 rename.

- Basic — WB (dropper + preset dropdown), Temp, Tint, Exposure, Contrast, Highlights, Shadows, Whites, Blacks, Texture, Clarity, Dehaze. Integer -100..100 except Exposure (EV, -5.00..5.00), Temp (KELVIN, 2000..50000, log ramp) and Tint (-150..150). All default to 0 (identity). See "White balance (absolute, Kelvin)" below for the WB row.

- Color — Red, Green, Blue (per-channel gain); Hue, Saturation, Vibrance, Luminance (global HSL). Integer -100..100 sliders, all default 0 (identity).

- Detail — Sharpening (sharpenAmount, 0..150 -> 0..1.5, default 0) + Radius (sharpenRadius, 50..300 -> 0.50..3.00 PIXELS at full res, default 1.0) + Detail (sharpenDetail, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 25) + Masking (sharpenMasking, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 0): capture sharpening, an edge-gated unsharp mask (see #8.5 above). Radius/Detail/Masking are inert until Amount is non-zero. Opt + a Masking DRAG shows the gate in grayscale (see "Seeing the mask" under #8.5). THE ONLY control in Develop measured in absolute pixels, so it is judged at 1:1 -- everything else is a fraction of the image. Also Denoise (localDenoiseLuma, luminance NR) + Denoise Color (localDenoiseChroma, chroma NR) -- two 0..100 sliders (mapped to 0..1), default 0. Local, maskable, post-demosaic. Distinct from the Global scope's "Denoise raw" (PMRID pre-demosaic, denoiseLuma/denoiseChroma). The denoise pair MOVED here from Effects (2026-08-20): NR and sharpening are one decision, so they share a panel as they do in Lightroom.

Effects  Vignette (vignetteExposure, EV -5.00..5.00, default 0) + Vignette Feather (vignetteFeather, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 50): a global radial corner-exposure falloff (see #8 above); shaped vignettes use masks. Also Grain (grainAmount, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 0) + grain Size (grainSize, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 25) + grain Roughness (grainRoughness, 0..100 -> 0..1, default 50): monochromatic film grain (see #9 above); size and roughness are inert until amount is non-zero.

Each scope maps onto one EditParams (DevelopProperties::editParams()). Binding a value change to a re-decode is deferred: itemChange() persists the value to settings and emits paramsChanged() so the hook is ready.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Curves Panel (Tone Curve)

Lightroom's Tone Curve, as its own Develop section (DevelopProperties::addCurves, header "CurvesHeader", preview group PV_Curves, EditParams::Group::Curves), placed directly under Basic because that is the order it is reached in.

TWO MODES over one square plot (Develop/Properties/curveeditor.{h,cpp}), with the image's histogram drawn behind it:

  Point       the free-form curve. A channel combo picks RGB -- the COMPOSITE, applied
              to every channel -- or Red / Green / Blue, which layer on top of the
              composite for that channel alone. Drag a point; click empty space to add
              one (it lands ON the current curve, so adding an anchor changes nothing
              until it is moved); double-click a point to delete it; double-click the
              background to reset the channel. Endpoints are pinned in x but FREE in y
              -- that is the black-point / white-point move. Max 16 points per channel.

Parametric  the curve Basic's tone sliders already produce, drawn from Develop::ParametricCurve. Dragging inside one of the four tonal bands writes that band's EXISTING Basic slider (blacks | shadows | highlights | whites, in x order) and moves the slider itself; double-clicking a band zeroes it.

THERE ARE NO PARAMETRIC PARAMS. The Curves panel is a SECOND VIEW of Basic's tone controls, not a second tone system -- Winnow already had Lightroom's four parametric regions as Basic sliders driven by the tone splits, and duplicating them would have left two sets of fields that could disagree about the same image. Which mode you are looking at, and which channel, are UI state and are deliberately not in the recipe.

THE TONE SPLITS LIVE IN TWO PLACES, ON PURPOSE. The three split handles (blacks | shadows | highlights | whites -- see "Develop scopes") appear BOTH under the histogram scope and under this panel's plot in Parametric mode. One set of params (toneShadowCenter / toneCrossover / toneHighlightCenter), two ToneRegionSlider instances; DevelopProperties::onToneSplitsChanged cross-pushes to whichever did not originate the change (setPositions emits nothing, so it cannot loop). They were not MOVED out of the scopes strip because the strip is where they align with real histogram data; they were not left there alone because G cycles the strip away entirely, which would have made them unreachable while the panel that depends on them was open.

CURVE MATH. Develop/tonecurve.h, header-only beside calibrate.h / colorgrade.h and unit-tested by tst_tonecurve. MONOTONE CUBIC HERMITE (Fritsch-Carlson), not Catmull-Rom: a plain cubic overshoots between closely spaced control points, and an overshoot in a tone curve is a locally INVERTED tone -- a bright halo inside a shadow ramp. The limiter is the whole reason for the choice, so

tst_tonecurve::monotoneControlPointsGiveMonotoneCurve is the test not to "tidy" away. Past x = 1 the curve continues with its END SLOPE rather than clamping, so pulling the white point down scales the ~3 stops of highlight headroom the LUT spans instead of flattening it into one value.

PIPELINE. Stage 5a: composed into the SAME perceptual tone LUT that already bakes contrast and the region lifts (Develop::buildPointCoeffs), after them, matching Lightroom's order -- so the curve costs LUT-build time and nothing per pixel. PointCoeffs::toneLut became toneLut[3][kLutSize], one table per channel, because the per-channel curves differ; the three are byte-identical when only the composite is in play, and the hot loop does the same single lookup either way. The parametric shape itself was factored into buildToneShape / applyToneShape in develop.cpp so ParametricCurve (what the panel DRAWS) and buildPointCoeffs (what actually RENDERS) cannot drift apart.

DATA. EditParams gains curveN[4] + curveX[4][16] + curveY[4][16] -- fixed-size arrays, not a container, so operator== stays defaulted, the struct stays cheap to copy per render and per history snapshot, and sanitizeParams stays numeric. UNLIKE the tone splits, the curve IS a primary control and DOES count towards isIdentity(). One sidecar key, "curves", holds ToneCurve::encode's compact string ("ch:x,y,...;ch:..." with identity channels omitted, so an untouched image adds nothing) -- and because MW::developCompositeStack keys the per-scope render cache on paramsToJson's output, adding that key is also what gives the curve correct cache invalidation.

THE ONE ENCODING. ToneCurve::encode / decode are shared by the sidecar, the preset collector and DevelopPresets::assignParam, so the two key namespaces the adjustment checklist warns about cannot drift here. Presets carry TWO leaves -- "curveRgb" (composite) and "curveChannels" (R/G/B) -- because a look built on the composite curve is often wanted without the colour crossover that came with it; each is encoded over a copy with the other channels reset, and assignParam merges each key back into only its own channels, so ticking one never clears the other.

MULTI-IMAGE. The curve cannot go in kFloatFields (that table is scalar member pointers). It gets one pseudo-field, "curves", with curvesDiffer / copyCurves as the two halves -- the same discipline the table enforces for scalars, and the thing to remember if the curve ever grows another channel.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Calibrate Panel (RGB Primaries)

Lightroom's Calibration panel: a hue shift + saturation scale for each of the three PRIMARIES of the working space (DevelopProperties::addCalibrate, header "CalibrateHeader", preview group PV_Calibrate), sitting between Color and Color Grade so the sections read in pipeline order.

NOT the same thing as the Color panel's Red/Green/Blue. Those are per-channel linear GAINS folded into the white-balance vector (develop.cpp buildPointCoeffs) -- a cruder second white balance. Calibration re-points the primaries themselves. Both exist; the gains stay until Calibrate has been used in anger, the same "keep the old control until the new one proves out" approach Color Grade got. Lightroom's "Shadows Tint" is deliberately NOT reproduced: the Color Grade shadows wheel already does that job with more freedom.

UI. Three Red / Green / Blue checkboxes on one row (calActiveMask, UI state not EditParams) then one shared wheel, mirroring Color Grade's grammar.

- The wheel (Develop/Properties/primarywheel.*) draws a dot per primary, but unlike the grade wheel a dot is a DELTA from a home angle (red 0 deg, green 120, blue 240), shown with a faint home spoke. Angle swept from home = hue (-100..100 -> +/-30 deg); radius about the mid point = sat (-100 = grey, +100 = 2x chroma).

- A drag moves every CHECKED primary by the same delta; double-click returns them to 0/0. HOLD SHIFT for a fine drag: the dots move by kFineGain (1/4) of the cursor's movement since an anchor taken when Shift goes DOWN, so entering fine mode mid-drag never jumps them (releasing Shift resumes absolute tracking, which rejoins the dots to the cursor). The anchor/blend state lives in HueSatWheel, so the grade and range wheels can adopt the same gesture for free.

- NUDGING is the Hue and Saturation SLIDERS below the wheel (keys "calHue" / "calSat"), not the wheel: a mouse cannot pick out one unit of a +/-30 degree disc, and the wheel shows no numbers at all. They write every checked primary (setCalAxis) and READ the lowest checked one (firstActivePrimary) -- the exact relationship Color Grade's Luminance slider has to ITS wheel, and consistent because a wheel drag also lands ONE value on every checked primary. Arrow keys step one unit, PageUp/Dn ten (Slider::keyPressEvent). The pair is kept in sync in three directions: itemChange -> setCalAxis -> refreshCalibrateRow moves the dot with the slider; onPrimaryWheelChanged -> refreshCalibrateRow moves the numbers with the drag; and the R/G/B checkboxes call it so the sliders re-point at the newly checked primary. Every push is wrapped in isPopulating or setValue echoes back through itemChange as an edit.

- KEYBOARD NUDGE ON THE WHEEL ITSELF WAS REJECTED. MW::developShortcutIntercept yields arrows only to a focused QAbstractSlider / QAbstractSpinBox / QLineEdit; a focused wheel is none of those, so rule 2 would swallow them as selection keys. Making it work means widening that guard AND giving a wheel focus + a focus cue, for something the sliders already do.

- THE CHECKBOXES ADJUST NOTHING. They only pick which primaries the wheel writes, which is easy to misread as "the R/G/B control" and then to report the panel as unwired. No caption was added (the row stays clean); instead the section's [:] menu carries CALIBRATE HELP -> Docs/developcalibratehelp.html. This was the FIRST such item; every band in the dock now carries one -- see "Develop panel help pages" below.

ALL THREE hue/sat wheels share one base, HueSatWheel (Develop/Properties/ huesatwheel.*), extracted when this panel added the third: it owns the cached HSV disc, centre/radius, paintDisc and the hue/sat <-> pixel mapping, leaving each subclass only its dots, interaction and signals. discMargin is the one thing they size differently -- a wheel that labels handles OUTSIDE the rim needs the room. Subclasses: ColorGradeWheel, ColorRangeWheel, PrimaryWheel.

Data + pipeline. EditParams gains six fields (calRed/Green/Blue x Hue/Sat), its own Group::Calibrate, and a per-scope showCalibrate preview flag. The math is header-only in Develop/calibrate.h (unit-tested by tst_calibrate); buildPointCoeffs turns the six into one 3x3 (c.calMat / c.calActive) and applyPointOps applies it in the fused pass right after channelGain, BEFORE the tone curve -- calibration is a change of colour space and belongs upstream of anything perceptual.

WHY THE ROWS ARE NORMALISED. Each primary's unit vector is rotated about the neutral axis and chroma-scaled, and those become the matrix COLUMNS; then every ROW is scaled to sum to 1. That row normalisation is what keeps grey grey, because (M*(1,1,1))_i is the sum of row i. It is an easy slip to "fix" this by normalising the columns instead (so each primary keeps its own neutral component) -- that sounds right, is tidier, and does not preserve white at all. tst_calibrate::rowsSumToOne pins it.

THE SLIDERS INTERACT, BY DESIGN. Turning red toward green gives reds a green response, and holding white fixed forces green's own response down to compensate. That is what a real primary change does, and Lightroom's calibration sliders interact the same way. What does survive is direction: an untouched primary's column stays parallel to its own axis and is only rescaled.

THE SECTION MUST BE ADDED TO EVERY PER-GROUP SWEEP. Three were missed when the panel landed and were fixed later: refreshPreviewButtons (the Calibrate eye never redrew as eye_off), resetSection(PV_Scope) ("Reset all" left the calibration in place), and buildTree's eye-pointer clear (calibrateEyeBtn/colorGradeEyeBtn dangled between removeRows and the rebuild). None of them affected the render, which is exactly why they survived -- a new section is only done when it appears in ALL of them.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Color Grade Panel (Colour Grading)

A minimalist take on Lightroom's Color Grading, added as its own Develop section (DevelopProperties::addColorGrade, header "ColorGradeHeader", preview group PV_ColorGrade) ALONGSIDE the Color panel (RGB + global HSL), which stays. Distinct function from the Color panel: grading selects pixels by TONE and adds a tint; the HSL sliders remap existing hues globally.

NAMING (historical). This section was called "Color Mix" everywhere in code until it was renamed -- the name had been claimed for a planned Lightroom-style 8-band HSL mixer that was then cancelled, leaving the grading panel wearing it. The header read "Color Grade" while groupLabel(), the section context menu and the Save Preset dialog still said "Color Mix", so history rows named a panel that did not exist. Everything is now ColorGrade. The per-scope preview flag's SIDECAR KEY moved from "showColorMix" to "showColorGrade"; editstack.h reads the legacy spelling first and lets the current one win, so pre-rename sidecars keep their preview state. The nine value keys were always grade* and did not change.

THE 8-BAND MIXER IS CANCELLED, not deferred. The Color Range mask (rangemask.h, colorCoverage) already selects by hue + saturation, with arbitrary windows, multiple samples, combine modifiers, submasks and the full adjustment set -- strictly more than a fixed 8-band H/S/L mixer. Building the mixer would mean maintaining a second, weaker selection system. Its one advantage (speed) is instead given to the mask as one-click hue presets. Do not resurrect it, and do not treat "Color Mix" as a free name.

THOSE HUE PRESETS: the Color Range mask's rows carry eight colour CHIPS (Red, Orange, Yellow, Green, Aqua, Blue, Purple, Magenta -- Lightroom's band set). Clicking one calls DevelopProperties::applyColorRangeHuePreset, which REPLACES the mask's samples with a synthetic one at the band's centre hue (sat 0.6, so it sits mid-radius on the wheel with room to widen either way) and sets hueLo/hueHi to the 22.5 deg half-band -- exactly what the pipette would have written given a perfectly representative pixel. No new selection maths: RangeMask::colorCoverage already takes a sample list plus hue/sat windows. Replace, not append, because a chip means "select this colour". Shift-clicking the image still adds further samples on top.

ISOLATING THE SELECTION is the pre-existing "Grayscale background" item on the action- row tint button's context menu (G::maskOverlayGrayscale, formerly the Mask panel's "Gray" chip): it desaturates the image while any overlay is shown, so the veil separates from the picture. It is a VIEW effect in ImageView/ScaledPixmapItem only. Do not add a second, competing isolation overlay -- the combine-modifier work settled on ONE overlay colour and one outcome preview, and a rival path would undo

  that.

UI (compact, per the mockup): four Shadows / Midtones / Highlights / GLOBAL checkboxes on one row, one shared hue/saturation wheel, a Luminance slider, then Blending + Balance below a divider.

- The wheel (Develop/Properties/colorgradewheel.*) is a directly-embedded index widget (setIndexWidget on a full-width first-column-spanned row), NOT a delegate editor. Hue = angle, saturation = radius; it draws all three range dots (active bright, inactive dim) and a drag moves EVERY checked range to the cursor's hue/sat -- so one checked range edits it alone, all three checked = a global tint.

- The checkboxes are UI selection state (gradeActiveMask), not EditParams: they pick which range(s) the wheel + Luminance slider write. Default: Mid checked.

- Luminance (key "gradeLum") writes to every checked range; on load it shows the lowest checked range's value.

- GLOBAL is a fourth range that is NOT tone-selective: it applies its tint and luminance to every pixel at weight 1, on top of the three windowed ranges. In the pipeline it is simply range [3] with no tonal weight (see applyPointOps).

- Blending (0..100, default 50) and Balance (-100..100, default 0) shape the three windows and are PANEL-WIDE, not per-range. Blending pushes the shadow and highlight edges apart (< 50, a broader pure-midtone band) or together (> 50, more overlap); Balance slides both, positive favouring highlights as in Lightroom. ColorGrade::gradeSplitPoints derives the two split points from them.

THE DEFAULTS ARE A COMPATIBILITY CONTRACT. Blending 50 / Balance 0 must return kGradeShadowEnd / kGradeHighStart unchanged -- those are the fixed splits the panel used before the sliders existed, so anything else silently re-renders every image graded before them the first time it is reopened. Pinned by

tst_colorgrade::defaultBlendingMatchesLegacySplit; do not "tidy" the arithmetic in gradeSplitPoints without rerunning it.

Data + pipeline. EditParams gains nine grade fields (gradeShadow/Mid/High x Hue/Sat/ Lum), its own Group::ColorGrade (reset/preview independent of the Color group), and a per-scope showColorGrade preview flag; all persist through editstack.h (forward- tolerant, so old sidecars load as identity). The math lives header-only in Develop/ colorgrade.h (gradeTintVector + gradeTonalWeights -- no Qt/OpenCV, unit-tested by tst_colorgrade); Develop::buildPointCoeffs/applyPointOps consume it as stage 5d, folded into the same fused point-op pass as HSL (per pixel: split perceptual lightness into three smooth tonal weights, then add each range's weighted zero-luma tint + weighted luminance gain). Strengths are tuning constants (kGradeTintStrength / kGradeLumStrength) in develop.cpp.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop Presets (the Presets Dock)

Named, reusable develop recipes (Lightroom's Presets panel). ONLY USER-DEFINED presets exist -- Winnow ships none. A preset is created from the current image (Cmd+Shift+N, the Develop dock context menu, the Presets dock [+] button, or that dock's right-click "New Preset..."), and applied by clicking it in the Presets dock.

THE APPLY IS A MERGE. A preset holds only the settings that were TICKED when it was saved, so applying it writes those and leaves everything else on the image alone: a Temp-only preset moves Temp and nothing else. This is Lightroom's behaviour and it is what makes small, stackable presets useful. Mechanically it falls out of the store: DevelopPresets::read reports only the keys QSettings actually holds, and DevelopProperties::mergePreset writes only those.

THE TARGET IS THE ACTIVE SCOPE. The preset records which scope it was captured from (sourceScope) but that is display metadata only. The values land on whatever scope is selected in the Develop panel when the preset is clicked, so a preset saved from a Sky mask can be dropped onto Global, or onto a different image's Subject mask. Nothing can fail to match. The per-image items are the exception: the raw decode engine, the raw NR pair and the tone splits belong to no scope (they are Global by definition) and the crop / level / warp / spots are geometry, so those always go to scope 0 / the Geometry regardless of what is active.

THE MASK DOES NOT TRAVEL. A mask's geometry is specific to the source image's content, so a preset captured from a mask scope carries its ADJUSTMENTS only. There is no scaffolding for mask-carrying presets.

The dock. Develop, History and Presets are ONE TOOL: all three are tabbed together in the right area and are shown / hidden together (createPresetsDock, and every setDevelopDockVisibility / setDevelopPanelEnabled / setOperationMode / showDevelopDock site mirrors History's handling). Develop is always raised as the front tab, so Presets is reached by clicking its tab, by "P" (menu View > Develop Presets Panel), or by the colour-wheel Preset button in the Develop action row. P is a Develop MODE LOCAL key (developShortcuts / developShortcutIntercept), NOT a QKeySequence -- QKeySequence("P") would clash with the global Pick. Like F3-F9 and H it shows / raises rather than toggles. Adding the dock bumped winnowStateVersion to 3 and needed the usual new-dock checklist (presets_white.png + qrc + graphicFor / dockFor + dockTextNames + a dockTabToolTip case). FULL SCREEN is the one exception to "shown / hidden together": Preferences > Full screen defaults has its own "Show develop presets" (and "Show develop history") checkbox, so each dock can be set independently for full screen (MW::toggleFullScreen / fullScreenDocks.isPresets, persisted as isFullScreenPresets). Presets and History are still applied BEFORE Develop there, and Develop raised after, to keep Develop the front tab.

The list (Develop/Presets/presetsview.{h,cpp}, PresetsView + PresetDelegate) is deliberately the same interaction as the History list beside it, and a trimmed copy of its code: a QListWidget with a hover-fill delegate, a DEBOUNCED hover (kHoverDelayMs) so a fast sweep does not queue a render per row, and a click emitted a tick late because applying rebuilds the Develop tree from inside the row's own event handler. Hovering previews the preset applied on the loupe (previewStack, the same override History uses -- proxy render only, no full-res settle); leaving the list, or tabbing the dock away, restores the real state. The one thing NOT previewed is the raw decode engine: switching it forces a full raw re-decode, far too expensive for a passing cursor, so it is applied on click only.

Applying records ONE history entry, labelled with the scope that received it ("Sky: Preset: Warm Portrait"), so a preset is undone by clicking the History row above it. An empty list shows a "click + to create one" placeholder row rather than an empty box.

Right-clicking a preset gives Update with Current Settings / Rename... / Delete (and New Preset... anywhere in the list). The view owns the confirm / input prompts so DevelopProperties only ever receives a decided action. "Update" keeps the preset's SHAPE -- the same ticked settings -- and refills their values from the current image's active scope, so updating never silently widens a preset.

Dialog (Dialogs/savedeveloppresetdlg.{h,cpp}, SaveDevelopPresetDlg). A preset-name field, a Scope combo, and a collapsible, scrollable checklist grouped exactly like the Develop panel's Scope tree:

  Global settings   a non-checkable container, and PER IMAGE (the Scope combo does
                    not affect it). Leaves: Raw demosaic (the decode engine,
                    G::decodeRawEngine), Raw noise reduction (Global denoiseLuma +
                    denoiseChroma), Histogram tonal ranges (the three tone splits),
                    Crop, Level (straighten) and Warp (each a SEPARATE leaf, from
                    the per-image Geometry), Spots (all spot heals). Raw demosaic +
                    raw NR only apply to raw files, and raw NR (PMRID) is the
                    Winnow engine only; both stay listed but are pre-checked only
                    when relevant.

Basic / Curves /  one checkable tri-state group per Develop section, each with

  Color /           its own [All] / [None] buttons. Leaves follow the panel's own

Calibrate /

Color Grade /

Detail /

  Effects           controls, so a control that folds several params in is ONE
                    tick: White balance = temp + tint, each Color Grade range =
                    its hue + sat + lum (Global included), Blending + Balance share
                    one tick, each Calibrate primary = its hue + sat,
                    Sharpening folds in radius + detail + masking,
                    Denoise = luma + chroma, Vignette folds in its feather, Grain
                    folds in size + roughness. Curves SPLITS rather than folds --
                    "Tone curve (RGB)" and "Tone curve (Red / Green / Blue)" are
                    separate ticks, because a look built on the composite curve is
                    often wanted without the per-channel colour that came with it.

Items that differ from their default IN THE SELECTED SCOPE are pre-checked and their group pre-expanded, so the user can Save immediately or fine-tune. Moving the Scope combo does not rebuild the tree -- the leaf keys are identical in every scope, so it just re-applies that scope's changed set (changedPerScope, computed by DevelopProperties::changedLeavesForScope). Buttons: Select all / Select none / Cancel / Save (Save is disabled until the name is non-empty; an existing name prompts to overwrite). Right-clicking the checklist pops Expand all / Collapse all.

GOTCHA: the global stylesheet sets "QPushButton { min-width: 100px }", which would force the dialog absurdly wide via the per-group [All] / [None] pairs. They clear it with a local "min-width: 0" -- the same override MaskPanel needs.

The dialog is UI-only: it renders a model of PresetGroup { title, checkable, showAllNone, leaves } and reports back the preset name, the chosen scope index and the checked leaf keys per group. It knows nothing of the develop data model or the settings layout.

Store (Develop/Presets/developpresets.{h,cpp}, DevelopPresets). Owns ALL preset persistence -- names / read / write / rename / remove -- so DevelopProperties never touches QSettings for presets. Layout:

  Develop Presets/<name>/sourceScope  = "Global" | "Sky" | ...   (display only)

Develop Presets/<name>/Global/<key> = the per-image settings Develop Presets/<name>/Edits/<key>  = the captured scope's adjustments Develop Presets/<name>/Global/spots = a QSettings array of FillSpot paramsJson

Leaf keys reuse the EditParams JSON field names and store the RAW param values (the source of truth, e.g. denoise 0..1), so applying is a straight assignment back -- DevelopPresets::assignParam mirrors EditStack::paramsFromJson, and is kept apart from DevelopProperties::applyKeyToParams, which takes the DOCK's slider keys and div-scales them (0..100 -> 0..1). Geometry is stored as crop ("x,y,w,h"), straighten and warp ("q0..q7"). Writing a preset first clears any prior group of the same name (overwrite). "Edits" was called "Global edits" in the first, save-only build, when a preset could only come from the Global scope; read() still accepts that name, and ignores the per-scope "Mask N" groups that build also wrote.

Shortcut wiring. Two separate actions:

- developSavePresetAction (SAVE, Cmd+Shift+N). Unlike the bare-key Develop actions (dispatched by developShortcutIntercept, which arbitrates only unmodified keys), this is a modified combo owned by no other action, so it carries a REAL QKeySequence ("Ctrl+Shift+N"). It is enabled only in Develop mode -- setOperationMode tracks the mode (a disabled QAction's shortcut does not fire) and syncDevelopMenuEnabled greys the menu item in Preview.

- presetsDockVisibleAction (SHOW the dock, "P" + the action-row Preset button + View menu). P is globally Pick, so like the other bare Develop keys it is dispatched by developShortcutIntercept via the developShortcuts table (loadDevelopShortcuts), NOT a real QKeySequence.

MASK TINT BUTTON. The action row's fourth button toggles the mask overlay tint, the same thing "O" does (MW::toggleMaskOverlay -> ImageView::toggleMaskTint), and gives that otherwise invisible key a home in the UI. It carries NO glyph: it IS the swatch -- MW::refreshDevelopMaskTintBtn paints a 12x12 rounded chip filled with G::maskOverlayColor into the 16x16 BarBtn (12 so the active border has room), dimmed to G::iconOpacity while the tint is off. It is repainted from two signals, so the swatch and the border stay true no matter what changed the state: ImageView's maskTintVisibilityChanged (the key, the scope menu, the slider auto-hide) and DevelopProperties::maskOverlayRefreshRequested (the colour changed).

NOTHING TO TINT (Global scope). The tint only exists while a mask tool is expanded or a committed mask's composite is on display (ImageView::maskTintAvailable() == maskEditMode || !scopeMaskTint.isNull()). On the Global scope neither holds, and the old button just did nothing when clicked. Now the swatch paints HOLLOW (outline in the overlay colour) at half G::iconOpacity, and a left-click (or "O") says why in a popup instead of no-op'ing. The button stays ENABLED on purpose: its right-click menu is meant to be reachable without a mask, and a disabled QToolButton would swallow that too. A third signal, ImageView::maskTintAvailabilityChanged, repaints it -- emitted from beginMaskEdit / endMaskEdit / setScopeMaskTint / clearScopeMaskTint, each guarded so only a real null <-> non-null transition fires (setScopeMaskTint runs every drag tick). The scope menu already omits its "Show mask overlay" row when unavailable (ScopeHeader::maskOverlayAvailable), so the two agree.

RIGHT-CLICK on it (MW::showDevelopMaskTintMenu) is where the overlay's two APPEARANCE settings now live -- the colour, and the grayscale background. THE ONLY PLACE: the Mask panel's swatch + "Gray" row was removed on 2026-08-15, because those settings describe the veil rather than any submask, and putting them in the submask editor made them unreachable without a mask open (and duplicated once the button existed). Colours and names come from MaskPanel::overlayColours() / overlayColourNames() (the palette stayed with that class); both settings are applied through DevelopProperties::setMaskOverlayColour / setMaskOverlayGrayscale, which update the G:: flag, persist it (Develop/maskOverlayColor, Develop/maskOverlayGrayscale) and ask for the right redraw -- a colour rebuilds the veil (maskOverlayRefreshRequested), grayscale is view-only so it just repaints (maskOverlayRepaintRequested).

Active border. The Develop action-row buttons that toggle a panel/tool -- Scopes, Transform, Spot, Mask tint, Preset -- carry a WHITE border while their panel is shown / the tool is armed, like a conventional checked toolbutton (BarBtn::setActive). It was G::appleBlue; white was chosen because the blue was too quiet against the dark panel to read as "this tool is on" at a glance. MW keeps them as members and calls setActive from the toggle handlers (toggleDevelopScopes, toggleDevelopTransform + cancelDevelopTransform, spotActiveChanged) so the border tracks the real state even when toggled by the keyboard (H / R / Esc / S). The Preset button is the awkward one, because "its panel is up" means "the Presets dock is the FRONT TAB", and a tabified dock keeps isVisible() == true when a sibling tab is selected -- so visibilityChanged never fires on a tab switch. MW::updateDevelopPresetBtn is therefore driven from three places: presetsDockVisibilityChange, QMainWindow::tabifiedDockWidgetActivated (the only reliable signal for a tab click) and showPresetsDock (raise() on an already "visible" dock emits nothing). Export -> lightning is still a stub with no border.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Copy / Paste Develop Settings (the Develop Clipboard)

Lightroom's Copy Settings / Paste Settings. AN UNNAMED PRESET, and deliberately not a second mechanism: Copy opens the SAME checklist dialog and captures with the same DevelopProperties::buildPreset; Paste merges with the same mergePreset. Everything the presets section says therefore holds unchanged -- only the ticked settings are written, the target is the ACTIVE scope, the per-image items (raw engine, raw NR, tone splits, crop / level / warp) go to scope 0 / the geometry whatever is active, spots APPEND rather than replace, and the mask does not travel. Pasting is one history step ("Sky: Paste settings"), so it is undone by clicking the row above it in History.

The buffer is a single slot that every Copy overwrites, held by the SAME store class (DevelopPresets) under its own QSettings root, "Develop Clipboard/buffer": read/write/migrate are shared with presets via readGroup/writeGroup, so the copy buffer gets the schema version and forward-tolerance for free, and cannot appear in names() or collide with a preset the user names "clipboard". It survives a restart, as Lightroom's does. DevelopPreset also carries sourceImage (display only), which is what lets the UI say WHAT would be pasted.

  UI.

- Dialog: SaveDevelopPresetDlg::Mode::CopySettings -- the same groups, Scope combo, pre-checked "differs from default" and All / None, minus the name field (there is nothing to name) and its overwrite check. Title "Copy Develop Settings", button "Copy". One dialog class: "pick a set of develop settings, from a scope" is the same question either way.

- Menu: Utilities > Develop, above Save Develop Preset. Paste RENAMES itself to "Paste Develop Settings from IMG_1234.NEF" in syncDevelopMenuEnabled, so the menu answers "have I copied anything?" without being opened twice.
- The Develop dock context menu carries the same three items.

- Shortcuts: Cmd/Ctrl+Opt/Alt+C and +V, real QKeySequences (like Save Preset's Cmd+Shift+N -- the bare-key arbiter only handles UNMODIFIED keys), enabled only in Develop mode by setOperationMode. Lightroom's own keys are Shift+Cmd+C/V, but Shift+Cmd+C is already "Copy images" in Winnow and two enabled actions on one sequence is an ambiguous shortcut, so the Opt/Alt pair was taken instead.

GOTCHA: Paste is NOT disabled on an empty clipboard. syncDevelopMenuEnabled runs on aboutToShow, and a disabled QAction's shortcut does not fire, so greying it there would leave Cmd+Opt+V dead until the menu happened to be opened again after a copy. The slot reports "No develop settings have been copied yet" instead.

MULTI-IMAGE. Paste lands on the WHOLE selection, not just the current image -- see the next section. Paste is the whole-object path: every other selected image gets the same mergePreset onto its scope 0, so the copied crop, straighten, warp and spot heals travel with it exactly as they do on the current image. The confirmation says how many images were written ("Pasted develop settings from IMG_1234.NEF onto 12 images"), since silence about a bulk overwrite is how a user loses forty recipes.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Multi-image Editing (Edits Apply to the Whole Selection)

With more than one image selected, EVERY develop edit made on the current image is copied to every other selected image -- Lightroom's Auto Sync, always on. Paste Settings and Apply Preset do the same. The Develop dock carries an AMBER warning row ("Multiple images will have edits applied.") whenever the selection is larger than one, because the images being changed are off-screen and the change is otherwise invisible until the user navigates to them. Amber, not red: the edit does work, it just works on more than the visible image -- red is reserved for a show stopper (see "Develop alerts and the greyed panel").

THE RULES (DevelopProperties, "Multi-image editing" block in the header):

- ABSOLUTE, not relative. The target gets the same VALUE, not the same delta. So Temp lands as the same Kelvin everywhere (temp/tint are absolute -- see White balance above) and Exposure +0.5 means +0.5 EV on every image, whatever each started from. A delta rule would be unpredictable on a mixed selection where some images are already edited.

- GLOBAL SCOPE ONLY. Only scope 0's EditParams travel. A mask scope's params describe pixels chosen from THIS image's content, so an edit made inside a mask scope stays on the current image -- the same rule presets follow (the mask never travels). Mask geometry, spot heals and crop / straighten / warp are per-image for the same reason and are never propagated by an adjustment. (A pasted preset is different: it carries its own per-image items and applies them, because the user explicitly asked for that recipe.)

- WHAT CHANGED, not the whole recipe. Each commit is diffed against propagateBase -- the current image's scope-0 params as of the previous commit -- and only the fields that actually moved are written. Propagation therefore never overwrites settings on the other images that the user did not just touch: syncing Exposure onto twelve images leaves each one's own Temp alone.

WHERE IT HOOKS. queuePropagation() is called from noteScopeEdit(), the single commit point every develop action already goes through. That is deliberate: any future edit path gets multi-image behaviour by construction rather than by remembering to add a call. The field-by-field diff and copy share ONE table of member pointers (kFloatFields / kIntFields, above diffParamFields) so they cannot drift apart -- see step 10 of the adjustment checklist.

BATCHING. A slider drag commits continuously and each target may need a sidecar read, so commits accumulate in pendingFields and land once edits settle (kPropagateMs, 400 ms). propagateTargets is captured when a batch OPENS, so changing the selection mid-drag cannot redirect edits the user has already made -- and the drag reads the selection once instead of once per tick. flushPropagation() forces the batch out early; MW::updateDevelopSelectionWarning calls it on every selection change, and it also runs before an image switch, a history revert, a preset / paste, and flushAll.

PERSISTENCE. Propagated images are marked dirty like any edited image and written by the ordinary flush path. The debounced write was widened from flushImage(current) to flushAll() for this: a crash should lose no more of the other selected images than it loses of the one being edited.

HISTORY is recorded on a propagated image only if it ALREADY has history, i.e. the user has opened it this session. Seeding an unvisited image would push the live one out of DevelopHistory's LRU store (kMaxImages = 40) for no benefit -- an image the user has never opened has nothing to revert to but its sidecar, which is exactly what its baseline entry will say when they do open it. Propagated entries coalesce on the field set, so a long Exposure drag reads as one "Exposure" step on every image.

VIDEOS in the selection are skipped: they have no develop recipe, and writing one would leave a sidecar the pipeline never reads.

STILL MISSING: thumbnails do not render develop, so a bulk edit changes nothing the user can SEE in the grid. The alert row is what covers that gap today; a per-row "edited" indicator in the datamodel is the real fix.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Develop History (the History Dock)

A Lightroom-style list of every develop action on the current image. Hovering an entry previews that state in the loupe; clicking it reverts the image to it. The dock is tabbed with the Develop and Presets docks in the right area and is shown / hidden with them -- the three are one tool. Develop is always raised as the front tab of the group (History and Presets are shown first, then Develop, then raised), so History is reached by clicking its tab -- or with "H" (menu View > Develop History Panel). H is a Develop

MODE LOCAL key (developShortcuts / developShortcutIntercept), not a QKeySequence, since the dock is Develop-only; the F-key series the other docks use ran out at F10 (F11 is taken by macOS). Like F3-F9 it shows/raises rather than toggles. In FULL SCREEN it is independent of the group: Preferences > Full screen defaults > "Show develop history" (fullScreenDocks.isHistory, persisted as isFullScreenHistory).

STARTUP. Winnow always opens in Preview mode, so MW::showEvent hides the Develop tool after restoreState() -- through closeDevelopDock(), which takes History and Presets with it and unchecks all three dock actions. Hiding developDock alone was not enough: restoreState() re-shows every dock the last session had visible, so quitting from Develop mode reopened with History on screen, visible but disabled.

GOTCHA (cost an afternoon): a TABIFIED dock keeps isVisible() == true even when a sibling tab is selected -- that is why isSelectedDockTab tests the visible REGION. developDockVisibilityChange used to raise() developDock on any visibilityChanged where isVisible() was true, which yanked Develop back to the front the moment a sibling tab was clicked, so the History tab appeared dead. It now raises only when Develop really is the front tab. Any future dock tabbed with Develop needs the same

  care.

THE SIDECAR DOES NOT RECORD ACTIONS. winnow:Develop holds ONE base64 blob: the current EditStack, i.e. the RESULT state (Metadata::readDevelopSidecar / writeDevelopSidecar, and DevelopProperties::flushImage). There is no action log to read, so History builds its own timeline of labelled EditStack SNAPSHOTS. It stays in sync with the sidecar by sharing the same object: an entry IS an EditStack, so restoring one just writes it back into stackCache and marks the image dirty, and the ordinary flush persists it. The sidecar remains the single source of truth for the current state.

SESSION SCOPED. Entries are built in memory as the user edits and are discarded on quit; nothing new is written to the sidecar (no format change). Snapshots are not free -- a brush / object mask paramsJson runs to tens of KB -- so DevelopHistory caps at kMaxEntries (100) steps per image, dropping the oldest EDITS (the baseline entry survives and adopts the dropped step's state), and kMaxImages (40) images LRU.

Recording seam (ONE function). Every develop action already ended with the same three lines: dirty.insert(currentImagePath) / emit paramsChanged() / debounceWriteTimer-> start(). That dirty.insert was the exact commit point and every one of them (~30) is now DevelopProperties::noteEdit(action, value, mergeKey) -- mark dirty, record the snapshot, arm the debounced write. Use noteScopeEdit(scope, ...) where the prefix is not the active scope: "Global" for the raw rows (they always write scope 0) and "" for whole-image actions (crop, spot heals) that belong to no scope.

- Labels. The scope prefix is the active EditScope's name, except an unrenamed "Mask N" carrying tools reads as its first tool (maskToolName), so an edit on a Subject mask logs as "Subject Mask: Hue  -18". Slider captions come from the row's own caption cell; values are formatted exactly as the slider shows them (integer when UR_Div is 0, else 2 dp) with a + on positives (historyValueText).

- Coalescing. A non-empty mergeKey that matches the top entry REPLACES it, so a whole slider drag or colour-wheel drag is ONE entry, not one per tick. Structural actions (add/delete mask, reset, crop) pass no mergeKey.

- Guards. isPopulating (pushing saved values into the editors) and isRestoringHistory (a revert repopulating the panel) suppress recording.

Revert model (Lightroom). pos is the entry currently applied. Recording a new action while pos is not the last entry DISCARDS everything after pos -- editing from an earlier state truncates, like an undo stack. Rows after pos are painted dimmed in the

  meantime.

Hover preview = PROXY RENDER ONLY. previewHistoryEntry sets previewStack/previewActive (read at the top of stackJob and editParams, so nothing else in the render path knows) and emits historyPreviewChanged, which createDevelopDock connects to renderDevelopPreview(false). It deliberately does NOT go through developParamsChange: that also arms the full-res settle render, and a cursor passing down the list must not queue one heavy render per row. The view debounces the hover (kHoverDelayMs 120) and ends the preview on leave; preview is skipped while a mask tool or the spot tool is armed (they own the canvas overlay). The click (applyHistoryEntry) takes the full path: retire any half-built mask tool, write the snapshot into stackCache, mark dirty, refreshScopeList + buildTree (scope/mask structure may differ), emit paramsChanged.

Files. Develop/History/develophistory.h (HistoryEntry + DevelopHistory, header-only, owned by DevelopProperties) and Develop/History/historyview.{h,cpp} (HistoryView, a QListWidget already themed by WidgetCSS::listWidget, plus HistoryDelegate painting action left / value right). MW::createHistoryDock runs AFTER createDevelopDock (it binds to developProperties). Adding the dock required the full new-dock checklist above, including the history_white.png glyph and the winnowStateVersion bump to 2.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Reset All Edits (Wipe One Image's Recipe)

The Edits (scope) header menu carries "Reset all edits" directly under "New mask", in BOTH header implementations (ScopeHeader::showScopeMenu and ScopeHeaderLab::showPanelMenu -> resetAllEditsRequested, declared on ScopeHeaderBase so one connect in bindScopeHeader binds whichever was built). It runs

  DevelopProperties::resetAllEdits.

WHAT IT DELETES. Everything the EditStack holds: every scope and its masks, the crop / straighten / warp geometry, and the spot heals. The stack is replaced with a fresh identity stack (one "Global" scope), not merely reset field by field -- that is what makes the geometry and the spots go too.

WHOLE SELECTION. Like a preset apply or a settings paste (and unlike Reset / Remove on a scope), it lands on every selected image: the current one, then each of otherSelectedPaths() -- which skips videos, they have no develop recipe. Per image the work is identical and lives in the helper resetImageEdits(fPath); resetAllEdits itself adds only what the DOCK needs for the image on screen (mask-tool teardown, refreshScopeList + buildTree, spot pins, propagate base, paramsChanged) and the "Reset all edits on N images." popup. A multi-image batch already queued is flushed first (flushPropagation): its targets were captured when the batch opened and need not be the current selection, so it cannot just be dropped.

HISTORY AND SIDECAR (per image, resetImageEdits). The image's whole timeline goes: history->forget(path) then seed(path, identity), so the dock shows one fresh "Original" baseline -- but only for an image that HAS a timeline (count > 0, i.e. visited this session); seeding an unvisited image would fill the LRU with images the user never opened. The sidecar is written IMMEDIATELY (dirty.insert + flushImage) rather than left to the debounce -- an identity stack makes flushImage write an empty blob, which clears the winnow:Develop attribute (Metadata::writeDevelopSidecar; it will not create a sidecar just to store nothing). The settings are then gone even if the app never gets a clean shutdown.

CONFIRMED, NOT UNDOABLE. History is wiped along with the edits, so there is nothing to revert to: a QMessageBox with a destructive "Reset" button (Cancel default) asks first, naming the image or the number of selected images.

TEARDOWN. Same order as applyHistoryEntry: retire a half-built mask tool (no commit prompt -- the scope it would land in is being deleted), clear selectedMaskIndex, hide the mask panel, drop any hover preview, then rebuild under isRestoringHistory (so the repopulation records nothing), re-seed the propagate base and emit paramsChanged.


⏺ Develop / Image Edit (Parametric Adjustments) > Adding or Changing a Develop Adjustment (Checklist)

A new slider (or a change to an existing one) is NOT a one-file edit: the value has to reach the pipeline, survive a round trip through the sidecar, repopulate the panel on the next image, and be capturable by a preset. Thirteen touch points, in dependency order. Working example: grainAmount (the Effects "Grain" slider) -- grep it to see every site at once.

1. Develop/editparams.h -- THREE edits, not one.
  • the field + its identity default + a block comment giving meaning and range

  • EditParams::resetGroup() -- add it to its Group case. Miss this and the section Reset button and the Preview eye silently skip it.

  • EditParams::isIdentity() -- add it ONLY if it is a primary control. Sub- modifiers (vignetteFeather, grainSize, grainRoughness, the tone splits) are deliberately left out: they are inert until their amount is non-zero, so counting them would make an untouched image non-identity and write a sidecar.
2. Develop/develop.h + develop.cpp -- the op itself.
  • tuning constants in the constexpr block at the top of develop.cpp
  • the function, declared in develop.h and implemented in develop.cpp

  • the call in Develop::Apply(), at the right place in the fixed pipeline order (Denoise #1 -> fused point pass -> Texture #6 -> Clarity #6.5 -> Dehaze #7 -> Vignette #8 -> Sharpen #8.5 -> Grain #9). A POINT op costs nothing extra if folded into buildPointCoeffs / applyPointOps; a SPATIAL op owns its own pass.
  • parallelFor every per-pixel loop; downscale for large-radius blurs.

  • A LOCAL-CONTRAST op does NOT hand-roll its luma prep or fold-back: use buildPerceptualLuma + shapeAndFoldLuma (and gaussianBase if the radius is a fraction of the long edge), with the scalar math in Develop/localcontrast.h. Adding one should be ~15 lines in develop.cpp -- if it is more, it is duplicating something that already exists.

3. Develop/editstack.h -- paramsToJson() AND paramsFromJson(). paramsFromJson is forward tolerant (an absent key keeps its default), so ADDING a field needs no version bump and old sidecars stay valid. Add the range clamp to EditStack::sanitizeParams() in the same pass -- a field with no clamp line is one a hand-edited sidecar can feed to the pipeline unchecked. paramsToJson is ALSO the per-scope render cache's invalidation key (MW::developCompositeStack), so a field added to the pipeline but not here renders stale from cache AND never persists.

4. developproperties.cpp, addBasic / addCurves / addColor / addCalibrate / addColorGrade / addDetail / addEffects -- the addSlider call: dock key, caption, tooltip, section header, min, max, divisor, colours, default. A sub-control of the slider above it uses a "   "-indented caption ("   Feather", "   Size").
