That trade -- a long one-off build for permanently fast browsing -- is the whole point of the feature, and it is why the preference is off by default.

THE DEFAULT-RENDER KEY HASHES THE RENDERER, NOT A RECIPE, because there is no recipe to hash: devPreviewKey returns empty for an unedited image, and an empty key is precisely what loadDevPreview reads as "no preview". So defaultRenderKey hashes everything OUTSIDE the recipe that changes the pixels -- the raw engine (Winnow demosaic vs Apple Core Image) and G::autoRunDenoise (whether PMRID is baked in) -- plus a version to bump when a pipeline change makes existing default renders wrong. Changing any of those makes every previously built default render MISS rather than serve pixels produced under the old setting; the stranded entries are never asked for again and age out through the LRU, so nothing has to hunt them down. The key is prefixed 'R', which is not a hex digit, so the recipe and renderer key spaces are provably disjoint.

ONLY THE LARGE TIER FOR AN UNEDITED RAW. The 256px grid thumbnail lives in the image's XMP sidecar, and an image the user never edited must not GAIN a sidecar as a side effect of a cache fill -- that would be writing to their library to fill a cache. The grid keeps the camera-embedded thumbnail, which was already read during the metadata pass and is no slower; only the tier that saves the demosaic is cached.

THE "DOES THIS NEED BUILDING?" FILTER RUNS OFF THE GUI THREAD. The question splits in two: which key the image should carry (model reads, plus a sidecar read for the few edited images) and whether the cache already holds that key (DevPreviewCache::contains -- one SQLite query AND one stat() per path). The first has to be on the GUI thread; the second used to be, which was survivable when only edited images were offered but is a thousand queries and a thousand stats on the 1000-image folders this feature exists for, arriving exactly when the folder finishes loading. buildDevPreviews now derives the keys on the GUI thread, hands the (path, key) pairs to a pool thread for the cache test, and resumes in startDevPreviewBuild with the filtered list, guarded by dm->instance in case the folder changed meanwhile.

IT RUNS ON THE EXPORTER'S ENGINE. The work is done by MW::developPixelSource, which already stages a full-resolution render correctly: the GUI thread owns the datamodel reads, the stored-recipe capture (stackJobFor, which ignores every view override), the orientation and the mask prerequisites, while the decode and the composite run on developRenderPool. Reusing it means a built preview cannot drift from what an export or a real develop render produces.

ONE AT A TIME. The next image starts when the previous one's render returns. That is what keeps the builder inside the raw-decoder concurrency budget (see "Memory Governor"): one in-flight scene-linear decode whatever the folder size. It also means a long build never competes with the image the user is looking at.

IT DECLINES TO RUN INSIDE DEVELOP MODE. Developing is a live edit session on the same path-keyed mask and working-image caches the builder would touch, and the user's own render must win. devPreviewBuildNext ends the run rather than interleaving.

THE KEY IS RE-DERIVED WHEN THE RENDER LANDS and compared with the one captured when the render was dispatched. A render takes seconds, and in between the user may have edited that image or switched raw engine; either would make the pixels in hand depict something other than what the key now says. On a mismatch the write is dropped and the image simply misses again, to be rebuilt correctly next time. (Previously only the recipe was re-read and the preview was then stored under whatever key that produced, which is the same staleness the check was meant to prevent.)

PROGRESS IS A STATUS-BAR ROW ("Dev Previews", progressDevPreviewRow), alongside Metadata, Raw Denoise and Demosaicing -- not a popup. A build is minutes of work the user is not waiting on: they keep browsing, and a popup parked over the image for the duration is in the way of the very pictures being rebuilt. The row appears when a run starts and collapses when it ends. Only a run the user ASKED for (Develop > Build Developed Previews) reports its result in a popup -- "nothing to do" up front, "Built N" or the Develop-mode pause at the end; a background build says nothing at all, because the row already said it was happening and the grid shows the result.

THE QUEUE IS DISPOSABLE. Changing folders (MW::stop) abandons it, and nothing is remembered: the queue is rebuilt from what is actually missing every time the command runs, so re-running after a partial build is free and a stale queue can never be wrong.


⏺ File Handling > File Operations and Companion Files (the FileOps Choke Point)

An image in Winnow is not one file. It is the image plus its companions -- the XMP sidecar holding ratings, labels, orientation and the ENTIRE develop recipe, plus a .txt sidecar when present. There is no catalogue, so THE SIDECAR IS THE PER-IMAGE DATABASE: losing one loses every edit ever made to that image.

WHY THIS EXISTS. Companion handling used to be implemented four different ways -- a .xmp+.txt helper used by four call sites, a .xmp-only helper used by one, a directory basename scan used by rename, and hardcoded baseName + ".xmp" in ingest and the metadata writers. They disagreed about .txt files, about case, and about whether an externally dropped file had a sidecar at all. Adding devPreviews -- a second piece of per-image state that has to track the first exactly -- made one definition mandatory. Utilities/fileops.h is it.

WHAT IT PROVIDES. companions() (one definition of "the sidecars belonging to this image"), flushPendingEdits(), copyFile/moveFile/trashFile (which do the whole job: image, companions, devPreview cache), and onCopied/onMoved/onDeleted for callers that must move the bytes themselves -- the rename dialog and ingest both rename companions to a NEW base name, which the generic helpers cannot express.

THE FLUSH IS NOT OPTIONAL. Develop edits are written on a 2s debounce. Before FileOps only copy and export flushed first, so deleting, renaming, moving or ingesting an image inside that window let the pending write land AFTERWARDS -- recreating a sidecar at the old path after a rename, or resurrecting one that had just been deleted. Every entry point now flushes first. This was a live data-loss bug for the recipe, independent of previews.

companions() IS DELIBERATELY NARROWER THAN THE RENAME SCAN. It matches only sidecar suffixes (.xmp, .txt), case-insensitively. Every file sharing the base name would sweep in the paired JPG of a raw+jpg pair, and trashing a NEF must not trash its JPG. Rename is the one operation that genuinely wants the wider net and keeps its own scan; it notifies FileOps per file so the devPreview cache follows.

COPY DOES NOT DUPLICATE THE devPreview. A copied image carries its sidecar, so the 256px thumbnail travels; the devPreview simply misses at the destination and is re-rendered on the next edit. Duplicating it would double the cache for every copy to buy back one decode.

ROUTED OPERATIONS: delete/trash (MW::deleteFiles), rename (all four paths in RenameFileDlg), both drop handlers (FSTree and BookMarks), the icon-view drag source, copy/paste, both ingest implementations, and Utilities::backup.

DEFECTS FIXED WHILE ROUTING. External drops from Finder/Explorer now carry sidecars (the block used to be gated on isInternal, so a dropped raw arrived without its ratings or recipe). Folder counts refresh after ANY drop, not only a CopyAction. Ingest no longer reports a spurious integrity failure for every image that simply has no sidecar, no longer files a failed backup copy under "integrity failure" against the wrong pair of paths, and no longer runs a second uniquifying rename that could desynchronise an image from its sidecar. Utilities::backup carries the sidecars, so restoring a backup no longer loses the image's edits.

MOVE DROPS STILL DELETE FROM THE DRAG SOURCE. Both drop handlers always copy; the source deletion comes from IconView::startDrag after drag->exec() reports a MoveAction. This looks split but is correct: it is also what makes dragging OUT of Winnow to Finder behave as a move. Do not "fix" it by deleting in the drop handler -- an internal move would then delete twice.


*** IMAGE COLOUR MANAGEMENT (EMBEDDED PROFILES) ***

⏺ Image Colour Management > HEIC and the colr Box

HEIC stores its colour space in a `colr` BOX inside the item properties (meta > iprp > ipco), not in a JPEG-style APP2 segment -- which is why the segment-based ICC read copied from the JPEG parser could never have worked for HEIC and sat commented out in Heic.cpp instead. Heic::colrBox already PARSED the box but did all of it inside `if (isDebug)` and stored nothing, and "heic"/"hif" were missing from Metadata::iccFormats, so HEIC opted out of colour management entirely.

THE SYMPTOM was iPhone photos rendering visibly UNDERSATURATED in ordinary browsing, not just in Develop: iPhone HEICs are Display P3, iccBuf stayed null, and "null means sRGB" applied. Verified against a real file -- IMG_0375.HEIC carries colour_type 'prof' with a 548-byte ICC whose description is literally "Display P3".

Two forms, per ISO 23008-12. `prof`/`rICC` is a full ICC profile taken verbatim; its payload is length-12, being the 8-byte box header plus the 4-byte colour_type (the old debug code read length-8 and would have over-read by four -- the profile's own internal size field is what confirms 12 is right). `nclx` is numeric ITU-T H.273 codes with no profile attached, so one is synthesised from QColorSpace rather than hand-built with lcms: primaries 11 or 12 with an SDR transfer (1 or 13) gives Display P3; primaries 1 is sRGB and stores NOTHING, since null already means sRGB and keeping the bytes would put a redundant profile on every datamodel row (the same reasoning as the JPEG reader).

SDR ONLY, DELIBERATELY: PQ (transfer 16) and HLG (18) are HDR and need tone mapping to an SDR display. Treating their code values as SDR would be far worse than the status quo, so they fall through untouched -- still assumed sRGB, exactly as before -- and remain separate work.

The chain from here is generic: Heic::colrBox fills m->iccBuf, DataModel writes it to G::ICCBufColumn, and ImageDecoder::colorManage reads it back (gated on Metadata::iccFormats, which is what the missing "heic"/"hif" entries defeated).

(Unrelated quirk noticed while doing this: "hif" is listed in noMetadataFormats, but that list only feeds a display string in Views/infostring.cpp and does not gate parsing, so it affects none of the above.)


*** KEYWORDS AND CATALOGUING ***

⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > Reading Keywords (dc:subject and lr:hierarchicalSubject)

WINNOW USED TO LOSE ALMOST EVERY KEYWORD IT WAS SHOWN. Two properties carry them: dc:subject, the flat list of leaf names that every application writes, and lr:hierarchicalSubject, Lightroom's parallel list of full keyword paths with '|' separators ie "Location|Canada|BC|Vancouver Island". Winnow read the first in exactly two places -- Jpeg::parse and PNG::parse -- and the second nowhere at all: it was not even in Xmp::definedElements, so getItemList would have refused the name.

THE CONSEQUENCE WAS WORST FOR RAW, which is most of a photographer's library. Nikon, Canon CR2, DNG, Tiff and Panasonic all build a valid Xmp from IFD tag 700 and read rating, label, title and creator out of it -- then simply did not ask for the keywords. Worse, Metadata::parseSidecar is the ONLY route by which a raw file's keywords can arrive at all, because Lightroom does not write into a NEF, CR2, CR3 or ARW; it writes a .xmp beside it. parseSidecar read rating, label, title, creator, rights, email, url and orientation, and skipped dc:subject. So a raw library keyworded over years showed an empty Filters > Keywords category, and nothing in the app hinted why.

ONE DEFINITION NOW, IN Metadata/xmpapply.h. The seven parsers above each carried a byte-identical twelve-line copy of the same XMP block, which is how they came to disagree in the first place: the keyword line was added to the JPEG copies and to no others. applyXmp() is that block, once, and each parser calls it. The saving is incidental; the point is that the next field added arrives everywhere at once.

IT IS A FREE FUNCTION IN ITS OWN HEADER, not a Metadata member, because Metadata/metadata.h includes every parser header -- so a parser including it back would compile only as long as its own header happened to be first, and would drag QtWidgets into seven more translation units. xmpapply.h depends on nothing but xmp.h and imagemetadata.h, which every parser already has. Same reasoning as Cache/pathkey.h.

PNG DELIBERATELY KEEPS ITS OWN COPY. It guards several fields on isEmpty() and mirrors the exif:/tiff: namespaces into the camera fields, because Lightroom and Photoshop drop the eXIf chunk when they write a PNG and put the camera data in the XMP instead. Folding it into the shared helper would change what a PNG reports.

THE SIDECAR READ IS NOT GUARDED ON isEmpty(), unlike the scalars beside it. An empty keyword list in a sidecar that is otherwise newer means the keywords were REMOVED, and honouring that is the whole point of the newer-wins rule parseSidecar already applies a few lines above. Guarding it would make keyword deletion impossible to sync.

HIERARCHY IS READ, THEN FLATTENED. G::KeywordPathsColumn holds the paths beside G::KeywordsColumn's leaf names, and both are folded into G::SearchTextColumn. A THIRD column, G::KeywordsAllColumn, holds what Winnow actually filters and searches on: the de-duplicated union of the leaves and every NODE of every path, so "Location|Canada|BC" contributes three keywords. Metadata/keywordflatten.h is the one definition of that, shared by the datamodel and the index -- if the two split a path differently the category and the search would disagree about the same picture. All three columns are appended before TotalColumns and marked GeekRole, like DevelopColumn and DevPreviewKeyColumn, because saved table column widths are keyed by index.

WHY FLAT RATHER THAN A TREE. Three reasons, and the first forced it. A hierarchical file carries the SAME tag twice -- Lightroom writes the leaf into dc:subject and the path into lr:hierarchicalSubject -- so storing both forms separately put "Heron" in the category list twice with its image count split between the entries, which reads as a bug because it is one. The hierarchy is also not universal: phone images, non-Adobe DAMs and IPTC-only files carry dc:subject and nothing else, so a tree is right for part of a library and overhead for the rest. And ancestor search, the only thing the tree bought, survives flattening: "Fauna" reaches an image tagged only "Fauna|Bird|Heron" because Fauna is now genuinely one of that image's keywords.

WHAT IT COSTS is ambiguity, and that is paid for elsewhere rather than avoided. A name under two parents -- "Location|Canada|BC|Vancouver" and "Location|USA|Washington|Vancouver" -- becomes ONE keyword meaning two places. The catalog records which parents a name has been seen under (keyword_context), both docks colour an ambiguous keyword and name its parents in the tooltip, and filter EXCLUSION resolves it: include Vancouver, exclude USA.

G::KeywordsColumn DELIBERATELY KEEPS THE LITERAL dc:subject LIST rather than being replaced by the flattened one, and DataModel::imMetadata still reads that one. The two are different facts and only one of them may ever be written back: emitting the flattened column as dc:subject would put every ancestor into a property that never held them. When keyword writing is implemented, dc:subject comes from KeywordsColumn and lr:hierarchicalSubject from KeywordPathsColumn -- never from KeywordsAllColumn.

WRITING KEYWORDS IS NOT IMPLEMENTED and is deliberately separate work. Xmp::setItem can only write an ATTRIBUTE on rdf:Description; no code path emits an rdf:Bag of rdf:li, so there is currently no way to write either property. ImageMetadata also has no _keywords shadow, which is what Metadata::writeXMP's change detection depends on. Until that exists, keywords are read-only and the files remain whatever Lightroom made them.

tests/unit/tst_xmpkeywords.cpp pins all of it: both properties off a Lightroom-shaped fixture, an absent-keyword document staying quiet rather than warning, applyXmp filling both lists, and applyXmp still taking the underscore snapshot that writeXMP needs.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > The Catalog (Cross-folder Search)

WHAT IT IS. A local index of what Winnow has learned about images it has seen, so a keyword or title search answers ACROSS FOLDERS instead of only inside the one currently loaded. Tables image / keyword / image_keyword / keyword_context plus the image_fts full-text index, added at schema 3 of the shared local index database and re-keyed at schema 4 (Cache/cachedb.h). Cache/catalog.h is the class.

IT IS AN INDEX, NOT A LIBRARY, and that distinction is the whole design. Winnow still keeps no catalogue in the Lightroom sense: the XMP sidecar remains the per-image database and the only source of truth, nothing here is ever written back to anyone's photographs, and every column is re-readable from the image and its sidecar. That is what lets the catalog share the preview cache's move-it-aside-and-rebuild failure policy -- losing this file costs a rescan, never data.

THE ONE THING THAT IS NOT DERIVED LIVES SOMEWHERE ELSE. The folders the user nominates for background scanning are user intent, not rebuildable, and CacheDb::moveAside discards this file without asking. So the root list goes in QSettings. Putting it in the database would mean a corrupt index silently forgot what the user asked to be indexed.

ROWS ARRIVE FROM MW::folderChangeCompleted, alongside the devPreview sweep and for the same reasons: metadata is complete by then, the user is no longer waiting, and the work runs off the GUI thread. DataModel::catalogRows() reads the model ON THE GUI THREAD into plain values -- QStandardItemModel is not thread-safe -- and only those values cross to the pool thread that inserts them. Commit is deliberately NOT called per image from addMetadataForItem: that fires once per image on a Reader thread whose backpressure is already tuned, and a database write there is the per-row signal fan-out that has twice been the cause of folder-load lag.

ROWS THAT ARE NOT MetaLoaded ARE SKIPPED. Cataloguing a half-read row is worse than cataloguing nothing: it would be stored with empty keywords, and its freshness stamp would then MATCH on the next visit, so the blank entry would look current and the image would stay missing from search results until its file changed on disk.

FRESHNESS IS (srcsize, srcmtime, sidecarmtime). The sidecar stamp is the load-bearing one for a raw library: Lightroom edits keywords by rewriting the .xmp and never touches the NEF, so a check that looked only at the image would never notice. Unchanged rows are skipped before any write, which matters because the opportunistic capture runs on EVERY folder change -- without the skip, a user pacing between two folders would rewrite both indefinitely.

KEYWORDS ARE NORMALISED into keyword + image_keyword rather than kept as text on the image row, so the category list the UI wants ("every keyword, with counts") is an index scan rather than a quarter of a million string splits. Links are REPLACED on re-index, not added to, so a keyword deleted in Lightroom disappears here too.

KEYWORDS ARE FLAT, KEYED ON THE NAME ALONE (schema 4). The hierarchy is flattened before it reaches the catalog -- DataModel::catalogRows and CatalogScanner both hand over flattenKeywords()'s output -- so an ancestor is an ordinary keyword and a tag Lightroom wrote both ways is ONE row. Schema 3 keyed on (pathfold, namefold) and kept both forms, which listed the same keyword twice with its count split; that was the defect, not a feature to preserve. Searching for a keyword is now plain equality on namefold, with no pathfold subtree walk, because an ancestor is linked to everything beneath it directly.

WHAT THE HIERARCHY LEAVES BEHIND is keyword_context (keyword_id, parent_id): which parents a name has been seen under, written from the raw paths that still travel beside the flat list. A name with more than one distinct parent is AMBIGUOUS, which Catalog::ambiguousKeywords() reports and both docks colour. Contexts are NOT deleted when an image is re-indexed: they describe the vocabulary, not the image, and clearing them per image would make ambiguity flicker as folders are browsed.

SCHEMA 4 MIGRATES IN PLACE AND RE-READS NOTHING. Everything needed is already in the file, and schema 3's writer linked the image to every ANCESTOR as well as the leaf, so the flat link set is already correct -- only the rows need merging. The migration picks a canonical id per namefold, re-points image_keyword at it (INSERT OR IGNORE, so the Lightroom double collapses onto the primary key), derives keyword_context from the old parent pointers, deletes the surplus rows, then swaps keyword_key for a unique index on namefold. path/pathfold/parent stay as columns -- this file's rule is additive-only -- but are blanked so nothing can read them and believe the hierarchy is still maintained. Rebuilding instead would have meant re-parsing an entire library to recover facts the database already held. tst_catalog::migrationFromVersionThreeMergesKeywords builds a version 3 file by hand and pins it.

THE FULL-TEXT TABLE IS A PLAIN fts5, not contentless and not external-content. Contentless needs the original text handed back on every delete, so a second copy is kept anyway; external-content cannot span the image_keyword join. The duplicated text costs ~200 bytes an image and buys prefix search, AND/OR/NOT, phrase queries and BM25 ranking with no query code of Winnow's own. What the user typed is turned into a MATCH expression by the SHARED grammar (Utilities/searchterms.h -- see "Searching Folders and Catalog"), not by anything the catalog owns. Quoting every term is not cosmetic -- an unquoted term containing punctuation is a MATCH syntax error, and a syntax error fails the whole query, so a user typing an apostrophe or a hyphen mid-word would see the search break rather than narrow.

NEGATED TEXT IS A SEPARATE NOT EXISTS, not FTS5's NOT operator, which is binary: "-heron" has no left-hand side to subtract from and MATCH offers no "everything" token to supply one. As a subquery against image_fts it works whether or not there is anything positive to go with it. Excluded KEYWORDS are a NOT EXISTS too, over image_keyword -- joining would multiply the rows and then need a DISTINCT to undo it, and "this image has no such keyword" is a question about the image, not about a row to return.

EVERY VALUE IS BOUND, NEVER INTERPOLATED, including the FTS expression. The search box is user text and the catalog shares its file with the preview index, so a query that pasted text into SQL would put the previews one apostrophe away from damage. tst_catalog::searchTextIsNotInjectable pins it.

TEXT COLUMNS ARE NOT NULL AND BIND THROUGH A HELPER. A default-constructed QString is SQL NULL to QSqlQuery, not '', and an image with no title, no lens or no GPS is the ORDINARY case -- so binding QString() directly aborted the whole folder's transaction on the first such row ("NOT NULL constraint failed: image.label"). The columns stay NOT NULL, so searching never has to reason about the difference between "no title" and "unknown title", and Catalog's text() supplies the empty string.

NEITHER TENANT MAY OVERRIDE THE OTHER'S DATABASE LOCATION. Both open lazily and either may be first, so Catalog::ensureLoadedLocked only supplies the DEFAULT path when nothing has been set. An unconditional setPath there would be a bug with teeth: setPath closes every open connection and bumps the generation, so a catalog call arriving after DevPreviewCache::setCacheDir had pointed the database elsewhere -- a test at a temp dir, or a future "move the cache" preference -- would silently drag it back to AppDataLocation and strand the previews.

CLEARING ONE TENANT DOES NOT CLEAR THE OTHER. "Clear preview cache" deletes devpreview rows and payload JPEGs only; clearing the catalog leaves the previews alone. They share a file, not a lifetime, and rebuilding a catalog means rescanning a library. tst_catalog::clearingPreviewsLeavesTheCatalog pins both directions.

SWEEP DEMOTES, NEVER DELETES, and skips unmounted volumes -- the same rule as the devPreview sweep and for the same reason: Winnow browses memory cards and external drives constantly, and an ejected drive must not read as a mass deletion. A demoted row stops appearing in searches (which would otherwise offer images that cannot be loaded) but survives, and the next commit that sees the file promotes it again. A delete Winnow performs ITSELF is certain rather than inferred, so FileOps::onDeleted removes the row outright -- including its image_fts row, which has no foreign key and would otherwise keep matching text for an image that no longer exists.

MountSnapshot AND SrcStamp MOVED to Cache/mountsnapshot.h when the catalog became the second thing that had to answer "which volumes are mounted" and "is this still the same file". Both tenants index paths across removable media and both have the same dangerous verdict to avoid, so they must decide it identically or the two indexes will disagree about the same file.

STILL TO COME: the unified Find dock, where the Filters and Catalog panels become one panel with a Folders/Catalog scope switch over a shared category vocabulary. Going flat is what makes that cheap -- with a tree on one side and a list on the other there is no shared category renderer to write. Writing keywords back to files remains deliberately out of scope -- see the previous subsection.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > The Catalog Dock and Loading Results

SUPERSEDED BY THE FIND DOCK (next subsection) when G::useFindDock is true, which is the default: the Catalog panel's search box, Keywords category and Load button became the Catalog scope of one panel, and this dock is not built at all. What follows describes the separate panel, which the flag still restores -- and the reasoning below (results are loaded not filtered, no thumbnails, debounced, an empty query does nothing) carries over to the Find dock unchanged.

THE CATALOG DOCK (Shift+F2, Window > Catalog Panel, Views/catalogview.cpp) searches the INDEX; the Filters dock searches the DATAMODEL. That is the whole distinction, and it is why they are separate docks rather than another Filters category: Filters narrows what is already loaded, the Catalog finds images that are not loaded at all and offers to load them. Shift+F2 pairs with F2 ("search here") because the dock F-keys F3-F9 are all taken, and the pairing says what it does better than the next free key would.

IT DOES NOT SHOW THUMBNAILS. A picture list in a narrow dock would be a worse version of the grid Winnow already has, so the dock reports how many images matched and offers to LOAD them. The images then appear in the ordinary views, where the loupe, Develop, ratings and filtering all work on them exactly as on a folder -- which is the entire point of loading results into the datamodel instead of inventing a second kind of model.

SEARCHING IS DEBOUNCED (250 ms), not run per keystroke: the query hits SQLite and FTS5 on the GUI thread, and at the 250,000 rows the database is sized for a query per character would be felt. The delay also stops half-typed FTS syntax from being executed at every intermediate state -- "hero" on the way to "heron OR eagle" is a valid query with a wrong answer.

AN EMPTY QUERY DELIBERATELY DOES NOTHING. Matching the whole catalog and offering to load a quarter of a million images is not a useful answer to having typed nothing, so with no text, no keyword and no rating the panel just reports how many images are catalogued and leaves Load disabled.

THE KEYWORDS CATEGORY IS A FLAT LIST, because the vocabulary is: a path like "Location|Canada|BC" is flattened into three ordinary keywords before it is ever indexed, so "Canada" is a keyword in its own right rather than a node to expand, and its count needs no summing. Clicking a keyword INCLUDES it, Opt+click EXCLUDES it, right-click offers Include / Exclude / Clear by name, and clicking an item that is already in that state clears it -- otherwise the only way out of a keyword filter would be to know some other gesture. An AMBIGUOUS keyword (one the catalog has seen under more than one parent) is drawn in amber with its parents listed in its tooltip, which is what tells the user that "Vancouver" is two places before they decide what to exclude. Excluded rows are struck through in red: exclusion is a FONT and ambiguity a COLOUR precisely so the two compose, since both facts matter at once while an ambiguity is being resolved.

THE SELECTION LIVES IN THE VIEW, NOT THE WIDGET. includedKeywords / excludedKeywords survive a rebuild of the list, so a refresh after a folder load cannot silently drop the filter the user is looking at; names that have left the catalog are dropped from both sets, because a restriction whose row is gone is invisible.

LOAD REPLACES, ADD APPENDS. Load is the same reset a folder change is; Add keeps what is loaded, which is what makes two searches comparable -- without it every search throws the previous one away and "the herons AND the eagles" cannot be assembled however the query is written. DataModel::addPaths was already additive (it starts at rowCount() and skips paths already in the model), so Add is a matter of NOT tearing down first rather than a second load path, and a path in both searches is not loaded twice. Both end by emitting folderChange, so MetaRead, folderChangeCompleted and the filter rebuild run either way.

WHICH FOLDERS ARE INDEXED IS NOT IN THIS PANEL any more -- see "Cataloguing Designated Roots". What remains is a one-line status ("84,102 images catalogued in 312 folders") and a Manage button. The status is always shown, whether or not a search has run, because a search that found nothing means something quite different when the catalog is empty and the user should not have to open a dialog to find out which case they are in.

WHEN THERE IS NO CATALOG THE PANEL SAYS SO, INLINE, and the controls are disabled. Two cases: the index will not open, or it is open and empty (nothing has been browsed yet). Letting the user type a search and only then explaining that nothing is indexed is exactly the post-hoc popup this avoids.

LOADING RESULTS IS THE SAME RESET AS A FOLDER CHANGE. MW::loadCatalogResults does what folderSelectionChange does -- stop(), drop the develop caches, refill, let folderChangeCompleted run -- because everything downstream of the model (MetaRead, the icon chunks, the image cache, Develop) assumes that when the model's contents are replaced it was told to stop first. The develop-cache reset is now MW::resetDevelopCachesForNewFolder, shared by both, so a cache added in future cannot be dropped on one path and forgotten on the other.

THE FOLDER PANEL DELIBERATELY DOES NOT FOLLOW. The results span many folders, so there is no one folder to select, and highlighting an arbitrary one of them would misrepresent what is loaded.

DataModel::addPaths IS THE MODEL HALF. Every other load path starts from a directory -- the folder queue's unit is a folder and addFolder enumerates one -- but a result set is a list of files from any number of folders, so there is nothing to enumerate. It reuses addFolder's BATCHED insert (one setRowCount, signals blocked, one wide dataChanged) because a result set is routinely thousands of rows, which is the exact shape that made per-row signals the folder-load bottleneck. It takes the same proxy-sort bracket as scheduleProcessing, or the one wide dataChanged would make the sorted proxy re-sort the inserted block Z-A. It registers EVERY distinct parent folder in folderList / folderSet / folderImageCount, because those are what the Folders filter category, removeFolder and isFolderLoaded read -- without them the rows would belong to no folder at all. And it ends by emitting folderChange, the same signal a folder load ends with, so nothing downstream needs to know where the images came from.

IT CHECKS THAT THE FILES ARE STILL THERE. The catalog is an index, not a guarantee: a row can name a file that has since been deleted or sits on an unmounted volume. Sweep demotes those, but only when it has run since, so addPaths stats each path as well -- returning fewer results is better than offering one that cannot be opened.

THE PANEL REFRESHES when the dock becomes visible and after a catalog commit completes, the latter only when the dock is actually visible (the category rebuild is a query plus a tree build, and the commit is on a pool thread precisely to keep catalog work off the GUI thread during a load). The refresh button in the title bar is the manual path.

DOCK REGISTRATION followed the checklist in "Responsive Dock Tab Titles": images/icon16/catalog_white.png (a magnifier, matching the existing glyph weight), registered in winnow.qrc, added to graphicFor / dockFor / dockForTabText, appended to dockTextNames, given a dockTabToolTip case, winnowStateVersion bumped to 4, and a row added to MW::placeDocksAddedSince so a layout saved before the panel existed tabs it beside Filters instead of stranding it in the left area. It is also wired into the solo-mode and dock-tab-update loops, the collapsed-state restore, workspaces, the full-screen dock set and its preference. Missing any one of those is what makes a tab bar flicker or a workspace restore inconsistent.

OFF BY DEFAULT in the shipped layout: it is a new panel, an empty catalog has nothing to say, and the left group is already several tabs deep.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > The Find Dock (Folders and Catalog)

ONE PANEL, TWO SCOPES. Filters searched the datamodel and the Catalog panel searched the index, and they looked alike because they are the SAME QUESTION asked of different sets. Keeping them apart produced exactly the problems that split predicts: two keyword lists that disagreed, two search boxes with different grammars under deliberately paired shortcuts, and a Catalog panel that could not reach most of what CatalogQuery already supported while Filters had those categories with counts and checkboxes all along. The Find dock (Views/findpanel.h) makes the difference between them a CONTROL -- Folders | Catalog -- rather than two panels. THE TWO WORDS NAME THE SETS THEY SEARCH. An earlier pair, Here and Everywhere, named them by distance from the user instead, and "Everywhere" promised more than it delivers: the catalog holds the folders Winnow has indexed, not every image on the machine.

IT IS THE SAME DOCK OBJECT AS THE FILTERS DOCK, deliberately: same DockWidget, same "FilterDock" objectName, re-titled "Find". Everything that reaches for filterDock -- the full-screen dock set, workspaces, solo mode, the view-mode enables, the collapsed-state restore, MW::showFilterDock -- keeps working untouched, and a WindowState saved before this change still restores, because the objectName Qt keys that state on has not changed. Only the visible tab TEXT differs, and a tab title is a label rather than an identity. That is also why winnowStateVersion did NOT need bumping: no dock was added, one was removed, and Qt ignores a saved entry for a dock that no longer exists.

THE CATEGORY TREE IS THE Filters WIDGET ITSELF, re-parented into the panel -- not a copy. Both scopes render the same categories, the same checkboxes, the same include/exclude gestures and the same colours; only where the values came from changes. A second category renderer is precisely what the two panels had, and it is what drifted.

CATALOG CATEGORIES COME FROM ONE SQL MAP. Catalog::categorySql keys the expression for a category on the DATAMODEL COLUMN the Filters panel maps that category to, and both categoryItems() and search() use it -- so a value the user checks cannot mean something different from the value that was offered. The strings must match what DataModel writes into the same column, because the user sees one list and does not know which scope produced it: Type is the suffix UPPER-cased, Year is "yyyy", Day is "yyyy-MM-dd", Folders is the folder's NAME not its path, Pick is the words "Picked"/"Unpicked", Rating is the digit as text, and focal length carries no trailing ".0" (or "400" and "400.0" would look like two lenses' worth of category). tst_catalog::categoryItemsMatchWhatTheDatamodelWrites pins every one of those.

A CATEGORY THE INDEX CANNOT ANSWER IS HIDDEN, NOT SHOWN EMPTY. Duplicates compares what is loaded and the Search category is the panel's own box; neither means anything across a library. categoryItems() returns nothing for those columns and the panel hides the row -- an empty category reads as "you have no camera models", which would be a lie.

EVERY CATEGORY ADDS UP TO THE CATALOG, and the shortfall is a BLANK first row. If 43,064 images are indexed and 3,000 carry a rating, the Ratings category lists the rated values and then a blank one counting 40,064 -- not a set of counts that quietly falls 40,064 short of the library with nothing to explain the gap. Checking the blank row means "the ones with nothing here" and loads exactly those, and Opt+clicking it excludes them. This is how FOLDERS has always behaved: BuildFilters counts the empty string like any other key, so a folder where some images have no lens has always shown a blank lens row. The Catalog half now matches. Two things make it work: categorySql wraps every expression in IFNULL(..., '') so a NULL title or an image with no capture date groups into the blank instead of vanishing, and search() binds category values through text() so a null QString does not go to SQLite as NULL -- "NULL IN (NULL)" is NULL, and the blank row would select nothing at all. A category whose ONLY value is blank is still hidden, since offering "" as the sole thing to check would filter to everything.

KEYWORDS ARE THE EXCEPTION and get no blank row, in either scope. An image carries many keywords, so the counts overlap and cannot sum to the catalog however they are presented; a blank row there would look like it completed an arithmetic it cannot complete. tst_catalog::blankCategoryItemAccountsForEveryImage pins the rest.

COUNTS IN THE CATALOG SCOPE ARE LIBRARY TOTALS, and the footer says so. Per-item counts under the live query would be one GROUP BY per category on every keystroke over a quarter of a million rows -- the exact shape the debounce exists to avoid -- so both count columns carry the catalog's total rather than a filtered number that is quietly the wrong one.

THE PROXY FILTER IS NOT RUN IN CATALOG SCOPE. MW::filterChange returns early while that scope is current, because the tree is then holding the CATALOG's values: running the proxy over them would narrow the loaded set by values it has never heard of -- almost always to nothing -- while the user is reading a result count for a different question entirely. The Catalog query is FindPanel's, and its result is a set to Load or Add, not a filter.

SWITCHING SCOPE CARRIES THE TEXT AND CLEARS THE CHECKS. The text is the question, and carrying it is the hand-off in both directions: a folder search that found nothing is one click from being asked of the library, and a library search can be narrowed once it is loaded. That is also why "seed the filters from the catalog query after a load" was never a separate feature -- it is this. The category CHECKS deliberately do not carry, because the two scopes are different VOCABULARIES: a folder offers the three camera models in it, the library offers forty, and re-applying checks that merely share a name would silently build a different query from the one the user set up. Returning to Folders therefore asks MW to rebuild the tree from the datamodel (buildFilters->reset() then build()), since only MW knows whether the model is ready.

THE SEARCH BOX IS SHARED, and replaces the editable searchTrue tree item as the thing the user touches -- that item stays as the STORAGE the predicate reads, via Filters::setSearchText. F2 focuses the box in Folders and Shift+F2 focuses the SAME box in Catalog, so the pairing those two shortcuts always implied is now literally true. Folders is not debounced (the proxy runs over loaded rows only, and a delay would feel like the panel had stopped responding); Catalog is.

THE DATAMODEL-READINESS GUARDS DO NOT APPLY TO CATALOG CATEGORIES, and getting that wrong was a bug with a confusing shape. Filters::itemClickedSignal, mousePressEvent and contextMenuEvent all bail out on !G::allMetadataAttempted || buildingFilters, which is right when the items describe the DATAMODEL -- filtering a folder before its metadata is read is meaningless. It is wrong for a catalog category item, which is answerable whether or not the loaded folder has finished reading, or whether a folder is loaded at all. The symptom was not an inert checkbox: QTreeWidget toggles the box itself, in the base class, BEFORE any of this runs -- so the tick appeared and only the NOTIFICATION was swallowed, leaving the Find dock's query un-rerun and its Load button disabled next to a category item that looked checked. Filters::categorySource() is what the guards now test, set by loadCatalogCategories and showAllCategories.

THE SCOPE BUTTONS CARRY THEIR OWN STYLESHEET. The app-wide QToolButton rule (Main/widgetcss.cpp) is "background:transparent; border:none" with no :checked state, because every other tool button in Winnow is a momentary icon button rather than a toggle -- so the two scope buttons inherited a look that was identical checked or not, and a two-way switch that cannot show which way it is set has no job left. It is styled on the widget rather than in widgetcss because this is the app's only segmented toggle and a global :checked rule would light up every other checkable tool button; MW::setBackgroundShade re-applies it so it follows the theme instead of freezing at the shade in force when the panel was built.

THE CATALOG PANEL IS NOT BUILT at all under this flag: catalogDock and catalogView stay null, createCatalogDock returns early, and every entry point that used to show it -- Shift+F2, the Window menu item (relabelled "Search Catalog"), the full-screen dock set -- switches the Find dock's scope instead. The null pointers are what the guards around catalogDock are for; catalogDock is initialised to nullptr in the header because createDocks and placeDocksAddedSince both reach for it before that is decided.

G::useFindDock IS AN ESCAPE HATCH, not a shelved feature. False rebuilds both original panels exactly as they were, which is what makes this reversible without a revert. It is read at dock-creation time, so changing it takes a restart.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > Scope: Catalog Beside the Folders (One Choice, Three Views)

TWO PARADIGMS IN ONE APP. Selecting a folder showed pictures; selecting the catalog showed a NUMBER and a Load button. Worse, the catalog was reachable only from inside the Find dock -- grep -i catalog over File/ returned nothing -- so it was invisible to anyone who did not already know the panel existed, even though it is a SCOPE, and scope is chosen in the tree. Three changes make it one paradigm: the catalog is offered where scope is chosen, an empty catalog scope shows images rather than a count, and results load without being asked.

G::scope IS THE ONE FACT, and MW::setScope is the only thing that changes it. It has three views -- the Catalog row above the Folders tree, the same row above Bookmarks, and the Find dock's Folders|Catalog buttons -- and every entry point (either row, either button, F2, Shift+F2, and selecting a folder) routes through setScope, which pushes the result back to all of them. The panel used to own the scope privately, which is why the trees and the panel could disagree about what the user was looking at. Selecting a folder IS selecting the Folders scope, so folderSelectionChange calls setScope first; setScope early-returns when the scope has not changed, so an ordinary folder click costs nothing.

IT RE-ASSERTS THE ROWS EVEN WHEN THE SCOPE DID NOT CHANGE. They are checkable QToolButtons, so clicking the Catalog row while Catalog is ALREADY current toggles it off before setScope runs -- and an early return there would leave the row unchecked while the catalog was still the scope. The push happens before the changed-check for that reason. setChecked emits toggled, not clicked, and the rows are connected on clicked, so putting them back cannot loop.

THE ROW IS NOT A ROW OF EITHER TREE, deliberately (Views/catalogscoperow.h). The Folders tree is a QTreeView over a QFileSystemModel, which is filesystem-backed and cannot carry a synthetic row without overriding its node structure; Bookmarks is a QTreeWidget where a real item would be draggable, deletable and indistinguishable from a bookmark. A widget above each tree reads as the first row, belongs to neither model and interferes with neither. The dock's widget becomes a plain container holding the row and the tree; the DOCK keeps its objectName, so a WindowState saved before the row existed still restores -- Qt keys dock state on the dock, not on what it contains, which is also why no winnowStateVersion bump was needed.

IT CARRIES THE CATALOGUED COUNT ("Catalog 84,102"), which answers "is there anything in there?" without opening the panel -- the discoverability point of putting it in the tree at all. A count of -1 (the index will not open) shows the bare name: an unopened index and an empty one are different facts. MW::updateCatalogScopeRows pushes it, from the same places that refresh the panel plus once at the end of initialisation, since the catalog opens lazily and a count taken earlier reads as "no index".

AN EMPTY CATALOG QUERY NOW SHOWS THE MOST RECENT IMAGES. It used to report nothing and disable Load, reasoning that offering to load a quarter of a million images is not a useful answer to having typed nothing. That reasoning holds for LOADING, and kResultLimit still caps the result -- but it made Catalog an empty room, which is the paradigm split in miniature. Catalog::search already orders by captured DESC, so the empty query needs no new SQL; the footer says which window of the library is on offer rather than implying it is a result set.

RESULTS LOAD WITHOUT BEING ASKED, which is what actually retires the Load button. Picking a folder shows pictures, so picking Catalog -- or narrowing it -- must too. Load stays for an explicit reload; ADD stays because it is the one thing this cannot do for the user, since combining two searches is a decision rather than a default. The auto-load is guarded three ways, because a load is a full model reset plus a metadata read and must not run per keystroke: only when the result set actually CHANGED, only up to kAutoLoadMax, and only after the 250 ms debounce has expired. kAutoLoadMax is a separate constant from kResultLimit even though they are equal today, because they answer different questions -- how much may be SHOWN, and how much may be REPLACED unasked -- and the second is the one to lower if a load ever feels heavy.

THE FOLDER STAYS SELECTED IN THE TREE while the catalog is the scope. It is still the folder that was loaded, and clearing the selection would leave the user with no way back to it except by finding it again; the lit Catalog row is what says which of the two is current.

VERIFIED BY DRIVING IT, not by reading it. A temporary probe at the --selftest exit reported the scope, both rows' checked state and the count, then flipped scope both ways: Folders with both rows unchecked and "Catalog 1,174" showing, then Catalog with BOTH rows checked and 40 images loaded and metadata read without a Load click, then Folders again with both unchecked. The 40-of-1,174 is addPaths doing its job -- the catalog is an index, not a guarantee, so it stats each path and skips what is no longer there.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > Filter Exclusion (Include This, Not That)

WHAT IT IS FOR. Flattening the keyword hierarchy made ancestors searchable but made a name used under two parents mean two things: "Location|Canada|BC|Vancouver" and "Location|USA|Washington|Vancouver" collapse to one keyword. Exclusion is how that is taken apart -- include Vancouver, exclude USA -- and it is why flattening is affordable at all. It applies to EVERY filter category, not just keywords: the predicate change is the same size, and excluding a folder or a camera model is immediately useful.

THREE STATES ON ONE CHECKBOX. Qt::Checked includes, Qt::PartiallyChecked excludes, Qt::Unchecked is "don't care". PartiallyChecked was chosen over a parallel data role because the ~29 existing checkState call sites overwhelmingly test == Qt::Checked and are therefore already correct; only four treated the state as a bool or as != Qt::Unchecked and needed fixing (Filters::isOnlyMostRecentDayChecked, both halves of invertFilters, and the predicate itself).

THE PREDICATE (SortFilter::filterAcceptsRow). Includes are OR-ed within a category and AND-ed between categories, as they always were. An exclusion is an outright rejection evaluated ACROSS categories: if the row carries an excluded value it is out, whatever else matches. An exclusion must NOT clear isCategoryUnchecked -- a category holding only exclusions is subtracting, not narrowing, and were it to count as "this category is filtering", every row lacking any of its items would be rejected, which is the whole set.

OPT+CLICK EXCLUDES, and a plain click on something already excluded clears it. Both are handled in mousePressEvent BEFORE the base class runs, because the base class would toggle the checkbox as well and the two would fight over the same state -- and because Qt's own two-state toggle turns PartiallyChecked into Checked, silently inverting what the user asked for. Opt is the modifier the mask combine tools already use for "subtract", so the gesture is one the user has met. A right-click menu (Include / Exclude / Clear) is the discoverable path: a modifier nobody is told about is not a feature.

invertFilters LEAVES EXCLUSIONS ALONE. "Show me everything except this" has no meaningful inverse, and turning it into an inclusion would silently reverse what the user asked for -- the one filter state where guessing is worse than doing nothing.

updateZeroCountCheckedItems UNCHECKS ONLY INCLUSIONS. An exclusion of something with a zero count excludes nothing, so it cannot produce the null result that guard exists for, and clearing it would discard the user's intent the moment the value came back into the folder.

STATE TRAVELS THROUGH save/restore AND THROUGH A RENAME. Filters::ItemState carries the Qt::CheckState, not just "was it checked", and updateCategoryItems carries the state of a disappearing item onto its replacement -- an exclusion restored as an inclusion would reverse the filter on every rebuild.

CLEARING MUST RESTYLE. Exclusion is drawn on the FONT (strikethrough) as well as the checkbox, so uncheckAllFilters and clearAll call styleFilterItem rather than only setting Qt::Unchecked; otherwise a cleared filter still reads as excluded.

THE PICK/RATING/LABEL MENU ACTIONS ARE BINARY and have no exclude representation. An excluded item leaves its action unchecked and the panel stays the source of truth.

AMBIGUITY MARKING COMES FROM THE CATALOG, not from the loaded folder. Ambiguity is a property of the VOCABULARY -- "Vancouver" is two places whether or not both are in front of you -- so computing it from the datamodel would make the same keyword change colour as the user browses, and would make the Filters panel disagree with the Catalog panel about the same word. Filters::refreshAmbiguousKeywords runs on the GUI thread when a filter build completes, not on the BuildFilters worker. WITH NO CATALOG NOTHING IS MARKED, and the category tooltip says so: that is not the same as "nothing is ambiguous", and colouring nothing while implying we had checked would be a quiet lie.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > Searching Folders and Catalog (One Grammar)

TWO SEARCH BOXES THAT USED TO DISAGREE. F2 searches the DATAMODEL (Filters > Search) with QString::contains; Shift+F2 searches the INDEX (the Catalog panel) with FTS5. The shortcuts are deliberately paired to read as "search the folders" and "search the catalog", which promises the same words mean the same thing -- and they did not. "heron OR eagle" found images in one and nothing in the other, because to contains() it was a single literal string containing the word OR.

Utilities/searchterms.h parses the text ONCE and each engine compiles the result its own way. Neither box owns the grammar, so a term type added there reaches both at the same time. The grammar is what a search box is expected to do: bare words AND-ed, OR between alternatives, quoted phrases, and -term or NOT term to exclude. AND is accepted and ignored. OR and NOT are recognised in UPPER CASE only, so a caption reading "black or white" is searched for as written rather than parsed.

ONE DIFFERENCE REMAINS AND IS DOCUMENTED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER: the datamodel matches a SUBSTRING and the index matches a PREFIX, because that is what each engine is good at -- contains() over a few thousand loaded rows is free, while an infix search over a quarter of a million FTS rows cannot use the index at all. So "eron" finds Heron in the Folders scope and not in the Catalog scope. The narrowing direction is the same in both, which is what the pairing actually promises.

PARSED ABOVE THE LOOP. DataModel::searchStringChange runs over every row on the GUI thread while the user types, so the parse happens once before the loop, not per row. Whitespace-only text parses to no terms, which would match every row and light the Search filter up for a query that asked for nothing -- so it is treated as no search, like the empty string beside it.

NEGATION NEEDS A SUBQUERY ON THE INDEX SIDE. FTS5's NOT is binary and MATCH has no "everything" token, so "-heron" has no left-hand side to subtract from. Catalog::search applies negatives as a separate NOT EXISTS against image_fts, which works whether or not there is anything positive to go with it; SearchTerms::hasPositive is how it knows. Several negatives are OR-ed into that one subquery, because an image carrying any of them must be excluded.

A QUOTE NEVER SURVIVES INTO A TERM -- the tokenizer treats every one as a phrase delimiter, opening or closing, and flushes whatever was accumulating. Both halves of that matter: without the closing flush '""' would merge the words either side of it, and without the opening one 'say"what more"' would glue 'say' onto the phrase and search for something nobody typed. The quote-doubling in the FTS compilation is therefore defensive rather than load-bearing, and tst_searchterms pins that so it starts earning its place if the tokenizer ever changes.


⏺ Keywords and Cataloguing > Cataloguing Designated Roots (the Background Scanner)

WHY THE OPPORTUNISTIC CAPTURE IS NOT ENOUGH. Opening a folder catalogues it, which is free -- the metadata has just been read anyway. But that only ever covers folders the user has visited, so search on a fresh install finds nothing and there is no way to say "index my library". Main/catalogscanner.cpp is that way: a list of nominated roots, walked in the background.

THE ROOT LIST LIVES IN QSettings, NOT IN THE INDEX DATABASE. Everything else about the catalog is derived and rebuildable, which is what lets CacheDb::moveAside discard the file without asking when it will not open. The root list is the one piece that is USER INTENT, and losing "these are the folders I want indexed" to a corrupt index is a different kind of loss from discarding a rebuildable index. Keys: catalogRoots, catalogRootsRecurse.

IT READS METADATA AND NOTHING ELSE -- no icon, no decode, no preview. A catalog row is text; rendering anything would turn a background index into hours of GPU work. isLoadXmp is passed TRUE, because the keywords are the entire point: without it dc:subject and lr:hierarchicalSubject are never read and every row would be catalogued blank.

IT OWNS ITS OWN Metadata INSTANCE, created on the scanner thread rather than in the constructor (which runs on the GUI thread, before moveToThread). Metadata holds one reusable ImageMetadata and one parser set and is not safe to share, which is why Cache/reader.cpp does the same.

ONE LOW-PRIORITY THREAD, NOT A POOL, and that is the point rather than an economy. Browsing must not get slower because an index is being built, so the scan gives way rather than competing: it pauses whenever the datamodel is being modified and resumes when the load finishes.

G::stop IS A PAUSE HERE, NOT AN ABORT, and getting that wrong is the subtle failure. G::stop is a TRANSIENT teardown latch -- MW::stop sets it and clears it around every folder change, and the memory-overrun path sets it while it drains. A scanner that treated it as an abort would die the first time the user clicked a folder, so a library scan could never finish on a session anybody was actually using. Pausing gives those callers what they want (no further allocation from here while they tear down) without throwing the pass away. Only an explicit Stop, or the object being destroyed, actually ends a scan.

IT SKIPS WORK ALREADY DONE, which is what makes "Scan Now" cheap enough to offer as a button. Each folder is stat'd into bare CatalogRows (path, size, mtime, sidecar mtime -- no parsing) and handed to Catalog::staleOf; only what comes back is opened. A rescan of an unchanged root is therefore one stat per file and no parsing at all. tst_catalog::staleOfSkipsUnchangedOnRescan pins it.

COMMITS ARE CHUNKED (200 rows) rather than one transaction per folder. A folder can hold tens of thousands of images, and the whole batch is lost if the app quits mid-scan; chunking bounds what a quit costs to a few hundred files re-stat'd. Nothing is lost either way -- the scan resumes from staleOf.

PROGRESS IS A STATUS-BAR ROW (progressCatalogRow), beside Metadata, Dev Previews and Raw Denoise. Minutes to hours of reading the user is not waiting on does not belong under a popup parked over the pictures. The row counts FOLDERS, not files: the file count is not known until each folder is enumerated, and a total that kept growing would make the bar run backwards. A background scan finishes silently -- the row already said it was happening and the panel shows the result.

REMOVING A ROOT DOES NOT UN-CATALOGUE WHAT IT CONTRIBUTED. Those images are still real and still findable, and silently dropping thousands of rows because a folder was taken off the scan list would be a surprising amount of deletion for an action that reads as "stop scanning this". Sweep still demotes them if they actually disappear.

THE ROOTS EDITOR IS ITS OWN DIALOG (Dialogs/catalogrootsdlg.h), not part of the Catalog panel. It was part of it, and it took the lower third of a panel whose whole job is asking questions: editing the root list is CONFIGURATION, done once and revisited rarely, while the panel above it is used constantly, so the two do not belong in one column competing for the same vertical space. It opens from the panel's Manage button and from Preferences > Catalog -- the latter because Preferences is where a user looks for "what does Winnow index".

IT IS A Qt::Tool WINDOW AND NOT MODAL. A scan runs for minutes to hours and this is where its state is shown; a modal dialog would either block the app for the duration or have to be closed to watch it. It is created on first use and kept, so it survives a scan and can be raised again.

A VARIABLE-LENGTH FOLDER LIST IS NOT A PROPERTY, which is why Preferences holds only the ENTRY POINT rather than the editor. PropertyEditor is a tree of scalar values; the Embellish borders panel shows what dynamic children cost, and a folder list with add/remove does not earn that machinery.

THE MANAGE BUTTON STAYS ENABLED WHEN THE CATALOG IS EMPTY, even though the search half is disabled with its inline explanation. It is how the user FILLS it -- disabling it would leave no way out of "nothing is catalogued yet", which is the exact trap the disable-with-a-reason rule exists to avoid. It is disabled only when the database will not open at all, where scanning genuinely cannot achieve anything.

WHILE A SCAN RUNS the dialog's button becomes Stop and its root list is frozen, and the panel's Manage button is disabled: editing the roots underneath a running scan would leave the user unsure whether the folder they just added is being scanned or not.
